About this edition

An English rendering of Tārīkh Khalīfa ibn Khayyāṭ, arranged by period, year and subject. This is a machine translation and has not had a scholarly review.

Chapter 1

Prologue: how the years are counted

In the name of God, Most Gracious, Most Merciful.

May God bless Muhammad and his family and grant them peace.

Khalīfa ibn Khayyāṭ said: This is the book of history. Through history people have known the matter of their pilgrimage and fasting, the completion of their wives’ waiting periods, and the due dates of their debts. God ﵎ says to His Prophet Muhammad ﷺ: “They ask you about the new moons. Say: They are appointed times for people and for the pilgrimage.” Khalīfa said: Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ narrated to us; he said: Saʿīd narrated to us, from Qatāda, concerning “They ask you about the new moons. Say: They are appointed times for people and for the pilgrimage.” He said: They asked why these new moons had been made, so God sent down what you hear: “They are appointed times for people and for the pilgrimage.” God made them for the fasting and breaking of the fast of Muslims, their pilgrimage and rites, the reckoning of their wives’ waiting periods, and the due dates of their debts, among other things. God knows best what is good for His creation. He said: “We made the night and the day two signs, then We erased the sign of the night and made the sign of the day luminous, so that you may seek bounty from your Lord and know the number of years and the reckoning.” And in another verse He said: “He is the One who made the sun a radiance and the moon a light, and determined for it phases— so that you may know the number of years and the reckoning. God did not create that except in truth; He sets out the signs for a people who know.

Khalīfa narrated to us; he said: Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad al-Kaʿbī narrated to me, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿImrān, who said: People have always had a dating system. In ancient times they dated from Adam’s descent from Paradise. That continued until God sent Noah, and they dated from Noah’s call to his people. Then they dated from the Flood. That continued until Abraham was burned, and they dated from Abraham’s burning. The descendants of Ismāʿīl dated from the building of the Kaʿba.

Khalīfa said: Muḥammad ibn Muʿāwiya narrated to me, from Abū ʿUbayda Maʿmar ibn al-Muthannā, who said: The Persians have always had a dating system by which they know their affairs. Their reckoning dates from the reign of Yazdegerd son of Shahriyār, and continues to this day. That was in year sixteen after the Hijra of the Messenger of God ﷺ and it is the dating people use today. He said: The Children of Israel have another dating system, by the years of Dhū al-Qarnayn. Khalīfa said: Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad al-Kaʿbī narrated to me; he said: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿImrān narrated to me; he said: The descendants of Ismāʿīl ibn Abraham dated from the building of the Kaʿba. That continued until Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy died, and they dated from his death. That continued until the Year of the Elephant, and they dated from the Year of the Elephant. Thereafter the Muslims dated from the emigration of the Messenger of God ﷺ. The Arabs also had a dating system— ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maslama ibn Qaʿnab and Isḥāq ibn Idrīs narrated to us; the two said: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Abī Ḥāzim narrated to us, from his father, from Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī, who said: People got the reckoning wrong. They did not count from the mission of the Messenger of God ﷺ nor from his death; they counted only from his arrival in Medina.

He said: ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn ʿAbd al-Aʿlā informed us; he said: Nāqira ibn Khālid, from Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn, said: An official said to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, “Will you not establish a date?” They wanted to establish a date, and said, “From the mission of the Messenger of God ﷺ or from his death?” Then they agreed to make it from his emigration. They wanted to begin with the month of Ramaḍān, then decided to set it in al-Muḥarram. Kathīr ibn Hishām narrated to us; he said: Jaʿfar ibn Barqān narrated to us, from Maymūn ibn Mihrān, who said: The Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ deliberated over how to write the dating. Some said, “Let us date it from the birth of the Messenger of God ﷺ.” Others said, “From when revelation came to him.” Others said, “From his emigration, when he left the abode of associationism for the abode of faith.” Their opinion then agreed that they should date it from his emigration.

Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr narrated to us; he said: Ḥibbān narrated to us, from Mujālid, from ʿĀmir, who said: Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī wrote to ʿUmar, “Letters come to us, and we do not know their dates.” ʿUmar consulted the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ. Some said, “From the mission,” and others said, “From his death.” ʿUmar said, “Date from his emigration, for his place of emigration separated truth from falsehood.”

Khalīfa narrated to us; he said: Isḥāq ibn Idrīs narrated to us; he said: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Muḥammad narrated to us; he said: ʿUthmān ibn ʿUbayd Allāh narrated to us, from Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab, who said: ʿUmar gathered the Emigrants and the Helpers and said, “From what point shall I write the dating?” ʿAlī said to him, “From when the Messenger of God ﷺ left the land of associationism; that is the day he emigrated.” So ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb recorded that.

Khalīfa narrated to us; he said: Bakr ibn Sulaymān narrated to us; he said: Ibn Isḥāq and Wahb ibn Jarīr narrated to us, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays ibn Makhrama ibn al-Muṭṭalib narrated to me, from his father, from his grandfather, Qays ibn Makhrama, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ and I were born in the Year of the Elephant.

He said: Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad narrated to me; he said: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿImrān narrated to us; he said: al-Zubayr ibn Mūsā narrated to me, from Abū al-Ḥuwayrith, who said: I was present when ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān said to Qubāth ibn Ashyam, “Are you older, or the Messenger of God?” He said, “He is greater than I, but I am older in years than he.” He asked, “When were you born?” He replied, “My mother stood me by the elephant’s dung, which had weathered, when I was old enough to recognize it. The Messenger of God ﷺ was born in the Year of the Elephant.”

He said: ʿAbd al-Salām ibn Muṭahhar narrated to me; he said: Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān narrated to us; he said: Bisṭām ibn Muslim al-ʿAwḏī narrated to us, from Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Kurayz, who said: A client of Hudhayl narrated to me; he said: I was passing by, leading my master, while ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān was seated among his companions. They said to him, “Commander of the Faithful, this is the oldest of the Arabs.” ʿUthmān said, “If he tells me how old he was on the Day of the Elephant, I will tell him how old he is.”

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: It is related from Mūsā ibn ʿUqba, who said: He was born thirty years after the Year of the Elephant.

He said: And Abū Zakariyyā al-ʿAjlānī said: “Forty years after the Elephant.” The agreed account is that it was the Year of the Elephant.

He said: Bakkār related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Bakr related to me, from ʿAmrah, from ʿĀʾishah, who said: “I saw the elephant’s guide and its keeper, both blind, begging for food.”

Wahb related to us, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq; and Bakkār, from Ibn Isḥāq, from al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays ibn Makhramah, who said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ was born in the Year of the Elephant.” He said: “Between the Elephant and the sacrilegious war (al-Fijār) were twenty years; between al-Fijār and the building of the Kaʿbah were fifteen years; and between the building of the Kaʿbah and the commissioning of the Messenger of God ﷺ were five years. The Messenger of God ﷺ was commissioned at five years”—then he repeated: “The Messenger of God ﷺ was commissioned when he was forty years old.”

He said: Shuʿayb ibn Ḥayyān related to us, from ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn Abī ʿAmr, from al-Kalbī, from his father Ṣāliḥ, from Ibn ʿAbbās: “The Prophet ﷺ was born on a Monday, fifteen years before the Elephant.”

He said: Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ related to us, saying: Yūnus ibn ʿUbayd related to us, from ʿAmmār ibn Abī ʿAmmār, the client of Banū Hāshim, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ was commissioned when he was forty. He stayed in Mecca for five years in concealment and ten openly, and in Medina for ten years.”

He said: Abū Dāwūd related to us, saying: Ḥarb ibn Shaddād related to us, from Yaḥyā ibn Abī Kathīr, from Abū Salamah, that ʿĀʾishah and Ibn ʿAbbās informed him that the Messenger of God ﷺ stayed in Mecca ten years while revelation was being sent to him, and in Medina ten years.

Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ related to us, saying: Saʿīd related to us, from Qatādah, from al-Ḥasan, who said: “Revelation was sent down upon the Prophet ﷺ for eight years in Mecca and ten in Medina.” Abū ʿĀṣim related to us, from Ashʿath, from al-Ḥasan, who said: “He was commissioned when he was forty-five, stayed in Mecca ten years and in Medina eight, and died when he was sixty-three.” Yazīd related to us, from Saʿīd, who said: Qatādah said: “He stayed in Mecca ten years and in Medina ten.” Abū ʿUbaydah related to me, from Ḥammād ibn Salamah, from Abū Ḥamzah, from Ibn ʿAbbās: “He stayed in Mecca thirteen years and in Medina ten.” Ziyād ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bakkāʾī related to us, saying: Ḥajjāj related to us, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar: “The Messenger of God ﷺ stayed in Medina ten years.” ʿAbd al-Wahhāb related to us, from Hishām, from ʿIkrimah, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said: “He stayed in Mecca thirteen years and in Medina ten.” Hishām said: Al-Ḥasan said: “He stayed in Mecca ten years and in Medina ten years.”

Chapter 2

The age of the Prophet

The Year 1The Prophet ﷺ arrives in Medina

Bakkār ibn Sulaymān related to us, saying: Ibn Isḥāq and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, related to us. He said: Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn al-Zubayr related to me, from ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿUwaym ibn Sāʿidah, who said: “Men from among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ related to me. They said: ‘When we heard that the Messenger of God ﷺ had set out from Mecca, we eagerly awaited his arrival. We used to go out after we had prayed the dawn prayer, to the open ground by our lava field, waiting for the Messenger of God ﷺ. By God, we would not leave until the sun overcame us. When the day came on which the Messenger of God ﷺ arrived, we sat until no shade remained, then went into our houses. The Messenger of God ﷺ arrived. The first to see him was a Jewish man, who cried out at the top of his voice: ‘O Banū Qaylah, here is your grandfather; he has come!’ We went out to the Messenger of God ﷺ while he was in the shade of a palm tree, and Abū Bakr was with him, about his age. Most of us had not seen the Messenger of God. Abū Bakr stood and shaded him, and we recognized the Messenger of God ﷺ. Then people crowded around him; before that we had not …; then people crowded around him, and we could not tell him apart from Abū Bakr until the shade moved away from the Messenger of God ﷺ.’”

Ibn Isḥāq said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ arrived in Medina on Monday, when the forenoon was well advanced, after twelve nights of the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal had passed.”

Ibn Isḥāq said: “He alighted at Qubāʾ with Kulthūm ibn Hidm, the brother of Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf. It is also said that he alighted with Saʿd ibn Khaythamah. He stayed among Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, and Thursday, and laid the foundations of their mosque. He left Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf, and Friday overtook him among Banū Sālim ibn ʿAwf. He performed the Friday prayer in the mosque that is in the bottom of the valley.”

Ibn Isḥāq said: “Then the Messenger of God ﷺ stayed with Abū Ayyūb Khālid ibn Zayd al-Anṣārī, and the Messenger of God ﷺ ordered that his mosque be built in that year.”

The Messenger of God ﷺ stayed in Abū Ayyūb’s house until he had built his mosque and dwellings; then he moved ﷺ. Ibn Isḥāq said: “In this year, the year one, Abū Umāmah Asʿad ibn Zurārah died. Al-dhabḥah seized him during those months, while the mosque was being built.” Ibn Isḥāq said: “In that year ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salām accepted Islam, and in it ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zayd saw the call to prayer. The Messenger of God ﷺ ordered Bilāl to announce the call to prayer.” This is according to what Bakkār related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn al-Ḥārith related to me, from his father.

The Year 2

The Campaign of al-Abwāʾ

In it was the campaign of al-Abwāʾ, the first campaign on which the Messenger of God ﷺ went forth. Bakkār related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: “The first campaign on which the Messenger of God ﷺ went forth: he left Medina after twelve nights of Ṣafar in the year two had passed, until he reached Waddān, intending to go against Quraysh and Banū Ḍamrah. Makhshī ibn ʿAmr al-Ḍamrī made a truce with him. This was the campaign of al-Abwāʾ.”

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to me, from his shaykhs, who said: “He set out on Monday in Ṣafar and returned at the beginning of Rabīʿ al-Awwal. It is four stages from Medina to Mecca.”

The Campaign of Buwāṭ. Ibn Isḥāq said: “In it he campaigned at Buwāṭ, in the direction of Raḍwā, in the month of Rabīʿ al-Ākhir, seeking to intercept a Quraysh caravan, but he encountered no resistance.” ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: “He set out on Tuesday, after three nights of Rabīʿ al-Ākhir had passed, and returned after ten nights had passed. It is three stages from Medina by the road to Syria.”

The Campaign of al-ʿUshayrah. Ibn Isḥāq said: “Then he campaigned in the campaign of al-ʿUshayrah in Jumādā al-Ūlā, but encountered no resistance, and made a truce with Banū Mudlij.” ʿAlī said: “He set out at the beginning of Jumādā al-Ūlā and returned when five nights remained in it.”

The Campaign of Ṣafwān. Ibn Isḥāq said: “Then he went out in pursuit of Kurz ibn Jābir, who had raided the grazing livestock of Medina. The Messenger of God ﷺ went out in pursuit of him until he reached Ṣafwān, in the direction of Badr, in Jumādā al-Ākhirah. He returned in Rajab and encountered no resistance. This was the first campaign of Badr.”

The Campaign in the Region of Juhaynah. ʿAlī said: “Then he campaigned in the region of Juhaynah on Tuesday, at the midpoint of Shaʿbān, but encountered no resistance.”

The Major Campaign of Badr. Ibn Isḥāq said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ learned that Abū Sufyān was coming from Syria with a Quraysh caravan, so he set out in pursuit of him.”

Ibn Isḥāq said: “He set out in pursuit of him on Wednesday, after three nights of the month of Ramaḍān had passed. This was the campaign of Badr.”

Ibn Isḥāq said: Abū Jaʿfar Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī related to me, saying: “The battle of Badr took place on Friday, after seventeen nights of the month of Ramaḍān had passed.” Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ related to us, from Saʿīd, from Qatādah, similarly. Abū Dāwūd related to us, saying: Shuʿbah related to us, from Abū Isḥāq, from Ḥujayr, from ʿAlqamah and al-Aswad, from ʿAbd Allāh, who said: “Seek the Night of Decree on the day the two forces met, on the nineteenth or twenty-first.”

He said: Abū Nuʿaym al-Faḍl ibn Dukayn related to us, saying: ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān ibn Mawhib related to us, saying: I heard Mūsā ibn Ṭalḥah say: “Abū Ayyūb was asked about the day of Badr, and he said: ‘It was after seventeen or nineteen nights had passed, or with thirteen or eleven nights remaining.’” Khalīfah related to me, saying: Sulaymān ibn Dāwūd related to us, saying: Hushaym related to us, saying: Ḥuṣayn ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān related to us, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Shaddād, who said: “The two forces met after nineteen nights of Ramaḍān had passed.”

The Campaign of al-Kudr

Ibn Isḥāq said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ returned from Badr at the end of the month of Ramaḍān; it is also said, at the beginning of Shawwāl. He stayed in Medina several nights, then went out in person and campaigned against Banū Sulaym. He reached a watering place called al-Kudr, stayed there three nights, then returned without encountering resistance.” Ibn Isḥāq said: “He appointed Muḥammad ibn Maslamah as his deputy in it.” Ibn Isḥāq said: “He set out at the beginning of Shawwāl and returned after ten nights of Shawwāl had passed.”

The Campaign of al-Sawīq. Ibn Isḥāq said: “He set out in Dhū al-Ḥijjah.” Ibn Isḥāq said: Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn al-Zubayr and Yazīd ibn Rūmān related to me that Abū Sufyān, when he returned to Mecca and the remnants of Quraysh returned, vowed that no water of ritual impurity would touch his head until he campaigned against Muḥammad. He set out with two hundred mounted men from Quraysh. The Messenger of God ﷺ went out in pursuit of him until he reached Qarqarat al-Kudr, then turned back. They said: “Messenger of God, do you hope that we will have a campaign?” He said: “Yes.” ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: “He set out on Sunday, after seven nights of Dhū al-Ḥijjah had passed, and returned on Monday, when eight nights remained in it.”

Names of those who were martyred with the Messenger of God ﷺ at Badr

Among Quraysh, from Banū al-Muṭṭalib—then, from Banū al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Quṣayy: ʿUbaydah ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Muṭṭalib. ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah struck off his leg, and he died at al-Ṣafrāʾ.

Among Banū Zuhrah

Among Banū Zuhrah: ʿUmayr ibn Abī Waqqāṣ, killed by ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Wudd; and Dhū al-Shimālayn ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn Naḍlah, an ally of theirs from Khuzāʿah, killed by Usāmah al-Jushamī.

Among Banū ʿAdī

Among Banū ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb: ʿĀqil ibn al-Bukayr, their ally from Banū Layth, killed by Mālik ibn Zuhayr; and Mihjaʿ, the client of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. An arrow struck him and killed him.

Among Banū al-Ḥārith

Among Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr: Ṣafwān ibn Bayḍāʾ, killed by Ṭuʿaymah ibn ʿAdī.

The martyrdom of Abū Anasah. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Abī Thābit, from Dāwūd ibn Ḥuṣayn, from ʿIkrimah, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said: “Abū Anasah, the client of the Messenger of God ﷺ was martyred on the day of Badr.”

Among the Anṣār, from Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf: Saʿd ibn Khaythamah, killed by Ṭuʿaymah ibn ʿAdī; it is also said, by ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Wudd. And Mubashshir ibn ʿAbd al-Mundhir Zubayr, killed by Abū Thawr; they say “Zanbar.”

Among Banū al-Khazraj

Among Banū al-Khazraj: Yazīd ibn al-Ḥārith, who was called Ibn Fushḥam, killed by Nawfal ibn Muʿāwiyah.

Among Banū Salimah

Among Banū Salimah: ʿUmayr ibn al-Ḥumām, killed by Khālid ibn al-Aʿlam.

Among Banū Ḥabīb

Among Banū Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd Ḥārithah: Rāfiʿ ibn al-Muʿallā, killed by ʿIkrimah ibn Abī Jahl.

Among Banū ʿAdī—and among Banū ʿAdī ibn al-Najjār—was Ḥāritha ibn Surāqa ibn al-Ḥārith. Ḥibbān ibn al-ʿAraqa killed him with an arrow while he was drinking at the water-trough.

Among Banū Mālik—and among Banū Mālik ibn al-Najjār—were ʿAwf and Muʿawwidh, the two sons of ʿAfrāʾ; ʿAfrāʾ was their mother. They were the two sons of al-Ḥārith ibn Sawād. Abū Musāfiʿ killed Muʿawwidh and wounded Muʿādh ibn Māʿiṣ, one of Banū Zurayq; he died in Medina from his wound.

The expedition of ʿUbayda to the coast. Khalīfa narrated to us; he said: Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: The first expedition in which ʿUbayda ibn al-Ḥārith was sent out was in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal, with eighty mounted men—it is also said, sixty—from the Emigrants; there was not a single Anṣārī among them. He reached the coast, as far as Aḥyāʾ Māʾ in the Ḥijāz, below the pass of al-Murra. There he met a group of Quraysh, but there was no fighting between them, except that Saʿd ibn Mālik shot an arrow that day, the first arrow shot in Islam. Then the two groups withdrew from one another.

Ibn Isḥāq said: That day, al-Miqdād ibn ʿAmr, an ally of Banū Zuhra, and ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān, an ally of Banū Nawfal ibn ʿAbd Manāf, fled from the polytheists to the Muslims. They were both Muslims who had gone out in order to join the Muslims. The polytheists were led by ʿIkrima ibn Abī Jahl.

Ibn Isḥāq said: ʿUbayda’s banner was the first banner that the Messenger of God ﷺ had raised in Islam. Ibn Isḥāq said: Some scholars claim that the Messenger of God ﷺ accompanied him when he returned from the expedition to al-Abwāʾ, before he reached Medina.

The expedition of Ḥamza to the coast. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: The first expedition sent by the Messenger ﷺ was Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib’s, in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal of the year two, to the coast, from the direction of al-ʿĪṣ, in the territory of Juhayna.

He said:

Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib with thirty mounted men from the Emigrants to the coast. He met Abū Jahl with three hundred mounted men from Quraysh. Majdī ibn ʿAmr al-Juhanī, who had a truce with both parties, intervened between them. They withdrew, and there was no fighting between them. Ibn Isḥāq said: Some people claim that Ḥamza’s banner was the first banner that the Messenger of God ﷺ raised.

ʿAmr ibn al-Munkhal narrated to me; he said: A man narrated to me, from ʿĀṣim al-Aḥwal, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: The first standard that the Messenger of God ﷺ raised was for ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaḥsh, an ally of Banū Umayya.

The expedition of Saʿd ibn Mālik. Ibn Isḥāq said: Then came the expedition of Saʿd ibn Mālik with a party of eight Emigrants. He set out and reached al-Kharrār in the territory of the Ḥijāz, but encountered no hostile force. Ibn Isḥāq said: And he sent …

During his expedition to the first Badr, in Rajab, he sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaḥsh ibn Riʾāb with eight of the Emigrants to Nakhlah. A Quraysh caravan passed by him, in which was ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥaḍramī. He captured ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra and al-Ḥakam ibn Kaysān, the client of Hishām ibn al-Mughīra. This took place at the end of Rajab. Al-Ḥakam remained with the Messenger of God ﷺ until he was killed at Biʾr Maʿūna. ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra was ransomed. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaḥsh said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ is entitled to one-fifth of what you have taken as spoils.” This was before God prescribed the one-fifth. He set aside one-fifth of the caravan for the Messenger of God and divided the rest among his companions. Ibn Isḥāq said: The report about this comes from al-Zuhrī and Yazīd ibn Rūmān, from ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr.

The mission of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ghālib al-Laythī. Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ghālib al-Laythī in Shawwāl.

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: He sent him on the tenth day of Shawwāl, and he returned on the sixteenth day of it.

Reconnoitering news of Abū Sufyān’s caravan.

Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh and Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl to reconnoiter news of the caravan, and he sent Basbas ibn ʿAmr and ʿAdī ibn Abī al-Zag̲hbāʾ to learn news of Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb.

The turning of the qibla toward the Kaʿba. In this year, the year two, the qibla was turned. God says to His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ:

“We have indeed seen your face turning toward heaven, so We shall surely turn you toward a qibla that pleases you. So turn your face toward the Sacred Mosque.”

He said: Abū Dāwūd narrated to us; he said: al-Masʿūdī narrated to us, from ʿAmr ibn Murra, from Ibn Abī Laylā, from Muʿādh ibn Jabal, that the Messenger of God ﷺ [was] toward the Sanctuary of Jerusalem after his arrival in Medina for sixteen months. Then God revealed: “We have indeed seen your face turning toward heaven, so We shall surely turn you toward a qibla that pleases you.” Abū Dāwūd narrated to us; he said: Shuʿba narrated to us, from Abū Isḥāq, from al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib, who said: He ﷺ prayed toward the Sanctuary of Jerusalem for sixteen months after his arrival in Medina. Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ narrated to us, from Saʿīd, from Qatāda, the same. He said: I heard ʿAbd al-Wahhāb ibn ʿAbd al-Majīd say: I heard Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd say: I heard Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib say: He ﷺ prayed toward the Sanctuary of Jerusalem for a year and ten months, and the qibla was changed two months before Badr. Al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Makhlad narrated to us; he said: ʿUthmān ibn Saʿd al-Kātib narrated to us, from Anas ibn Mālik, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ was turned away from the Sanctuary of Jerusalem after nine or ten months. While he was praying the noon prayer in Medina, he ﷺ had prayed two rakʿas toward the Sanctuary of Jerusalem; he turned his face toward the Kaʿba. The fools said: “What has turned them away from the qibla they had been facing?” Abū al-Walīd narrated to us, from Zāʾida, from Simāk, from ʿIkrima, from Ibn ʿAbbās: after sixteen months. Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: The qibla was turned in Rajab, after seventeen months from his arrival.

Ibn Abī ʿAdī narrated to us, from Ashʿath, from al-Ḥasan, who said: He ﷺ prayed toward the Sanctuary of Jerusalem for two years, then he was commanded regarding the qibla. In that year the Messenger of God ﷺ consummated his marriage with ʿĀʾisha. Wakīʿ informed us; he said: Sufyān narrated to us, from Ismāʿīl ibn Umayya, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUrwa, from ʿUrwa, from ʿĀʾisha, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ married me in Shawwāl and consummated the marriage with me in Shawwāl. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad narrated to me, from Abū Zakariyyā al-ʿAjlānī, from Abū al-Zubayr, from Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ consummated his marriage with ʿĀʾisha after his return from Badr. In that year ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr was born, and he was the first newborn among the Emigrants. In it al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr ibn Saʿd was born. In it Ruqayya, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ, died. Ibn Isḥāq said: Ruqayya, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ, died when news came of the Messenger of God ﷺ and of what God had granted him at Badr. In it ʿAlī married Fāṭima. In it the obligation of the month of Ramaḍān was revealed. In it ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn died. Year three: the expedition to Dhū Amr, then the expedition to Baḥrān.

Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ returned from the expedition of al-Sawīq and stayed in Medina for the remainder of Dhū al-Ḥijja and Muḥarram, or close to that. Then he raided Najd, intending to go against Ghaṭafān; this was the expedition of Dhū Amr. He continued until Ṣafar began, then returned without encountering a hostile force. Then, in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal, he raided, intending to go against Quraysh and Banū Sulaym, until he reached Baḥrān.

A mine in the Ḥijāz, in the direction of al-Furʿ. He stayed until Jumādā al-Awwal had passed, then returned without encountering a hostile force.

The affair of Banū Qaynuqāʿ. Ibn Isḥāq said: Among the expeditions that took place in the interval was the affair of Banū Qaynuqāʿ. Ibn Isḥāq said: ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn Qatāda narrated to me that Banū Qaynuqāʿ were the first Jews to break what was between them and the Messenger of God ﷺ, in the period between Badr and Uḥud. The Messenger of God ﷺ besieged them until they surrendered to his judgment, whereupon the Messenger of God ﷺ handed them over to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl.

Ibn Isḥāq said: My father, Isḥāq ibn Yasār, narrated to me, from ʿUbāda ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit, who said: When the Messenger of God ﷺ fought Banū Qaynuqāʿ, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl took up their cause. They have a story whose telling would be lengthy.

In this year the Messenger of God ﷺ married Zaynab bt. Khuzayma of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa—she was “the Mother of the Poor”—in Ramaḍān. She lived with him for two or three months. In it he married Ḥafṣa bt. ʿUmar, in Shaʿbān.

In it ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān married Umm Kulthūm, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ, and in it al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib was born.

The expedition of Uḥud.

Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, from al-Zuhrī and Yazīd ibn Rūmān and others, that the Messenger of God ﷺ set out on Friday evening, on the fourteenth night of Shawwāl; the battle took place on Saturday, at the middle of Shawwāl.

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Sayf narrated to us, from Sallām ibn Abī Muṭīʿ, from Qatāda, from Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib, who said: The banner of the Messenger of God ﷺ on the day of Uḥud was a black, patterned mantle from among the garments that belonged to ʿĀʾisha. The banner of the Anṣār was called al-ʿUqāb. On his right flank was ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, and on the left flank al-Mundhir ibn ʿAmr al-Sāʿidī. Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām was over the men; it is also said, al-Miqdād. Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was at the center, and over the archers was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jubayr al-Anṣārī, with Saʿd ibn Mālik alongside him. The standard was with Muṣʿab ibn ʿUmayr, the brother of Banū ʿAbd al-Dār ibn Quṣayy. He was killed, so the Prophet of God gave it to ʿAlī. It is also said that he had three standards: the standard of the Emigrants was with Muṣʿab ibn ʿUmayr; one standard was with ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and al-Mundhir ibn ʿAmr together, with the Anṣār; and the Quraysh standard was with Ṭalḥa ibn Abī Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib killed him, and Abū Saʿd ibn Abī Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā took the standard. Saʿd ibn Mālik killed him, and ʿUthmān ibn Abī Ṭalḥa took it. Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib killed him, and Musāfiʿ ibn Ṭalḥa took it. ʿĀṣim ibn Thābit ibn Abī al-Aqlaḥ killed him, and al-Julās ibn Ṭalḥa took it. ʿĀṣim ibn Thābit also killed him.

Kilāb ibn Ṭalḥa and al-Ḥārith ibn Ṭalḥa: both were killed by Quzmān, an ally of Banū Ẓafar. Arṭāh ibn ʿAbd Shuraḥbīl ibn Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn ʿAbd al-Dār was killed by Muṣʿab ibn ʿUmayr. Abū Yazīd ibn ʿUmayr ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn ʿAbd al-Dār was killed by Quzmān. Ṣuʾāb, a black enslaved man belonging to them, was killed by Quzmān.

Ibn Isḥāq said: The standard remained with no one taking it, and Quraysh was routed. Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbbād ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr narrated to me, from his father, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr, who said: By God, I saw Hind and her companions with their garments hitched up, fleeing. Nothing, little or much, stood between us and seizing them, until I saw anklets on her legs, when the archers turned toward the camp after we had driven the people from it. They wanted to plunder and left our rear exposed to the cavalry, which came upon us from behind. A caller shouted: “Indeed, Muḥammad has been killed!” We turned back, and the people turned back upon us, after we had struck down the standard-bearers, so that no one among the people would come near it.

The names of those who were martyred with the Messenger of God ﷺ on the day of Uḥud.

Among Banū Hāshim: among Banū Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Quṣayy was Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, killed by Waḥshī, the boy of Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim.

Among Banū Umayya: among Banū Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaḥsh ibn Riʾāb, their confederate, from Banū Asad ibn Khuzayma.

Among Banu ʿAbd al-Dār: Muṣʿab ibn ʿUmayr ibn Hāshim, killed by Ibn Qumayʾa al-Laythī.

Among Banu Makhzūm: Shammās ibn ʿUthmān ibn al-Sharīd, one of the Anṣār. Among the Anṣār, then among Banu ʿAbd al-Ashhal: ʿAmr ibn Muʿādh ibn al-Nuʿmān; al-Ḥārith ibn Anas ibn Rāfiʿ; ʿUmāra ibn Ziyād ibn al-Sakan; Salama ibn Thābit ibn Waqsh; ʿAmr ibn Thābit ibn Waqsh. Isḥāq said: ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn Qatāda claimed to me that their father, Thābit, was killed that day. Also: Rifāʿa ibn Waqsh; Ḥusayl ibn Jābir—who was al-Yamān, Abū Ḥudhayfa. The Muslims struck him in the battle, not knowing who he was, so Ḥudhayfa gave his blood-money in charity to whoever had struck him; Ṣayfī ibn Qayẓī; Ḥubāb ibn Qayẓī; ʿAbbād ibn Sahl; and al-Ḥārith ibn Aws ibn Muʿādh: twelve men.

Among Banu Ẓafar: Yazīd ibn Ḥāṭib ibn Umayya ibn Rāfiʿ: one man.

From Rātij: among the people of Rātij, Iyās ibn Aws ibn ʿAtīk ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn Zaʿūrāʾ ibn Jushm ibn ʿAbd al-Ashhal; ʿUbayd ibn al-Tayyihān; and Ḥabīb ibn Zayd ibn Tamīm: three men.

Among Banu Ḍayʿa: among Banu ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf, then among Ḍubayʿa ibn Zayd, Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Qays ibn Zayd; and Ḥanẓala ibn Abī ʿĀmir ibn Ṣayfī, who was washed by the angels, killed by Shaddād ibn al-Aswad ibn Shaʿūb: two men.

Among Banu ʿUbayd: among Banu ʿUbayd ibn Zayd, Anīs ibn Qatāda: one man.

Among Banu Thaʿlaba: among Banu Thaʿlaba ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf, Abū Ḥayya, who was a maternal half-brother of Saʿd ibn Khaythama; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jubayr ibn al-Nuʿmān, commander of the archers: two men.

Among Banu Salima: among Banu Salima, Ibn Imriʾ al-Qays ibn Mālik ibn al-Aws: Khaythama, the father of Saʿd ibn Khaythama: one man.

Among Banu al-ʿAjlān: among their allies, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salama: one man.

Among Banu Muʿāwiya: among Banu Muʿāwiya ibn Mālik, Subayʿ ibn Ḥāṭib ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Qays ibn Haysha.

Among Banu al-Najjār: among Banu al-Najjār, then among Banu Sawād ibn Mālik, ʿAmr ibn Qays; his son Qays ibn ʿAmr; Thābit ibn ʿAmr ibn Zayd; and ʿĀmir ibn Makhlad: four men.

Among Banu ʿAmr ibn Mabdhūl: among Banu ʿAmr ibn Mabdhūl, Abū Hubayra ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAlqama ibn ʿAmr ibn Thaqf ibn Mālik ibn Mabdhūl; and ʿAmr ibn Muṭarrif ibn ʿAlqama ibn ʿAmr: two men.

Among Banu ʿAmr ibn Mālik: among Banu ʿAmr ibn Mālik, Aws ibn Thābit ibn al-Mundhir: one man.

Among Banu ʿAdī: among Banu ʿAdī ibn al-Najjār, Anas ibn al-Naḍr ibn Ḍamḍam ibn Zayd ibn Ḥarām: one man.

Among Banu Māzin: among Banu Māzin ibn al-Najjār, Qays ibn Makhlad and Kaysān, a slave belonging to them: two men.

Among Banu Dīnār: among Banu Dīnār ibn al-Najjār, Sulaym ibn al-Ḥārith.

Among Banu al-Ḥārith: among Banu al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj, Khārija ibn Zayd Abū Zuhayr and Saʿd ibn al-Rabīʿ ibn ʿAmr ibn Abī Zuhayr, buried in one grave; and Aws ibn al-Arqam ibn Zayd ibn Qays: three men.

Among Banu al-Abjar: among Banu al-Abjar, who are Banu Khudra, Mālik ibn Sinān ibn ʿUbayd ibn Thaʿlaba ibn ʿAbd ibn al-Abjar, who was the father of Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī; Saʿd ibn Suwayd ibn Qays; and ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿ ibn Rāfiʿ: three men.

Among Banu Sāʿida: among Banu Sāʿida ibn Kaʿb ibn al-Khazraj, Thaʿlaba ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik and Thaqf ibn Farwa ibn al-Badī: two men. They say: al-Birk.

Among Banu Ṭarīf: among Banu Ṭarīf, the clan of Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Wahb ibn Thaʿlaba; and Ḍamra, an ally of theirs from Juhayna: two men.

Among Banu ʿAwf: among Banu ʿAwf ibn al-Khazraj, then among Banu Sālim, then among Banu Mālik ibn al-ʿAjlān ibn Zayd ibn Ghanm ibn Sālim, Nawfal ibn ʿAbd Allāh; ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbāda ibn Naḍla ibn Mālik ibn al-ʿAjlān; Nuʿmān ibn Mālik ibn Thaʿlaba; and al-Mujadhdhar ibn Dhiyāl, an ally of theirs from Baliyy, killed by al-Ḥārith ibn Suwayd ibn al-Ṣāmit, who then went to Mecca as an unbeliever; and ʿAbbād ibn al-Ḥasḥās, buried together with Nuʿmān ibn Mālik and al-Mujadhdhar ibn Dhiyāl in one grave: five men.

Among Banu Sāʿida: among Banu Sāʿida ibn Kaʿb ibn al-Khazraj, Thaʿlaba ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik.

Among Banu al-Ḥubلى: from al-Ḥubلى, Rifāʿa ibn ʿUmar: one man.

Among Banu Salima: among Banu Salima, then among Banu Ḥarām, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām, the father of Jābir, killed by Usāma al-Aʿwar ibn ʿUbayd—though it is said, ibn Zayd—of Banu al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd; and ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ, both buried in one grave; Khallād ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ; and Abū Ayman, the freedman of ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ: four men.

Among Banu Sawād: among Banu Sawād ibn Ghanm, Sulaym ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥadīda; the client woman of ʿAntara; and Sahl ibn Qays ibn Abī Kaʿb ibn al-Qayn: three men.

Among Banu Zurayq: among Banu Zurayq ibn ʿĀmir, Dhakwān ibn ʿAbd Qays: one man; and ʿUbayd ibn al-Muʿallā ibn Lawdhān. The total number of Muslims martyred with the Messenger of God ﷺ—from the Emigrants and the Anṣār was sixty-five men. Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ related to us, from Saʿīd, from Qatāda, from Anas ibn Mālik, who said: “Seventy men were martyred that day.”

The Messenger’s departure ﷺ—to Ḥamrāʾ al-Asad

Ibn Isḥāq said: When the day after Uḥud came, Sunday, with sixteen nights of Shawwāl having passed, a caller of the Messenger of God ﷺ—announced the pursuit of the enemy. His caller announced: “No one is to go out with us except one who was present with us yesterday.” Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām spoke to him and said: “My father had left me in charge of my sisters.” The Messenger of God ﷺ—gave him permission, and he went out with him.

He said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—set out and reached Ḥamrāʾ al-Asad, which is eight miles from Medina. He stayed there on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday, then returned to Medina. Ibn Isḥāq said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Bakr related to me: Maʿbad al-Khuzāʿī passed by the Messenger of God ﷺ—while he was, that day, a polytheist. He said: “O Muḥammad, by God, what has befallen you has grieved us.” Then Maʿbad set out while the Messenger of God ﷺ—was at Ḥamrāʾ al-Asad, until he met Abū Sufyān and those with him at al-Rawḥāʾ. They had resolved to return to the Messenger of God ﷺ—and said: “We struck down the best of his companions, their leaders and their notables.” When Abū Sufyān saw Maʿbad, he said: “What is the news, Maʿbad?” He replied: “Muḥammad has set out with a force such as I have never seen; they burn with fury against you. Those who had stayed behind on your day have joined him.” So Abū Sufyān and those with him turned back.

The Day of al-Rajīʿ, including the affair of al-Rajīʿ

Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq; and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn Qatāda related to me: A delegation from ʿAḍal and al-Qāra came to the Messenger of God ﷺ—after Uḥud. They said: “Messenger of God, there is Islam among us. Send with us some of your companions to instruct us in religion, recite the Qurʾān to us, and teach us the ordinances of Islam.” The Messenger of God ﷺ—sent with them six men from among his companions, including: Marthad ibn Abī Marthad al-Ghanawī, an ally of Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib; Khālid ibn al-Bukayr al-Laythī, an ally of Banu ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb; ʿĀṣim ibn Thābit ibn Abī al-Aqlaḥ, of Banu ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf; and Khubayb ibn ʿAdī, of Banu Jaḥjaba ibn

Kulfa ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf; Zayd ibn al-Dathina, of Banu Bayāḍa ibn ʿĀmir; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṭāriq, an ally of Banu Ẓafar. The Messenger of God appointed Marthad ibn Abī Marthad over the group. They went out with the delegation until they reached al-Rajīʿ, a watering place belonging to Hudhayl. On the Ḥijāz side, at the head of al-Hadʾa, Hudhayl treacherously set upon them, while Marthad, Khālid ibn al-Bukayr, and ʿĀṣim ibn Thābit had let their guard down. They fought and were killed. As for Khubayb, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṭāriq, and Zayd ibn al-Dathina, they surrendered and were taken captive. They then took them toward Mecca. When they reached al-Ẓahrān, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṭāriq pulled his hand out of the bonds, seized his sword, and they pelted him with stones until they killed him. His grave is at al-Ẓahrān. They brought Khubayb and Zayd ibn al-Dathina to Mecca and sold them. Ḥujayr ibn Abī Ihāb al-Tamīmī, an ally of Banu Nawfal, bought Khubayb. As for Zayd, Ṣafwān ibn Umayya bought him. He was killed ﵀. As for Khubayb, they brought him to al-Tanʿīm to crucify him. He said: “If you would allow me to perform two rakʿas, then do so.” They said: “Go ahead.” He performed two rakʿas, completing them and performing them well. Khubayb was the first to establish the practice of performing two rakʿas at the time of execution. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dāwūd informed us: Maʿmar related to us, from al-Zuhrī, who said: “The first to establish the practice of two rakʿas at the time of execution was Khubayb.” Khalīfa said: Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq; and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbbād ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr related to me, from his father, from ʿUqba ibn al-Ḥārith, who said: I heard him say: “By God, I did not kill Khubayb, for I was younger than that. Rather, Abū Hubayra, of Banu ʿAbd al-Dār, took the spear and placed it in my hand, then took my hand, with the spear in it, and thrust it into him with it until he killed him.”

He said: Jaʿfar ibn ʿAwn informed us: Ibrāhīm ibn Ismāʿīl informed us, from al-Zuhrī, from Jaʿfar ibn ʿAmr ibn Umayya, from his father, who said: “The Messenger of God sent me to Khubayb. I climbed his wooden structure at night, cut the cord, and let him down. I heard his thud behind me, turned around, and saw nothing.”

The Year 4The account of Biʾr Maʿūna. In it is the account of Biʾr Maʿūna

Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq; and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: My father, Isḥāq ibn Yasār, related to me, from al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm. They said: Abū Barāʾ ʿĀmir ibn Mālik ibn Jaʿfar, “the Spear-Player,” came to the Messenger of God ﷺ. He presented Islam to him, but he neither accepted Islam nor distanced himself from it. He said: “Muḥammad, if you were to send men from your companions to Najd to call them to your cause…” He said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—sent them. Ibn Isḥāq said: That was in Ṣafar, four months after Uḥud. He sent al-Mundhir ibn ʿAmr, of Banu Sāʿida, with forty men from among his companions, some of the best of the Muslims. Among them were al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ṣimma; Ḥarām ibn Milḥān, of Banu ʿAdī ibn al-Najjār; ʿUrwa ibn Asmāʾ ibn al-Ṣalt al-Sulamī; Nāfiʿ ibn Budayl ibn Warqāʾ al-Khuzāʿī; and ʿĀmir ibn Fuhayra, the freedman of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq, along with other men from among the best of the Muslims. They went until they encamped at Biʾr Maʿūna, which lies between the territory of Banu ʿĀmir and the lava plain of Banu Sulaym. When they arrived there, they sent the Messenger of God’s letter with one of them to ʿĀmir ibn al-Ṭufayl. He did not even look at the letter before killing its bearer. Then he called on Banu ʿĀmir to come to his aid against them, but they refused to answer him and said: “We will not violate the protection of Abū Barāʾ.” So he called on them to come to his aid against them…

[He called upon] the tribes of Sulaym: ʿUṣayyah, Riʿl, and Dhakwān. They answered him, fought them, and killed all of them except Kaʿb ibn Zayd, who was left with a breath of life. He lived until he was killed on the Day of the Trench. Ibn Isḥāq said: Hishām related to me that this was in Jumādā I. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: He set out at the beginning of Jumādā. They were warned of his coming and fled to the mountains, so the Messenger of God ﷺ—proceeded to ʿUsfān. Ibn Isḥāq said: ʿUyaynah ibn Ḥiṣn ibn Ḥudhayfah ibn Badr raided the grazing animals of Medina, so the Messenger of God ﷺ—went out in pursuit of him. This was the expedition of Dhū Qarad.

The Year 5

He sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn Unays to Khālid ibn Sufyān of Banū Liḥyān, and he killed him. He sent ʿAmr ibn Umayyah and Salamah ibn Ashyam to kill Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb. He sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rawāḥah, who killed Yasīr ibn Rizām. He sent Zayd ibn Ḥārithah to Wādī al-Qurā, to the Fazārah, and he killed most of his companions. Then came Zayd’s second expedition, against Umm Qirfah, and he killed her. Then he sent ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb to al-Qārah, but they took refuge in the mountains. He sent Bilāl ibn Mālik al-Muzanī to Banū Mālik ibn Kinānah. They were warned of his coming, and he did not capture any of their dwellings except one horse. He sent Bashīr ibn Suwayd al-Juhanī to Banū al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd Manāh ibn Kinānah. They took refuge in a thicket, so he burned them. The Messenger of God ﷺ—reproached him.

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: These three expeditions were dispatched by the Messenger of God ﷺ—while he was on campaign against Banū Liḥyān. From there he then came to ʿUsfān. He sent Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ with an army toward the allies, against Ṭayyiʾ and Asad. They were warned of his coming, so Abū ʿUbaydah returned without encountering resistance. He sent ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb to Turbah, between Mecca and Medina, but he encountered no resistance; Turbah is in the territory of Banū ʿĀmir. He sent Muḥammad ibn Maslamah against the Qurāṭāʾ of Banū Kilāb. He began with Banū Jaʿfar, but they were warned of his coming; his companions were killed, and he escaped alone. He sent Bashīr ibn Saʿd, one of Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj, to Fadak. Banū Murrah fought him, his companions were killed, and he returned wounded. He sent Ghālib ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Laythī, who struck those who had killed Bashīr’s companions. Ghālib was with sixty riders.

Bakr related to us; he said: Ibn Isḥāq related to us; he said: Yaʿqūb ibn ʿAṭāʾ related to me, from Muslim, from Jundub ibn Makīth al-Juhanī, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—sent Ghālib ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Kalbī, of Kalb Layth, to Banū al-Mulawwaḥ at al-Kudayyah and ordered him to raid them. He set out, and I was in his expedition. We killed and drove off the livestock, in a report he mentioned.

The Year 6

In the year six, he sent Bashīr ibn Saʿd to Khaybar, and he encountered no resistance. He sent Kaʿb ibn ʿUmayr al-Ghifārī to Dhāt Aṭlāḥ; his companions were struck down, killed by Quḍāʿah. He sent ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf to Kalb, and he married Tamāḍir daughter of al-Aṣbagh. He sent ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib to Banū ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd, of the people of Fadak, and he seized it. ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān went with the sacrificial animals, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rawāḥah went to Khaybar so that he would be between ʿAlī and Khaybar. In this year, which was year six, the Messenger of God married Umm Ḥabībah and consummated the marriage with her in year seven. In it, ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ accepted Islam. The sending of messengers to the commanders and kings: In it, Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah was sent to al-Muqawqis, Shujāʿ ibn Abī Wahb to al-Ḥārith ibn Abī Shimr, Sulayṭ ibn ʿAmr to Hawdhah ibn ʿAlī al-Ḥanafī, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥudhāfah to Kisrā, and Diḥyah ibn Khalīfah to Caesar, during the truce. Abū ʿUbaydah said: In it, Shīrūyah killed his father, Kisrā Khusraw Parvīz. In it, there was the plague of Shīrūyah, and in it Shīrūyah died.

The expedition against Banū al-Muṣṭaliq, and in the year six, the expedition against Banū al-Muṣṭaliq.

Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq; and Wahb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn Qatādah, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Bakr, and Muḥammad ibn Yaḥyā ibn Ḥibbān related to me. They said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—was informed that Banū al-Muṣṭaliq were mustering against him, so he went out against them and met them at one of their watering places, called Muraysīʿ, in the direction of Qudayd. They fought, and God routed Banū al-Muṣṭaliq. Some of them were killed, and the Messenger of God ﷺ—granted the Muslims their children, women, and property as spoils. Among the captives he took was Juwayriyyah daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Abī Ḍirār.

Ibn Isḥāq said: Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn al-Zubayr related to me, from ʿUrwah, from ʿĀʾishah, who said: When the Messenger of God ﷺ—distributed the captives of Banū al-Muṣṭaliq, Juwayriyyah daughter of al-Ḥārith fell to the share of Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās, or to that of a cousin of his. She made a contract to purchase her freedom, and the Messenger of God ﷺ—paid the amount of her contract and married her.

ʿAbd al-Wahhāb ibn ʿAbd al-Majīd informed us; he said: Ayyūb related to us, from Abū Qilābah, that the Messenger of God ﷺ—captured Juwayriyyah daughter of al-Ḥārith. Her father came and said: “My daughter should not be taken captive, for she is a noble woman.” He said: “Go and give her the choice.” He said: “You have done well and acted graciously.” Her father said to her: “Let him take you, so that you do not bring shame upon your people.” She said: “I have chosen God and His Messenger.” Her father said: “May God do this and that to you.” During this campaign, the people of the Slander said about ʿĀʾishah what they said, and the following verses of the Qurʾān were revealed concerning her: “Indeed, those who came with the slander are a group among you”—these verses.

The truce of al-Ḥudaybiyyah. Ibn Isḥāq said: The campaign took place in Shaʿbān. Then the Messenger of God ﷺ—returned to Medina and stayed there for the months of Ramaḍān and Shawwāl. He set out in Dhū al-Qaʿdah intending the visitation, not war.

Ibn Isḥāq said: Al-Zuhrī related to me, from ʿUrwah, from al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—set out in the year of al-Ḥudaybiyyah intending to visit the House, not intending to fight. He drove with him seventy sacrificial camels, and the people numbered seven hundred.

ʿAbd al-Wahhāb informed us, from Khālid al-Ḥadhdhāʾ, from al-Ḥakam ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Aʿraj, from Maʿqil ibn Yasār, who said that he witnessed the Prophet ﷺ—on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, while he was holding aside a branch of the tree from above the Messenger of God’s head as he gave the people the pledge. They pledged to him that they would not flee. They numbered that day one thousand four hundred.

Ibn Zurayʿ informed us; he said: Saʿīd informed us, from Qatādah, who said: I said to Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab: “I have heard that Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh says they numbered one thousand.” He replied: “Jābir forgot. They numbered one thousand five hundred.” Bishr ibn al-Mufaḍḍal related to us; he said: Qurrah related to us, from Qatādah, from Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab, who said: “Jābir ﵀—was mistaken. He himself told me that they numbered one thousand five hundred.”

Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—sent ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān to Mecca to inform them that he did not intend to fight. He said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Bakr related to me; he said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—was informed that ʿUthmān had been killed, so he gave the people the pledge and said: “We will not depart until we engage them.” Then he learned that this report was false. Ibn Isḥāq said: Then Suhayl ibn ʿAmr made peace with him on the condition that he return that year and return the following year.

The Year 7

The expedition of Khaybar. Ibn Isḥāq said: When the Messenger of God ﷺ—returned from al-Ḥudaybiyyah, he stayed in Medina for Dhū al-Ḥijjah and part of al-Muḥarram. Then he set out during the remainder of al-Muḥarram for Khaybar. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: He set out in al-Muḥarram, conquered it in Ṣafar, and returned at the beginning of Rabīʿ I. Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—conquered their fortresses one by one. The first of their fortresses to be conquered was Nāʿim, then al-Qamūṣ, the fortress of Banū Abī al-Ḥuqayq. The Messenger of God took captives from them, among them Ṣafiyyah daughter of Ḥuyayy ibn Akhṭab, whom the Messenger of God chose for himself. The last of their fortresses to be conquered were al-Waṭīḥ and al-Sulālim. He besieged them for twenty-odd nights. Ibn Isḥāq said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sahl ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sahl, one of Banū Ḥārithah, related to me, from Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh, who said: Marḥab the Jew came out from their fortress, and Muḥammad ibn Maslamah went out to meet him and killed him. Then Yāsir, Marḥab’s brother, came out. Ibn Isḥāq said: Hishām ibn ʿUrwah claimed that al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām went out to meet him and killed him. The Messenger of God ﷺ—besieged them in their two fortresses, al-Waṭīḥ and al-Sulālim. They asked the Messenger of God to let them depart and spare their lives, and he did so. They asked the Messenger of God to make an agreement with them concerning the property on the basis of half, and he did so.

The settlement with Fadak. Ibn Isḥāq said: When the people of Fadak heard what the Messenger of God ﷺ—had done to the people of Khaybar, they sent to the Messenger of God asking him to let them depart and spare their lives, while they would relinquish the property to him. He did so. Khaybar thus became a fayʾ shared among the Muslims, while Fadak belonged exclusively to the Messenger of God ﷺ—because they had not brought horses or camels against it.

Abū al-Walīd related to us; he said: Ḥammād ibn Salamah related to us, from Thābit, from Anas, that Ṣafiyyah fell to the share of Diḥyah al-Kalbī, so the Messenger of God ﷺ—bought her for seven heads. Ibn Isḥāq said: Diḥyah had asked the Messenger of God for Ṣafiyyah. When he chose her for himself, the Prophet ﷺ—gave him her two paternal cousins.

Naming those Muslims killed at Khaybar: From Quraysh, from Aslam: ʿĀmir ibn al-Akwaʿ; and Rabīʿah ibn Aktham ibn Sakhbarah, of Banū Asad ibn Khuzaymah, an ally of Banū Umayyah ibn ʿAbd Shams; and Thaqf ibn ʿAmr, an Asadī and their ally as well; and Rifāʿah ibn Masrūḥ; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Hubayb ibn Uhayb, of Banū Saʿd ibn Layth.

From the Anṣār: from Banū Salimah, Bishr ibn al-Barāʾ ibn Maʿrū—he died from the sheep that had been poisoned—and al-Fuḍayl ibn al-Nuʿmān, of Banū Zurayq; and from Banū Zurayq, Masʿūd ibn Saʿd.

From al-Aws: from al-Aws, then from Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal, Maḥmūd ibn Maslamah, the brother of Muḥammad ibn Maslamah; Abū al-Ḍayyāḥ ibn Thābit, of Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf; Mubashshir ibn ʿAbd al-Mundhir ibn Zubayr; Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith; al-Ḥārith ibn Ḥāṭib; ʿUrwah ibn Murrah; Aws ibn al-Fātik; and Anīf ibn Ḥabīb.

From Banū Ghifār: from Banū Ghifār, ʿUmārah ibn ʿUqbah, who was struck by an arrow. Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ—set out for Khaybar in al-Muḥarram. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: He set out in al-Muḥarram, conquered it in Ṣafar, and returned at the beginning of Rabīʿ I.

The Siege of Wādī al-Qurāʾ. Ibn Isḥāq said: When the Messenger of God ﷺ had finished with Khaybar, he departed for Wādī al-Qurāʾ and besieged its people for several nights, then departed.

The Settlement with Fadak. Ibn Isḥāq said: The people of Fadak sent word to the Messenger of God ﷺ and made a settlement with him for half of Fadak. He accepted that from them, and it became his exclusive property, because no horses or camels had been driven against it.

His Expeditions in the Year Seven. Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ stayed in Medina during the months of Rabīʿ, the two Jumādās, Rajab, Shaʿbān, and the month of Ramaḍān. During that time he dispatched expeditions. Among those he dispatched were Zayd ibn Ḥārithah to Judhām; ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ to Dhāt al-Salāsil; a detachment under Abū al-ʿArjāʾ to Banū Sulaym, he and his companions; a detachment under ʿUkkāshah ibn Miḥṣan to al-Ghamrah, who returned without encountering resistance; a detachment under Zayd ibn Ḥārithah to al-Ṭaraf, in the direction of Iraq; a detachment under ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Ḥadrad, with two men accompanying him, to al-Ghābah, against Rifāʿah ibn Qays al-Jushamī, eight miles from Medina; and a detachment under ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī to Iḍam, where he encountered ʿĀmir ibn al-Aḍbaṭ.

The Compensatory ʿUmrah. Ibn Isḥāq said: Then he set out in Dhū al-Qaʿdah to perform the Compensatory ʿUmrah. He stayed in Mecca for three days, then departed. During it, he married Maymūnah daughter of al-Ḥārith. Ibn Isḥāq said: Abān ibn Ṣāliḥ and Ibn Abī Najīḥ related to me, from ʿAṭāʾ, from Ibn ʿAbbās, that the Messenger of God ﷺ married Maymūnah daughter of al-Ḥārith on that journey while he was in a state of consecration. Al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married her to him, and he consummated the marriage with her at Sarif in Dhū al-Ḥijjah. In that year, Umm Ḥabībah daughter of Abū Sufyān arrived in Medina, and the Messenger of God ﷺ consummated the marriage with her. In that year, Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah arrived from al-Muqawqis with Māriyah, the mother of Ibrāhīm, son of the Messenger of God ﷺ his mule Duldul, and his donkey Yaʿfūr. In that year, Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib arrived from Abyssinia. In that year, the Messenger of God ﷺ married Ṣafiyyah daughter of Ḥuyayy. In that year, Umm Kulthūm daughter of ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ arrived as an emigrant. In that year, Abū Hurayrah and ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn became Muslims at the time of Khaybar, and Khālid ibn al-Walīd [became Muslim] between al-Ḥudaybiyyah and Khaybar.

The Year 8

The Battle of Muʾtah. In it was the Battle of Muʾtah, in which Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib, Zayd ibn Ḥārithah, and Ibn Rawāḥah were killed. Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn al-Zubayr related to me, from ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ dispatched a force to Muʾtah in Jumādā al-Ūlā of the year eight, and appointed Zayd ibn Ḥārithah over them. If he were killed, then Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib [was to take command]; if he were killed, then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rawāḥah. The forces of Heraclius met them at al-Balqāʾ, and Zayd, Jaʿfar, and Ibn Rawāḥah were killed. Khālid ibn al-Walīd took the banner and withdrew with the Muslims.

The Conquest of Mecca. In this year, the year eight, the Messenger of God ﷺ conquered Mecca. Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, from al-Zuhrī, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUtbah, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ set out when ten days of the month of Ramaḍān had passed, intending for Mecca. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to me, from Ḥammād ibn Salamah, from ʿAlī ibn Zayd, from Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ conquered [Mecca] in the year eight of his emigration, in the month of Ramaḍān. He stayed for fifteen days, then departed, and appointed ʿAttāb ibn Usayd over Mecca. Ibn Isḥāq said: He conquered it with ten [days] remaining in the month of Ramaḍān.

Dispatching Expeditions Around Mecca. Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ dispatched his detachments around Mecca, calling people to God, and did not command them to fight.

Khālid’s Expedition to Banū Judhaymah. Ibn Isḥāq said: Ḥakīm ibn Ḥakīm ibn ʿAbbād ibn Ḥanīf related to me, from Abū Jaʿfar Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī, who said: When the Messenger of God ﷺ conquered Mecca, calling people [to Islam], he sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd, who came to al-Ghumayṣāʾ, a watering place belonging to the watering places of Judhaymah ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAbd Manāf, and killed some of them; they have an account concerning this.

The Demolition of al-ʿUzzā. Then the Messenger of God ﷺ sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to al-ʿUzzā. It was a great sanctuary of Quraysh, Kinānah, and all of Muḍar, at Nakhlah. He demolished it. Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd informed us: Ajlaḥ informed us, from Ibn al-Hudhayl, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent Khālid to al-ʿUzzā, and he demolished it, saying: “Disavowal of you, not praise of you; I have seen that God has humiliated you.”

The Campaign of Ḥunayn.

Wahb narrated to us, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb related to me, from his father, from his grandfather. Ibn Isḥāq said: Al-Zuhrī related to me that when Hawāzin heard about the Messenger of God and what God had granted him, Mālik ibn ʿAwf al-Naṣrī assembled them. The Messenger of God set out from Mecca with two thousand people from Mecca, along with ten thousand of his Companions. He appointed Ibn Usayd over Mecca. They met at Ḥunayn. The Muslims scattered, then rallied against their enemy, and God routed the polytheists. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: The Messenger of God ﷺ set out for Ḥunayn on Sunday, in the middle of Shawwāl. Ibn Isḥāq said: Among those of the Muslims martyred on the day of Ḥunayn was Abū ʿĀmir al-Ashʿarī, among men of whom I know no one, other than Abū ʿĀmir, who preserved a hadith from the Messenger of God ﷺ. Among them were Ayman ibn ʿUbayd; Yazīd ibn Rabīʿah ibn al-Aswad ibn al-Muṭṭalib, whose horse bolted with him; and Surāqah ibn al-Ḥabbāb, an Anṣārī.

The Campaign of al-Ṭāʾif. Ibn Isḥāq said: When the Messenger of God ﷺ had finished with Ḥunayn, he marched to al-Ṭāʾif and besieged them for a little more than twenty nights, then withdrew from it. ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Mūsā narrated to us, from Ṭalḥah ibn Jabr, from al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from Muṣʿab ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, from his father, that the Messenger of God ﷺ besieged them for seventeen or nineteen [nights], but did not conquer it. During that siege, Abū Bakrah came down to the Messenger of God ﷺ.

Umayyah ibn Khālid narrated to us, from Shuʿbah, from ʿĀṣim al-Aḥwal, from Abū ʿUthmān, who said: Abū Bakrah reached the Messenger of God ﷺ ahead of [the others] from al-Ṭāʾif. ʿUmar ibn ʿAlī said: Ḥajjāj narrated to us, from al-Ḥakam, from Miqsam, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ freed whichever of the boys of al-Ṭāʾif came out to him.

His Encampment at al-Jiʿrānah. Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ withdrew from al-Ṭāʾif and encamped at al-Jiʿrānah. There he divided the property and captives of Hawāzin and gave to those whose hearts were to be reconciled.

ʿUbaydah narrated to us, from Ḥammād, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwah, from his father, who said: The Messenger of God ﷺ divided the captives, who numbered six thousand persons. The names of those whose hearts were to be reconciled: Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb, Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah, Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām, al-Naḍr ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Kaldah, al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām, Suhayl ibn ʿAmr, Ḥuwayṭib ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā, al-ʿAlāʾ ibn Jāriyah, ʿUyaynah ibn Ḥiṣn, al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis, and Mālik ibn ʿAwf al-Naṣrī. He gave each man among them one hundred camels. He gave Makh़ramah ibn Nawfal, ʿUmayr ibn Wadhfah, Hishām ibn ʿAmr, Saʿīd ibn Yarbuʿ, and ʿAbbās ibn Mirdās each less than one hundred.

The Names of the Muslims Martyred on the Day of al-Ṭāʾif. From Banū Umayyah of Quraysh, then from Banū Umayyah: Saʿīd ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayyah; and ʿUrfuṭah ibn Ḥabāb ibn Ḥabīb, of al-Azd, an ally of theirs.

From Taym ibn Murrah, and from Banū Taym ibn Murrah: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq was struck by an arrow and died in Medina after the death of the Messenger of God ﷺ.

From Makhzūm, and from Banū Makhzūm ibn Yaqaẓah: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Umayyah ibn al-Mughīrah; he died from a wound caused by a projectile thrown at him.

From Banū ʿAdī, and from Banū ʿAdī: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Rabīʿah, an ally of theirs from the people of Yemen.

From Banū Sahm, and from Banū Sahm ibn ʿAmr: al-Sāʾib ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Qays and his brother ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith.

From Banū Saʿd, and from Banū Saʿd ibn Layth: Julayḥah ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥārib.

From the Anṣār, and from the Anṣār of Banū Salimah: Thābit ibn al-Jadhaʿ; al-Jadhaʿ was Thaʿlabah.

From Banū Māzin, and from Banū Māzin ibn al-Najjār: al-Ḥārith ibn Sahl ibn Abī Ṣaʿṣaʿah.

From Banū Sāʿidah, and from Banū Sāʿidah: al-Mundhir ibn ʿAbd Allāh.

From al-Aws, and from al-Aws: Ruqaym ibn Thābit. Those martyred altogether were twelve men. Ibn Isḥāq said: Then the Messenger of God ﷺ set out from al-Jiʿrānah to perform ʿumrah, then returned to Medina. He appointed ʿAttāb ibn Usayd over Mecca. ʿAttāb led the Muslims in the pilgrimage in the year eight, while the polytheists performed the pilgrimage as they had previously done. In that year, ʿIkrimah ibn Abī Jahl became Muslim. In that year, Ibrāhīm, son of the Messenger of God ﷺ was born. In that year, Zaynab, daughter of the Messenger of God, died. In that year, he married Fāṭimah daughter of al-Ḍaḥḥāk al-Kilābiyyah.

The Year 9

The Campaign of Tabūk. In it was the Campaign of Tabūk. Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ set out for Tabūk, and the ruler of Aylah made peace with him; the Messenger of God ﷺ wrote him a document. Abū al-Ḥasan said: He set out at the beginning of Rajab.

The Three Who Were Left Behind. During the Campaign of Tabūk was the account of the three who were left behind: Kaʿb ibn Mālik, Hilāl ibn Umayyah, and Murārah ibn al-Rabīʿ.

The Expedition against Ukaydir of Dūmah. Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to Ukaydir of Dūmah. He was Ukaydir ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, a man from the people of Yemen who had been a king. He captured him Khalid brought him to the Messenger of God ﷺ. He spared his life and granted him the jizya, then sent him back to his village.

The revelation of Sūrat Barāʾa. Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of God ﷺ sent Abū Bakr in the year nine to lead the people in the pilgrimage. Then he sent ʿAlī with Sūrat Barāʾa and ordered that after that year no polytheist should perform the pilgrimage and no one should circumambulate the House naked.

ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn ʿAbd al-Aʿlā narrated to us. He said: Maʿmar narrated to us, from Abū Isḥāq, from al-Ḥārith, from ʿAlī, who said: “I was ordered to do four things: that no one but a believer enter Paradise; that after this year no polytheist approach the Sacred Mosque; that no one circumambulate the House naked; and that every person bound by a treaty be released from his treaty.”

The arrival of the tribal delegations to the Messenger ﷺ. In that year Arab delegations came to the Messenger of God. ʿUṭārid ibn Ḥājib ibn Zurāra, al-Zibriqān ibn Badr, Qays ibn ʿĀṣim, and ʿAmr ibn al-Ahtam came among leading men of Tamīm. Banū Saʿd ibn Bakr sent Ḍimām ibn Thaʿlaba, who embraced Islam and returned to his people, and they embraced Islam. Al-Jārūd came with al-Mundhir ibn Sāwā among ʿAbd al-Qays. Banū Ḥanīfa came, including Musaylima ibn Ḥabīb the Liar. Zayd al-Khayl came among Ṭayyiʾ. As for ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim, he came after that. Farwa ibn Māsik al-Murādī and ʿAmr ibn Maʿdīkarib al-Zubaydī also came.

In the year nine, the Messenger of God ﷺ announced the death of the Negus Aṣḥama. In that year Umm Kulthūm, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ died. Abū ʿUbayda said: In the year nine of the dating era, the Persians killed Shahrbarāz and Ardashīr ibn Shīrawayh; in it they killed Shahrbarāz and made Būrān, daughter of Kisrā, queen.

The Year 10

The Farewell Pilgrimage. In it the Messenger of God ﷺ performed the Farewell Pilgrimage.

The people of Najrān embrace Islam. In it the Messenger of God ﷺ sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd in the month of Rabīʿ al-Ākhir or Jumādā al-Ūlā to the Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Kaʿb in Najrān. They embraced Islam. Then he wrote to him, and he came, bringing with him men from the Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Kaʿb. They embraced Islam and returned to their people. The Messenger of God ﷺ sent ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm to instruct them in religion, teach them the Sunna, and collect their alms. In it Ibrāhīm, son of the Messenger of God ﷺ died. Abū ʿUbayda said: In it Būrān, daughter of Kisrā, died, and the Persians made her sister Āzarmā, daughter of Kisrā, queen.

The Year 11

The death of the Messenger ﷺ. In it the Messenger of God ﷺ died on Monday, twelve nights having passed in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal; it is also said, with two nights having passed in it. He was buried on Wednesday night ﷺ.

His age ﷺ. There is disagreement about his age ﷺ. Abū Dāwūd narrated to us. He said: Zuhayr narrated to us, from Abū Isḥāq, who said: ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUtba said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ died at the age of sixty-three.” ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd said: “Jarīr told me,” and said:

“We were with Muʿāwiya, and he said: ‘The Messenger of God ﷺ died at the age of sixty-three.’” Abū Aḥmad and Salm ibn Qutayba narrated to us. They both said: Yūnus ibn Abī Isḥāq informed us, from Abū al-Safar, from al-Shaʿbī, from Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān, who said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ died at the age of sixty-three.” ʿAbd al-Wahhāb ibn ʿAbd al-Majīd narrated to us. He said: I heard Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd say: I heard Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab say: “The Messenger of God ﷺ died at the age of sixty-three.” Abū ʿUbayda told me, from Ḥammād ibn Salama, from Abū Jamra, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ died at the age of sixty-three.” Abū al-ʿAbbās narrated to us, from ʿAbd al-Razzāq, from Ibn Jurayj, from al-Zuhrī, from ʿUrwa, from ʿĀʾisha.

She said: “He was taken in death at the age of sixty-three.” Ibn Abī ʿAdī narrated to us, from Dāwūd, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: “He died at the age of sixty-three.” ʿAbd al-Wahhāb and Ibn Zurayʿ narrated to us, from Hishām, from ʿIkrima, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said: “He died at the age of sixty-three.” Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ narrated to us. He said: Yūnus ibn ʿUbayd narrated to us, from ʿAmmār ibn Abī ʿAmmār, client of Banū Hāshim, who said: “I asked Ibn ʿAbbās, and he said: ‘He died at the age of sixty-five.’” Muʿādh ibn Hishām narrated to us. He said: My father told me, from Qatāda, from al-Ḥasan, from Daghfal ibn Ḥanẓala, who said: “He ﷺ died at the age of sixty-five.” Abū ʿĀṣim narrated to us, from Ashʿath, from al-Ḥasan, who said: “He died at the age of sixty-three.” ʿAbd al-Wahhāb narrated to us, from Hishām, from al-Ḥasan, who said: “He died at the age of sixty.” Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad al-Madanī narrated to us. He said: Rabīʿa ibn Abī ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated to us, from Anas ibn Mālik, who said: “He died at the age of sixty.” Ismāʿīl ibn Sinān narrated to us. He said: Ḥammād ibn Salama narrated to us, from ʿAmr ibn Dīnār, from ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr, who said: “He died at the age of sixty.”

Sahl ibn Bakkār narrated to us, from Wuhayb, from Yūnus, from al-Ḥasan, who said: “He died at the age of sixty.” Muʿādh ibn Hishām narrated to us. He said: My father told me, from Qatāda, who said: “He died at the age of sixty-two.”

The death of Fāṭima ﵂. In it Fāṭima, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ died after his death. It is said that it was eight months later; it is also said six months later; and it is also said seventy days later. Abū Wahb al-Sahmī narrated to us. He said: Ḥātim ibn Abī Ṣaghīra narrated to us, from ʿAmr ibn Dīnār, who said: “Fāṭima died eight months after her father.” Abū ʿĀṣim narrated to us, from Kahmas ibn al-Ḥasan, from Ibn Burayda, who said: “She lived seventy days and nights after her father.” Muḥammad ibn Muʿāwiya told me, from Sufyān, from ʿAmr ibn Dīnār, from Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī, who said: “She remained six months after her father.” He said: Ibn Shihāb said: “She remained three months.” Aḥmad ibn ʿAlī told me, from Jarīr, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ziyād, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith, who said: “She died eight months after her father.”

The naming of his deputies ﷺ. He appointed Ibn Umm Maktūm as his deputy over Medina thirteen times during his campaigns: in the campaigns of al-Abwāʾ, Buwāṭ, and Dhū al-ʿAshīra; when he went toward the territory of Juhayna in pursuit of Karz ibn Jābir; and when he set out for Badr. Then he sent Abū Lubāba back and appointed him over it. Also in the campaign of al-Sawīq, Ghatafān, Uḥud, Ḥamrāʾ al-Asad, Buḥrān, Dhāt al-Riqāʿ, and the Farewell Pilgrimage. He appointed Abū Ruhm al-Ghifārī, Kulthūm ibn Ḥuṣayn, when he set out for Mecca, and for Ḥunayn and al-Ṭāʾif. He appointed Muḥammad ibn Maslama in the campaign of Qarqara al-Kudr, and in the campaign against Banū al-Muṣṭaliq, Numayla ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Laythī.

In the campaign of al-Ḥudaybiya, ʿUwayf ibn al-Aḍbaṭ of Banū al-Dayl; in the campaign of Khaybar, Abū Ruhm al-Ghifārī; and in the compensatory ʿumra, Abū Ruhm as well. In the campaign of Tabūk, Sibāʿ ibn ʿArfaṭa al-Ghifārī; and in some of his campaigns, Ghālib ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Laythī. When he departed from Mecca, he appointed ʿAttāb ibn Asīd over it, and he remained in charge there until he died and Abū Bakr died. ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ al-Thaqafī was over al-Ṭāʾif, and Sālim ibn Muʿattib was over the Aḥlāf of Thaqīf, over Banū Mālik. ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ was over the Arab villages of Khaybar, Wādī al-Qurā, Taymāʾ, and Tabūk. The Messenger of God ﷺ died while ʿAmr was in charge of them. Al-Ḥakam ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ was over the market. He divided Yemen and appointed Khālid ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ over Ṣanʿāʾ; al-Muhājir ibn Abī Umayya over Kinda and al-Ṣadaf; and Ziyād ibn Labīd al-Anṣārī, one of Banū Bayāḍa, over Ḥaḍramawt. Muʿādh ibn Jabal was over al-Janad, the judiciary, teaching the people Islam and its laws, and reciting the Qurʾān. He appointed Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī over Zabīd, Rimāʿ, ʿAdan, and the coast, and assigned the collection of alms from the officials there to Muʿādh ibn Jabal. He sent ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm to the Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Kaʿb, and Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb to Najrān. He had also sent ʿAlī to Najrān; he collected their alms and came to the Messenger of God ﷺ during the Farewell Pilgrimage. Saʿīd ibn al-Qishb al-Azdī, ally of Banū Umayya, was over Jurash and its sea. Al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Ḥaḍramī was over al-Baḥrayn, then he removed him and appointed Abān ibn Saʿīd over it. The Messenger of God ﷺ died while Abān was over al-Baḥrayn. ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ was sent to ʿUmān; the Messenger of God ﷺ died while ʿAmr was in charge there. It is also said that he had sent Abū Zayd al-Anṣārī to ʿUmān, and Sulayṭ ibn Sulayṭ, one of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy, to the people of al-Yamāma. They embraced Islam, and the Messenger of God ﷺ confirmed them in what they possessed and their property.

The naming of his envoys ﷺ.

He sent ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān to the people of Mecca in the year of al-Ḥudaybiya, and ʿAmr ibn Umayya al-Ḍamrī with a gift to Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb in Mecca; ʿUrwa ibn Masʿūd al-Thaqafī to his people in al-Ṭāʾif; and Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh to Dhū Kilāʿ and Dhū Ruʿayn in Yemen. He sent to the al-Abnāʾ in Yemen Wabr ibn Yuḥannis—it is also said, Wabr ibn Muḥṣin al-Ghalafānī; and it is also said, Makhshī ibn Wabara; and it is also said, Ḥunays al-Azdī. He sent Khubayb ibn Zayd ibn ʿĀṣim to Musaylima the Liar, and Musaylima killed him; Sulayṭ ibn Sulayṭ to the people of al-Yamāma; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥudhāfa al-Sahmī to Kisrā; Diḥya ibn Khalīfa al-Kalbī to Caesar; Shujāʿ ibn Wahb al-Asadī to al-Ḥārith ibn Abī Shimr al-Ghassānī—it is also said, to Jabala ibn al-Ayham; Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿa to al-Muqawqis, ruler of Alexandria; and ʿAmr ibn Umayya al-Ḍamrī to the Negus in Abyssinia.

The naming of his collectors of alms

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf was over the alms of Kalb; ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim over the two allied tribes, Ṭayyiʾ and Asad; it is also said, over Asad al-Abāʾ. al-Abāʾ ibn Qays al-Asadī; ʿUyayna ibn Ḥiṣn over Fazāra; al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba ibn Abī Muʿayṭ over Banū al-Muṣṭaliq; al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAwf al-Murrī over Banū Murra; Masʿūd ibn Dukhayla al-Ashjaʿī over Ashjaʿ, Banū ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ghatafān, and Banū ʿAbs; al-Aʿjam ibn Sufyān al-Balawī over ʿUdhra, Salmān, and Balī of Juhayna; Mālik ibn Nuwayra over Banū Ḥanẓala; al-Zibriqān ibn Badr over ʿAwf and the al-Abnāʾ; Qays ibn ʿĀṣim al-Minqarī over Muqāʿis and the branches of Asad and Ghatafān; and ʿUṭārid over the alms of Dārim. ʿĀmir ibn Mālik ibn Jaʿfar over Banū ʿĀmir ibn

Ṣaʿṣaʿa; ʿAbbās ibn Mirdās over Banū Sulaym and over the outlying groups of Hawāzin: Jusham, Naṣr, Thaqīf, and Saʿd ibn Bakr; Mālik ibn ʿAwf al-Naṣrī; al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Sufyān al-Kilābī over Banū Kilāb.

The naming of those who wrote for him ﷺ

Zayd ibn Thābit, the scribe of the revelation, and Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān wrote for him. Ḥanẓala ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Usaydī wrote for him, as did ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ, but then he apostatized and joined Mecca.

His gatekeeper, the one in charge of his expenses, his treasurer, his servant, his two callers to prayer, and his guards ﷺ. Anasa, his client, used to admit people to him. Bilāl was in charge of his expenses; Muʿayqīb ibn Abī Fāṭima was his treasurer—it is also said that Muʿayqīb was in charge of his seal—and Anas ibn Mālik served him. His two callers to prayer were Bilāl and Ibn Umm Maktūm. His guard at Badr was Saʿīd ibn Zayd al-Anṣārī; when he returned from Badr, Dhakwān ibn ʿAbd Qays; at Uḥud, Muḥammad ibn Maslama; at the Trench, al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām and others; at Khaybar, on the night of his marriage to Ṣafiyya, Abū Ayyūb; and at Tabūk, Abū Qatāda. Saʿd ibn Mālik and ʿĀʾidh ibn ʿAmr al-Muzanī also guarded him.

Chapter 3

The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq

In the year eleven, Abū Bakr received the general pledge of allegiance on Tuesday, the day after the death of the Messenger of God ﷺ. Abū Bakr’s name was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Quḥāfa, and Abū Quḥāfa’s name was ʿUthmān ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra ibn Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy ibn Ghālib ibn Fihr ibn Mālik ibn al-Naḍr ibn Kināna. His mother was Umm al-Khayr, daughter of Ṣakhr ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra. Then he dispatched the army of Usāma ibn Zayd.

Dispatching the army of Usāma. ʿAlī and Mūsā ibn Ismāʿīl narrated to us, from Ḥammād ibn Salama, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwa, from his father, that the Messenger of God ﷺ said during the illness in which he died: “Dispatch Usāma’s army.” The Messenger of God ﷺ died while Usāma was at al-Jurf. Usāma wrote to Abū Bakr: “A momentous event has occurred, and I think the Arabs will all renounce the faith. The leading Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ are with me, and only they. If you think we should stay…” Abū Bakr wrote back to him and said: “I would not begin with anything more deserving than carrying out the command of the Messenger of God ﷺ. Even if birds were to snatch me away, that would be dearer to me than doing otherwise. But if you think you should grant ʿUmar permission…” He granted it to him, and Usāma went on his way. Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, that Abū Bakr ordered the people to dispatch Usāma’s army. The people said to him: “The Arabs have broken away from you, and you will accomplish nothing by dispersing the people from around you.” He said: “By the One in whose hand is Abū Bakr’s soul, even if I thought that wild beasts would devour me in this village, I would still send forth this expedition that the Messenger of God commanded…”

“Abū Bakr’s own soul is in His hand: even if I thought the beasts would devour me in this village, I would still send out this expedition which the Messenger of God ﷺ ordered to be sent out.” ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to me, from ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from al-Zuhrī, something similar. He said: Usāma marched at the end of Rabīʿ I until he reached the land of Syria; then he returned. His journey out and back took forty days.

The Apostasy

The Arabs apostatized and withheld the alms. The Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ said to Abū Bakr, “Accept it from them.” He said, “If they withhold from me even a tether that they used to give to the Messenger of God, I will fight them.”

Abū Bakr’s Departure for Dhū al-Qaṣṣa

Then he set out for Dhū al-Qaṣṣa and appointed Sinān al-Ḍamrī over Medina, and Ibn Masʿūd over the outposts of Medina. ʿAbd al-Wahhāb, from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, reported that Abū Bakr said, “If they withhold from me a tether or a rope, I will fight them.” ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to us, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar al-Anṣārī, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwa, from his father, who said: Abū Bakr left Medina halfway through Jumādā II. ʿAlī said, from ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from al-Zuhrī, who said: Abū Bakr went to Dhū al-Qaṣṣa ten days into Jumādā I, after the arrival of Usāma ibn Zayd. He alighted there; it was two stages from Medina, on the road toward Iraq. He appointed Sinān al-Ḍamrī over Medina and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd over the guards at Medina’s outposts.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mahdī related to us, saying: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Salama informed us, from ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn Abī ʿAwn, from al-Qāsim ibn Muḥammad, from ʿĀʾisha, who said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ died. Had what befell my father descended upon the firmly rooted mountains, it would have crushed them. Hypocrisy raised its head in Medina, and the Arabs apostatized. By God, whenever they disputed over a single point, my father flew to the greatest matter in Islam.”

The Apostasy of Ṭulayḥa al-Asadī

Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Abū Bakr appointed Khālid ibn al-Walīd over the army and Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās over the Anṣār; the command of the people as a whole belonged to Khālid. He marched, and Abū Bakr marched with him until he alighted at Dhū al-Qaṣṣa, two stages from Medina. There he set up his camp, drew up his troops, and gave Khālid instructions, ordering him to make for Ṭulayḥa, who was at a water source called Qaṭan, and another water source called Ghamr Marzūq. Then he returned to Medina.

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad, from Maslama, from Dāwūd, from ʿĀmir and Abū Maʿshar, from Yazīd ibn Rūmān, reported that Abū Bakr went out to Dhū al-Qaṣṣa and intended to march in person. The Muslims said to him, “Your marching in person will accomplish nothing, and we do not know what you intend to do.
