Appoint a man whom you trust and rely on, and return to Medina, for you have left it seething with hypocrisy.” He therefore appointed Khālid ibn al-Walīd over the people, and appointed Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās over the Anṣār specifically, with Khālid over them all. He ordered him to make for Ṭulayḥa. Abū Bakr devised a stratagem and said to Khālid, “I will meet you at such-and-such a place.”

Maslama said, from Dāwūd, from ʿĀmir; and ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from al-Zuhrī, reported that Khālid marched from Dhū al-Qaṣṣa with between two thousand seven hundred and three thousand men, intending to go against Ṭalḥa. He sent ʿUkāsha ibn Miḥṣan and Thābit ibn Aqram ibn Thaʿlaba al-Anṣārī, an ally of theirs from Balī. They reached Qaṭan.

There they encountered Ḥibāl, carrying his baggage and heading to Ṭulayḥa. They killed Ḥibāl and took what he had with him. Ṭulayḥa and Salama, the two sons of Khuwaylid, came out and met ʿUkāsha and Thābit; they killed ʿUkāsha and Thābit. Khālid marched to Buzākha, accompanied by ʿUyayna ibn Ḥiṣn al-Fazārī and Qurra ibn Hubayra al-Qushayrī. They fought fiercely, and God routed Ṭulayḥa, who fled to Syria. ʿUyayna and Qurra ibn Hubayra were captured, and Khālid sent them to Abū Bakr, who spared their lives. The people dispersed from Buzākha. Some went to Ghamr Marzūq, and Khālid marched against them. He killed many of them, while the rest fled after fierce fighting.

Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa ibn Yazīd ibn Rikāna related to me, from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUtba, who said: ʿUyayna fought alongside Ṭulayḥa with seven hundred men of Banū Fazāra. The people were routed, Ṭulayḥa fled to Syria, and his force dispersed.

The Apostasy of Banū Sulaym

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad and Mūsā ibn Ismāʿīl related to me, from Ḥammād ibn Salama, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwa, from his father, who said: The Arabs disbelieved, and Banū Sulaym came to Abū Bakr and said, “The Arabs have disbelieved, so equip us with weapons.” He ordered weapons to be given to them, but they came forward to fight Abū Bakr. ʿAbbās ibn Mirdās said:

“Why do you take his weapons to fight him? You will have sins for that before God.”

Abū Bakr sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd against Banū Sulaym. He put them in enclosures, then set fires around them. Khālid went on and met Asad and Ghaṭafān at Buzākha; God routed them. Then he met them at Buṭāḥ.

They came forward with their banners and submitted. Then he said, “By God, I will not stop until I engage Musaylima.” The Anṣār said, “This is a course Abū Bakr did not order you to take. Return to Medina.” He said, “No, by God, not until I engage him.” The Anṣār turned back and marched for a night. Then they said, “By God, if our companions are victorious, we will have disgraced ourselves; and if they are defeated, we will have abandoned them.” So they returned. As for Bakr, he related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, that Thābit ibn Qays said, “We will not march with you,” and he mentioned something similar to the first account. He said: They sent word to Khālid, who had marched one or two stages ahead, “Stay until we catch up with you.” He stayed until they caught up with him.

The Apostasy of Banū Tamīm

Then he marched until he alighted at al-Buṭāḥ, in the land of Banū Tamīm. He sent out detachments, but they encountered no resistance. Mālik ibn Nuwayra was brought to him with a group of Banū Ḥanẓala, and he struck off their heads. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to us, from ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from al-Zuhrī, a report similar to Ibn Isḥāq’s. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to us, from Abū Zakariyyā Yaḥyā ibn Maʿīn al-ʿAjlānī, from Saʿīd ibn Isḥāq, from his father, from Abū Qatāda, who said: Abū Bakr instructed Khālid—and those he had sent against the apostasy—that whenever they came to a dwelling, they should stay there. If they heard a call to prayer or saw someone praying, they were to hold back until they asked the people about the matter they objected to and for which they had withheld the alms. If they heard no call to prayer and saw no one praying, they were to launch a raid, kill, and burn. He said: I was with Khālid until he had finished fighting Ṭulayḥa, Ghaṭafān, Hawāzin, and Sulaym. Then he marched to the lands of Banū Tamīm, and we sent Khālid ahead of him. We came upon a household of theirs as the sun was beginning to set. They rushed out toward us and said, “Who are you?” We said, “We are servants of God, Muslims.” They said, “And we are servants of God, Muslims.” Khālid had already sent out his detachments, and they had heard no call to prayer. Some people fought them at al-Baʿūḍa, from the direction of al-Marrār. They brought Mālik ibn Nuwayra with captives from his people. Khālid ordered their weapons taken, then in the morning ordered them killed.

Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr informed us, from Abū Qatāda, with a report like his. He said: “When we came upon the people, they took up their weapons. We said, ‘We are Muslims.’ They said, ‘And we are Muslims.’ We said, ‘Then why do you have weapons with you?’ They said, ‘Then why do you have weapons with you?’ We said, ‘If you are as you say, lay down your weapons.’ So they laid down their weapons. Then we prayed, and they prayed.

“Abū al-Yaqẓān said, from Ṭufayl, who said: Khālid alighted at al-Baʿūḍa. Their muezzin, Abū al-Jalāl, was absent, and no one gave the call to prayer. So Khālid raided them and killed some of them, including Bishr ibn Abī Sawd al-Ghudānī. Mirdās ibn Adiyya, who was ten years old, escaped that day.

“ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to us, from Ibn Abī Dhiʾb, from al-Zuhrī, from Sālim, from his father, who said: Abū Qatāda came to Abū Bakr and informed him of the killing of Mālik and his companions. He was deeply distressed by this. Abū Bakr wrote to Khālid, who came to him. Abū Bakr said, ‘Could Khālid have done anything beyond interpreting [the matter] and being mistaken?’ Abū Bakr sent Khālid back, paid the blood money for Mālik ibn Nuwayra, and returned the captives and the property. Bakr related, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Khālid entered upon Abū Bakr and told him the story. He offered him an apology, and Abū Bakr excused him. Mutammim ibn Nuwayra elegized his brother Mālik ibn Nuwayra, saying:

“We lived well in life, and before us Death struck the people of Kisrā and Tubbaʿ.

…And for a time we were like the people of Judhayma, Until it was said, ‘They will never be sundered.’”

“…When we parted, it was as though Mālik and I, after so long together, had never spent a night together…

“…No aged she-camel ever moaned, then repeated her moan, whose grief made all the pack weep…

“…Nor any of three nursing she-camels, tenderly caring, that saw the trail of a young camel and its place of slaughter…

“…They remind the sorrowful man, burdened with grief, of his sorrow, when the first moans and the others answer together…

“…More sorrowful than I was on the day a clear-voiced herald stood over Mālik and proclaimed the parting, making it heard…

“…What signs of patience do I see? Yet I see every bond after your bond severed…

“…May God give rain to the land where Mālik’s grave lies, with the rain-bearing, cloud-laden evening showers, so it may grow lush…”

“…And the valley’s two bottoms were favored with a steady rain that brought forth early-season growth from the plants, the castor-oil plant…”

“…His greeting from me, though he is far away and the earth has become bare dust above him…”

There is much more in this poem and in his other elegies.

The Account of al-Yamāma

Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, that Abū Bakr sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to al-Yamāma and ordered him to make for Musaylima the Liar. When he drew near al-Yamāma, he alighted in one of their valleys and came upon Mujjāʿa ibn Murāra with twenty men of theirs, who had gone out in pursuit of a man from Banū Numayr. Khālid said to them, “O Banū Ḥanīfa, what do you say?” They said, “We say: a prophet from among us and a prophet from among you.” Khālid put them to the sword and killed them, except for Mujjāʿa, whom he securely bound in iron. Then he marched, and they fought. The first of the polytheists to be killed was Rijāl ibn ʿUnfuwa. They fought fiercely, and the Muslims were driven back. Then they rallied. Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās said, “How wretchedly you have trained yourselves, company of Muslims! O God, I disavow before You what these people are doing.” Then he fought until he was killed.

Muʿādh ibn Muʿādh related to us, saying: Ibn ʿAwn informed us, from Mūsā ibn Anas ibn Mālik, who said: When the people were driven back on the day of al-Yamāma, Anas ibn Mālik came upon Thābit ibn Qays. He had uncovered his thighs and was applying scent for burial. Then he came and sat down. He said, “Uncle, will you not come?” He replied, “Indeed, nephew, now.” He continued applying the scent, then came and sat down and said, “Clear the way in front of us so we can strike the people.” This is not how we used to…

“…fight alongside the Messenger of God ﷺ. How wretchedly you have trained your peers!” Then he fought until he was killed.

Ibn ʿUlayya related to us, from Ayyūb, who said: Thumāma ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Anas related to me, from Anas, who said: I came upon Thābit ibn Qays on the day of al-Yamāma while he was applying scent for burial. I said, “Uncle, do you not see?” He replied, “Now, nephew.” Then he advanced and fought until he was killed. ʿAlī and Mūsā related to us, from Ḥammād ibn Salama, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwa, from his father, who said: The Muslims wheeled back until they reached the baggage. Al-Sāʾib ibn al-ʿAwwām said, “People, you have reached the baggage, so no one can flee beyond his baggage. Return!” They returned, and God routed the polytheists, and Musaylima was killed. Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Zayd ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said, when the people fell back from their baggage, “There is no flight after the baggage.” Then he fought until he was killed. Muʿādh ibn Muʿādh said: Ibn ʿAwn informed us, from Muḥammad, who said: They used to think that Abū Maryam al-Ḥanafī killed Zayd ibn al-Khaṭṭāb on the day of al-Yamāma. He said: Abū Maryam said to ʿUmar, “Commander, God honored Zayd by my hand and did not humiliate me by his hand.” ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to us, from al-Mubārak ibn Faḍāla, from al-Ḥasan, who said: They used to think that Abū Maryam killed Zayd ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. Abū al-Ḥasan related, from Abū Khuzayma al-Ḥanafī, from Qays ibn Ṭalq, who said: Salama ibn Ṣubayḥ, Abū Maryam’s cousin, killed him.

Al-Anṣārī related to us, from his father, from Thumāma, from Anas ibn Mālik, who said: Al-Barāʾ fought Muḥakkim of al-Yamāma in single combat. They exchanged two blows. Muḥakkim struck a shield that al-Barāʾ had with him until the sword bit into his hand. Al-Barāʾ struck his leg and cut it off, then took his sword and beheaded him with it.

Bakkar related to us, from Ibn Ishaq, who said: Muhakkim of al-Yamama, son of Tufayl, was shot with an arrow by ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Abi Bakr al-Siddiq, and ʿAli ibn Muhammad killed him. From Ayyub ibn ʿUtba, from Yahya ibn Abi Kathir, from Abu Salama ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman, who said: ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Abi Bakr shot Muhakkim of al-Yamama with an arrow, which struck his throat and killed him.

Bakkar, from Ibn Ishaq, who said: The Muslims advanced against them until they drove them into the Garden, where the enemy of God Musaylima was. Al-Baraʾ said, “O Muslims, cast me in among them!” He was carried until he was above the wall, then leapt inside and fought them in the Garden until he opened it for the Muslims. God killed Musaylima.

Al-Ansari, from his father, from Thumama, from Anas ibn Malik, who said: Al-Baraʾ threw himself in among them and fought them until he opened the gate. He had more than eighty wounds, from arrows and blows. He was carried to his lodging for treatment, and Khalid stayed with him for a month. Bakkar, from Ibn Ishaq, who said: ʿAbd Allah ibn al-Fadl ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn ʿAbbas ibn Rabiʿa al-Hashimi related to us, from Sulayman ibn Yasar, from Ibn ʿUmar, who said: I heard a man that day shouting, “The black slave killed him!” Bakkar related to us—he said, “Ibn Ishaq related to us”—who said: ʿAbd Allah ibn al-Fadl ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn ʿAbbas ibn Rabiʿa ibn al-Harith related to us, from Sulayman ibn Yasar, from Jaʿfar ibn ʿAmr ibn Umayya al-Damri, who said: Wahshi related to us: “When the people went out against Musaylima, I went out with them and took the spear with which I had killed Hamza. When the people met, I saw Musaylima standing with a sword in his hand, though I did not know him. I readied myself against him, and a man from the Ansar readied himself against him from the other side. Both of us wanted him. I brandished my spear until I was satisfied with it, then thrust it at him and it struck him. The Ansari struck him with his sword. Your Lord knows which of us—”

—killed him. “If I was the one who killed him, then I killed the best of people after the Messenger of God ﷺ and I killed the worst of people.” It is also said that ʿAbd Allah ibn Zayd ibn ʿAsim ibn Kaʿb, of Banu Mazin ibn al-Najjar, killed him. God knows best.

Khalifa related to us, saying: Ibn Ishaq said: When Musaylima was killed, Khalid went out with Mujjaʿa ibn Marara, who walked beside him in chains, to show him Musaylima. He began turning over the slain until he passed Muhakkim of al-Yamama, son of al-Tufayl, who was a large man. Khalid said, “Is this your man?” He replied, “No, this one is better than him. This is Muhakkim of al-Yamama.” Khalid continued until he entered the Garden. There he saw a little, pale, short man. Mujjaʿa said, “This is our man.” Khalid said, “Woe to you! Is this the one who did all that to you?” He replied, “That was indeed so.”

Ibn Ishaq said: Then Mujjaʿa asked him to make peace on behalf of his people. He made peace with him in return for the gold, the silver, the armor, and half the captives—meaning the servants. When the peace agreement was concluded, the fortresses were opened, and there was nothing in them but women and children.

Ibn Ishaq said: Abu Bakr sent Salama ibn Salama ibn Waqsh and Abu Nahik ibn Aws, one of Banu ʿAbd al-Ashhal, to Khalid ibn al-Walid, ordering him not to spare any man of Banu Hanifa who had reached puberty. They found that he had already concluded the peace agreement.

Ibn Ishaq said: ʿAbd Allah ibn Abi Bakr related to me that Abu Bakr al-Siddiq sent a man from the Ansar to Khalid, ordering him to kill those of Banu Hanifa who had reached puberty.

Abu ʿUbayda related to us, from Hammad ibn Salama, from Thabit, from Anas, who said: Abu Dujana threw himself into the Garden, and his leg was broken. He fought until he was killed. Abu al-Hasan, from Abu Maʿshar, from Zayd ibn Aslam and others, who said: One hundred and forty men were killed from among the Emigrants and the Ansar, and the total number of the slain was four hundred and fifty men. Abu al-Hasan, from Sallam ibn Abi Mutiʿ, from Qatada, from Ibn al-Musayyab, who said: The martyrs of al-Yamama numbered five hundred; among them were fifty—or thirty—bearers of the Qurʾan.

Names of those martyred on the day of al-Yamama from Banu ʿAbd Shams of Quraysh. Then, from Banu ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Manaf: Abu Hudhayfa ibn ʿUtba ibn Rabiʿa ibn ʿAbd Shams, a veteran of Badr; and Salim ibn Maʿqil, the client of Abu Hudhayfa, a veteran of Badr, of Banu Asad ibn Khuzayma. Among their allies from Banu Asad ibn Khuzayma was Shujaʿ ibn Wahb ibn Rabiʿa, a veteran of Badr, of Banu Sulaym. Among Banu Sulaym, allies of Banu Asad ibn Khuzayma, were Safwan ibn Umayya ibn ʿAmr and his brother Malik ibn Umayya ibn ʿAmr, a veteran of Badr, from Hadramawt. ʿAli ibn Muhammad said, from Abu Maʿshar, from Yazid ibn Ruman and others, who said: Among their allies from Hadramawt was Makhrama ibn Shurayh, an ally of Banu ʿAbd Shams; and Tufayl ibn ʿAmr al-Dawsi.

Abu Maʿshar said—and al-Hakam ibn Saʿid ibn al-ʿAs, in Abu Maʿshar’s account:

He said: From Banu al-Muttalib. Among Banu al-Muttalib ibn ʿAbd Manaf ibn Qusayy was Jubayr ibn Malik, whose mother was Buhayna bint al-Harith ibn al-Muttalib; he was an ally of theirs from al-Azd.

From Banu Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzza: al-Saʾib ibn al-ʿAwwam ibn Khuwaylid, the brother of al-Zubayr.

From Banu ʿAbd al-Dar: Yazid ibn Aws, an ally of theirs.

From Banu Zuhra. Ibn Ishaq said, in an account other than Abu Maʿshar’s: From Banu Zuhra ibn Kilab. Ibn Ishaq said: Hayy ibn Jariya. Abu Maʿshar said: Yaʿla ibn Jariya al-Thaqafi, an ally of theirs. Abu Maʿshar also said: Habib ibn Asid ibn Jariya, the brother of Abu Basir, ʿUtba ibn Asid.

From Banu Makhzum: From Banu Makhzum ibn Yaqaza, al-Walid ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn al-Mughira. Ibn Ishaq said: And Hakim ibn Hazn ibn Abi Wahb ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAʾidh. Abu Maʿshar said: Hazn ibn Abi Wahb, the grandfather of Saʿid ibn al-Musayyab, and Hakim ibn Abi Wahb.

From Banu ʿAdi. The two said: From Banu ʿAdi ibn Kaʿb, Zayd ibn al-Khattab ibn Nufayl and ʿAbd Allah ibn ʿUmar ibn Bujra. Abu Maʿshar said: They were a household from Yemen whom Bujra ibn Khalaf ibn Sudad ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Qurt ibn Rizah had adopted.

From Banu Layth: ʿAmir ibn al-Bukayr, of Banu Layth, a veteran of Badr.

From Banu Sahm: From Banu Sahm ibn ʿAmr, Abu Qays ibn al-Harith ibn Qays, one of those who emigrated to Abyssinia. Ibn Ishaq said: And ʿAbd Allah ibn al-Harith ibn Qays.

From Banu ʿAmir ibn Luʾayy: From Banu ʿAmir ibn Luʾayy, ʿAbd Allah ibn Makhrama ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzza ibn Abi Qays, a veteran of Badr; and ʿAbd Allah ibn Suhayl ibn ʿAmr, a veteran of Badr. Ibn Ishaq said: Al-Sulayt ibn al-Sulayt ibn ʿAmr; ʿAmr ibn Abi Uways ibn Saʿd ibn Abi Sarh ibn al-Harith ibn Habib ibn Hudhayfa ibn Nasr ibn Malik ibn Hisl; and Rabiʿa ibn Abi Kharasha.

From Banu Munqidh: From Banu Munqidh ibn ʿAmr ibn Muʿawiya, Abu ʿAli ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn al-Harith ibn Rakhsa. Abu Maʿshar said: Sulayt was not killed. The total of all these was twenty-four men, nine of them veterans of Badr.

From the Ansar. Those martyred from among the Ansar, then from Banu ʿAbd al-Ashhal of al-Aws, were: ʿAbbad ibn Bishr ibn Waqsh, a veteran of Badr; ʿAbd Allah ibn ʿAtik; Rafiʿ ibn Sahl, an ally; Hajib ibn Yazid, an ally; and Suhayl ibn ʿAdi. Ibn Ishaq said: The two were from the people of Ratij, of Azd Shanuʾa. Also Malik ibn Aws and ʿUmayr ibn Aws, from Banu Jahjaba. From Banu Jahjaba: Talha ibn ʿUtba and Rabah, the client of al-Harith ibn Malik.

From Banu Anif: Abu ʿAqil, a veteran of Badr.

From Banu al-ʿAjlan: From Banu al-ʿAjlan, Maʿn ibn ʿAdi ibn al-Jadd ibn ʿAjlan. Ibn Ishaq said: And Jarwal ibn al-ʿAbbas ibn ʿAmir ibn Thabit. Abu Maʿshar said: ʿAmir ibn Thabit was from Khazraj. From Khazraj, then from Banu al-Harith ibn al-Khazraj: Thabit ibn Qays ibn Shimas; Bashir ibn ʿAbd Allah; and Kulayb ibn Bishr ibn Tamim, an ally of theirs from Banu al-Hubla. From Banu al-Hubla: ʿAbd Allah ibn Ubayy ibn Salul, a veteran of Badr; and ʿAbd Allah ibn ʿAtban, an ally from Banu Asad.

From Banu Salim: From Banu Salim ibn ʿAwf, Thabit ibn Hazal and Iyas ibn Wadafa, both veterans of Badr.

From Banu Saʿida: From Banu Saʿida, Asʿad ibn Yarbuʿ; Saʿd ibn Jariya ibn Lawdhan ibn ʿAbd Wadd ibn Zayd; Abu Dujana Simak ibn Kharasha; and Saʿd ibn Hammaz, an ally.

From Banu Salima: From Banu Salima, then from Banu Haram, ʿUtba ibn ʿAmir ibn Nabi, a veteran of Badr. Abu Maʿshar said: And Mikhash al-Himyari, an ally.

From Banu Ghanm: From Banu Ghanm ibn Kaʿb, Salama ibn Masʿud ibn Sinan.

From Banu Sawad: From Banu Sawad, Damra ibn ʿIyad, who was a cousin of ʿAbd Allah ibn Unays.

From Banu Mazin ibn al-Najjar: Abu Habbah ibn Ghaziyya ibn ʿAmr. Ibn Ishaq said: And Habib ibn Zayd.

From Banu ʿAmr ibn Mabdhul: From Banu ʿAmr ibn Mabdhul, Habib ibn ʿAmr ibn Muhsin, who was killed on the road while he was on his way.

From Banu Malik ibn al-Najjar: From Banu Malik ibn al-Najjar, ʿUmara ibn Hazm ibn Zayd, a veteran of Badr; Yazid ibn Thabit ibn al-Dahhak, the brother of Zayd, who was struck by an arrow and died on the road; Thabit ibn Khalid ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Nuʿman ibn Khansa; and Farwa ibn al-Nuʿman ibn Isaf.

From Banu Zurayq: From Banu Zurayq, ʿAʾidh ibn Maʿis. All those martyred from among the Ansar numbered thirty-four men. Thus the total from among the Emigrants and the Ansar was fifty-eight men.

The Apostasy in Bahrain

Abu ʿUbayda, from Hammad, from ʿAli ibn Zayd, from al-Hasan, [reported] that al-Hutam bound al-Jarud tightly. ʿAli said: ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Abi Bakra related to me, from his father, who said: Abu Bakr sent al-ʿAlaʾ ibn al-Hadrami to Bahrain. They had apostatized, except for a few who remained steadfast with al-Jarud. They met at Juwaytha, and God routed them; many of them were killed. Bakkar related to us, from Ibn Ishaq, who said: Al-ʿAlaʾ besieged them at Juwaytha until the Muslims nearly perished from hardship. They heard many loud voices, and ʿAbd Allah ibn Hudhaf said, “Let me descend from the fort and bring you news.” He descended from the fort, and they seized him. They asked, “Who are you?” He gave his lineage and began calling out, “O Abjar!” Abjar recognized him and spared him. He returned to his companions and informed them that the people were drunk. Al-ʿAlaʾ attacked them by night, and they killed them in a severe slaughter.

The Apostasy in Oman, al-Nujayr, Hadramawt, and Yemen. Abu Bakr sent ʿIkrima ibn Abi Jahl to Oman. Abu Bakr sent al-Muhajir ibn Abi Umayya al-Makhzumi and Ziyad ibn Labid al-Ansari to the people of al-Nujayr. They had apostatized, and among them was al-Ashʿath ibn Qays al-Kindi. They besieged them. Al-Ashʿath asked them for safe conduct for himself, his children, and his property, on condition that he open the fort to them. They agreed, and he opened it for them. They killed those who were in the fort and sent al-Ashʿath to Abu Bakr, who spared him and protected his life.

Ibn Ishaq said: A group of Kinda, along with Jariya ibn Suraqa, fell upon Ziyad ibn Labid. Ibn Ishaq said: ʿAbd Allah ibn Abi Bakr related to me that Ziyad ibn Labid attacked them by night and killed the four kings: Jamad, Makhwas, Mashriq, and Abdaʿa. It was there that al-Aswad al-ʿAnsi was killed.

The Liar. Abū al-Ḥasan, from Yaʿqūb ibn Dāwūd al-Thaqafī, said: “Our elders in Ṣanʿāʾ were asked about the killing of al-ʿAnsī. They said: ‘We used to hear our fathers mention that Dādhūwayh, Qays, and Fayrūz entered his house. Fayrūz smashed his neck and killed him.’ It is also said that Qays ibn Makshūḥ killed him.” Abū al-Ḥasan narrated to us from ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from al-Zuhrī, who said: “Fayrūz, Dādhūwayh, and Qays entered upon him.” In that year, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq died. The wound from the arrow he had shot on the day of al-Ṭāʾif reopened, and he died from it. ʿAnāb ibn Asīd ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya conducted the pilgrimage; it is also said that ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf did so, and it is also said that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb did so. Bakr, from Ibn Isḥāq; and Wahb ibn Jarīr, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Nāfiʿ narrated to me that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb conducted the pilgrimage in the year eleven, and that Aslam, his freedman, was purchased from some people of the Ashʿarīs. Wakīʿ ibn al-Jarrāḥ said: al-Aʿmash informed us, from Abū Wāʾil, in a report he mentioned, that Abū Bakr sent ʿUmar, who conducted the pilgrimage for the people. In that year Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda al-Anṣārī died; it is also said that he died in the year fifteen.

The Year 12

In it Abū Bakr sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to the land of Baṣra, which was called the land of India. ʿAwn ibn Kahmas ibn al-Ḥasan narrated to us, saying: ʿImrān ibn Ḥudayr informed us, saying: A man among us, called Muqātil, narrated to us from Quṭba ibn Qatāda al-Sadūsī, who said: “Khālid ibn al-Walīd charged against us with his cavalry. We said, ‘We are Muslims,’ so he left us alone. We campaigned with him against al-Ubulla and conquered it, until they were even letting their dogs lap [from the water].”

Abū ʿUbayda, ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad, and others said: Khālid ibn al-Walīd came to ʿAyn al-Tamr and besieged them until they submitted to his judgment. He killed and took captives. Among those captives was Sīrīn, Abū Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn. Among them was Yasār, who was a slave of Qays ibn Makhrama; among his descendants was Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq ibn Yasār, the author of the sīra. Among them was Nuṣayr, Abū Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr. Among them was Rabāḥ, father of ʿAbd Allāh and ʿUbayd Allāh, the two sons of Rabāḥ. Among them was Hurmuz; they are called in Baṣra the Harāmis in a group whose number reaches forty. I dislike mentioning them.

In it Abū al-ʿĀṣ ibn al-Rabīʿ, the husband of Zaynab, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ—died. In it Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq led the people in the pilgrimage. Before that, in Rajab, Abū Bakr went out to perform the lesser pilgrimage and appointed ʿUmar over Medina; when he went on pilgrimage, he appointed ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān.

The Year 13

Bakr, from Ibn Isḥāq, said: When Abū Bakr returned from the pilgrimage, he sent ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ toward Palestine, and Yazīd ibn Abī Sufyān, Abū ʿUbayda ibn al-Jarrāḥ, and Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasana. He ordered them to take the route through al-Balqāʾ. Ibn Isḥāq said: Abū Bakr wrote to Khālid ibn al-Walīd, and he set out for Syria. He raided Ghassān at Marj Rāhiṭ, then proceeded and camped at the canal of Buṣrā. Yazīd ibn Abī Sufyān, Abū ʿUbayda ibn al-Jarrāḥ, and Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasana came to him. Buṣrā made terms, and it was the first of the cities of Syria to be conquered. On that expedition he also made terms with the people of Tadmur. He passed through Ḥawārīn, killing and taking captives, and raided the villages of Ghassān at Marj Rāhiṭ, killing and taking captives. Ibn Isḥāq said: Then they all proceeded toward Palestine and met at Ajnādayn, between al-Ramla and Bayt Jibrīn. Each commander was over his own troops. Some people claim that ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ was over all of them, and that al-Qubuqlār was over the Romans. Al-Qubuqlār was killed, and God routed the polytheists. That was on Saturday, with three days remaining in Jumādā al-Ūlā, in the year thirteen.

Ibn Isḥāq said: Among those who were martyred on the day of Ajnādayn, and from whom hadith was preserved, were Nuʿaym ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Naḥḥām al-ʿAdawī and Hishām ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil al-Sahmī. Abū al-Ḥasan said: Also martyred that day were al-Faḍl ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and Abān ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. Ibn al-Kalbī said: Al-Faḍl ibn ʿAbbās was martyred that day. Ibn Isḥāq said: In this year was the battle of Marj al-Ṣuffar, on Thursday, with twelve days remaining in Jumādā al-Ūlā, in the year thirteen; the commander was Khālid ibn Saʿīd. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām narrated to me from his father, from his grandfather, who said: Khālid ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ was martyred on the day of Marj al-Ṣuffar; it is also said that ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ was killed. Al-Faḍl ibn ʿAbbās and ʿIkrima ibn Abī Jahl were also killed; it is also said that Abān ibn Saʿīd was martyred that day. Ibn Isḥāq said: That day they routed [the enemy?] and killed a great number of the polytheists, and God defeated them. Ibn Isḥāq said: Then they met at Fihl in Dhū al-Qaʿda of the year thirteen. God routed the polytheists, and they killed a great number of them. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf led the people in the pilgrimage. Umayya ibn Khālid, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar: When ʿUmar was appointed caliph, he sent ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, who led the people in the pilgrimage. Then ʿUmar performed the pilgrimage for the remainder of his rule, until he died.

The death of al-Ṣiddīq and his age. In it Abū Bakr ﵀ and be pleased with him—died. Ibn Isḥāq said: At the end of two years, three months, and twelve days from the death of the Messenger of God ﷺ. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad, Abū al-Yaqẓān, and two others narrated to me; they said: He died on Tuesday, with eight days remaining in Jumādā al-Ākhira. Abū Bakr died at the age of sixty-three; there is no disagreement about his age. Abū Dāwūd, from Zuhayr, from Abū Isḥāq, said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUtba said, “The Messenger of God ﷺ—died at the age of sixty-three.”

ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd said: Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated to me from Muʿāwiya, who said: “The Messenger of God ﷺ—died at the age of sixty-three, and Abū Bakr died at the age of sixty-three.”

Abū Aḥmad and Salm, from Yūnus ibn Abī Isḥāq, from Abū al-Safar, from al-Shaʿbī, from Muʿāwiya, said: Abū Bakr died at the age of sixty-three. Ibn Abī ʿAdī, from Dāwūd, from ʿĀmir, said: He died at the age of sixty-three. ʿAbd al-Wahhāb said: I heard Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd say, “I heard Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab say: He died at the age of sixty-three.” Ibn Abī ʿAdī, from Ḥabīb ibn al-Shahīd, from Maymūn ibn Mihrān, from Yazīd al-Aṣamm: The Prophet ﷺ—said to Abū Bakr, “Which of us is older, you or I?” He said, “Rather, you are greater, nobler, and better, while I am older than you.”

The duration of the caliphate of al-Ṣiddīq: his rule lasted two years, three months, and twenty days; it is also said ten days. Abū Bakr was born in the house of Abū Quḥāfa in Mecca.

The caliphate of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵁.

In it ʿUmar was pledged allegiance. ʿUmar’s mother was Ḥatma bint Hāshim ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm. In it ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was pledged allegiance, and he dismissed Khālid ibn al-Walīd from Syria and al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha from the region of the Sawād, the Sawād of Kūfa. Muʿādh used to raid in that region. From Ibn ʿAwn, from Muḥammad, who said: When ʿUmar took office, he said, “I shall certainly dismiss Khālid, so that he may know that God grants victory to His religion alone.” ʿAlī and Mūsā, from Ḥammād ibn Salama, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwa, from his father, said: When ʿUmar was appointed caliph, he wrote to Abū ʿUbayda: “I have appointed you and dismissed Khālid.” Abū Bakr went out from Medina to Dhū al-Qaṣṣa to fight the people of apostasy and appointed Sinān al-Ḍamrī over Medina; it is also said that it was Usāma ibn Zayd. Ibn Masʿūd over the passes of Medina. The list of Abū Bakr’s officials: over Bahrain, al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Ḥaḍramī, until Abū Bakr died; ʿUmar confirmed him in office.

Al-Anṣārī narrated to us from Ibn ʿAwn, from Mūsā ibn Anas, that Abū Bakr appointed Anas over Bahrain. Abū Bakr sent ʿIkrima ibn Abī Jahl to ʿUmān. Its people had apostatized, and he prevailed over them. Then Abū Bakr sent him to Yemen and appointed Ḥudhayfa al-ʿAlqānī over ʿUmān; he remained there until Abū Bakr died. Abū Bakr sent al-Muhājir ibn Abī Umayya al-Makhzūmī and Ziyād ibn Labīd al-Anṣārī to Yemen.

Al-Muhājir was over Ṣanʿāʾ and Ziyād over the rest of the coast—or that was after he had besieged the people of al-Nujayr. We have written the account of al-Nujayr. Yaʿlā ibn Umayya was over Khawlān. Abū Bakr confirmed ʿAttāb ibn Asīd in office; Abū Bakr and ʿAttāb died on the same day. Abū Bakr appointed ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ over al-Ṭāʾif and appointed Sulayṭ ibn Qays over al-Yamāma. We have written about Syria and the account of Khālid ibn al-Walīd in Iraq. Abū Bakr performed the pilgrimage in the year twelve and appointed Qatāda ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Ẓafarī, one of the Anṣār, over Medina; it is also said that he appointed Ibn Umm Maktūm. Ibn Isḥāq said: It is also said that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb performed the pilgrimage. He said: It is also said that ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf [was the pilgrim leader]. ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān was his scribe, and Shadīd, his freedman, was his chamberlain. It is also said that Zayd ibn Thābit also wrote for him. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was in charge of all his affairs and of the judiciary. He had appointed Abū ʿUbayda ibn al-Jarrāḥ over the treasury, then sent him to Syria. His muezzin was Saʿd al-Qaraẓ, the freedman of ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir.

The conquests of Abū ʿUbayd al-Thaqafī in Iraq.

Bakr, from Ibn Isḥāq, said: In that year ʿUmar sent Abū ʿUbayd ibn Masʿūd al-Thaqafī to Iraq. He met Jābān between al-Ḥīra and al-Qādisiyya, scattered his forces, and captured his companions. Jābān ransomed himself. Then he raided Kaskar and met Narsī; God routed them. Abū ʿUbayd entered Bārūsmā, and Ibn al-Andarzaghr made terms with him for four dirhams per head. Abū ʿUbayd sent al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha to Zandaward. They fought him, and he killed and took captives. ʿĀṣim ibn ʿAmr al-Usaydī was sent to Nahr Jawbar, and ʿUrwa ibn Zayd al-Khayl to al-Zawābī; they made terms with him on the same terms as Bārūsmā. When the defeated marzbāns returned to Yazdegerd, he reviled them and banished them. He summoned Bahman ibn Kharrar Mazman, Dhu al-Ḥājib, appointed him over twelve thousand, and handed him the Drafsh Kābīān, which they used to seek blessing from. He gave him many weapons and loaded him with quantities of war equipment, and handed him the white elephant. Abū ʿUbayd heard of their advance, crossed the Euphrates, and cut the bridge. Dhu al-Ḥājib arrived and camped at Quss al-Nāṭif, with the Euphrates between him and Abū ʿUbayd. He sent to Abū ʿUbayd: “Either you cross to us, or we cross to you.” Abū ʿUbayd said, “We will cross to you.” Ibn Ṣalūtā built the bridge for him, and they crossed. They met in a narrow pass. That was at the end of Ramaḍān or the beginning of Shawwāl, in the year thirteen. Dhu al-Ḥājib brought Jālīnūs with him, along with the white elephant and the Drafsh Kābīān. They fought fiercely. Abū ʿUbayd struck the elephant’s trunk, and Abū Miḥjan struck its hock. Abū ʿUbayd was killed ﵀. Abū ʿUbayd had said: “If I am killed, then command is yours, Jabr ibn Abī ʿUbayd. If he is killed, then command is yours, Abū Jabr ibn Abī ʿUbayd. If he is killed, then command is yours, Ḥabīb ibn Abī Rabīʿa ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUmayr. If he is killed, then command is yours, Abū Qays ibn Ḥabīb ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUmayr. If he is killed, then command is yours, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUmayr, who is the brother of Abū ʿUbayd.”

It is also said that the first to whom command was entrusted was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd. All the commanders were killed, and al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha took the banner. The killing among the Muslims became severe, so they made for the bridge. Al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha, ʿUrwa ibn Zayd, al-Kalaj al-Ḍabbī, ʿĀṣim ibn ʿAmr al-Usaydī, and ʿAmr ibn al-Ṣalt al-Sulamī protected them until they reached the bridge. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Khaṭmī had got there before them; it is also said that he was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Thaqafī. He cut the bridge and said, “Fight for your religion!” The people plunged into the Euphrates, and many drowned. Then al-Muthannā repaired the bridge, and the Muslims crossed. That day, eighteen hundred Muslims were martyred; it is also said that there were four thousand, killed or drowned. Al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha al-Shaybānī withdrew with the people. ʿUmar then sent Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bajalī. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām, from his father, from his grandfather, said something similar.

Al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Ḥaḍramī conquers al-Zāra and al-Ghāba. Abū ʿUbayda said: Abū Bakr died while al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Ḥaḍramī was besieging the people of al-Zāra, and ʿUmar confirmed him in his post. Al-Barāʾ ibn Mālik fought the marzbān of al-Zāra in single combat and killed him. Al-Barāʾ took his weapon and his belt, which amounted to thirty thousand, and said, “This is my property.” ʿUmar took a fifth of it. Then a man came out to al-ʿAlāʾ seeking safe-conduct and showed him a spring outside al-Zāra from which they used to drink. From outside, al-ʿAlāʾ blocked it, and they made peace with him on the terms that he should have a third of the city and a third of the gold and silver in it. Al-ʿAlāʾ raided the city of al-Ghāba, killed the non-Arabs who were in it, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf led the pilgrimage in the year thirteen.

Chapter 4

The caliphate of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb

The Year 14

the conquest of Damascus. In it Damascus was conquered. Abū ʿUbayda ibn al-Jarrāḥ set out with Khālid ibn al-Walīd and besieged them

They made peace with him and opened for him the Jābiya Gate. Khālid forced open one of the gates, and Abū ʿUbayda completed the peace agreement for them.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: Khālid was in command of the people and made peace with them. He had not finished the peace agreement when he was dismissed and Abū ʿUbayda was appointed. Abū ʿUbayda ratified Khālid’s peace agreement and did not alter the document; the document they have is in Khālid’s name. This is an error, because ʿUmar dismissed Khālid when he assumed office.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra related to us, from his father, who said: Abū ʿUbayda made peace with them on the terms that they would retain half their churches and dwellings, and that they would pay a poll tax. [The source repeats:] on the terms that they would retain half their churches and dwellings, and that they would pay a poll tax; they would not be prevented from holding their festivals, and none of their churches would be demolished. The people of the city made peace on those terms, while he took the rest of the land by force.

Ibn al-Kalbī said: The peace was made on Sunday, in the middle of Rajab, in the year fourteen. Abū ʿUbayda ibn al-Jarrāḥ made peace with them. Bakr related to me, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Abū ʿUbayda made peace with them in Rajab.

The battle of Faḥl. Ibn al-Kalbī said: Then came the battle of Faḥl, on Saturday, with eight nights remaining in Dhū al-Ḥijja of the year fourteen. The Muslims gained control of the land after fierce fighting. They asked Abū ʿUbayda for a peace agreement, and he made peace with them; they wrote an agreement between them.

Bakr ibn ʿAṭiyya related to me, who said: Abū ʿUbayda besieged them during Rajab, Shaʿbān, the month of Ramaḍān, and Shawwāl, and the peace agreement was made in Dhū al-Qaʿda.

Bakr, from Ibn Isḥāq, said: Faḥl was in the year thirteen, before Damascus.

The conquest of Ḥimṣ and Baʿlabakk. Ibn Isḥāq and others said: In it—that is, the year fourteen—Ḥimṣ and Baʿlabakk were conquered by a peace agreement at the hands of Abū ʿUbayda in Dhū al-Qaʿda. It is also said that this happened in the year fifteen.

The conquests of Baṣra

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad, from his shaykhs, said: They said: In the year fourteen, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb sent Shurayḥ ibn ʿĀmir, one of Banū Saʿd ibn Bakr, to Baṣra and said, “Be a support for the Muslims.” He set out for al-Ahwāz and was killed at Dāris. ʿUmar then sent ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān, one of Banū Māzin ibn Manṣūr, in the month of Rabīʿ of the year fourteen. He remained for several months without raiding. ʿUmar appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān—also said to be ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sahl al-Anṣārī—to govern in his place, but he died before reaching Baṣra. ʿUmar wrote to al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Ḥaḍramī, who was in Bahrain: “Set out for ʿUtba; I have appointed you to his post.” Al-ʿAlāʾ set out but died at Tiyās, in the land of Banū Tamīm, before arriving. Then ʿUtba raided and conquered al-Ubulla, the Euphrates, and Abzqubādh. He took captives from Maysān, among them Yasār, Abū al-Ḥasan ibn Abī al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī.

The one who conquered the Euphrates was Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd, whom ʿUtba had appointed to it. It is also said that al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba, whom ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān had appointed, conquered Maysān, the district of Maysān, Abzqubādh, and the banks of the Tigris. Muslim and al-Ḍaḥḥāk said: Sawāda ibn Abī al-Aswad related to us, from Qatāda, that ʿUmar sent ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān, who raided al-Ubulla. Marḥūm ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz said: My father related to me, from Khālid ibn ʿUmayr al-ʿAdawī, who said: We raided al-Ubulla with ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān and conquered it, then crossed to the Euphrates.

ʿAwn ibn Kahmas said: ʿImrān ibn Ḥudayr informed us: A man from among us, called Muqātil, related to us

From Quṭba ibn Qatāda al-Sadūsī, who said: We raided al-Ubulla with Khālid ibn al-Walīd and conquered it. This is an error: Khālid passed through Baṣra during Abū Bakr’s governorship.

Among the captives from Maysān was Arṭabān, the grandfather of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwn.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām said: My father related to me, from Ibn ʿAwn, from his father, from Arṭabān, who said: I was a deacon in the church of Maysān, and I fell as a share to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dhurra al-Muzanī.

Abū ʿUmar al-Shaybānī, from one who informed him, from Mujālid, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: Ṭamāhīj, daughter of Kisrā and sister of Shīrawayh, made peace with ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān concerning Maysān. They say that the mistress of the Nahr al-Marʾa sent [word] at Umm Azdān, and Ibn Ghazwān made peace concerning the territory beyond her river as far as the site of the Greater Bridge.

Abū al-Yaqaẓān, from Ṣadaqa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-Māzinī, said: Thābit ibn ʿAmāra related to us, from Ghunaym ibn Qays, who said: We were with ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān. When he reached the mainland beyond the reed beds, he said, “This is not among the dwelling places of the Arabs.” So he settled at al-Khurayba.

Ṣafwān ibn ʿĪsā said: Abū Naʿāma related to us, from Khālid ibn ʿUmayr al-ʿAdawī, who said: ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān passed by the site of al-Mirbad and found thick, coarse gravel. He said, “This is Baṣra. Settle here, in God’s name.”

Ghundar related to us, from Shuʿba, from ʿUqayl ibn Ṭalḥa, from Qabīṣa, who said: We were with ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān at al-Khurayba.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maymūn related to us, from ʿAwf, from al-Ḥasan, who said: ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān conquered al-Ubulla. Seventy Muslims were killed at the site of the mosque of al-Ubulla. Then he crossed to the Euphrates and took it by force.

Ibrāhīm ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn Dirham related to us, from his father, who heard Abū Hurayra, from the Prophet ﷺ

—and grant him peace: Martyrs will be gathered from the Mosque of al-ʿAshshār at al-Ubulla; none will stand alongside the martyrs of Badr except them.

In it, ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān ordered Miḥjan ibn al-Adraʿ to lay out the site of the Great Mosque of Baṣra, and he built it with reeds. Then ʿUtba set out on pilgrimage, leaving Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd in charge and ordering him to march to the Euphrates. He ordered al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba to lead the people in prayer until Mujāshiʿ arrived. The people of Maysān assembled against al-Mughīra, with al-Fīlkān, a great man among the people of Abzqubādh, at their head. Al-Mughīra prevailed over them and wrote to ʿUmar with news of the conquest. ʿUmar ordered ʿUtba to set out for his post, but he died before setting out, so ʿUmar confirmed al-Mughīra over Baṣra.

In it, ʿUmar sent Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bajalī to al-Sawād. Al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha had been raiding in that region. Jarīr encountered Mihrān and killed him. This was in Ṣafar of the year fourteen. Jarīr and al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha disputed over the command, so ʿUmar sent Saʿd ibn Mālik and wrote to them both, “Listen to him and obey him.” They listened to him and obeyed.

In it, al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha died. In it, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakra was born in Baṣra; he was the first child born there. In it, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ordered the people to assemble for the night prayers in Ramaḍān. In it, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf led the wives of the Prophet ﷺ—on pilgrimage. In it, Abū Quḥāfa ʿUthmān ibn ʿAmr, father of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq, died. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵁ led the pilgrimage from the year fourteen through the year twenty-three.

The Year 15The conquests of the Jordan, al-Biqāʿ, Baʿlabakk, and Ḥimṣ

ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra related to me, from his father, who said: Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasana conquered all of the Jordan

Ibn Mughīra, from his father, said: He made peace with them on the terms that they would retain half their dwellings and churches and that the land tax would be imposed. Ibn al-Kalbī said: Then Abū ʿUbayda set out intending for Ḥimṣ, and sent Khālid ahead of him. They fought him fiercely, then the Byzantines were routed until they entered their city, where he besieged them. They asked him for peace on the terms that their property, persons, and churches would be secure, and for the land of Ḥimṣ, in return for one hundred thousand dinars and seventy thousand dinars. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra related to me, from his father, who said: Abū ʿUbayda made peace with them concerning the city on the same terms as those on which he had made peace with the people of Damascus, and took their other towns by force. Ḥātim ibn Muslim related to me, from someone who related to him, from Ibn Isḥāq, something similar.

The decisive battle of the Yarmūk. In it was the battle of the Yarmūk. Bakr, from Ibn Isḥāq, said: The Byzantines encamped at the Yarmūk. They numbered one hundred thousand Byzantines, and the tribes of Quḍāʿa were with them. Al-Sufilār, a eunuch of Heraclius, was in command of them.

Ibn al-Kalbī said: The Byzantines numbered three hundred thousand, and Bahān was in command of them, a man descended from the Persians who had become Christian and joined the Byzantines. Abū ʿUbayda gathered the contingents and the commanders of the armies under him, and ʿUmar reinforced him with Saʿīd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḥudhaym. God routed the polytheists after fierce fighting, and a great number of them were killed.

Ibn al-Kalbī said: The battle took place on Monday, five days into Rajab of the year fifteen.

Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq: ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ was martyred on the day of the decisive battle of the Yarmūk

Abū al-Ḥasan said: Sahl ibn ʿAmr and al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām were martyred on the day of the Yarmūk.

The conquest of Nahr Tīrī and Dasht Maysān in Iraq. In this year in Iraq, Nahr Tīrī, Dasht Maysān, and its villages were conquered. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather, that al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba made peace with them for one million and one hundred thousand dirhams. Then they broke the agreement, and Abū Mūsā conquered it afterward.

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to me, from al-Naḍr ibn Isḥāq, from Qatāda, that al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba conquered Nahr Tīrī by force. He found al-Nawshajān’s ḥadd there; he was its owner at that time.

The battle of al-Qādisiyya. In it was the battle of al-Qādisiyya. Saʿd ibn Mālik commanded the Muslims, and Rustam commanded the polytheists, with al-Jālinūs and Dhū al-Ḥājib alongside him. More than one person related to me, from Abū ʿAwāna, from Ḥuṣayn, from Abū Wāʾil, who said: The Muslims numbered between seven and eight thousand, while Rustam faced us with sixty thousand. Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ, from al-Ḥajjāj, from Ḥumayd ibn Hilāl, from Khālid ibn ʿUmayr, who said: They numbered forty thousand. Al-Ḥajjāj said: ʿAbd Allāh related to me that they had seventy elephants with them.

Bakr, from Ibn Isḥāq, said: Rustam had sixty thousand from the elite of his dīwān, and the Muslims numbered six or seven thousand

One who heard Sharīk related to us, from ʿUbayda, from Ibrāhīm, who said: They numbered between eight and nine thousand. About two thousand came to reinforce them. They remained for about a month without the enemy meeting them. Saʿd sent Zahra ibn Ḥawiyya on a raid, and he encountered Shārzādh ibn Āzādhba at al-Sīlayḥīn. Shārzādh was killed—he was killed by Bukayr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Laythī—and they seized much jewelry or precious stones. Saʿd wrote to ʿUmar asking him for reinforcements.

Ibn Zurayʿ, from Ḥajjāj, from Ḥumayd ibn Hilāl, from Khālid ibn ʿUmayr, who said: The people of Baṣra reinforced them with one thousand five hundred men; I was among them.

Ibn Isḥāq said: Al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba marched with four hundred men, and Qays ibn Makshūḥ with seven hundred.

Abū al-Ḥasan said: They fought fiercely for three days. The first was Monday, with three nights remaining in Shawwāl; it is also said, with [some] days remaining in Ramaḍān. God routed the polytheists, and Rustam was killed. It is said that Zahra ibn Ḥawiyya killed him; it is also said Hilāl ibn ʿUlfa, or ʿAmr ibn Maʿdīkarib; and it is also said that he died of thirst. Ḥanẓala ibn Rabīʿa al-Asadī killed Dhū al-Ḥājib. Saʿd ordered Zahra ibn Ḥawiyya to pursue the Persians. He caught up with them at al-Kharrār and killed al-Jālinūs, taking his spoils. It is also said that Kathīr ibn Shihāb killed him. They killed them from al-Kharrār to al-Sīlayḥīn and to al-Najaf. When evening came, Zahra held back from them and returned.

In the report of Abū ʿAwāna, from Ḥuṣayn, from Abū Wāʾil, who said: We pursued them to the Euphrates, and God routed them; we pursued them to al-Ṣarāt, and God routed them, driving them into al-Madāʾin.

In the report of Ibn Zurayʿ, from Ḥajjāj, from ʿAbd Allāh, who said: The infantry felled seventy elephants in the trench.

One who heard Abū Muḥṣin related to us, from Ḥuṣayn, from Abū Wāʾil, who said: I saw myself crossing the trench on foot over the men; some of them were killing others. He said: They had no weapons.

The Conquest of al-Madāʾin

Abū al-Ḥasan said: Then Saʿd set out from al-Qādisiyyah, with them following him. The people of al-Ḥīrah came to him and said, “We remain true to our agreement.” Bisṭām, the master of the Bisṭām Canal, also came to him, and he made a treaty with him. Saʿd crossed the Euphrates and encountered a force at Nars, commanded by Baṣbahri. Zuhrah ibn Ḥawiyyah killed him. Then he encountered a force at Kūthā, commanded by al-Fayrūzān, and God defeated them. Then he encountered a force at Dayr Kaʿb, commanded by al-Farrukhān, and God defeated them. Then Saʿd and the Muslims proceeded until they alighted at al-Madāʾin and conquered it. Saʿd ibn ʿUbayd ibn al-Nuʿmān was killed on the day of al-Qādisiyyah, months later.

There is also a report about it. Muḥammad ibn ʿUmar narrated to us; he said: Muḥammad ibn Khāzim narrated to us, from al-Aʿmash, from Ḥabīb ibn Ṣahbān, who said: I was with Saʿd ibn Mālik when a man came to him and said, “Nothing prevents you from crossing except this little stream.” Then he plunged his horse in and made it cross the Tigris. Then he recited, “No soul can die except by God’s permission.” The people plunged their horses in. When the Persians saw them, they cried, “Dīwān! Dīwān!” The people crossed and found nothing missing except a drinking cup that had been hanging from the tassel of the saddle. I saw it floating on the water, bobbing up and down. We seized their encampment. In it were sacks of camphor as large as men. We also took some of their cattle and slaughtered them. The people began putting camphor on the meat and saying, “How bitter is the salt of the Persians!” He said: We found so many golden vessels that a man would exchange yellow for white—that is, gold for silver.

Someone who heard Abū Muḥṣin narrated to us, from Ḥuṣayn, from Abū Wāʾil, who said: We forced them back to al-Madāʾin. They entered it, and the Muslims alighted at Dayr al-Masāliḥ. We began fighting them. The Muslims said, “These people are inside houses, while we are out in the open and in the deserts. Take us across to them.” The Muslims crossed above al-Madāʾin and below it. We plunged into the water until we crossed to them, then besieged them on the eastern side until they ate the dogs and cats there. They came out under a safe-conduct, with their families and baggage, and proceeded until they alighted at Jalūlāʾ.

Someone who heard Maslamah narrated to us, from Dāwūd ibn Abī Hind, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: The first to plunge his horse into the Tigris was Saʿd.

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad narrated to me, from Abū al-Dhayyāl, from Ḥumayd ibn Hilāl, that the first to cross was Hilāl ibn ʿUlafah. It is also said that the first to cross was a man from ʿAbd al-Qays.

Abū al-Ḥasan, from Ḥabbāb ibn Mūsā, from ʿĀṣim ibn Bahdalah, from Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh, who said: Saʿd crossed with four hundred men. They used to converse on their horses’ backs just as they conversed on land. Maslamah mentioned, from al-Muthannā, from Abū ʿUthmān, who said: That day a man riding a chestnut horse drowned. He slipped from its back, and the horse came out shaking off its sweat.

Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab was born two years into the caliphate of ʿUmar; Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith died with two years remaining of ʿUmar’s caliphate.

In this year ʿUmar appointed ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ over the land of ʿUmān and al-Baḥrayn. He set out for ʿUmān and sent his brother al-Ḥakam ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ to al-Baḥrayn.

The Year 16

The conquest of al-Ahwāz: In it al-Ahwāz was conquered, then its people rebelled. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām narrated to me, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: Al-Mughīrah set out for al-Ahwāz, and al-Bīrzān made a treaty with him for two million dirhams, eight hundred thousand, and ninety thousand. Then al-Ashʿarī campaigned against them afterward.

The conquest of Aleppo, Antioch, Manbij, and Bayt al-Maqdis

In this year Aleppo, Antioch, and Manbij were conquered. ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīrah said: My father narrated to me that Abū ʿUbaydah sent ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ after he had finished from al-Yarmūk to Qinnasrīn. He made treaties with the people of Aleppo, Manbij, and Antioch, and conquered the rest of the land of Qinnasrīn by force.

Ibn al-Kalbī said: Abū ʿUbaydah made a treaty with the people of Aleppo and wrote them a document. Then Abū ʿUbaydah set out, with Khālid ibn al-Walīd at the head of his vanguard, and besieged the people of Īliyāʾ. They asked him for a treaty on condition that ʿUmar himself grant it to them and write them a guarantee of security. Abū ʿUbaydah wrote to ʿUmar, and ʿUmar came and made a treaty with them. He stayed for some days, then set out for Medina. Ibn al-Kalbī said: That was in the year sixteen. Bakr narrated to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥah ibn Rakānah narrated to us, from Sālim ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar, who said: The people of Īliyāʾ came out to ʿUmar and made a treaty with him on payment of the jizyah, and they opened it to him. In this year Māriyah, mother of Ibrāhīm, the Messenger of God ﷺ—died. In it Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah al-Anṣārī died in Syria.

The Year 17

In it ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb went to Sargh and appointed Zayd ibn Thābit as his deputy in Medina. The plague was there, so he returned. In it Abū Bakrah, Nāfiʿ ibn al-Ḥārith, Shibl ibn Maʿbad, and Ziyād bore witness against al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah. ʿUmar removed him from Baṣra and appointed Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī over it.

The conquests of Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī

ʿĀmir ibn Ḥafṣ said: Abū Mūsā arrived in Baṣra in the year seventeen. ʿUmar wrote to him, “March to the districts of al-Ahwāz.” Abū Mūsā set out and appointed ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn. He came to al-Ahwāz and conquered it; it is said by force, and it is said by treaty. ʿUmar imposed on it ten million and four hundred thousand.

Rayḥān ibn ʿIṣmah said: ʿUmar ibn Marzūq informed us, from Abū Farqad, who said: We were with Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī at al-Ahwāz, and on his cavalry were quilted brocade coverings.

Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from ʿAbd al-Razzāq, from Maʿmar, from Ayyūb, from Ibn Sīrīn, who said: Abū Mūsā conquered al-Ahwāz.

Abū al-Ḥasan, from Khallād ibn ʿUbaydah, from ʿAlī ibn Zayd, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakrah, who said: Al-Ahwāz was conquered by treaty or by force. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām said: Maslamah ibn Muḥārib narrated to me; he said: Qaḥdham narrated to us: Ziyād strove during his governorship to distinguish what had been taken by treaty from what had been taken by force, but he could not.

Abū ʿĀṣim narrated to us; he said: ʿImrān ibn Ḥudayr narrated to us, from Abū Mijlaz, who said: ʿUmar returned al-Ahwāz to the jizyah after they had divided it among the Muslims and their women had been violated. Then Abū Mūsā made treaties with al-Sabbān and the people of the Tīrī Canal. Then he set out for Manādhir and besieged its people, then withdrew from it and appointed al-Rabīʿ ibn Ziyād al-Ḥārithī in his place. He conquered it by force, killing and taking captives. Among the Muslims killed there was al-Muhājir ibn Ziyād al-Ḥārithī.

The battle of Jalūlāʾ

In it was the battle of Jalūlāʾ. Yazdajird son of Kisrā fled to Jalūlāʾ after the battle of al-Madāʾin, while Saʿd remained at al-Madāʾin. Yazdajird wrote to the mountains, and they assembled the fighters and sent them to Jalūlāʾ. A large force gathered there under Khurzād ibn Khurrahurmuz. Saʿd wrote to ʿUmar to inform him, and ʿUmar wrote: “Remain where you are, and send an army against them, for God is your supporter and will fulfill His promise to you.” So he appointed…

…Saʿd appointed Hāshim ibn ʿUtbah ibn Abī Waqqāṣ over them. They met, and the Muslims made a brief retreat. Then God defeated the polytheists, and a great number of them were killed. The Muslims captured their camp and obtained great wealth, weapons, mounts, and captives. The spoils amounted to eighteen million. Shuʿayb ibn Ḥayyān narrated to me, from ʿAmr ibn Yaḥyā, from Sayf, who said: Mujālid narrated to us, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: The booty of Jalūlāʾ was divided at thirty million.

Someone who heard Abū Muḥṣin narrated to me, from Ḥuṣayn, from Abū Wāʾil, who said: We fought them at Jalūlāʾ, and the Muslims made a brief retreat. Hāshim called out, “O Muslims, where are you going? Have you not seen what you left behind? Are you going to come to ʿUmar in defeat?” The Muslims turned back against them, and God defeated them. Jalūlāʾ was named “the Conquest of Conquests.”

ʿUthām ibn ʿAlī, from al-Aʿmash, from Shimr ibn ʿAṭiyyah, who said: The shares at Jalūlāʾ were three thousand.

Abū Madīn, from ʿAmr ibn Yaḥyā, from Sayf, from ʿUbaydah, from Shaqīq, who said: Jalūlāʾ was named al-Waqīʿah because of the evil that enveloped it. More than one person narrated to us, from Abū ʿAwānah, from Ḥuṣayn, from Abū Wāʾil, who said: Jalūlāʾ was named “the Conquest of Conquests.”

Shuʿayb narrated to me, from ʿAmr ibn Yaḥyā, from Sayf, who said: Jalūlāʾ took place in the year seventeen.

Muʿādh ibn Hishām narrated to us; he said: My father narrated to me, from Qatādah, who said: It took place in the year nineteen.

Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Umm al-Hudhayl, Bisṭām, and Hayyāj ibn ʿImrān ibn al-Fuḍayl were among the captives of Jalūlāʾ.

Ḥātim ibn Muslim narrated to me that Umm al-Shaʿbī was among the captives of Jalūlāʾ.

Then the Muslims returned to al-Madāʾin, and the people of the Ṭasāsīj came to Saʿd and made a treaty with him, and he confirmed them in their lands.

The founding of Kūfa

Someone who heard Abū Muḥṣin narrated to me, from Ḥuṣayn, from Abū Wāʾil, who said: The Muslims returned and alighted at al-Madāʾin, but found it unhealthy, so they complained about that to ʿUmar. ʿUmar said, “Can camels endure al-Madāʾin?” They said, “No, there are gnats there.” He said, “The Arabs cannot endure a land where camels cannot endure, so look for a place.” He said: We went out intending to go to al-Ḥīrah. We met a man from al-Ḥīrah who wanted to turn us away from it. He said, “I will direct you to a town that rises above the mosquitoes, slopes down below the bugs, extends into the open country, and joins the cultivated land.” He directed us to Kūfa, and the people laid out plots and settled there.

The Year 18

The Year of the Ashes. Bakr, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: In it was the Year of the Ashes. A severe famine afflicted the people, and ʿUmar went out to pray for rain, with al-ʿAbbās accompanying him. He said, “O God, we beseech You for rain by the uncle of Your Prophet.”

The Plague of ʿAmwās. He said: In it was the Plague of ʿAmwās in Syria. Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ, Muʿādh ibn Jabal, Yazīd ibn Abī Sufyān, Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasanah, and al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām ibn al-Mughīrah died in it.

The conquest of al-Ruhāʾ, Sumaysāṭ, and al-Jazīrah. Ibn Isḥāq said: In the year eighteen al-Ruhāʾ was conquered…

Ḥātim ibn Muslim narrated to me that Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī conquered al-Ruhāʾ and Sumaysāṭ by treaty, and what lay around them by force. He said: Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ had sent ʿIyāḍ ibn Ghanm al-Fihrī to al-Jazīrah. He met Abū Mūsā after the conquest of these towns, and proceeded onward; Abū Mūsā went with him. The two conquered Ḥarrān, Naṣībīn, and the districts of al-Jazīrah by force. It is also said that Abū ʿUbaydah sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to al-Jazīrah. He met Abū Mūsā after he had conquered al-Ruhāʾ and Sumaysāṭ. Khālid then sent Abū Mūsā and ʿIyāḍ to Ḥarrān, and the two made a treaty with its people. Then Khālid proceeded to Naṣībīn and conquered it, then returned to Āmid and conquered it by treaty, and what lay between the two by force.

A shaykh from the people of al-Jazīrah narrated to me that ʿIyāḍ ibn Ghanm was responsible for making treaties with these towns and others in al-Jazīrah, and wrote them a document that is still with them today under the name of ʿIyāḍ.

Wakīʿ ibn al-Jarrāḥ narrated to us; he said: Thawr informed us, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿĀʾidh, that Khālid ibn al-Walīd entered a bathhouse at Āmid; he mentioned a report about it.

A man narrated to us, from al-Mughīrah ibn Ziyād al-Mawṣilī, from ʿUbādah ibn Nasī, that Abū Mūsā was reinforced with people from Kūfa and encamped at Sumaysāṭ; he mentioned a report about it.

Muʿādh ibn Hishām said: My father informed us, from Qatādah, from Yūnus ibn Jubayr, that Abū Mūsā performed the fear prayer at Dārā, a town in the land of al-Jazīrah, several parasangs from Naṣībīn.

Ḥātim ibn Muslim narrated to me that ʿUmar sent ʿIyāḍ, who conquered al-Mawṣil and left ʿUtbah ibn Farqad at one of the two fortresses. He conquered the entire region by force, except for the fortress; its people made a treaty with him. That was in the year eighteen.

The conquest of Ḥulwān, al-Māhāt, and Mā Sabadhān. Muḥammad ibn Muʿāwiyah narrated to me; he said: Abū ʿUbaydah said, from al-Samarī: Saʿd sent Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bajalī to Ḥulwān after Jalūlāʾ, and he conquered it by force…

He said: It is also said that instead he sent Hāshim ibn ʿUtbah. Then they rebelled when they set out for Nihāwand. Then Hāshim proceeded to Māh Dīnār and drove them out to Ādharbayjān. Then they sent to Saʿd and made a treaty with him, and Hāshim conquered al-Māhāt and Mā Sabadhān.

The conquest of Jundaysābūr and al-Sūs. In it Jundaysābūr and al-Sūs were conquered by treaty; he made a treaty with them—Abū Mūsā did so. Then he returned to al-Ahwāz.

The conquest of Sarq and Rāmhurmuz. ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīrah narrated to me, from his father, who said: The first to arrive at Rāmhurmuz were four hundred Muslims. They raided the village of al-ʿAbbādī, killing and taking captives. Then they withdrew to Ṣihr Tāj of Sarq, where they killed a group. Abū Mūsā set out and made a treaty with the people of Sarq and the people of Rāmhurmuz, up to a town in al-Jabal one parasang from Rāmhurmuz. They made a treaty with him for certain terms whose total amounted to eight hundred thousand each year. The land tax of Sarq amounted to the same. ʿAbd Allāh narrated to me, from Marwān ibn Muʿāwiyah, from Ḥumayd al-Ṭawīl, from Ḥabīb ibn Yaḥyā, from Khālid ibn Zayd, whose eye had been injured at Sarq, who said: We besieged them. Ḥammād ibn Zayd narrated, from ʿĀṣim al-Aḥwal, from Fuḍayl al-Raqāshī, who said: We besieged the people of Ṣihr Tāj. A slave wrote a guarantee of security and shot it with an arrow. They came out, and ʿUmar wrote: “The lowest-ranking among the Muslims may grant protection on behalf of them all.” Then Abū Mūsā set out and appointed Abū Maryam al-Ḥanafī. It is also said that Abū Maryam was responsible for making treaties with the two districts. It is also said that Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh conquered Rāmhurmuz; ʿUmar had sent him as reinforcement for Abū Mūsā while he was besieging the people of Tustar.

The conquest of Tustar. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām narrated to me, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: Abū Mūsā conquered most of Rāmhurmuz. Then Abū Mūsā proceeded to Tustar and remained there…

The conquest of Rīshahr. Abū ʿUbayda said: There Hurm ibn Ḥayyān besieged the people of Rīshahr. Their king saw a woman eating her child and said, “Now I will make peace with the Arabs.” He made peace with Hurm on the condition that he leave the city to them.

The building of Kūfa. There the people settled in Kūfa, and Saʿd built its congregational mosque.

The Year 19

The conquest of Caesarea. There Caesarea was conquered. Its commanders were Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān and Saʿīd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḥudhaym, each commanding his own troops. God routed the polytheists, and a great many of them were killed. Ibn al-Kalbī said: That was in the year nineteen. Ibn Isḥāq said: In the year twenty.

The conquest of Tikrīt. There Tikrīt was conquered in the year nineteen.

The conquest of Ṣihāb and Tawwaj. There Shahrak was killed in the land of Fāris. Bāb ibn Dhī al-Jarra killed him. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Jadīd ibn Mālik al-Yaḥmadī killed him. Abū al-Ḥasan said: The battle at Ṣihāb against the Muslims, under al-Ḥakam ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ, took place in Dhū al-Ḥijja of the year nineteen. Abū ʿUbayda said: They met at Ṣihāb, with Hurm ibn Ḥayyān al-ʿAbdī commanding them.

Ghassān ibn Muḍar narrated to us, from Saʿīd ibn Yazīd, from Abū Naḍra—or someone else—who said: ʿUthmān and al-Ḥakam cut off [the enemy’s route], and they met Shahrak at Rīshahr and conquered it. A man of the Yaḥmad, called Jadīd ibn Mālik—or Mālik ibn Jadīd—brought Shahrak’s head to ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ. They settled at Tawwaj and built there, then moved away from it.

From Abū Usāma, who said: al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Manhāl informed us, from ʿĀṣim ibn Kulayb, from his father, who said: We besieged Tawwaj, with Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd over us, and conquered it. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra narrated to me, from his father, who said: When Shahrak was killed, a man called Burtiyān fled. He was one of the great men of Fāris. He took refuge in al-Tūjān, in the district of Sabīl, belonging to Ramhurmuz, and a group gathered around him. Abū Mūsā sent against him and besieged him for a year or thereabouts. He then came out and joined the people of Iṣṭakhr.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām narrated to me, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: They remained in the fortress until ʿUmar wrote them a document and gave them four months, allowing them to go wherever they wished. They went to Iṣṭakhr. There the Byzantines captured ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥudhāfa al-Sahmī.

The Year 20

The conquest of Egypt. There Egypt was conquered. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām narrated to me, from his father, from his grandfather; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father; and others, that ʿUmar wrote to ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, ordering him to march to Egypt. He marched, and ʿUmar sent al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām as reinforcements for him, along with ʿUmayr ibn Wahb al-Jumaḥī, Bisr ibn Arṭāʾ al-ʿĀmirī, and Khārija ibn Ḥudhāfa. They came to the Gate of al-Yūn, but its people resisted, so he conquered it by force. The people of the fortress made peace with him. Al-Zubayr was the first to climb the city wall, then the people followed him. Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām spoke to ʿAmr about dividing it among those who conquered it.

ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ wrote to ʿUmar, and ʿUmar wrote back: “A morsel and morsels are better than parceling it out.”

One who heard Ibn Lahīʿa, from Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad al-Ḥaḍramī, from Ibn Abī al-ʿĀliya, from his father, said: I heard ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ say from this pulpit: “I have taken this seat, and none of the Copts of Egypt has a pact or treaty with me. If I wish, I kill; if I wish, I sell; and if I wish, I take a fifth—except for the people of Antābulus, for they have a pact that must be honored.”

And from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from Sufyān ibn Wahb al-Khawlānī, who said: We conquered Egypt by force with ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ.

One who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, from Mūsā ibn ʿAlī, from his father, said: The whole of the Maghrib was conquered by force. One who heard Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from ʿAmr ibn Yazīd, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dīnār, from Ibn ʿUmar, said: Egypt was conquered without a treaty.

Ibn Lahīʿa said: Al-Ṣalt ibn Abī ʿĀṣim, the scribe of Ḥayyān ibn Shurayḥ, informed me that he had read ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz’s letter to Ḥayyān ibn Shurayḥ, stating that Egypt had been conquered by force, without a pact or treaty. Ibn Lahīʿa said: Abū Sarjūn informed me, from ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Junāda, from his father, who was among those who conquered Egypt, that they entered Egypt without a treaty or pact. One who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd al-Ḥaḍramī, from ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ, said: Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq sent Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿa to al-Muqawqis in Egypt, in the region of the eastern villages. They gave to him, and they remained in that state until ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ entered it and fought them, whereupon that peace agreement was annulled.

One who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, from al-Layth, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, [said] that al-Muqawqis made peace—

The conquest of Antābulus. One who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, from al-Layth, from Sahl ibn ʿUqayl, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Hubayra, said: ʿUmar made peace with the people of Antābulus, which is in the territory of Barqa between Ifrīqiya and Egypt, on the basis of the poll tax: they could sell whichever of their sons they wished toward their poll tax.

One who heard Ibn Abī Maryam, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Mirthad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥaḍramī, [said] that he came to the people of Antābulus, when he was appointed over Antābulus, with their treaty document.

One who heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ, from Ibn Lahīʿa, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, said: There is no treaty or pact between the people of Egypt and the Black people; rather, there is only a truce between us and them. We give them some wheat and lentils, and they give us enslaved people, and we must buy their enslaved people.

The battle of Tustar.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām, from his father and his uncle, [reported] that when Abū Mūsā had finished with al-Ahwāz, Manādhir, Nahr Tīrī, Jundaysābūr, and Ramhurmuz, he set out for Tustar. He encamped at the Eastern Gate and wrote to ʿUmar asking for reinforcements. ʿUmar wrote to ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir, ordering him to reinforce Abū Mūsā. ʿAmmār wrote to Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh, who was at Ḥulwān, ordering him to march to Abū Mūsā. Jarīr marched with one thousand, and they remained there for several months.

Then Abū Mūsā wrote to ʿUmar that they had been of no help to him. ʿUmar wrote to ʿAmmār, ordering him to march to Tustar, and he marched; ʿUmar sent him reinforcements from Medina.

ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated to me, saying: Qirād Abū Nūḥ narrated to me, saying: ʿUthmān ibn Muʿāwiya al-Qurashī narrated to me, from his father, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr, who said: They remained for a year or thereabouts. A man from the people of Tustar came and said to Abū Mūsā, “I ask you to spare my life and the lives of my household, and to leave us our property and dwellings, on condition that I show you the entrance.” He said, “That is granted to you.” The man said, “Find me a man who can swim and is intelligent, to come with you and give you clear information.” Abū Mūsā sent for Mujzaʾa ibn Thawr al-Sadūsī and said, “Find me a man from your people who can swim and is intelligent.” Mujzaʾa said, “Make me that man.” He set out with him and brought him in through the waterway: at some points the passage was narrow, so that he had to lie on his belly; at others it widened, so he could walk upright; and in some places he had to crawl. He entered the city. Abū Mūsā had ordered him to note the route to the gate, the route to the wall, and the dwelling of al-Hurmuzān, and had said, “Do not act ahead of me in anything.” The non-Arab took him until he reached al-Hurmuzān, who was about to kill him. Then he recalled Abū Mūsā’s words, “Do not act ahead of me in anything,” and returned to Abū Mūsā. Abū Mūsā then called for volunteers, and a little over three hundred volunteered. He ordered each man to wear no more than two garments, and to carry his sword. They did so. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: He cried “God is greatest” and plunged into the water, and the men cried “God is greatest” and plunged in after him. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: They were like ducks. They swam across, then he led them to the opening through which the water entered. He cried “God is greatest,” then entered with thirty-five or thirty-six men. He took some of them toward the gate and positioned them there; he took some toward the wall; and with the rest he climbed the wall. A non-Arab came down upon him carrying a spear, and Mujzaʾa stabbed him and felled him. The Muslims cried “God is greatest” on the wall and at the gate. They opened the gate, and the Muslims came forward until they entered the city. Al-Hurmuzān fortified himself in his citadel. Abū al-Ḥasan said: The man who asked Abū Mūsā for safe conduct and showed them the entrance was Sīnāba. Abū al-Ḥasan, from al-ʿAlāʾ ibn Muʿādh al-Māzinī, said: Elders from among the people of Tustar narrated to me—

Abū al-Ḥasan, from Salama ibn ʿUthmān, from ʿAlī ibn Zayd, from Anas ibn Mālik, said: We fought them until dawn broke. None of us prayed the morning prayer until midday. I would not exchange that prayer for the whole world.

Ibn Zurayʿ narrated to us, from Saʿīd, from Qatāda, from Anas, who said: That day we did not pray the morning prayer until midday. I would not exchange that prayer for the whole world.

Abū ʿAmr al-Shaybānī said: Abū Hilāl al-Rāsibī narrated to us, from Ibn Sīrīn, who said: Al-Barāʾ ibn Mālik was killed on the day of Tustar.

Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd narrated to us, from Ḥabīb ibn Shihāb, from his father, who said: I was the first to light a fire at the gate of Tustar. Abū Mūsā was struck by an arrow and fell, and he put me in command of ten of my people.

Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm narrated to us, saying: Ḥabīb ibn Shihāb informed me, from his father, who said: I witnessed the conquest of Tustar with Abū Mūsā. He would combine the noon and afternoon prayers, and the sunset and night prayers.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿUthmān, from Ḥabīb ibn Shihāb, from his father, who said: Abū Mūsā said to me, “Choose ten men from the army to be with you in guarding the captives.” When he divided the spoils, he gave a share to the horseman, a share for his horse, a share to the dājl, and a share to the foot soldier. He said, “Do not separate a woman—” He said: That day he favored me with a head. Abū al-Ḥasan, from Mubārak ibn Faḍāla, from Muʿāwiya ibn Qurra, said: The first to enter the city gate at Tustar was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughaffal al-Muzanī.

ʿAlī ibn Abī Sayf, from al-Mubārak ibn Faḍāla, from al-Ḥasan, [reported] that Abū Mūsā besieged the people of Tustar for two years.

And he narrated to us, from Ibn al-Mubārak, from Mujālid, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: He besieged them for eighteen months. Al-Hurmuzān remained in the fortress at Tustar, then later surrendered under ʿUmar’s judgment.

ʿAbd al-Wahhāb said: Ḥumayd narrated to us, from Anas, who said: We besieged Tustar, and al-Hurmuzān surrendered under ʿUmar’s judgment. When we came to him, ʿUmar said, “Speak.” He said, “The speech of a living man or a dead one?” ʿUmar said, “Speak; there is no harm.” He said, “As long as God left us and you alone, we, the company of Arabs, used to defeat and kill you. But when God was with you, we were powerless against you.”

ʿUmar said, “Anas, what do you say?” I said, “Commander of the Faithful, you have left behind you a numerous people and great strength. If you kill him, the people will despair of life, and their strength will become greater.” ʿUmar said, “I would be ashamed to spare the killer of al-Barāʾ ibn Mālik and Mujzaʾa ibn Thawr.” When I feared that he would kill him, I said, “There is no way to kill him: you told him, ‘Speak; there is no harm.’” He said, “Bring me someone besides you to testify to that.” I met al-Zubayr, and he testified with me. So ʿUmar refrained from him. He embraced Islam, and ʿUmar assigned him a stipend.

ʿAlī narrated to me, from Qirād, from ʿUthmān ibn Muʿāwiya, from his father, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakra, who said: They surrounded al-Hurmuzān but could not reach him until they granted him safe conduct. He surrendered under ʿUmar’s judgment, and Abū Mūsā and his companions sent him to ʿUmar.

There died ʿIyāḍ ibn Ghanm al-Fihrī, and there died Ṣafiyya daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

The Year 21

The battle of Nihāwand. There was the battle of Nihāwand.

Al-Anṣārī narrated to us, saying: al-Nahhās ibn Qahm narrated to us, from al-Qāsim ibn ʿAwf, from his father, from a man, from al-Sāʾib ibn al-Aqraʿ, who said: A force advanced against the Muslims such as had never advanced against them before: the people of Māh, Iṣfahān, Hamadhān, al-Rayy, Qūmis, Ādharbayjān, and Nihāwand advanced against them. The news reached ʿUmar, and he consulted the Muslims. They disagreed. Then ʿAlī said, “Commander of the Faithful, send to the people of Kūfa so that two-thirds of them march, leaving one-third to protect their families, and send to the people of Baṣra.” He said, “Advise me: whom should I appoint over them?” They said, “Commander of the Faithful, you are the best of us in judgment—”

Mūsā ibn Ismāʿīl narrated to us, saying: Ḥammād ibn Salama narrated to us, saying: Abū ʿImrān al-Jawnī narrated to us, from ʿAlqama ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Muzanī, from Maʿqil ibn Yasār, that ʿUmar consulted al-Hurmuzān about Iṣfahān, Fāris, and Ādharbayjān—which of them he should begin with. Al-Hurmuzān said: “Iṣfahān is the head, and Fāris and Ādharbayjān are the two wings. If you cut off one wing, the head will lean toward the other wing; but if you cut off the head, both wings will fall.” ʿUmar entered the mosque and saw al-Nuʿmān ibn Muqarrin praying. He dispatched him and sent word to the people of Kūfa to reinforce him. They went, accompanied by Ḥudhayfa ibn al-Yamān, al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām, al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba, al-Ashʿath ibn Qays, ʿAmr ibn Maʿdīkarib, and Ibn ʿUmar, until they came to Nihāwand.

Returning to the account of al-Sāʾib, he said: They met at Nihāwand on Wednesday. The right wing was driven back, while the left wing held firm and the line stood. Then they met on Thursday: the left wing was driven back, while the right wing and the line held firm. Then they met on Friday. Al-Nuʿmān ibn Muqarrin came forward on a dark bay, close to the ground, stopping at each standard to address and exhort its people. He would say: “These people have staked a great stake against you, and you have staked a great stake against them. They have staked against you inherited sacks; you have staked against them Islam and your families. Let me not find a man entrusting his opponent to another, for that is baseness. Let each man among you engage his own opponent. I am about to shake the standard, so let every man among you cast aside his belongings and take cover.” Then he shook it—

The second time, let each man of you stand in his position. Then he raised it a third time: “Now charge, and charge with the blessing of God. Let none of you look back.” Al-Nuʿmān was the first to be killed, and Ḥudhayfa took the banner. God granted them victory.

Returning to the account of Ḥammād ibn Abī ʿImrān, from ʿAlqama, from Maʿqil ibn Yasār, who said: Al-Nuʿmān said, “I witnessed the Messenger of God ﷺ—when, if he did not fight at the beginning of the day, he delayed fighting until the sun had passed its zenith, the winds had blown, and victory had descended. Al-Nuʿmān said, ‘I shall raise the banner three times. At the first raising, let a man attend to his need and perform ablution. At the second raising, let a man cast off his clothes and weapons. At the third raising, charge, and let no one turn aside for anyone. If al-Nuʿmān is killed, let no one turn aside for him. I shall call upon God with a supplication, and I have resolved that no man will fail to say “Amen” to it.’ He said: ‘O God, grant al-Nuʿmān martyrdom through the Muslims’ victory, and grant them victory.’ The people said ‘Amen.’ He raised the banner three times, then charged, and the people charged. He was the first to fall.”

In this year, ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣī alighted at Tawwaj and made it his garrison town.
