He sent Sawwār ibn Habbār al-ʿAbdī to Sābūr, where he was killed at the Pass of al-Ṭīn. ʿUthmān raided as far as the coast of the sea and the shorelands. ʿUthmān sent al-Jārūd, who was killed at the Pass of al-Jārūd.

In this year, Saʿd sent al-Nuʿmān ibn Muqarrin to Kaskar, and he made terms with the people of Zandawand.

In this year, the people of Kūfa complained to ʿUmar about Saʿd ibn Mālik. ʿUmar dismissed him and appointed ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir over the prayer, Ibn Masʿūd over the treasury, and ʿUthmān ibn Ḥunayf over the land survey. In this year, Bilāl, the Messenger of God’s ﷺ—caller to prayer, died. In this year, Zaynab daughter of Jaḥsh, the wife of the Messenger of God ﷺ—died. In this year, Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr died. It is also said that Abū al-Haytham ibn al-Tayyihān died in it. It is said that Abū al-Haytham lived until Ṣiffīn, but that is an error. Al-Aṣmaʿī said: “I asked his people, and they said, ‘He died during the lifetime of the Messenger of God ﷺ.’” In this year, al-Ḥasan ibn Abī al-Ḥasan and ʿĀmir al-Shaʿbī were born.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to us. He said: ʿUmar related to us, from Ziyād al-Aʿjam, who said: Abū Mūsā came to us with ʿUmar’s letter. It was read to us: “From ʿAbd Allāh ʿUmar, Commander of the Faithful, to ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣī: Peace be upon you. To proceed: I have sent you ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays as reinforcement. When the two of you meet, ʿUthmān is the commander; cooperate with one another. Peace.” Ziyād al-Aʿjam said: When the siege of Iṣṭakhr grew prolonged, ʿUthmān said to Abū Mūsā, “I want to send commanders to these rural districts around us to raid them. Whatever they obtain, they will share with the troops stationed at the city.” Abū Mūsā said, “I do not think they should share it with them; rather, it should belong to them.” ʿUthmān said, “If I do this, no one will remain at the city; they will all leave, and the Muslims will hope for booty.” The Muslims agreed upon ʿUthmān’s opinion. In this year, Khālid ... in Syria died ﵀.

The conquest of Alexandria. Al-Walīd said: In this year, Alexandria was conquered; ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ conquered it. Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān related to us, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Wahb, who said: Ḥarmala ibn ʿImrān related to us that Abū Tamīm told him he witnessed the later conquest of Alexandria, when ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ was in command, in the year twenty-two.

The conquest of Dinawar, Māh Sabadhān, and Māh Dīnār. Abū ʿUbayda said: After Nihāwand, Ḥudhayfa ibn al-Yamān proceeded to the city of Nihāwand, and Dīnār made terms with him for eight hundred thousand dirhams each year. Then Ḥudhayfa ibn al-Yamān raided the city of Dinawar and conquered it by force. It had previously been conquered for Saʿd, but then rebelled. Then Ḥudhayfa raided Māh Sabadhān and conquered it by force. It too had previously been conquered for Saʿd and then rebelled. Another account has been given concerning Māh. It is said that Abū Mūsā conquered Māh Dīnār; it is also said that al-Sāʾib ibn al-Aqraʿ conquered Māh Dīnār.

Ghunder and Yazīd ibn Hārūn related to us, from Shuʿba, from Qays ibn Muslim, from Ṭāriq ibn Shihāb, who said: The people of Baṣra raided Māh, and the people of Kūfa sent them reinforcements, with ʿAmmār in command. They wanted to share in the booty, but the people of Baṣra refused. They wrote to ʿUmar, and ʿUmar wrote back: “The booty belongs among those who witnessed the battle.”

The conquest of Hamadhān and al-Rayy.

Abū ʿUbayda said: Ḥudhayfa raided Hamadhān and conquered it by force; it had not been conquered before that. Then he raided al-Rayy and conquered it by force; it had not been conquered before that. At al-Rayy, Ḥudhayfa’s conquests came to an end.

Abū ʿUbayda said: All these conquests of Ḥudhayfa took place in the year twenty-two. It is said that al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba conquered Hamadhān in the year twenty-four. It is also said that Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh conquered it at al-Mughīra’s command.

The conquest of Ādharbayjān. In this year, Ādharbayjān was conquered. It was related to us from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: It was conquered in the year twenty-two, with al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba as their commander. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad related to us, saying: Ḥudhayfa made terms with them in the year twenty-two for eight hundred thousand. Abū ʿUbayda said: Ḥabīb ibn Maslama al-Fihrī conquered it by force with the people of Syria, and the people of Kūfa were with them, during ʿUmar’s caliphate; Ḥudhayfa was with them, after fierce fighting. It is said that ʿUtba ibn Farqad conquered it. Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ related to us. He said: Al-Taymī related to us, from Abū ʿUthmān, who said: ʿUmar’s letter came to us while we were with ʿUtba ibn Farqad. Among those taken captive in Ādharbayjān were the family of ʿAbd Allāh and Yaḥyā, the two sons of Isḥāq al-Ḥaḍramī, and the family of Abū al-ʿĀliya the scribe.

The conquest of Ṭarābulus and Alexandria. In this year, ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ conquered Ṭarābulus by treaty. In this year, ʿUmar dismissed ʿAmmār from Kūfa. In this year, ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ conquered Alexandria. Al-Walīd related to me, from his father and his paternal uncle, from his grandfather, that ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ conquered Alexandria, then went to Labda in the land of Ṭarābulus and conquered it, then returned in the year twenty-four. Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān related to us, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Wahb, who said: Ḥarmala ibn ʿImrān related to us that Abū Tamīm informed him that he witnessed the later conquest of Alexandria, when ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ was in command. He said: Ibn Lahīʿa related to us, from al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd, who said: Abū Tamīm al-Jayshānī related to us, saying: We were with ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ when he conquered the city of Ṭarābulus.

The Year 23

The first expedition to Iṣṭakhr. In this year was the first expedition to Iṣṭakhr. Bakr related to us, from Ibn Isḥāq, who said: The first expedition to Iṣṭakhr was in the year twenty-three, and it was not conquered. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, saying: My father related to me, from my grandfather, who said: ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣī raided from Tawwaj for a number of years during ʿUmar’s caliphate. ʿUthmān would raid in the summer, return, and spend the winter at Tawwaj.

The killing of ʿUmar, his age, and the duration of his caliphate. In this year, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was killed ﵀. He was stabbed with three nights of Dhū al-Ḥijja remaining. He lived three days; it is also said seven days.

Ibn ʿUlayya related from Saʿīd, from Qatāda, from Sālim ibn Abī al-Jaʿd, from Maʿdān ibn Ṭalḥa, who said: ʿUmar was killed on Wednesday, with four nights of Dhū al-Ḥijja remaining.

Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿImrān, from Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Hudhayl, who said: ʿUmar was born thirteen years after the Elephant. It was related from Usāma ibn Zayd, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: I heard ʿUmar say, “I was born four years before the greater Sacrilegious War.” Abū Dāwūd, from Zuhayr, from Abū Isḥāq, from ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd, from Jarīr, from Muʿāwiya, who said: ʿUmar died at the age of sixty-three. Abū Aḥmad and Sallm, from Yūnus ibn Abī Isḥāq, from Abū al-Safar, from al-Shaʿbī, from Muʿāwiya, with the same report. Ibn Abī ʿAdī, from Dāwūd, from ʿĀmir, with the same report. They related from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUmar, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar, who said: He died at the age of a little over fifty. One who heard Ibn ʿUyayna related to us, from al-Zuhrī, who said: He was fifty-four. Muʿādh related to us, from his father, from Qatāda, who said: He was fifty-two. Ṣuhayb ibn Sinān prayed over him between the grave and the pulpit. His rule lasted ten years, six months, five—or days—and nine ... . In this year, Qatāda ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Anṣārī died, and ʿUmar prayed over him. Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib died during ʿUmar’s caliphate.

The names of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb’s governors.

Over Mecca: Muḥriz ibn Ḥāritha ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿAbd Shams. Then he dismissed him and appointed Qunfudh ibn ʿUmayr ibn Judʿān al-Taymī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Nāfiʿ ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥārith al-Khuzāʿī. Nāfiʿ went to ʿUmar and appointed his client, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abzā, as his deputy. ʿUmar dismissed him and appointed Khālid ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Hishām ibn al-Mughīra al-Makhzūmī. During his pilgrimage, he appointed Zayd ibn Thābit as deputy over Medina.

For two pilgrimages, five from the Anṣār and three from Kināna. When he departed for Syria, he appointed Zayd ibn Thābit as his deputy.

Ḥātim ibn Muslim related to me, from someone who informed him, from Ibn Isḥāq, from Yaʿqūb ibn ʿUtba, that ʿUmar appointed Zayd as his deputy and wrote to him from Syria: “To Zayd ibn Thābit, from ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.” Someone who heard Abū Muʿāwiya related to me, from al-Ḥajjāj ibn Arṭāʾa, from Nāfiʿ, that ʿUmar would appoint Zayd as his deputy when he went on pilgrimage. Someone who heard ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Ḥumayd related to me, from Abū al-Malīḥ, that ʿUmar appointed a maternal uncle of his, called ʿAbd Allāh, as his deputy during one of his pilgrimages. ʿUmar appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Rabīʿa al-Makhzūmī over Yemen, and al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Ḥaḍramī over Bahrain. Then he wrote to him, and he set out for the land of Baṣra, but died before reaching it.

ʿUmar appointed Qudāma ibn Maẓʿūn over Bahrain, then dismissed him and appointed ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣī. Among ʿUmar’s governors over it were also Abū Hurayra and ʿAyyāsh ibn Abī Thawr. Over ʿUmān was Bilāl, a man from the Anṣār; then ʿUmar attached it to the jurisdiction of ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣī. Over Baṣra was Shurayḥ, who was away on campaign. He said to al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba, “Lead the people in prayer until Mujāshiʿ arrives.” He confirmed Ibn ʿĀmir, one of the Banū Saʿd ibn Bakr, who was killed in the region of al-Ahwāz. Then he appointed ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān, one of the Banū Māzin ibn Manṣūr. ʿUtba later left and appointed Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd as his deputy. ʿUmar dismissed al-Mughīra and appointed Abū Mūsā, who remained in charge of it until ʿUmar was killed. When Abū Mūsā went on campaign, he would appoint ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn as his deputy, and sometimes he would appoint Ziyād.

The judges. ʿUmar appointed Abū Maryam al-Ḥanafī as judge of Baṣra, then dismissed him and appointed Kaʿb ibn Sawr al-Laqīṭī, who remained judge until ʿUmar was killed. Over Kūfa was Saʿd ibn Mālik; then ʿUmar dismissed him and appointed ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir. He reinstated Saʿd for a second time, then dismissed him and appointed al-Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim. He dismissed him before he set out, and appointed ...

Al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba; he remained in charge of it until ʿUmar was killed. Salmān ibn Rabīʿa al-Bāhilī: ʿUmar appointed him. Saʿd, for the second time, held the judgeship of Kūfa. Then ʿUmar appointed Shurayḥ. It is said that before Shurayḥ he had appointed ʿUbayda al-Salmānī. Then he appointed Shurayḥ in the year twenty-two. Over al-Yamāma was Salama ibn Salāma ibn Waqsh al-Anṣārī. He confirmed ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣī over al-Ṭāʾif, then dismissed him and appointed Sufyān ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Thaqafī.

The Syrian provinces.

When he took office, he dismissed Khālid and appointed Abū ʿUbayda ibn al-Jarrāḥ. When the Syrian provinces were conquered, Abū ʿUbayda appointed Yazīd ibn Abī Sufyān over Palestine and its districts, Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasana over the Jordan district, Khālid ibn al-Walīd over Damascus, and Ḥabīb ibn Maslama over Ḥimṣ. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qurṭ al-Thumālī; then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit al-Anṣārī; then he dismissed him and reinstated ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qurṭ. Then the plague of ʿAmwās occurred, and Abū ʿUbayda died and appointed Muʿādh as his successor. Muʿādh died and appointed Yazīd ibn Abī Sufyān as his successor. He died and appointed his brother Muʿāwiya, whom ʿUmar confirmed. ʿUmar appointed ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ over Palestine and the Jordan district, Muʿāwiya over Damascus, Baʿlabakk, and the Balqāʾ, and Saʿīd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḥudhaym over Ḥimṣ. Then he brought all of Syria together under Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān.

Egypt and al-Jazīra.

He wrote to ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, and he set out for Egypt and conquered it. He remained governor until ʿUmar died. ʿUmar sent ʿIyāḍ ibn Ghanm to al-Jazīra; we have already written his account. Then he dismissed him and appointed Ḥabīb ibn Maslama al-Fihrī, attaching Armenia and Ādharbayjān to his charge. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUmayr ibn Saʿd al-Anṣārī ...

And Saʿīd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḥudhaym. He wrote to ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, who set out for Egypt and conquered it. He remained commander until ʿUmar was killed.

His secretary, chamberlain, treasurer, and the person in charge of his treasury.

ʿUmar’s secretary was Zayd ibn Thābit. Muʿayqīb also wrote for him. His secretary over the register of Baṣra was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khalaf al-Khuzāʿī, Abū Ṭalḥa al-Ṭalḥāt. His secretary over the register of Kūfa was Abū Jubayra ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk al-Anṣārī. His chamberlain was Yarfaʾ, his client; his treasurer was Yasār; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Arqam was in charge of his treasury. Umayya ibn Khālid related to us, from Mālik ibn Anas, from Zayd ibn Aslam, that ʿUmar appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Arqam over the treasury. ʿUmar was born in Mecca, in the house of al-Khaṭṭāb, in the house to the left of its doorway. In the year twenty-three, Abū Kabsha, the client of the Messenger of God ﷺ—died. At the end of ʿUmar’s caliphate, Rāfiʿ ibn ʿUmar al-Ṭāʾī died. It is said that ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr was born at the end of ʿUmar’s caliphate, in the year twenty-three.

Chapter 5

The caliphate of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān

The Year 24

the caliphate of ʿUthmān ﵁. In this year, ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣī ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Manāf was appointed caliph. His mother was Arwā daughter of Kurayz ibn Ḥabīb ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd Shams, and her mother was al-Bayḍāʾ, Umm Ḥakīm daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ibn Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf.

The conquest of Hamadhān. In it, Hamadhān, in the land of al-Jabal, was conquered. Abū ʿAmr al-Shaybānī said: “Al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba conquered it in Rabīʿ or Jumādā I of the year twenty-four.” ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: “Al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba, while governor over Kūfa, sent Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh, who conquered it.” Abū ʿUbayda said: “Ḥudhayfa conquered it in the year twenty-two.” Al-Aṣmaʿī reported to us from Ibn Abī al-Zinād, who said: “We are from the people of Hamadhān.”

The conquest of Rayy. In it, Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī campaigned with the people of Baṣra and conquered Rayy. Abū ʿUbayda said: “Ḥudhayfa had conquered Rayy before that, but they rebelled, so Abū Mūsā campaigned against them.” Abū ʿAmr al-Shaybānī said: “Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib conquered it in the year twenty-four, by treaty or by force.” Ḥātim ibn Muslim said: “Qarẓa ibn Kaʿb al-Anṣārī conquered it.” ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: “Abū Mūsā conquered part of it, and Qarẓa ibn Kaʿb part of it.” ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf led the pilgrimage in the year twenty-four. In it, Surāqa ibn Mālik ibn Juʿshum died.

The Year 25

Muḥammad ibn Muʿāwiya reported to us from Abū ʿUbayda Maʿmar ibn al-Muthannā, who said: “Al-Samarī told me that the people of Rayy rebelled, so Abū Mūsā campaigned against them in the year twenty-five.” It is also said that part of it rebelled. In it, ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān removed Saʿd ibn Mālik from the governorship of Kūfa and appointed al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba ibn Abī Muʿayṭ. Al-Walīd then sent Salmān ibn Rabīʿa al-Bāhilī, one of the Banū Qutayba ibn Maʿn ibn Mālik, with twelve thousand men to Bardaʿa. He killed and took captives. Abū ʿUbayda said, from al-Samarī: “ʿUmar sent Salmān ibn Rabīʿa to Bardaʿa, and he conquered it.”

The rebellion of Alexandria and its reconquest. In it, the people of Alexandria rebelled, so ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, who was governor over Egypt, campaigned against them. He killed and took captives, and ʿUthmān returned the captives to their protected status. Muḥammad ibn Saʿīd al-Bāhilī told me: “In it, the king of the Romans sent the eunuch Manuel in ships to Alexandria. Its people rebelled, except for al-Muqawqis. ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ campaigned against them in Rabīʿ I of the year twenty-five and conquered it by force. ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ conquered the land of Egypt by force, except for ʿAyn Shams, which was taken by treaty.” ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān led the pilgrimage.

The Year 26

The conquest of Sābūr. In it, Sābūr was conquered, and its governor was ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ al-Thaqafī. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām reported from his father, from his grandfather, who said: “ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ made a treaty with them for three thousand thousand and three hundred thousand, in weight seven.”

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad reported from Yaḥyā ibn Zakariyyā, from Mujālid, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: “ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ made a treaty with the people of Sābūr in the year twenty-six for three thousand thousand and some additional amount. They included Kāzarūn in their treaty, and he was the agent over the fortresses.”

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām reported from his father, from his grandfather, who said: “ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ sent Hurm ibn Ḥayyān al-ʿAbdī to a fortress at Baḥra, called the Fortress of the Shaykhs. He conquered it by force and took its people captive. He made a treaty with the people of a fortress and made a treaty with the people of the Fortress of the Monks at Kāzarūn. Then they acted treacherously and killed two Muslim horsemen. ʿUthmān came to the fortress, killed the fighters, and took the offspring captive.” In it, ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān enlarged the Sacred Mosque, and he led the pilgrimage from the year twenty-six until the year thirty-four.

The Year 27

The conquest of Arrajān and Darābjird. In it, Arrajān and Darābjird were conquered. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad reported from Maslama ibn Muḥārib, from Dāwūd ibn Abī Hind, who said: “ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ and Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī made a treaty with the people of Arrajān in the year twenty-seven for two thousand thousand and two hundred thousand. They made a treaty with the people of Darābjird for two thousand thousand and two hundred thousand.” Al-Walīd ibn Hishām said: “My father told me, from my grandfather, who said: ‘The marzbān made a treaty for Darābjird on the basis that it would pay five thousand thousand, in weight seven, and two hundred thousand, as well as sacks and saqāṭāt.’”

Ibn Abī Sarḥ campaigns in Ifrīqiya. In it, ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān removed ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ from Egypt and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ over it. Ibn Abī Sarḥ campaigned in Ifrīqiya with the ʿAbādila: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr. He encountered Jarjīr, who was with two hundred thousand men, at Sufayṭula, seventy miles from al-Qayrawān. Jarjīr was killed, and they took captives and booty.

Muḥammad ibn Saʿd said: “The cavalryman’s share amounted to three thousand mithqāls of gold, and the foot soldier’s share to one thousand mithqāls of gold. Ibn Abī Sarḥ stayed at Sufayṭula, the city of Qamūda. The people of the palaces and towns sent to him and made a treaty with him for two hundred thousand ratls of gold.” From one who heard Ibn Lahīʿa, who said: “Abū al-Aswad reported to us.” He said: “Abū Idrīs told me that he campaigned in Ifrīqiya with ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd.” He said: “He conquered it, and each person received one thousand dinars.”

The Year 28

The campaign in Azerbaijan. In it, Azerbaijan was raided. The commander of the people was al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Shabīl al-Aḥmasī arrived, and they granted him the treaty that Ḥudhayfa had made with them.

The campaign to Cyprus. Ibn al-Kalbī said: “In it, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān campaigned by sea, accompanied by his wife Fākhita bint Qarẓa of Banū ʿAbd Manāf. ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit was with him, accompanied by his wife Umm Ḥarām bint Milḥān al-Anṣāriyya. He came to Cyprus, where Umm Ḥarām died; her grave is there.” In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr arrived to ʿUthmān with news of the conquest of Ifrīqiya. In it, ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān married the daughter of al-Firāfisa al-Kalbī, according to what Ibn al-Kalbī told me from his authorities.

The Year 29

The appointment of Ibn ʿĀmir over Baṣra and Fārs. In it, ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān removed Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī from Baṣra and ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ from Fārs, and combined all of that under ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām told me: “My father told me, from my grandfather, from al-Ḥasan, who said: ‘Abū Mūsā said: A youth of noble grandmothers and paternal aunts will come to you; the two armies will be gathered under him.’” I heard Abū al-Yaqẓān mention something similar and say: “Ibn ʿĀmir arrived when he was twenty-four or twenty-five years old.”

The conquest of Iṣfahān. Al-Walīd reported from his father, from his grandfather, from al-Ḥasan, who said: “Ibn ʿĀmir campaigned, with ʿAbd Allāh ibn Budayl al-Khuzāʿī at the head of his advance guard. He came to Iṣfahān and left someone in charge of Baṣra. They made a treaty with him on the condition that they pay him what the people of Fārs pay.” Abū al-Yaqẓān said similarly. ʿUthmān al-Qurashī told me from ʿAbbād ibn Rāshid, from al-Ḥasan, who said: “Abū Mūsā conquered Iṣfahān.” It is also said that Sāriya ibn Zunaym al-Duʾalī conquered Iṣfahān by treaty or by force, with the people of Baṣra.

The conquest of Iṣṭakhr. Al-Walīd said in his report, from his father, from his grandfather, and Abū al-Yaqẓān and Abū al-Ḥasan, that Ibn ʿĀmir

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām said: “My uncle told me, from his father, who said: ‘They fought him fiercely, and Ibn Maʿmar was killed. Ibn ʿĀmir swore that if he gained victory over it, he would kill until blood flowed from the city gate. The Muslims made a breach from their city, and they did not realize what had happened until the Muslims were among them. Ibn ʿĀmir killed until he went too far in the killing, but the blood would not flow. He was told, “You have wiped out the people.” He ordered water to be poured on the blood until it flowed out of the city gate.’”

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām said in his report: “Among those taken captive at Iṣfahān were Ḥammād ibn Abī Sulaymān, the Kūfan jurist; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān Abū Jabala ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Bāhilī; Qaḥdham, the client of Abū Bakra; and Wardān, the client of ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ.” Al-Aṣmaʿī said to me: “Nāfiʿ ibn Abī Nuʿaym, the reciter of the people of Medina, said to me: ‘Our origin is from Iṣfahān.’” Al-Walīd reported from his uncle, who said: “Among those taken captive at Iṣfahān were Mihrān the interpreter, the grandfather of ʿUbayd Allāh the scribe, and the family of ʿAṭiyya.” Al-Walīd reported from his father, from his grandfather, and Abū al-Yaqẓān and Abū al-Ḥasan said: “Ibn ʿĀmir marched to Ḥulwān. They had broken the treaty, so he conquered it by treaty and by force. That was in the year twenty-nine, and he greatly increased the killing among them.”

In it, ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān removed al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba from Kūfa and appointed Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. He campaigned in Armenia, and Salmān ibn Rabīʿa al-Bāhilī advanced to a region of it. Saʿīd encountered an enemy, and Salmān went ahead to Balanjar, where he was killed ﵀. It is also said that ʿUmar sent Salmān to Balanjar.

The campaigns against al-Baylaqān, Bardaʿa, Jurzān, Ḥayzān, and Balanjar. Abū Khālid said: Abū al-Barāʾ said: “Salmān campaigned against al-Baylaqān, and they made a treaty with him. Then he came to Bardaʿa, and they made a treaty with him; he took control of it. He sent the commander of his cavalry to Jurzān, and they made a treaty with him. Salmān went on to Ḥayzān, and they made a treaty with him. Then he reached Musqaṭ, and its people made a treaty with him. He was killed at Balanjar, so ʿUthmān wrote to Ḥabīb ibn Maslama al-Fihrī, ordering him to march from Syria with an army. Ḥabīb went by way of Darb al-Ḥadath. The people of Jurzān made a treaty with him, and he ransomed the al-maṭāmīr and wrote them a document.”

The conquest of Jurjān. In it, Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ campaigned against Jurjān; it is also said that this was in the year thirty, and he conquered it.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām told me, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: “At Jurjān, Saʿīd struck a man on the cord of his shoulder, and the sword came out from his elbow.”

Abū ʿUbayda said: “Azerbaijan also rebelled, so Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ campaigned against them and conquered it.” In it, ʿUthmān enlarged the Mosque of the Messenger of God ﷺ.

The Year 30

The conquests of Fārs, Khurāsān, and Sijistān. In it, Gūr, in the land of Fārs, was conquered, according to what al-Walīd told me from his father, from his grandfather, and Abū

“Abū al-Ḥasan told me, from Maslama, from Dāwūd ibn Abī Hind, who said: ‘When Ibn ʿĀmir conquered the land of Fārs—that was in the year thirty—Yazdegerd ibn Kisrā fled, and Ibn ʿĀmir and Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd al-Sulamī pursued him.’

“Al-Walīd told me, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: ‘ʿAbd Allāh and ʿUbayd Allāh, the sons of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr were with Ibn ʿĀmir at Gūr. He conquered it and gained much booty. He conquered al-Kāriyān and al-Fīshajān and gained much booty from what had been collected in the fire temple.’

“Abū al-Ḥasan said: ‘Mujāshiʿ marched until he encamped at Sīrjān. It is also said that Ibn ʿĀmir sent Hurm ibn Ḥayyān, and it is said that Hurmuz was conquered by Rāshid ibn ʿAmr. Then Ibn ʿĀmir marched, intending to go to Khurāsān, and sent al-Rabīʿ ibn Ziyād al-Ḥārithī to Sijistān. He conquered Zāliq, Sharwādh, and Nāsharūdh. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān Abū Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, the governor of the tax revenue of Iraq, was captured with his mother. Ibrāhīm ibn Yasār, the client of Banū Layth, was captured from one of the villages of Sijistān. He besieged the city of Zarang, and they made a treaty with him for one thousand slave boys, each slave boy with a gold bowl. Ibn ʿĀmir proceeded to Khurāsān, with al-Aḥnaf ibn Qays at the head of his advance guard. He encountered the people of Herat and defeated them. Ibn ʿĀmir conquered Abrashahr by treaty; it is also said, by force. Ibn ʿĀmir sent Amīr ibn Aḥmar al-Yashkurī, who conquered Ṭūs and its environs. He made a treaty with those of the people of Sarakhs who came, for one hundred and fifty thousand. Ibn ʿĀmir sent al-Aswad ibn Kulthūm al-ʿAdawī of ʿAdī Taym to Bayhaq, in the land of Abrashahr. He conquered it and was killed there ﵀. Then Kanārī ibn ʿĀmir made a treaty for what remained of Abrashahr, on”

A million dirhams and one hundred fārd of grain. The people of Marw sought a peace agreement, and Ibn ʿĀmir made peace with them for two million two hundred thousand. The person who made peace with him was Māhūyah ibn Aẓar, the marzbān of Marw. It is said that the person who made peace with the people of Marw was Ḥātim ibn Nuʿmān al-Bāhilī; Ibn ʿĀmir had sent him. Ibn ʿĀmir sent al-Aḥnaf ibn Qays with four thousand men. The people of Ṭukhāristān, al-Jūzjān, al-Fāriyāb, and al-Ṭāliqān assembled against him, with Ṭūqān Shāh in command. They fought fiercely, and God routed the polytheists.

Azhar ibn Saʿd narrated to us: Ibn ʿAwn narrated to us, from Muḥammad, who said: “Al-Aḥnaf ibn Qays would charge, saying: … Every chief has a duty … … to stain the spear or have it splinter …”

Abū al-Ḥasan said: The Muslims killed them at a distance of thirteen farsakhs. Then al-Aḥnaf marched from Marw al-Rūdh to Balkh, and its people made peace with him for four hundred thousand. Then he came to Khwārazm but could not overcome it, so he returned. Ibn ʿĀmir sent Khulayḍ ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zuhayr to Bādhghīs and Herat, and he conquered them; then they apostatized.

The conquest of Ṭabaristān. In this year Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ raided Ṭabaristān and besieged its people. They asked him for a guarantee of safety on condition that he kill not a single man among them, but he killed them all except one man.

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad, from ʿAlī ibn Mujāhid, from Ḥanash ibn Mālik, said: Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ raided Ṭabaristān in the year thirty; he mentioned a similar account.

In this year Maʿḍad al-Shaybānī was killed; it is also said that it was Salmān ibn Rabīʿah.

Abū Khālid said: Abū al-Khaṭṭāb al-Asadī said: Salmān was killed in the year thirty-one.

The digging of the Euphrates Canal at Baṣra.

In this year Ziyād dug the Euphrates Canal until it reached the place of the mountain. The person who supervised its digging for Ziyād was ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakrah, who was then sixteen years old.

In this year Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah died. In this year Abū Usayd al-Sāʿidī, Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, died. Sufyān informed us: Ashʿath ibn Sulaym narrated to me, from al-Aswad ibn Hilāl, from Thaʿlabah ibn Zuhdam, who said: “We were with Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ in Ṭabaristān. He asked, ‘Which of you prayed the fear prayer with the Messenger of God ﷺ?’ Ḥudhayfah said, ‘I did,’ and he related the report.”

The Year 31

In this year Ibn ʿĀmir entered the state of consecration at Nīshāpūr and appointed Qays ibn al-Haytham al-Sulamī, Nāfiʿ ibn Khālid al-Ṭāḥī, Khulayḍ ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥanafī, and Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Bāhilī as his deputies. It is said, rather, that he appointed Qays ibn al-Haytham alone. In this year Ibn Abī Sarḥ raided Zandān, in the region of al-Maṣṣīṣah, from Egypt. In this year Ibn ʿĀmir dug the Fayḍ of Baṣra from al-Ṭāzāt, cutting it through the middle of Baṣra. He dug the Canal of the Asāwirah until it reached al-Shubbāk. His mother, Dujājah daughter of al-Ṣalt, dug her canal, called the Canal of Umm ʿAbd Allāh, in the middle of Baṣra. Abū ʿUbaydah said: The canal nearer to the Fayḍ of Baṣra and the second canal, which is in the middle of Baṣra in its market, were both dug during the caliphate of ʿUthmān; he gave no date for them. In this year Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb died.

The Year 32

In this year ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zayd, the companion of the call to prayer, Abū Ṭalḥah al-Anṣārī, and Abū Dharr died. Abū Dharr died in it before Ibn Masʿūd and Ibn

Masʿūd; he prayed over him. It is also said that Ubayy ibn Kaʿb died in this year, but it is said instead that Ubayy died during the caliphate of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. Ibn al-Kalbī said: In this year Muʿāwiya raided the strait of Constantinople.

The Year 33

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khāzim’s battle against Qārin. In this year Qārin assembled a large army at Bādhghīs and Herat and advanced with forty thousand men. Qays ibn al-Haytham abandoned the territory, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khāzim al-Sulamī took charge of the people. He met Qārin with four thousand men, killed Qārin, and routed his companions. They captured many captives. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Among those captives were Umm al-Ṣalt ibn Ḥurayth al-Ḥanafī, Umm Ziyād ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī, and Umm ʿAwn ibn Arṭabān, the grandmother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwn the jurist. He wrote to Ibn ʿĀmir about the victory, and Ibn ʿĀmir confirmed him in Khurāsān until ʿUthmān was killed.

The conquest of Zaranj. In this year Ibn ʿĀmir dispatched ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Samurah ibn Ḥabīb to Sijistān. The ruler of Zaranj made peace with him, and he remained there until ʿUthmān’s affairs became unsettled.

The raid on Malatya and Ifrīqiyah. Ibn al-Kalbī said: In this year Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān raided Malatya and Ifrīqiyah. He also raided the Fort of the Woman, in the land of the Byzantines.

The raid on Abyssinia. In this year Ibn Abī Sarḥ raided Abyssinia, and Muʿāwiyah ibn Ḥudayj’s eye was injured. In this year al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib died ﵀. In this year al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad died ﵀. ʿĀmir ibn Rabīʿah died when people began to probe into the affair of ʿUthmān ﵁.

The Year 34

In this year the people of Kūfa expelled Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ and appointed Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī. They wrote to ʿUthmān asking him to appoint Abū Mūsā, and he appointed him. In this year was the Day of al-Jarʿah. ʿUthmān had sent Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ back to Kūfa, but the people of Kūfa came out and prevented him. Ibn al-Kalbī said: In this year Ibn Abī Sarḥ raided al-Ṣawārī from Egypt. In this year ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit and Abū ʿAbs ibn Jabr died.

The Year 35

The dissension in the time of ʿUthmān ﵁. In this year The killing of ʿUthmān ﵀ and his siege. Abū al-Ḥasan said: The people of Egypt came with ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿUdays al-Balawī at their head; the people of Baṣra had Ḥakīm ibn Jablah al-ʿAbdī at their head; and among the people of Kūfa was al-Ashtar, Mālik ibn al-Ḥārith al-Nakhaʿī. They came to Medina concerning ʿUthmān’s affair. The Egyptian delegation arrived on the night of Wednesday, at the new moon of Dhū al-Qaʿdah.

Al-Muʿtamir ibn Sulaymān narrated to us: I heard my father say: Abū Naḍrah narrated to us, from Abū Saʿīd, the client of Abū

Usayd al-Anṣārī, who said: ʿUthmān heard that the delegation from Egypt had arrived, so he went out to meet them. They said, “Call for the codex,” and he called for it. They said, “Open the seventh,” for they used to call Sūrat Yūnus “the seventh.” He recited until he came to this verse: “Say: Has God authorized this for you, or do you invent lies about God?” They said to him, “Stop! What about the pastureland you set aside—is that authorized by God, or are you inventing lies about God?” He said, “Go on; it was revealed concerning such-and-such. As for the pastureland, ʿUmar set it aside before me for the charity camels. When I took office, the charity camels increased, so I expanded the pastureland. ʿUmar set it aside before me for the charity camels. When I took office, the charity camels increased, so I expanded the pastureland in proportion to the increase in the charity camels. Go on.” He said: They kept taking him to task by citing the verse, and he would say, “Go on; it was revealed concerning such-and-such.” They did not get any further. They took his pledge and wrote down conditions binding on him; he also took from them a pledge not to break ranks or leave the community so long as he upheld their conditions. Then they returned satisfied. While they were on the road, a rider began to approach them, then leave them, then return to them, then leave them again. They asked, “What is the matter with you?” He said, “I am the Commander of the Believers’ messenger to his governor in Egypt.” They searched him and found a letter bearing ʿUthmān’s seal, addressed to the governor of Egypt, ordering him to crucify them, kill them, or cut off their hands and feet. They turned back and came to Medina. They went to ʿAlī and said, “Did you not see that God’s enemy wrote such-and-such about us, and that God has made his blood lawful? Come with us to him.” He said, “By God, I will not go with you.” They said, “Then why did you write to us?” He said, “By God, I did not write you a letter.” They looked at one another, and ʿAlī left Medina. They went to ʿUthmān and said, “You wrote such-and-such about us.” He said, “There are only two alternatives: that you bring two Muslim men to verify it, or that I swear by God, besides whom there is no god, that I did not write it, dictate it, or know of it. A letter may be written in a man’s name, and a seal may be engraved over a seal.” They said, “God has made your blood lawful. You have broken the covenant and pledge.” Then they besieged him in the palace ﵁.

Ibn ʿUlayyah, from Ibn ʿAwn, from Muḥammad: ʿUthmān sent ʿAlī and another man to them. ʿAlī said, “Will you accept the Book of God and be satisfied regarding all that has displeased you?” Some of their leading men came with him

They reached an agreement on five points: the exiled man would be brought back, the deprived man would be given his due, the fayʾ would be increased, distribution would be equitable, and a person of trustworthiness and strength would be appointed. They wrote that in a document, and [agreed] that Ibn ʿĀmir would be returned to Baṣra and Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī to Kūfa.

Ibn ʿUlayyah informed me: Ibn ʿAwn narrated to us, from al-Ḥasan, who said: Waththāb informed me: “ʿUthmān sent me, and I summoned al-Ashtar for him. He asked, ‘What do the people want from me?’ I said, ‘There are three things, and one of them is unavoidable.’ He asked, ‘What are they?’ I said, ‘They will give you the choice either to relinquish authority over them and say, “This is your affair, so choose whom you wish,” or to submit to retaliation yourself. If you refuse, the people will kill you.’ He asked, ‘Is one of them unavoidable?’ I said, ‘One of them is unavoidable.’ He said, ‘As for relinquishing authority over them, I would not take off a garment with which God has clothed me.’

“He [the transmitter] said: Someone other than al-Ḥasan said: ‘By God, having my neck struck is dearer to me than relinquishing the community of Muḥammad, one part against another. As for submitting myself to retaliation, by God, I know that my two predecessors used to punish, and my body could not withstand retaliation. As for your killing me, by God, if you kill me, you will never again love one another, never again pray together, and never again fight an enemy together.’

“Kahmas ibn al-Minhal narrated to us: Saʿīd ibn Abī ʿArūbah informed us, from Yaʿlā ibn Ḥakīm, from Nāfiʿ, who said: Ibn ʿUmar entered upon ʿUthmān, with al-Mughīrah ibn al-Akhnas there. He said, ‘Look at what these people are saying. They say, “Renounce it and do not kill yourself.”’ Ibn ʿUmar said, ‘If you renounce it, will you live forever in this world?’ He said, ‘No.’ He asked, ‘If you do not renounce it, can they do anything beyond killing you?’ He said, ‘No.’ He asked, ‘Can they grant you Paradise or Hell?’ He said, ‘No.’ He said, ‘Then I do not think you should take off a shirt with which God clothed you, so that it becomes the practice that whenever a people dislike their caliph or imam, they kill him.’”

ʿUmar ibn Abī Khalīfah narrated to me: Umm Yūsuf daughter of Māhak narrated to us, from her mother, who said: “I entered upon ʿUthmān while he was besieged, with the codex in his lap. They were saying, ‘Separate yourself from us,’ while he said, ‘I will not take off a garment with which God has clothed me.’”

Yazīd ibn Hārūn narrated to us: ʿAbd al-Malik narrated to us, from Abū al-Kindī, who said: “ʿUthmān appeared above them and said, ‘Do not kill me, for if you kill me you will be like this’—and he interlaced his fingers.”

Abū Dāwūd narrated to us: Sahl al-Sarrāj narrated to us, from al-Ḥasan, who said: ʿUthmān said, “Do not kill me, for by God, if you kill me, you will never again fight an enemy together, divide fayʾ together, or pray together.” Al-Ḥasan said: “By God, even when the people pray together, their hearts are divided.”

Abū Bakr al-Kalbī said: Misʿar narrated to us, from ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maysarah, from al-Nazzāl ibn Sabrah, who said: “I heard ʿUthmān say, ‘I ask God’s forgiveness if I have wronged anyone; if I have wronged anyone, I have pardoned him.’”

Ghunder narrated to us, from Shuʿbah, from Saʿd ibn Ibrāhīm, from his father, who said: “I heard ʿUthmān say, ‘If you find it right to put my legs in fetters, then do so.’”

Kahmas ibn al-Minhal narrated to us: Saʿīd ibn Abī ʿArūbah narrated to us, from Qatādah, who said: “When ʿUthmān was besieged, he appeared above them and said, ‘Bring me a man to speak with.’ They brought out Ṣaʿṣaʿah ibn Ṣawḥān. ʿUthmān said, ‘What fault do you find with me?’ He said, ‘You drove us from our homes without right, except that we said, “Our Lord is God.”’ ʿUthmān said, ‘You lie; you are not those people. We are those people. The people of Mecca drove us out.’ Then God said: ‘Those who, if We establish them in the land, establish prayer, give alms, enjoin what is right, and forbid what is wrong.’ This was praise before a trial.

“Ghunder narrated to us: Shuʿbah narrated to us, from Sammāk ibn Ḥarb, who said: I heard Ḥanẓalah ibn Qunān say: ‘ʿUthmān appeared above us and said, “Are the two sons of Miḥdaj among you?” He said, “I adjure you both by God: do you not know that ʿUmar …”’”

He said, “Rabīʿah is wicked or treacherous, and by God, I will not make their stipends equal to those of people who came from a month’s journey away. One of them has his dwelling beside his tent peg. I increased their allotments by five hundred in a single morning, until I brought them up to the others.” They said, “Yes.” He said, “I adjure you both by God: do you not, do you not know that you came to me and said, ‘Kindah is a head-eating people, and Rabīʿah is the head, and al-Ashʿath ibn Qays has consumed them’? So I removed him and appointed you two.” They said, “Yes.” He said, “O God, if they have repaid my kindness with ingratitude and exchanged my favor, then do not grant them contentment with their imam, and do not grant an imam contentment with them.”

Al-Muʿtamir narrated to us, from his father, from Abū Naḍrah, from Abū Saʿīd, the client of Abū Usayd, who said: “One day he appeared above them and said, ‘Peace be upon you.’ I heard no one return his greeting, except for a man who returned it inwardly. He said, ‘I adjure you by God: do you know that I bought Rūmah with my own money, made its water pleasant to drink, and made my bucket rope there like the bucket rope of any Muslim?’ They said, ‘Yes.’ He said, ‘Then why do you prevent me from drinking its water, so that I break my fast with seawater’—meaning the salty water of the well. He said, ‘I adjure you by God: do you know that I bought such-and-such land and added it to the mosque? Do you know of anyone who was prevented from praying in it before me? I adjure you by God: do you know that the Prophet of God mentioned such-and-such things concerning me?’ He also mentioned the writing of al-Mufaṣṣal. Then the prohibition spread, and people began saying, ‘Take it easy with the Commander of the Believers.’

“Yaḥyā ibn Abī al-Ḥajjāj, Abū Ayyūb al-Khāqānī, narrated to us: al-Jarīrī narrated to us, from Abū al-Ward ibn Thumāmah, who said: ‘ʿUthmān appeared above them and said, “I adjure you by God: do you know that the Messenger of God ﷺ was on Thabīr with Abū Bakr, ʿUmar, and me? The mountain moved beneath them until its stones nearly fell. The Messenger of God ﷺ said, ‘Be firm, for upon you are a prophet, a truthful one, and a martyr.’”’”

They said, “Yes, by God.” He said, “Bear witness for me, by the Lord of the Kaʿba.”

Ibn ʿUlayya related to us, saying: Ayyūb related to us, from Ibn Abī Mulayka, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr, who said: I said to ʿUthmān, “We are with you in the house, a band of clear-sighted men. God can grant victory with fewer than them, so give us permission.” He said, “I adjure by God any man who would spill my blood”—or he said, “blood.”

Ibn Mahdī said: Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān related to us, from Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn, who said: Sulayṭ ibn Sulayṭ said, “ʿUthmān forbade us to fight them. Had he given us permission, we would have struck them until we drove them out from every quarter of it.” I heard ʿAbd al-Wahhāb ibn ʿAbd al-Majīd say: I heard Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd say: I heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Rabīʿa say: “I was with ʿUthmān in the house. He said, ‘I solemnly charge everyone who considers himself bound to hear and obey to hold back his hand and weapon. The most valuable of you to me is the one who holds back his hand and weapon.’ Then he said, ‘Get up, Ibn ʿUmar, and mediate between the people.’ Ibn ʿUmar got up, and men from Banū ʿAdī ibn Surāqa and Ibn Muṭīʿ got up with him. They opened the door and went out; they entered the house and killed ʿUthmān ﵁.” Muʿādh related to us, from Ibn ʿAwn, from Nāfiʿ, who said: Ibn ʿUmar was with ʿUthmān in the house. ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwn related to us, from his father, from Nāfiʿ, who said: Ibn ʿUmar put on the coat of mail twice on the day of the house. Kahmas related to us, saying: Ibn Abī ʿArūba related to us, from Qatāda, that Zayd ibn Thābit said to ʿUthmān, “These Anṣār are at the door, saying, ‘If you wish, we shall be God’s helpers once again.’” He said, “I have no need of that. Hold back.” Kahmas related to me, saying: Ibn Abī ʿArūba related to us, from Yaʿlā ibn Ḥakīm, from Nāfiʿ or someone else, that Ibn ʿUmar was wearing his sword that day until ʿUthmān charged him to leave, for fear that he would be killed. It was related to us from al-Aʿmash, from Abū Ṣāliḥ, from Abū Hurayra, who said: I said to ʿUthmān, “Today it is right to strike alongside you.” He said, “I charge you to leave.” Kahmas related to us, from Ibn Abī ʿArūba, from Qatāda, that Abū Hurayra was wearing his sword until ʿUthmān forbade him.

ʿAbd al-Aʿlā and Kahmas related to us, from Ibn ʿArūba, from Qatāda. ʿAbd al-Aʿlā added that al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī was the last to leave ʿUthmān. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mahdī said: Ḥuṣayn ibn Bakr related to us, from Yaḥyā ibn ʿAtīq, from Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn, who said: Al-Ḥasan, al-Ḥusayn, Ibn ʿUmar, Ibn al-Zubayr, and Marwān set out, all armed, until they entered the house. ʿUthmān said, “I charge you all to return, lay down your weapons, and stay in your homes.” Ibn ʿUmar, al-Ḥasan, and al-Ḥusayn went out. Ibn al-Zubayr and Marwān said, “We charge ourselves not to leave.” Al-Muʿtamir related to us, from his father, from Abū Naḍra, from Abū Saʿīd, the client of Abū Usayd, who said: ʿUthmān opened the door and placed the codex before him. A man entered and said, “Between me and you is the Book of God,” then went out and left him. Another man entered and said, “Between me and you is the Book of God.” He struck at him with a sword; ʿUthmān warded it off, and the blow cut it. I do not know whether it severed it or cut it without severing it. He said, “By God, this was the first hand to write the Mufaṣṣal.”

Ismāʿīl related to us, from Ibn ʿAwn, from al-Ḥasan, from Waththāb, who said: “A little man came, as if he were a wolf. He peered in through a door, then went back. Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr came with thirteen men and seized ʿUthmān by the beard, pulling it until I heard his teeth click. He said, ‘What good has Muʿāwiya done you? What good has Ibn ʿĀmir done you? What good have your letters done you?’ ʿUthmān said, ‘Let go of my beard, nephew.’ I saw him summon a particular man from among the group with his eye”—meaning, he pointed to him—“and the man went to him with a broad arrowhead and thrust it into his head.” I said, “What happened next?” He said, “Then they all fell upon him, by God, until they killed him. ﵀.”

Al-Muʿtamir related to us, from his father, from al-Ḥasan, that Ibn Abī Bakr seized ʿUthmān by the beard. ʿUthmān said, “You have put me in a position—or taken a stance toward me—that your father would never have taken.” He went out and left him. In al-Muʿtamir’s report, from his father, from Abū Naḍra, from Abū Saʿīd, he said: “A man from Banū Sadūs, called the Black Death, entered upon him and strangled him—he strangled him before striking with the sword. He said, ‘By God…’”

“I have never seen anything more gentle than his strangling. He strangled him until I saw his breath fluttering in his body like a jinn.” In a report other than that of Abū Saʿīd, he said: “Al-Tujībī entered and pierced him with a broad arrowhead, and the blood spattered onto the words, ‘God will suffice you against them.’ It is in the codex as it was.”

Kahmas related to us, from Ibn Abī ʿArūba, from Qatāda, who said: “The one who took charge of killing ʿUthmān was Rūmān, a man from Banū Asad ibn Khuzayma. Ibn Abī Bakr seized him by the beard, and Rūmān slaughtered him with broad arrowheads he had with him.”

Abū al-Ḥasan related to us, from Abū Zakariyyā al-ʿAjlānī, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar, who said: “Ibn Abī Bakr struck him with broad arrowheads in the veins of his neck, and Sūdān ibn Ḥumrān stabbed him with a spear.”

Abū Dāwūd related to us, saying: Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa related to us, saying: Kināna, the client of Ṣafiyya, related to us, saying: “I witnessed the killing of ʿUthmān.” I said, “Who killed him?” He said, “A man from Egypt called Ḥimār.” Khālid ibn al-Ḥārith said: ʿImrān ibn Ḥudayr related to us, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Shaqīq, who said: “The first to strike ʿUthmān was Rūmān al-Yamānī, with a mace.”

Khālid ibn al-Ḥārith related to us, saying: ʿImrān ibn Ḥudayr related to us, saying: “If it were not that ʿAbd Allāh ibn Shaqīq told me that the first drop to fall of his blood was on ‘God will suffice you against them’—for Abū Ḥurayth mentioned that he and Suhayl al-Numayrī went and brought the codex out to him, and there was the drop on ‘God will suffice you against them’—he said: ‘It is in the codex as it was.’”

Abū ʿĀṣim said: ʿUmar ibn Abī Zāʾida related to us, from his father, from Abū Khālid al-Wālibī, who said: ʿĀʾisha said, “You kept asking him to repent until you left him like a freshly washed garment, then you killed him.” Abū Qutayba related to us, saying: Yūnus ibn Abī Isḥāq related to us, from ʿAwn ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUtba, who said:

ʿĀʾisha said, “I was angered on your behalf by the whip, yet should I not be angered for ʿUthmān by the sword? You kept asking him to make amends until, when you had left him like a polished heart, you killed him.”

Rūḥ ibn ʿUbāda related to us, saying: Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān related to us, from Ibn Sīrīn, who said: ʿĀʾisha said, “You sucked him dry, as one sucks a vessel, then you killed him.”

Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr said: Abū Muʿāwiya related to us, from al-Aʿmash, from Khaythama, from Masrūq, who said: ʿĀʾisha said, “You left him like a garment clean of filth, then brought him close to slaughter him as a ram is slaughtered.” Masrūq said: I said, “This was your doing: you wrote to the people, ordering them to rise against him.” ʿĀʾisha said, “By Him in whom the believers believed and whom the unbelievers rejected, I did not write to them a single black mark on white until I sat in this place of mine.” Al-Aʿmash said: They used to think that it had been written in her name. In that year, ʿUthmān dispatched ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās, who conducted the pilgrimage, and ʿAlī led the people in prayer in Medina on the Day of Sacrifice.

In that year, ʿUthmān ﵀ was killed on a Friday, with some days of Dhū al-Ḥijja remaining.

Al-Muʿtamir ibn Sulaymān said: I heard my father say: Abū ʿUthmān al-Nahdī related to us, saying: “ʿUthmān was killed in the middle of the Days of Tashrīq.”

Abū al-Ḥasan related from Abū Maʿshar, from Nāfiʿ, who said: “He was killed on a Friday, when seventeen or eighteen days of Dhū al-Ḥijja had passed.” ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn al-Haytham related to us, saying: My father related to me, saying: I said to al-Ḥasan, “Was anyone among the Emigrants or the Anṣār among those who killed ʿUthmān?” He said, “No; they were non-Arabs from Egypt.” Yaḥyā related to me, from Ismāʿīl, from Qays, who said: I heard Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl saying, “Had anyone been outraged by what was done to ʿUthmān, he would have been justified.” ʿUthmān was born in Mecca, in the house of Abū al-ʿĀṣ, which is called the House of al-Ḥakam. It is said that he was killed on the Day of Sacrifice; and he was killed in Medina. Concerning this, al-Farazdaq said:

“ʿUthmān, when they killed him and violated his blood, on the morning of the night of the Day of Sacrifice…”

And Nābigha of Banū Jaʿda said:

“And Ibn ʿAffān, upright and Muslim… and the flesh of the sacrificial camels when they are moved…”

Al-Qāsim ibn Umayya ibn Abī al-Ṣalt said:

“By my life, what an evil slaughtered offering you sacrificed, in opposition to the Messenger of God, on the Day of Sacrifice…”

ʿUthmān was buried at night. Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim prayed over him; it is also said that it was Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām, and it is also said al-Miswar ibn Makhrama.

The duration of ʿUthmān’s caliphate and his age

His rule lasted eleven years, eleven months, and eighteen days; it is also said fourteen days. There is disagreement about his age. Muʿādh ibn Hishām related to us, saying: My father related to me, from Qatāda, who said: “He was killed when he was eighty-six years old.” Abū al-Yaqẓān related to me, saying: Abū al-Miqdām related to me, saying: “He was killed when he was eighty-two years old”; it is also said eighty-four. Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad related to me, saying: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Abī ʿUmrān related to me, saying: Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Makhzūmī related to me, saying: “He was killed when he was eighty-two years old.” In that year, al-Mughīra ibn al-Akhnas ibn Shurayq al-Thaqafī was killed on the day of the house alongside ʿUthmān ﵀.

The governors appointed by ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān

Mecca: He appointed ʿAlī ibn ʿAdī ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Manāf over Mecca. He also appointed Khālid ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Hishām ibn al-Mughīra al-Makhzūmī over it; ʿUthmān was killed while he was governor there.

Medina: When he went on pilgrimage, he would appoint Zayd ibn Thābit as his deputy over Medina.

Baṣra: He kept Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī over Baṣra for four years. When Abū Mūsā went on campaign, he would appoint ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn as his deputy, and at times he would appoint Ziyād. Then he dismissed Abū Mūsā and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz. We have recorded the governors appointed by Ibn ʿĀmir over Khurāsān and Sijistān in the Chronology, under Ibn ʿĀmir’s governorship.

Kūfa: ʿAmr al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba was over Kūfa. ʿUthmān confirmed him briefly, then dismissed him and appointed Saʿd ibn Mālik; then he dismissed him and appointed al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba ibn Abī Muʿayṭ; then he dismissed him and appointed Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. The people of Kūfa expelled him and appointed Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī. They wrote to ʿUthmān asking him to appoint Abū Mūsā, and ʿUthmān confirmed him until ʿUthmān was killed ﵀.

Egypt: He dismissed ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ from Egypt and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ. He remained there until ʿUthmān was killed ﵀.

Syria: He confirmed Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān over Syria.

His chief of police, his chamberlain, the person in charge of the treasury, and his scribe. ʿUthmān was the first to appoint a chief of police. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qunfudh, of Quraysh’s Banū Taym, was in charge of his police; his chamberlain was Ḥumrān ibn Abān; ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Arqam was in charge of the treasury, then asked to be excused, and ʿUthmān excused him; his scribe was Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam.

The judges: Kaʿb ibn Sūr was judge in Baṣra. Then ʿUthmān ordered Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī to judge between the people. Later Ibn ʿĀmir was appointed and made Kaʿb ibn Sūr judge; he remained so until he was killed during the days of the Camel. A stray arrow struck and killed him; he did not fight. In Kūfa, it was Shurayḥ until ʿUthmān was killed. In Yemen, it was Yaʿlā ibn Umayya, of BalʿAdawiyya; his mother was Munya. A man called Thumāma was over Ṣanʿāʾ until ʿUthmān was killed.

Khurāsān: Ibn ʿĀmir was appointed over Baṣra in the year twenty-nine. Ibn ʿĀmir went on campaign and appointed Ziyād as his deputy over Baṣra. He conquered Iṣfahān, Ḥulwān, Kirmān, and most of Khurāsān. We have already recorded his conquests in the history. Then he entered iḥrām at Sābūr and appointed Qays ibn al-Haytham al-Sulamī over Khurāsān, in the year thirty-three. Qārin assembled an army, and Qays abandoned the lands. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khāzim al-Sulamī took charge of the people, met Qārin, killed Qārin, and routed his companions. He wrote to Ibn ʿĀmir announcing the conquest, and Ibn ʿĀmir confirmed him over Khurāsān until ʿUthmān was killed.

Sijistān: Ibn ʿĀmir appointed al-Rabīʿ ibn Ziyād al-Ḥārithī over it. He conquered Zāliq and made a treaty with the people of Zarang, then departed and appointed a man from Balḥārith as his deputy. Then Ibn ʿĀmir appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Samura ibn Ḥabīb in the year thirty-three. He remained there until ʿUthmān’s affairs became unsettled. He returned and appointed Amīr ibn Aḥmar al-Yashkurī, whom the people of Sijistān expelled.

Al-Sind: ʿUthmān sent Ḥakīm ibn Jabala al-ʿAbdī. He went to Makrān, then came to ʿUthmān. ʿUthmān asked him about it, and he said, “Its water is scant, its thief is valiant, and its plain is a mountain. If the troops there are numerous, they go hungry; if they are few, they perish.” So ʿUthmān sent no one there until he was killed.

Bahrain: Abū Khālid related from Abū al-Khaṭṭāb, who said: “Ibn ʿĀmir sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār al-ʿAbdī during ʿUthmān’s rule. He remained there until ʿUthmān was killed.” Among those appointed over it was Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam.

The summer campaign: ʿUthmān wrote to Muʿāwiya ordering him to raid the lands of the Byzantines. He sent Yazīd ibn al-Ḥurr al-ʿAbsī, then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Khālid ibn al-Walīd over both summer expeditions together. Then he dismissed him and appointed Sufyān ibn ʿAwf al-Ghāmidī. Sufyān would campaign by land and appoint Junāda ibn Abī Umayya as his deputy at sea. He continued in that way until Sufyān died. Muʿāwiya then appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Khālid ibn al-Walīd, then appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Rabāḥ; he spent the winter in the land of the Byzantines.

Chapter 6

The caliphate of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib

The Year 36

The caliphate of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁. In it, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib received the oath of allegiance. His mother was Fāṭima daughter of Asad ibn Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Quṣayy ibn Kilāb.

The departure of Ṭalḥa, al-Zubayr, and ʿĀʾisha. In it, Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh and al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām arrived at Baṣra with ʿĀʾisha, Mother of the Believers.

ʿUthmān ibn Ḥunayf al-Anṣārī was there as governor for ʿAlī. ʿUthmān ibn Ḥunayf sent Ḥakīm ibn Jabala al-ʿAbdī, who met Ṭalḥa and al-Zubayr at al-Zābūqa, the town of provisions near Kalāʾ in Baṣra. Ḥakīm ibn Jabala was killed, and Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd al-Sulamī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—was also killed. ʿUthmān ibn Ḥunayf left Baṣra. In that year, ʿAlī left Medina and appointed Sahl ibn Ḥunayf al-Anṣārī over it. ʿAlī sent al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir to Kūfa to summon the people, and ʿAlī arrived in Baṣra.

The Battle of the Camel

In that year, the Battle of the Camel took place in Baṣra, at al-Zāwiya, in the district of Ṭaff Baṣra, on Friday, ten days into Jumada II, in the year thirty-six.

In that year, Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh was killed in the battle. A stray arrow struck him and killed him. Muʿādh ibn Hishām narrated to us: he said, “My father narrated to me, from Qatāda, from al-Jārūd ibn Abī Sabra, who said: Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam looked at Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh on the day of the Camel and said, ‘After today I will not seek vengeance for my blood.’ Then he shot him with an arrow and killed him.”

Someone who heard Juwayriya ibn Asmāʾ narrated to us, from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, from his uncle, that Marwān shot Ṭalḥa with an arrow and killed him. Al-Zubayr turned away and rode down; he was killed at Wādī al-Sibāʿ by ʿUmayr ibn Jurmūz al-Mujāshiʿī. Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAttāb ibn Asīd were killed. In the first encounter of the Camel, before ʿAlī’s arrival, Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd al-Sulamī and Ḥakīm ibn Jabala al-ʿAbdī were killed.

Nuʿaym ibn Masʿūd al-Ashjaʿī lived to see ʿUthmān. In that year, Ḥudhayfa ibn al-Yamān died, at the beginning of the year thirty-six. Abū al-Yaqẓān and Abū al-Ḥasan said: Māhūya ibn Azar, the marzbān of Marw, came to ʿAlī after the Battle of the Camel, in the year thirty-six, agreeing to a peace settlement. ʿAlī wrote him a document, but afterward they reneged. ʿAlī then sent ʿAwn ibn Jaʿda ibn Hubayra ibn Abī Wahb al-Makhzūmī, but he accomplished nothing. In that year, ʿImrān ibn al-Fuḍayl al-Burjumī and Ḥasaka ibn ʿAttāb al-Ḥayṭī set out and raided the outskirts of Sijistān; the ruler of Zarang made peace with them. In that year, ʿAlī left Baṣra and arrived in Kūfa, then set out intending to go against Muʿāwiya. He appointed Abū Masʿūd ʿUqba ibn ʿAmr al-Badrī as his deputy over Kūfa.

A detailed account of the Battle of the Camel

In that year the Battle of the Camel took place. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh, al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām, and ʿĀʾisha arrived in Baṣra at the upper part of al-Mirbad. When they reached the tanners’ quarter, which was near the palace of Zarbay in Sikkat al-Mazīd, the people gathered until, if a stone had been thrown, it would have landed on someone’s head. Ṭalḥa spoke, and ʿĀʾisha spoke, and the shouting grew loud. Abū Bakr narrated to me: he said, “ʿAwf narrated to us: Abū Rajāʾ al-ʿUṭāridī narrated to us: I came to Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh while the people had crowded around him and he was on his mount. He kept saying, ‘O people, will you be quiet?’ They kept pressing around him and would not be quiet. He said, ‘Ugh, ugh! Moths around a fire and flies around greed.’” Abū al-Ḥasan, from al-Hudhalī, said: They descended from the tanners’ quarter, and the people pelted them with stones. They made their way through Banū Nahd until they came out by the cemetery of Banū Māzin, then by the cemetery of Banū Ḥiṣn, then came out onto the embankment, until they alighted at al-Jabal.

Ḥātim ibn Muslim, from Yūsuf ibn ʿAbda, from Ibn Sīrīn, said: Ṭalḥa and al-Zubayr alighted in the district of Banū Saʿd.

Abū al-Yaqẓān said: ʿAlī had appointed ʿUthmān ibn Ḥunayf al-Anṣārī over Baṣra at that time. He said: Ṭalḥa, al-Zubayr, and those with them marched until they reached al-Zābūqa. ʿUthmān ibn Ḥunayf came out to them, and they faced one another until the sun had passed its zenith. Then they made peace and drew up a document between them, agreeing that they would refrain from fighting; ʿUthmān would retain the governor’s residence, the mosque, the treasury, and al-Kalāʾ; Ṭalḥa and al-Zubayr would alight wherever they wished outside Baṣra; and neither party would interfere with the other until ʿAlī arrived. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: ʿĀʾisha, Ṭalḥa, and al-Zubayr moved and alighted at Ṭāḥiya ibn ʿUbayd. Abū al-Ḥasan, from al-Hudhalī, from al-Jārūd ibn Abī Subra, from Sinān ibn Salama ibn al-Muḥabbiq al-Hudhalī, said: Ibn al-Zubayr went in the morning to al-Zābūqa, the town of provisions, intending to allot provisions to his companions. Ḥakīm ibn Jabala al-ʿAbdī came with seven hundred men from ʿAbd al-Qays and Bakr ibn Wāʾil. They fought, and Ḥakīm ibn Jabala, his brother al-Raʿl ibn Jabala, and his son al-Ashraf ibn Ḥakīm were killed. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: A man of al-Ḥadān called Ḍakhīm killed Ḥakīm; it is also said that Yazīd ibn al-Aṣḥam al-Ḥadānī killed him. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Ḥanẓala al-Hazzānī was killed alongside Ḥakīm. Abū al-Ḥasan, from Maslama, from Dāwūd ibn Abī Hind, said: Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd al-Sulamī was wounded alongside Ibn al-Zubayr and was carried to his house among Banū Yashkur, where he died and was buried. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: ʿAlī sent al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī and ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir to Kūfa to summon the people.

Ghundar narrated to us: Shuʿba said, from al-Ḥakam, who said: I heard Abū Wāʾil say: When al-Ḥasan and ʿAmmār called the people of Kūfa to arms, ʿAmmār said, “By God, I know that she is his wife in this world and the next, but God has tested you through her, to see whether you follow him or her.” Ghundar, from Shuʿba, from Saʿd ibn Ibrāhīm, said: A man from Aslam narrated to me: We were with ʿAlī—four thousand people from Medina. He said: Abū Ghassān narrated to us: Yaʿqūb al-Qummī narrated to us, from Jaʿfar Abū al-Mughīra, from Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, who said: With ʿAlī on the day of the Camel were eight hundred Anṣār and four hundred of those who had witnessed the Pledge of al-Riḍwān. Abū Bakr, from ʿAbd al-Malik, from Salama ibn Kuhayl, said: Al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī and ʿAmmār arrived and called the people to arms. Between six and seven thousand went out, until they reached ʿAlī at Dhū Qār. He marched with them, and with him were about ten thousand, until he came to Baṣra. Abū ʿUbayda said: ʿAlī marched from Dhū Qār and placed ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās in command of his vanguard. Then he appointed the commanders, set up the standards, and handed the banner to his son Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: ʿAlī’s standard was with his son Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī. Abū ʿUbayda said: ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir commanded the cavalry, Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr the foot soldiers, and ʿAlbāʾ ibn al-Haytham al-Sadūsī the right wing, consisting of Rabīʿa of Baṣra and Kūfa. It is also said that ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar commanded it. Al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī commanded the left wing, consisting of Muḍar of Baṣra and Muḍar of Kūfa. He said: It is also said that al-Ḥasan commanded the right wing and al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī the left. The banners of Ṭalḥa and al-Zubayr were with ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām. Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh commanded the cavalry and ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr the foot soldiers. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir commanded the right wing, which was Muḍar; it is also said that it was ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith. Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam commanded the left wing, consisting of the people of Yemen. Abū al-Ḥasan, from al-Hudhalī, from Qatāda, narrated to us: ʿAlī marched from al-Zāwiya, and Ṭalḥa, al-Zubayr, and ʿĀʾisha marched from the landing place. They met at the site of the palace of ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād, in the middle of Jumada II, in the year thirty-six, on Thursday, although the battle took place on Friday. ʿAlī ibn ʿĀṣim narrated to us: Ḥuṣayn said: ʿAmr ibn Jawwān narrated to me: I heard al-Aḥnaf ibn Qays say: When they met, the first person killed was Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh. Kaʿb ibn Sūr came out from Baṣra with the Qurʾān codex, holding it open between the two ranks and appealing to the people on account of their blood. He was killed in that state. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Kaʿb ibn Sūr came out with a codex hanging from his neck, wearing a hooded cloak, and carrying a staff in his hand. He took hold of the Camel’s halter, and a stray arrow struck and killed him.

Muʿādh ibn Hishām narrated to us: My father narrated to me, from Qatāda, from al-Jārūd ibn Abī Subra al-Hudhalī, who said: Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam looked at Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh on the day of the Camel and said, “After today I will not seek vengeance for my blood.” Then he shot him with an arrow and killed him. Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Qurashī narrated to me, from Ḥammād ibn Zayd, from Qurra ibn Khālid, from Ibn Sīrīn, who said: Ṭalḥa was shot with an arrow that struck the hollow at the base of his throat. He said: Marwān admitted that he had shot him. He also said: Juwayriya ibn Asmāʾ narrated to me, from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, from his uncle, who said: Marwān shot Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh with an arrow, then turned to Abān ibn ʿUthmān and said, “We have taken care of one of your father’s killers for you.” ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mahdī, from Ḥammād ibn Zayd, from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, said: Ṭalḥa said: “I regretted, with the regret of al-Kusaʿī, when I bought the approval of Banū Jarm at the cost of my own displeasure. O God, exact vengeance for ʿUthmān from me until You are pleased.”

ʿAlī ibn ʿĀṣim narrated to us, from Ḥuṣayn, from ʿAmr ibn Jawwān, from al-Aḥnaf, who said: When al-Zubayr withdrew, ʿAmr ibn Jurmūz killed him at Wādī al-Sibāʿ.

Bishr ibn al-Mufaḍḍal said: Manṣūr ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated to us, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: Whoever tells you that more than four of the people of Badr witnessed the Battle of the Camel—or that a fifth came—then ʿAlī and ʿAmmār were on one side, and Ṭalḥa and al-Zubayr on the other.

We were narrated to, from Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Khālid, from Qays, who said: On the day of the Camel, Ṭalḥa was shot in the knee. When they restrained it, it swelled; when they let it go, it bled. He said, “Leave it alone, for it is an arrow sent by God.” The people were routed, and a great number of them were killed.

Kahmas ibn al-Minhāl narrated to me: Saʿīd narrated to us, from Qatāda, who said: Twenty thousand were killed on the day of the Camel.

Wahb ibn Jarīr narrated to us: My father narrated to me, from Muḥammad ibn Abī Yaʿqūb al-Ḍabbī, who said: Among the people of Baṣra, two thousand five hundred from al-Azd and eight hundred from Banū Ṣabba were killed. Būhab said.

Al-Muʿallā Abū Ḥātim narrated to me: Wahb—and al-Muʿallā Abū Ḥātim narrated to me: My grandmother narrated to me, saying: We went out to the dead of the Battle of the Camel and counted them with reeds: twenty thousand. Abū al-Yaqẓān, from al-Rukayn ibn al-Qāsim, from ʿAlī ibn Zayd, said: Seven thousand were killed on the day of the Camel. Ḥātim ibn Muslim narrated to us, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Khālid ibn al-ʿĀṣ, from his father, who said: Thirteen thousand of ʿAlī’s companions were killed, between four and five hundred. Abū al-Ḥasan, from Muḥammad ibn Ṣāliḥ al-Thaqafī, from Ibn Jurayj, from Ibn Mulayka, said: Five hundred of ʿAlī’s companions were struck down. Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated to me, from Ḥammād ibn Zayd, from al-Zubayr ibn al-Khurayt, who said: Abū Labīd was asked, ‘Do you love ʿAlī?’ He said, ‘How could I love a man who killed two thousand five hundred of my people between the time the sun was here and the time it came to here?’

A listing of those whose names have been preserved among those killed on the day of the Camel

From Banū Umayya: from Banū Umayya, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAttāb ibn Asīd.

From Banū Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd Shams: from Banū Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd Shams, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir.

From Banū ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿAbd Shams: from Banū ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿAbd Shams, ʿAlī ibn ʿAdī ibn Muḥriz ibn Ḥāritha ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā.

From Banū Asad: from Banū Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā, al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām, killed by ʿUmayr ibn Jurmūz, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām.

From Banū ʿAbd al-Dār: from Banū ʿAbd al-Dār ibn Quṣayy, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Musāfiʿ ibn Ṭalḥa ibn Abī Ṭalḥa.

From Banū ʿAbd ibn Quṣayy: from Banū ʿAbd ibn Quṣayy, ʿAbd Allāh, client of al-Ḥārith ibn Nuqaydh.

From Banū Zuhra: from Banū Zuhra ibn Kilāb, al-Aswad ibn ʿAwf and ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra ibn al-Akhnas ibn Shurayq; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī ʿUthmān ibn al-Akhnas ibn Shurayq, both their allies from Thaqīf; and Maʿbad ibn al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad, his ally from Bahraʾ.

From Banū Makhzūm: from Banū Makhzūm ibn Yaqaẓa, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd Shams, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Burda ibn Maʿbad ibn Wahb ibn ʿĀʾidh, and Maʿbad ibn Zuhayr ibn Abī Umayya.

From Banū Taym ibn Murra: from Banū Taym ibn Murra, Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh, his son Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUthmān, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Salama ibn al-Ḥārith.

From Banū Jumḥ: from Banū Jumḥ, Ṣafwān, client of Muṭīʿ; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Wahb ibn Asīd; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī ibn Khalaf; a son of ʿUmayr ibn Wahb; Muslim ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḥumayl; Nuʿaym ibn al-Ṣalt, their ally from Kinda; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Hāniʾ, client of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Salama.

From Banū Sahm: from Banū Sahm ibn ʿAmr, Ibn Luqays ibn ʿAdī.

From Banū ʿĀmir: from Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy, ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Qays, Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥuwayṭib, and a client of theirs, Abū al-Akhnas.

Among Banū al-Ḥārith: among Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr, a man.

Among Banū Tamīm: among Banū Tamīm, Hilāl ibn Wakīʿ al-Dārimī and Abū al-Jarbāʾ al-Ghaylānī.

Among Banū Ghaylān: among Banū Ghaylān ibn Mālik, the brothers of Māzin ibn Mālik ibn ʿAmr ibn Tamīm. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: al-Sajf ibn Saʿd ibn ʿAwf al-ʿAjīfī was killed, along with Farāʾiḍa and ʿAmmār, two men.

Among Balhajīm: among Balhajīm, Ḥanẓala ibn Ḍirār al-Ḍabbī.

Among Qays ʿAylān: among Qays ibn ʿAylān, then among Banū Sulaym, ʿĀṣim ibn Qays ibn al-Ṣalt, his son ʿAmr ibn ʿĀṣim, Shabīb ibn al-Haytham, Muʿawwaḍ ibn Asmāʾ ibn al-Ṣalt, and Miʿraḍ ibn ʿAlāṭ, the brother of al-Ḥajjāj ibn ʿAlāṭ.

Among Bāhila: Kulayb ibn ʿAmr, the paternal uncle of Qutayba ibn Muslim, was killed among Bāhila.

Among the Yemenis: among the Yemenis, Kaʿb ibn Sawr al-Laqīṭī and Ibn Luṣabra ibn Shimān al-Ḥadānī.

Among Ṭāḥiya, Abū al-Yaqẓān said: thirty men of Ṭāḥiya were killed; they were buried by the mosque of Nāfiʿ ibn Khālid al-Ṭāʾī.

Among the Jahaḍim: thirty men of the Jahaḍim were killed, among them Qays ibn Ṣahbān and Jawdān ibn ʿĀʾidh, Abū ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jawdān. ʿAmr ibn al-Ashraf was killed—he was Abū Ziyād ibn ʿAmr—and he was holding the camel’s halter. Al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Shāriq al-Ghāmidī killed him, and ʿAmr ibn al-Ashraf killed him: each of the two killed the other. Among ʿAlī’s companions, those whose names have been preserved for us were killed: Zayd and Sayḥān, the sons of Ṣūḥān; ʿAlbāʾ ibn al-Ḥārith al-Sadūsī; Hind al-Jamalī; and al-Ṣaqʿab and ʿAbd Allāh, the sons of Sulaym and brothers of Mikhnaf ibn Sulaym.

Ashhal ibn Ḥātim and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwn related to us, from Ibn ʿAwn, from Abū Rajāʾ, who said: “That day I saw the camel as though it were a hedgehog made of arrows, and a man holding the halter, saying:

We are the sons of Ḍabba, the companions of the Camel; we meet death when death descends.

…Death is sweeter to us than honey; we seek Ibn ʿAffān at the points of the spears…”

He said: “By God, he did not stop until he had cut through the camel’s legs, and it fell. They cried, ‘Our mother! Our mother!’ A man said to Abū Rajāʾ, ‘What did you do that day?’ He said, ‘I shot arrows, but I do not know what they did.’”

ʿAlī ibn ʿĀṣim related to us, from Ḥuṣayn, who said: Abū Jamīla al-Bakkāʾī related to me: “I was in the ranks with ʿAlī when they hamstrung the Mother of the Believers’ camel. I saw Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr and ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir rushing across the space between the two lines, each of them trying to reach her first. They cut the saddle’s girth, lifted her in her howdah, and carried her.”

One who heard Hubayra ibn Ḥudayr al-ʿAdawī related to us: Awfā ibn Dilham al-ʿAdawī related to us, saying: ʿĀʾisha said, “I did not notice my camel’s head until I no longer heard the voices of Banū ʿAdī.”

Ghundar and Abū Dāwūd related to us; they said: Shuʿba related to us, from ʿAmr ibn Murra, who said: I heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salama and al-Ḥārith ibn Suwayd recalling the Day of the Camel. Al-Ḥārith said: “By God, I have never seen a day like the Day of the Camel. They thrust their spears into our chests, and we thrust ours into their chests, until, had the men wished to walk over them, they could have done so. By God, I wish I had not witnessed that day, and that ʿAlī had such-and-such.”

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salama said: “By God, I would not be pleased that I had been absent from that day, nor that I had been absent from any battle ʿAlī witnessed, even if I had such-and-such.”

ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Mūsā related to us; he said: Misʿar related to us, from ʿAmr ibn Murra, from al-Ḥārith ibn Jumhān al-Juʿfī, who said: “When the Day of the Camel came, we thrust our spears into their chests, and they thrust theirs into ours, until, had the men wished to walk on the spears, they could have done so.” He said: “I heard these people saying, ‘There is no god but God, and God is greatest,’ and those people saying, ‘There is no god but God, and God is greatest.’

In that year Salmān al-Fārisī and Qudāma ibn Maẓʿūn al-Jumaḥī died. ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib led the pilgrimage for the people; it is also said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās.

The campaign in India. In that year al-Ḥārith ibn Murra al-ʿAbdī called the people to campaign in India. He went beyond Makrān into the land of Qandābīl and penetrated deep into the mountains of al-Qīqān. He took many captives, but they waylaid him at a mountain pass, and al-Ḥārith and those with him were killed.

The Year 37

The Battle of Ṣiffīn. In that year the Battle of Ṣiffīn took place on Wednesday, with seven days elapsed in Ṣafar of the year thirty-seven. The truce was on the night of Saturday, with ten days elapsed in Ṣafar. In that year ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir and Hāshim ibn ʿUtba were killed, and the two arbiters, Abū Mūsā

The departure of Shabath ibn Ribʿī. Abū ʿUbayda said: “In that year the people of Ḥarūrāʾ went out, twenty thousand strong, with Shabath ibn Ribʿī over them. ʿAlī came to them and argued with them, and they returned.” Al-Muʿtamir ibn Sulaymān said: I heard my father relate from Anas, who said: Shabath ibn Ribʿī said, “I was the first to establish the Ḥarūriyya.” A man said, “There is nothing in that for you to boast about.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib led the pilgrimage.

The Year 38

The killing of Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr. In that year ʿAlī appointed al-Ashtar over Egypt, but he died at al-Qulzum before reaching it. ʿAlī then appointed Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq. ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ marched against him, and they fought. Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr was defeated. He entered a ruin in which there was a dead donkey, entered its belly, and was burned inside the donkey. It is also said that Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj killed him in battle; and it is also said that he was brought to ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, who executed him.

Ghundar related to us; he said: Shuʿba ibn Dīnār related to us, saying: Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr was brought as a captive. He said, “Do you have a covenant with you? Do you have a pledge from anyone?” He said: He ordered that he be killed, and he was killed.

Detailed account of the report of Ṣiffīn

Abū al-Ḥasan related to us, from Maslama ibn Muḥārib, from Ḥarb ibn Khālid ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya, who said: “Muʿāwiya departed from Syria for Ṣiffīn with seventy thousand.” He said: “I asked Zayd ibn ʿAlī ibn Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, ‘How many were with ʿAlī?’ He said, ‘One hundred thousand.’” Abū al-Ḥasan related to us, from Abū al-Wazīr, from Ibn Isḥāq and Ismāʿīl ibn Mujālid, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: “ʿAlī marched with fifty thousand.”

Abū al-Ḥasan, from Ḥabbāb ibn Mūsā, from Jābir, from Abū al-Ḥamrāʾ, who said: “ʿAlī was with ninety thousand.” Muʿāwiya arrived first and encamped by the Euphrates. ʿAlī and his companions came, and they were denied water. ʿAlī sent al-Ashʿath ibn Qays with two thousand; guarding the water for Muʿāwiya was Abū al-Aʿwar al-Sulamī with five thousand. They fought fiercely, and al-Ashʿath gained control of the water.

Abū Nuʿaym related to us; he said: Mūsā ibn Qays related to us; he said: I heard Ḥujr ibn ʿAnbas say: “ʿAlī was kept from the water, so he said, ‘Send for al-Ashʿath ibn Qays.’ He drove them away from the water.” Then the people met on Wednesday, with seven nights remaining in Ṣafar of the year thirty-seven. ʿAlī’s standard was with Hāshim ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Waqqāṣ. On ʿAlī’s left wing were Rabīʿa, with Ibn ʿAbbās over them; and on ʿAlī’s right wing were the Yemenis, with al-Ashʿath ibn Qays over them. ʿAlī was in the center among Muḍar of Baṣra and Kūfa. Muʿāwiya’s standard was with al-Mukhāriq ibn al-Ṣabāḥ al-Kilāʿī. On Muʿāwiya’s left wing were Muḍar, with Dhū al-Kalāʿ over them; on his right wing were the Yemenis. Muʿāwiya was among the Shuhbāʾ, the men with helmets and armor.

Abū Ghassān related to us; he said: ʿAbd al-Salām ibn Ḥarb related to us, from Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Jaʿfar

—I think he was Ibn Abī al-Mughīra—from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abzā, from his father, who said: “We were present with ʿAlī, eight hundred of us. They fought on Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, and the night of Saturday. Then the codices were raised and they called for peace. They parted with seventy thousand dead: forty-five thousand from the people of Syria and twenty-five thousand from the people of Iraq.” It is also said that the dead numbered sixty thousand.

ʿAbd al-Aʿlā related to us, from Hishām, from Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn, who said: “They parted with seventy thousand dead, counted with reeds.”

Among those killed with Muʿāwiya were Dhū al-Kalāʿ, Ḥawshab, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥaḍramī, Ḥābis ibn Saʿd al-Ṭāʾī, and ʿUrwa ibn Dāwūd al-Dimashqī, along with a great many others.

Among ʿAlī’s companions who were killed were ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir, Hāshim ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Waqqāṣ, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Budayl ibn Warqāʾ al-Khuzāʿī, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Kaʿb al-Murādī, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Kilda al-Jumaḥī, along with a great many others.

He said: Yaḥyā ibn Arqam related to us, from Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, from his father, from Ḥabīb ibn Abī Thābit, who said: “ʿAlī’s banner was with Hāshim ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Waqqāṣ; over the cavalry was ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir, and over the infantry ʿAbd Allāh ibn Budayl. Over the right wing was al-Ashʿath ibn Qays, and over the left wing ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās. Over the right-wing infantry was Sulaymān ibn Ṣurad al-Khuzāʿī, and over the left-wing infantry al-Ḥārith ibn Murra al-ʿAbdī.
