The center was Muḍar of Baṣra and Kūfa; the right wing was the Yemenis, and the left wing Rabīʿa. Over Quraysh, Asad, and Kināna was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib; over Kinda, Ḥujr ibn ʿAdī; over Bakr of Baṣra, Ḥuḍayn ibn al-Mundhir; over Tamīm of Baṣra, al-Aḥnaf ibn Qays; over Khuzāʿa, ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥamiq; over Bakr of Kūfa, Nuʿaym ibn Hubayra; and over Saʿd and al-Ribāb

Jāriya ibn Qudāma; over Bajīla, Rifāʿa ibn Shaddād; over the people of Kūfa, Ruwaym ibn al-Ḥārith; over ʿAmr and Ḥanẓala of Baṣra, Aʿyan ibn Ḍubayʿa al-Mujāshiʿī; over Quḍāʿa and Ṭayyiʾ, ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim; over Lahāzim of Kūfa, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥajal al-ʿIjlī; over Tamīm of Kūfa, Muḥammad ibn ʿUṭārid; over the Azd of Yemen, Jundub ibn Zuhayr; over ʿAmr of Kūfa and its Ḥanẓala, Shabath ibn Ribʿī; over Hamdān, Saʿīd ibn Qays; over Lahāzim of Baṣra, Ḥurayth ibn Jābir al-Ḥanafī; over Saʿd of Kūfa and its Rabāb, al-Ṭufayl ibn Shabramah; over Madhḥij, al-Ashtar ibn al-Ḥārith; over ʿAbd al-Qays of Kūfa, Ṣaʿṣaʿa ibn Ṣūḥān; over Qays of Kūfa, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṭufayl al-Kinānī; over ʿAbd al-Qays of Baṣra, ʿAmr ibn Jablah, the brother of Ḥakīm ibn Jablah; over Quraysh of Baṣra, al-Ḥārith ibn Nawfal al-Hāshimī; over Qays of Baṣra, Qubayṣa ibn Shaddād al-Hilālī.

Muʿāwiya’s standard was with ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Khālid ibn al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīra al-Makhzūmī. Over the cavalry was ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, and over the infantry Muslim ibn ʿUqba al-Murrī. Over the right wing was ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, and over the left wing Ḥabīb ibn Maslama al-Fihrī. Over the people of Ḥimṣ on the right wing was Dhū al-Kalāʿ; over the people of Qinnasrīn on the right wing, Zufar ibn al-Ḥārith; over the people of Jordan on the left wing, Abū al-Aʿwar al-Sulamī; over the people of Palestine on the left wing, Maslama ibn Mukhallad; over the infantry of the people of Damascus, Busr ibn Arṭāt; over the infantry of the people of Ḥimṣ, Ḥawshab Dhū Ẓulm; over the infantry of the people of Qinnasrīn, Ṭarīf ibn al-Ḥaṣḥāṣ al-Hilālī; and over the infantry of the people of Jordan

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Qaysī; over the infantry of the people of Palestine, al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Azdī; over all the right-wing infantry, Ḥābis ibn Saʿd al-Ṭāʾī; over the left-wing infantry, Bilāl ibn Abī Hurayra al-Dawsī; over Qays of Damascus, Ḥassān ibn Baḥdal al-Kalbī; over Quḍāʿa of Egypt, ʿAbbād ibn Yazīd al-Kalbī; over Kinda of Damascus, Ibn Ḥuwayy al-Saksakī; over Kinda of Ḥimṣ, Yazīd ibn Ḥubayra al-Sakūnī; over the Ḥaḍramīs and Ḥimyarīs, Ibn ʿAfīf; over Quḍāʿa of Jordan, Ḥubaysh ibn Dalja; over Kināna of Palestine, Sharīk al-Kinānī; over Madhḥij of Jordan, Mukhāriq ibn al-Ḥārith al-Zubaydī; over Judhām of Palestine and its Lakhm, Nātil ibn Qays al-Judhāmī; over Hamdān of Jordan, Ḥamza ibn Mālik; over Khathʿam and its allies, so-and-so ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Khathʿamī; and over Ghassān of Jordan, Yazīd ibn Abī al-Nams.

Abū ʿAbd Allāh related to us, from Ḥammād ibn Zayd, from Hishām ibn Ḥassān, from Ibn Sīrīn, who said: “The dead at Ṣiffīn numbered seventy thousand.”

Abū ʿUbayda related to us, from Ḥammād ibn Salama, from ʿAlī ibn Zayd, from al-Ḥasan, that Jundub was with ʿAlī at Ṣiffīn.

Abū ʿAbd Allāh related to us, from Sharīk, from Manṣūr, who said: “I asked Ibrāhīm, ‘Was ʿAlqama present with ʿAlī at Ṣiffīn?’ He said, ‘Yes; his sword was stained [with blood], and his brother Ubayy ibn Qays was killed.’”

One who heard Shuʿba related to us, saying: “I asked al-Ḥakam, ‘Was Abū Ayyūb present at Ṣiffīn?’ He said, ‘No, but he was present at al-Nahrawān.’”

Abū Ghassān related to us; he said: ʿAbd al-Salām ibn Ḥarb related to us, from Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Jaʿfar—I think he was Ibn Abī al-Mughīra—from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abzā, from his father, who said: “Eight hundred of us, among those who had pledged the Pledge of Riḍwān, were present with ʿAlī. Sixty-three of us were killed, among them ʿAmmār ibn Yāsir.” In that year Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥaḍramī to Baṣra to take it.

In it, Ziyād was Ibn ʿAbbās’s deputy. Ibn al-Ḥaḍramī settled among Banū Tamīm, while Ziyād moved to the Azd. ʿAlī stationed Ṣabra ibn Shaymān al-Ḥadānī there. Ziyād wrote to ʿAlī informing him of this, and ʿAlī sent Aʿyan ibn Ḍubayʿa al-Mujāshiʿī, who was killed treacherously on his bed. ʿAlī then sent Jāriya ibn Qudāma al-Saʿdī, who besieged al-Ḥaḍramī in the house of Sunbayl and then set it ablaze around him.

The Battle of Nahrawān: In it was the Battle of Nahrawān against the Khārijites under ʿAbd Allāh ibn Wahb al-Rāsibī. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Wahb and his companions were killed, except for a few of them. On ʿAlī’s right wing was Qays ibn Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda, and on his left wing was Ḥujr ibn al-Adbar al-Kindī.

Abū ʿUbayda said: The battle took place in Shaʿbān of the year thirty-eight. On the Khārijites’ right wing was Ḥurqūṣ ibn Zuhayr al-Saʿdī, and on their left wing were Shabīb ibn Bajra al-Ashjaʿī together with Shurayḥ ibn Awfā al-ʿAbsī. They fought fiercely. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Wahb was killed. Abū Bilāl Mirdās ibn Udayya was carried from the battlefield wounded and survived; Shabīb ibn Bajra, al-Mustawrid ibn ʿUlfa, al-Birk, the companion of Muʿāwiya, and Wardān ibn Mujmaʿ al-ʿUklī also survived. ʿUlfa, Abū al-Mustawrid, was killed. Among ʿAlī’s companions, Yazīd ibn Nuwayra al-Anṣārī and Abū ʿĀmir ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir al-Juhanī were killed. Abū Nuʿaym said: Mūsā ibn Qays narrated to us, from Salama ibn Kuhayl, from Zayd ibn Wahb, who said: ʿAlī met them, and they fought and were killed. Twelve or thirteen men from among ʿAlī’s companions were killed. In it, the Khārijites killed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khabbāb ibn al-Aratt; Masʿar ibn Fadakī was their commander.

In it, Sahl ibn Ḥunayf died in Kūfa, and ʿAlī and Ṣuhayb ibn Sinān prayed over him. Qutham ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib led the pilgrimage.

The Year 39

In it, Abū Maryam revolted in the vicinity of the Euphrates, and ʿAlī sent Yaḥyā ibn Hāniʾ. Then ʿAlī marched and killed Abū Maryam. Abū ʿUbayda mentioned this. Abū ʿUbayda said: Then al-Mustawrid ibn ʿUlfa, one of Banū ʿAdī, revolted. Maʿqil ibn Qays al-Riyāḥī met him, and each of them killed the other in single combat. This was in the year thirty-nine. In it, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān sent Yazīd ibn Shajara al-Rahāwī to lead the pilgrimage for the people, but Qutham ibn ʿAbbās disputed it with him. Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī and others acted as arbiters between them, and they reached an agreement that Shayba ibn ʿUthmān would lead the pilgrimage and lead the people in prayer.

The Year 40

In it, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān sent Bisr ibn Arṭāʾ, one of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy, to Yemen, where ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was in charge. ʿUbayd Allāh withdrew, and Bisr remained in charge there. ʿAlī sent Jāriya ibn Qudāma al-Saʿdī, so Bisr fled and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās returned. He remained in charge there until ʿAlī ﵀ was killed. In it, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵀ was killed on Friday morning, with seven nights remaining in the month of Ramaḍān. There was disagreement about his age.

ʿAlī narrated to us, from ʿAbd al-Razzāq, from Maʿmar, from Qatāda, from al-Ḥasan, that ʿAlī became Muslim when he was fifteen years old. Ḥātim ibn Muslim narrated to me, from someone who informed him, from al-Shaʿbī, who said: ʿAlī was killed when he was fifty-eight years old. Yaḥyā narrated to us, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿUmrān, from Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Muʾammal al-Makhzūmī, who said: ʿAlī was born in Mecca, in the ravine of Banū Hāshim; he was killed in Kūfa, and his son al-Ḥasan prayed over him.

The pledge of allegiance to al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī: ʿAlī’s rule lasted four years, nine months, and six days; it is also said three days, and it is also said fourteen days. Then the pledge of allegiance was given to al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, whose mother was Fāṭima, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ. In it, ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās was born on the night ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib was killed, on the morning of which he was killed. In it, al-Ashʿath ibn Qays and Muʿayqīb ibn Abī Fāṭima died, and al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba led the pilgrimage.

The list of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib’s officials:

Khurāsān: He sent ʿAwn ibn Jaʿda al-Makhzūmī there, but they turned him back. He sent Khulayd ibn Qurra al-Tamīmī. Sijistān: After the Battle of the Camel had ended, Ḥaska ibn ʿAttāb al-Ḥabṭī and ʿImrān ibn al-Fuḍayl al-Burjumī set out with bands of Arab outlaws. They came to Zāliq and seized women and booty. The governor of Zaranj made peace with them, and they entered it. ʿAlī sent ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jarw al-Ṭāʾī, but Ḥaska killed him. ʿAlī wrote to Ibn ʿAbbās: “Send a man to Sijistān.” So he sent Rubʿī ibn Kaʾs al-ʿAnbarī, who prevailed over Ḥaska and ʿImrān. He remained there until ʿAlī was killed and Muʿāwiya was given the pledge of allegiance.

Sind: During ʿAlī’s days, al-Ḥārith ibn Murra al-ʿAbdī assembled a force and marched to the lands of Makrān. He prevailed and took booty. People came to him from every direction, so the inhabitants of that frontier region assembled an army against him and killed those who were with him, except for a small band. That frontier was not raided again until the days of Muʿāwiya. Bahrain: Among ʿAlī’s officials there were ʿUmar ibn Abī Salama, Qudāma ibn al-ʿAjlān, and al-Nuʿmān ibn al-ʿAjlān al-Anṣārī. Yemen: ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās was in charge there. Muʿāwiya sent Bisr ibn Arṭāʾ, so ʿUbayd Allāh withdrew and Bisr remained in charge. ʿAlī sent Jāriya ibn Qudāma, so Bisr fled and ʿUbayd Allāh returned. He remained there until ʿAlī ﵀ was killed. Al-Jazīra: al-Ashtar, Mālik.

The judiciary: As judge of Baṣra, Ibn ʿAbbās, during ʿAlī’s caliphate, appointed Abū al-Aswad al-Duʾalī. It is also said that al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Hilālī judged there; it is also said that it was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Faḍāla al-Laythī. Kūfa: He confirmed Shurayḥ in office there, then dismissed him and appointed Muḥammad ibn Zayd ibn Khulayda al-Shaybānī for several months. Then he dismissed him and reinstated Shurayḥ, who remained until ʿAlī was killed.

The police: Maʿqil ibn Qays al-Riyāḥī and Mālik ibn Khubayb al-Yarbūʿī; and chief of the Khamīs corps was al-Aṣbagh ibn Nubāta al-Mujāshiʿī.

His scribes: Saʿīd ibn Nimrān al-Hamdānī and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Rāfiʿ.

His chamberlain was Qanbar Abū Yazīd, his client. ʿAlī was born in Mecca, in the ravine of Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib; he was killed in Kūfa, his son al-Ḥasan prayed over him, and he was buried in the courtyard of Kūfa. It is also said that he was buried at Najaf al-Ḥīra.

Mecca: ʿAlī removed Khālid ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Hishām ibn al-Mughīra al-Makhzūmī from its governorship and appointed Abū Qatāda al-Anṣārī over it. Then he removed him and appointed Qutham ibn ʿAbbās, who remained its governor until ʿAlī was killed. ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās was in charge of Yemen until ʿAlī was killed.

Medina: When ʿAlī set out for Baṣra, Sahl ibn Ḥunayf was in charge of Medina. Then he removed him and appointed Tammām ibn ʿAbbās. Then he removed him and appointed Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī. Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī departed and appointed a man from the Anṣār as his deputy, who remained until ʿAlī ﵀ was killed.

Egypt: He appointed Muḥammad ibn Abī Ḥudhayfa ibn ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿa over Egypt, then removed him and appointed Qays ibn Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda. Then he removed him and appointed al-Ashtar Mālik ibn al-Ḥārith al-Nakhaʿī, who died before reaching it. He appointed Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr, who was killed there, and ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ took control of Egypt.

Baṣra: He appointed ʿUthmān ibn Ḥunayf al-Anṣārī over Baṣra, but Ṭalḥa and al-Zubayr expelled him. Then ʿAlī arrived, and when he departed from Baṣra, he appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās. Ibn ʿAbbās departed and appointed Ziyād as his deputy. Muʿāwiya sent ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥaḍramī, whose accounts we have already written. Then Ibn ʿAbbās returned to Baṣra, then departed for the Ḥijāz and appointed Abū al-Aswad al-Duʾalī, who remained in charge there until ʿAlī was killed.

Kūfa: He appointed Qurẓa ibn Kaʿb al-Anṣārī over Kūfa. Then ʿAlī arrived, and when he departed for Ṣiffīn, he appointed Abū Masʿūd al-Badrī. Then ʿAlī returned and, when he set out for Nahrawān, appointed Hāniʾ ibn Hawdha al-Nakhaʿī as his deputy. He remained in Kūfa until ʿAlī was killed. During the days of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, Muʿādh ibn ʿAfrāʾ, Abū Masʿūd, Kaʿb ibn Mālik, Abū Rāfiʿ, Ḥassān ibn Thābit, and Muʿayqīb died.

Chapter 7

The caliphate of Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān

The Year 41

Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān. The Year of Unity: In it was the Year of Unity. Al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Muʿāwiya came together at Maskin, in the Sawād and in the vicinity of al-Anbār. They made peace, and al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī handed over authority to Muʿāwiya. This was in the month of Rabīʿ II or in Jumādā I of the year forty-one.

Al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī’s rule lasted seven months and seven days. He confirmed his father’s officials. Al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba fabricated a covenant in al-Ḥasan’s name. He led the pilgrimage in the year forty. Al-Ḥasan died in Medina in the year forty-nine, and Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ, then governor of Medina, prayed over him. Al-Ḥasan died at the age of forty-six. Al-Ḥasan was born in Medina in the year three. His mother was Fāṭima, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ. The people united under Muʿāwiya. His mother was Hind, daughter of ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Manāf.

The revolt of Ibn Abī al-Ḥawsāʾ against Muʿāwiya: He entered Kūfa, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī al-Ḥawsāʾ rose against him at al-Nukhayla. Muʿāwiya sent Khālid ibn ʿUrfuṭa al-ʿUdhrī, ally of Banū Zuhra, against him with a force of people from Kūfa. He killed Ibn Abī al-Ḥawsāʾ in Jumādā of the year forty-one, according to what Abū ʿUbayda and Abū al-Ḥasan mentioned.

The revolt of Ḥawthara ibn Dhurāʿ: Abū ʿUbayda and Abū al-Ḥasan said: When Ibn Abī al-Ḥawsāʾ was killed, Ḥawthara ibn Dhurāʿ revolted. Muʿāwiya dispatched ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwf ibn Aḥmar against him with a thousand men, and Ḥawthara was killed in Jumādā II of the year forty-one.

The revolt of Sahm and al-Khaṭīm: Abū ʿUbayda and Abū al-Ḥasan said: In it, Sahm ibn Ghālib al-Hujaymī revolted, with al-Khaṭīm al-Bāhilī, whose name was Ziyād ibn Mālik, in the vicinity of the bridge of Baṣra. They killed ʿUbāda ibn Qurṣ al-Laythī, a Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir went out against them. Sahm and al-Khaṭīm requested safe conduct, and he granted them safe conduct. A number of their companions were killed. Abū ʿUbayda said: Sahm ibn Ghālib also killed Saʿd, the client of Qudāma ibn Maẓʿūn.

The appointment of ʿUqba ibn Nāfiʿ over Ifrīqiya: In it, ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, while governor of Egypt, appointed ʿUqba ibn Nāfiʿ al-Fihrī over Ifrīqiya. He reached Lūbiya and Marāqiya, and they submitted; then they rebelled, so he raided them in that same year, killing and taking captives. In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz was appointed over Baṣra, and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam over Medina.

And ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Khālid ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Hishām ibn al-Mughīra [was appointed over] Mecca. It is said, rather, al-Ḥārith ibn Khālid ibn Hishām; then he combined the two [appointments], and al-Ṭāʾif was assigned to Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam. In it, Muʿāwiya made peace with the Romans, and ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān ibn Ḥarb led the pilgrimage. In it, al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf was born.

The Year 42

In it, Ibn ʿĀmir sent ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Samura to Sijistān. With him on that expedition were al-Ḥasan ibn Abī al-Ḥasan, al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra, and Qaṭarī ibn al-Fujāʾa. He conquered Zaranj and districts among the districts of Sijistān. In it, ʿUqba ibn Nāfiʿ al-Fihrī raided Ifriqiya, conquered Ghadāmis, and killed and took captives. In it, Ibn ʿĀmir appointed Rāshid ibn ʿAmr al-Judaydī over the frontier of India. Abū Khālid said: Abū al-Khaṭṭāb said: Rāshid remained there, launched raids, and advanced deep into the land of Sind. In it, Ḥabīb ibn Maslama al-Fihrī died in the land of Armenia; Ṣafwān ibn Umayya, ʿUthmān ibn Ṭalḥa, and Rukāna ibn ʿAbd Yazīd died at the beginning of Muʿāwiya’s caliphate; and Abū Burda ibn Niyār and Rifāʿa ibn Rāfiʿ died at the beginning of Muʿāwiya’s rule. ʿAnbasa ibn Abī Sufyān ibn Ḥarb led the pilgrimage.

The Year 43

In it, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Samura conquered al-Rukhkhaj and Zābulistān, in the land of Sijistān.

In it, ʿUqba ibn Nāfiʿ al-Fihrī raided and conquered districts in the land of the Blacks. He also conquered Waddān, which is within the region of Barqa; all of these are in the land of Ifriqiya. In it, Bisr ibn Arṭāʾa wintered in Roman territory. In it, Muʿāwiya appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār al-ʿAbdī over the land of Makrān. In it, ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ died in Egypt on the day of the Feast of Breaking the Fast; it is said that this was in the year forty-two. Muḥammad ibn Maslama al-Anṣārī and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salām also died. Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam led the pilgrimage.

The Year 44

The conquest of Kabul. In it, Ibn ʿĀmir conquered Kabul. Abū Qatāda al-ʿAdawī was killed at Kabul; it is said that the one who was killed was Abū Rifāʿa al-ʿAdawī. Among those taken captive at Kabul were Makḥūl al-Shāmī, Sālim ibn ʿAjlān al-Afṭas, Kaysān Abū Ayyūb ibn Abī Tamīma al-Sakhtiyānī, and among them Nāfiʿ, the client of Ibn ʿUmar, and Mihrān Abū Ḥumayd al-Ṭawīl. In it, al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra raided the land of India. He traveled to Qandābīl, then turned toward Batta and al-Ahwar, both on the slope of Mount Kabul. An enemy met them, but God routed them; the Muslims took abundant spoils and returned safely.

In it, the events involving Muʿāwiya and Ziyād took place—the events that took place. In it, Ibn ʿĀmir went as a delegation to Muʿāwiya and appointed Qays ibn al-Haytham al-Sulamī as his deputy over Baṣra. Ibn al-Kalbī said: In it, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Khālid wintered in Roman territory. Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage.

The Year 45

In it, Muʿāwiya removed Ibn ʿĀmir from Baṣra and appointed al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAmr al-Azdī. He arrived at the beginning of the year; then Muʿāwiya dismissed him and appointed Ziyād. Ziyād arrived in Baṣra in the month of Rabīʿ and killed Sahm ibn Ghālib al-Hujaymī, who had rebelled in the vicinity of the bridge of Baṣra, and crucified him. In it, Ibn ʿĀmir sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār al-ʿAbdī, who conquered al-Qīqān and obtained spoils, including horses from there. The Qīqānī Barādhīn horses descend from those horses. Then he returned and appointed Ḥazzāz ibn Karāz al-ʿAbdī as his deputy. He came to Muʿāwiya, who sent him back to his post and dismissed Ibn ʿĀmir. In it, Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj raided Ifriqiya. He encamped at a mountain, where rain fell on him, and it was therefore called the Mountain of the Rained-On. Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam led the pilgrimage. In it, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Khālid ibn al-Walīd also wintered in Roman territory. In it, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān sent Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj on an expedition. He reached Muḥṣan, took some captives, but conquered neither a city nor a fortress; then he withdrew. In it, Zayd ibn Thābit and Salama ibn Salāma ibn Waqsh died. Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam led the pilgrimage.

The Year 46

In it, Muʿāwiya removed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Samura from Sijistān and appointed al-Rabīʿ ibn Ziyād ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī over it. The Turks surged forth, and the Kabul Shah gathered forces and advanced against the Muslims. They drove the Muslims who were in Kabul out of it, took control of Zābulistān and al-Rukhkhaj, and advanced as far as Bust. Al-Rabīʿ ibn Ziyād met them at Bust. God routed Rutbīl, and al-Rabīʿ pursued him to al-Rukhkhaj. Ibn al-Kalbī said: In it, Mālik ibn ʿAbd Allāh Abū Ḥakīm wintered in Roman territory; it is said instead that Mālik ibn Hubayra al-Fazārī wintered there. ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān ibn Ḥarb led the pilgrimage.

The Year 47

In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār al-ʿAbdī raided al-Qīqān. The Turks mustered against him and killed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār and the bulk of that army. The polytheists took control of the land of al-Qīqān. Ibn al-Kalbī said: In it, Mālik ibn Hubayra wintered in Roman territory, and Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Qaynī wintered in Antioch. In it, Ruwayfiʿ ibn Thābit al-Anṣārī raided from Antābulus, entered Ifriqiya, then returned that same year. ʿAnbasa ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage.

The Year 48

In it, Muʿāwiya removed Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam from Medina and appointed Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. Abū al-Yaqẓān. When ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār was killed, Muʿāwiya wrote to Ziyād: “Find a man suited to the frontier of India.” He sent him, and Ziyād sent Sinān ibn Salama ibn Muḥbiq al-Hudhalī. Ibn al-Kalbī said: In it, Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Qaynī also wintered in Antioch. Some said: Ibn Makraz, from Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ led the pilgrimage.

The Year 49

In it, Ziyād in Baṣra killed al-Khaṭīm al-Bāhilī, the Kharijite rebel, one of Banū Wāʾil; his name was Ziyād ibn Mālik. A descendant of Saʿīd ibn Salm related to me from his father, who said: “Qutayba ibn Muslim was born on the day al-Khaṭīm was killed, and that was in the year forty-nine.”

The revolt of Shabīb ibn Bajra. During the governorship of al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba over Kūfa, Shabīb ibn Bajra al-Ashjaʿī rebelled. Al-Mughīra sent Kathīr ibn Shihāb al-Ḥārithī against him, and he killed him in Azerbaijan. Abū ʿUbayda said: “Shabīb ibn Bajra, who had been among those present at Nahrawān, rebelled in Kūfa against al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba at Dār al-Rizq, and he was killed.” Ibn al-Kalbī said: In it, Mālik ibn Hubayra wintered in Roman territory; it is said instead that Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd al-Anṣārī wintered there, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿada wintered on the mainland. Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ led the pilgrimage. In it, al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib died ﵀.

The Year 50

In it, al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba died in Kūfa in Shaʿbān. His son ʿUrwa was appointed as his successor; it is said that Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh was appointed instead. Muʿāwiya appointed Ziyād over Kūfa together with Baṣra and placed Iraq under his authority. Ziyād removed al-Rabīʿ ibn Ziyād al-Ḥārithī from Sijistān and appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Bakra over it. He ordered him to kill the hārbadhs and extinguish the fires between him and Sijistān.

The founding of al-Qayrawān. In it, Muʿāwiya sent ʿUqba ibn Nāfiʿ to Ifriqiya. He laid out al-Qayrawān and stayed there for three years.

ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn ʿAbd al-Aʿlā related to us from Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAlqama, from Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥāṭib, who said: “When ʿUqba ibn Nāfiʿ conquered Ifriqiya, he stood at al-Qayrawān and said: ‘O people of the valley, we are settling here, God willing, so depart!’ He said this three times. He said: ‘We saw no stone or tree from beneath which some creature did not emerge, until they descended to the bottom of the valley. Then he said: “Alight, in the name of God.”’”

The raid into Ifriqiya and the conquest of Jalūlāʾ in the Maghrib. In it, Maslama ibn Mukhallad, who was governor in Egypt, sent Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj on an expedition. He took captives and returned safely. Abū Khālid said: “Maslama ibn Mukhallad sent Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj on an expedition.” Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān wrote to Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam, his governor over Medina: “Send ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān at the head of Medina’s expedition to the lands of the Maghrib.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Marwān arrived and joined Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj in Ifriqiya. Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj dispatched him with cavalry to Jalūlāʾ in the land of the Maghrib. He besieged its inhabitants and set up mangonels against it. Muʿāwiya ibn Ḥudayj wrote to him to withdraw, so he withdrew. He had already weakened the wall, and the wall collapsed. He turned back with the people, retreating. He killed the combatants and took the offspring captive. Ibn Ḥudayj sent an army, and they encamped at a city. Its people asked him for a peace agreement, so he made peace with them and returned in the year fifty-one. In it, Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya raided Roman territory, and Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī was with him. In it, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān summoned the people of Syria to pledge allegiance to his son Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. They responded and pledged allegiance to Yazīd. In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir wintered in Roman territory. In it, Rāshid ibn ʿAmr al-Judaydī was killed in India. Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya led the pilgrimage after returning from Roman territory. In it, Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī died in Roman territory; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Samura died, and Ziyād performed the funeral prayer over him; Abū Mūsā died in Kūfa; al-Ḥakam ibn ʿAmr al-Ghifārī died in Khurāsān; Rāshid ibn ʿAmr al-Judaydī died in the land of India; and al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba died. In it, al-Rabīʿ ibn Ziyād al-Ḥārithī arrived in Khurāsān on Ziyād’s behalf. He raided Balkh, which had closed its gates after al-Aḥnaf, and its people made peace with al-Rabīʿ. Then al-Rabīʿ raided Qūhistān and conquered it by force.

Iraq entrusted to Ziyād.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām, from his father, from his grandfather, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father, both said: “And [Iraq] was assigned…”

…Iraq to Ziyād in the year fifty. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥiṣn, one of Banū Thaʿlaba ibn Yarbuʿ, was in charge of his security force in Baṣra; Shaddād ibn al-Haytham al-Hilālī was in charge of his security force in Kūfa; Zādhān Farrūkh was the tax clerk; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakra and Jubayr ibn Ḥayya were the letter writers; and Mihrān, his client, was his chamberlain. He died aged fifty-three. In it, ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥamq al-Khuzāʿī was killed at Mosul. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿUthmān al-Thaqafī, the uncle of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Umm al-Ḥakam, killed him. Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Ibn Lahīʿa, who said: Bukayr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ashajj related to me from Sulaymān ibn Yasār, who said: “We raided Ifriqiya with Ibn Ḥudayj, and he allotted us half after the fifth.”

The raid on al-Qīqān. In it, Ziyād appointed Sinān ibn Salama ibn al-Muḥbiq over the frontier of India after Rāshid was killed.

Abū al-Yamān al-Nabbāl related to us. He said: “We raided al-Qīqān with Sinān, and a large number of the enemy came upon us. Sinān said: ‘Rejoice, for you have one of two things: Paradise or booty.’ Then he took seven stones and stood facing the enemy. He said: ‘When you see me attack, then attack.’ When the sun was at the middle of the sky, he threw a stone into the faces of the enemy and cried ‘God is greatest.’ Then he threw them one stone at a time until only the seventh remained. When the sun had moved from the middle of the sky, he threw the seventh, then said, ‘Ḥā Mīm. They shall not be helped,’ cried ‘God is greatest,’ and attacked. We attacked with him, and they turned their backs to us. We killed them and traveled four farsakhs, until we came upon people fortified in a fortress. They said: ‘By God, it was not you who killed us, and we killed only men whom we do not see among you.’”

Now on piebald horses, wearing white turbans. We said, “That is God’s help.” By God, we returned with only one man of ours wounded. We said to Sinān, “You stood your ground against the people until the sun had passed its zenith, then you fought them.” He said, “That is how the Messenger of God ﷺ used to do.” Muʿāwiya performed the pilgrimage in this year.

The Year 51

The killing of Ḥujr ibn ʿAdī. In it, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān killed Ḥujr ibn ʿAdī ibn al-Adbar, along with Muḥriz ibn Shihāb, Qubayṣa ibn Ḍubayʿa ibn Ḥarmala al-Qaysī, and Ṣayfī ibn Fusayl of Rabīʿa. In it, Kaʿb ibn ʿUjra died.

Taking the oath of allegiance for Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. In it, Muʿāwiya took the people’s oath of allegiance for Yazīd. Wahb ibn Jarīr ibn Ḥāzim reported to us. He said: My father reported to me: al-Nuʿmān ibn Rāshid reported to us, from al-Zuhrī, from Dhakwān, the client of ʿĀʾisha, who said: When Muʿāwiya resolved to take the oath of allegiance for his son Yazīd, he went on pilgrimage and arrived in Mecca with about a thousand men. When he drew near Medina, Ibn ʿUmar, Ibn al-Zubayr, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr went out. When Muʿāwiya arrived in Medina, he ascended the pulpit, praised God and extolled Him, then mentioned his son Yazīd and said, “Who is more entitled to this authority than he?” Then he departed and arrived in Mecca, completed his circumambulation, and entered his residence. He sent for Ibn ʿUmar, who gave the testimony of faith and said, “To proceed: Ibn ʿUmar, you used to tell me that you would not like to spend a dark night without an emir over you. I warn you against splitting the Muslims’ unity and striving to sow discord among them.” When he fell silent, Ibn ʿUmar spoke. He praised God and extolled Him, then said, “To proceed: Before you there were caliphs who had sons. Your son is no better than their sons, yet they did not see in their sons what you see in yours. Rather, they chose for the Muslims what they knew to be the best. You warn me against splitting the Muslims’ unity and striving to sow discord among them, but I would not do that.”

“I am only one of the Muslims, and when they agree on a matter, I am only one of them.” He said, “May God have mercy on you.” Ibn ʿUmar went out. He sent for ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr, who gave the testimony of faith and began to speak, but Muʿāwiya cut him off, saying, “By God, you would like us to entrust the matter of your son to God, but by God, we will not do so. By God, you will either return this matter to consultation among the Muslims, or we will take it up afresh against you.” Then he sprang up and stood.

Muʿāwiya said, “O God, suffice me against him as You will.” Then he said, “Wait, man. Do not bring the people of Syria down upon yourself, for I fear they may get ahead of me and take your life. Then tonight I will announce that you have pledged allegiance; after that, you may do as you see fit.” Then he sent for Ibn al-Zubayr and said, “Ibn al-Zubayr, you are a wily fox: whenever you leave one burrow, you enter another. You went to these two men, blew into their nostrils, and made them adopt a view other than their own.” Ibn al-Zubayr spoke and said, “If you have grown tired of the rule, then relinquish it and bring your son here, and let us pledge allegiance to him. Tell me: if we pledge allegiance to your son along with you, which of you are we to hear and which of you are we to obey? We will never pledge allegiance to you both.”

Then he stood up and Muʿāwiya went out. He ascended the pulpit, praised God and extolled Him, then said, “We have heard people’s reports and various allegations. They claim that Ibn ʿUmar, Ibn al-Zubayr, and Ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq have not pledged allegiance to Yazīd. They have heard and obeyed, and have pledged allegiance to him.” The people of Syria said, “No, by God! We will not be satisfied until they pledge allegiance in public; otherwise, we will strike off their heads.” He said, “Enough! Glory be to God! How quickly people speak ill of Quraysh. I do not want to hear this statement from anyone again after today.” Then he descended. The people said, “Ibn ʿUmar, Ibn al-Zubayr, and Ibn Abī Bakr have pledged allegiance,” while those men said, “No, by God, we have not pledged allegiance,” and the people said, “Indeed, you have.” Muʿāwiya departed and joined the people of Syria. Wahb reported to us: My father reported to me, from Ayyūb, from Nāfiʿ, who said: Muʿāwiya gave a sermon and mentioned Ibn ʿUmar, saying, “By God, he will pledge allegiance or I will kill him.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar went to his father, informed him, and traveled to Mecca for three days. When he informed him, Ibn ʿUmar wept. The news reached ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān, who went in to Ibn ʿUmar and said, “Did he give a sermon saying such-and-such about you?” He said, “Yes.” He asked, “What do you intend? Do you intend to fight him?”

He said, “Ibn Ṣafwān, patience is better than that.” Ibn Ṣafwān said, “By God, if he intends that, I will fight him.” Muʿāwiya arrived in Mecca and alighted at Dhū Ṭuwā. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān went out to meet him and said, “Are you the one who claims that you will kill Ibn ʿUmar if he does not pledge allegiance to your son?” He said, “Would I kill Ibn ʿUmar? By God, I would not kill him.”

Wahb ibn Jarīr said: Juwayriyya ibn Asmāʾ reported to me: I heard the elders of Medina relate that when Muʿāwiya was near Mecca, as he set out from Marr, he said to his chief of guard, “Let no one travel with me except someone I myself have mounted.” He went on alone until, when he was in the middle of al-Arāk, al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī met him. He stopped and said, “Welcome, son of the daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ the master of the young men of the Muslims. Bring a mount for Abū ʿAbd Allāh to ride.” A mule was brought, and al-Ḥusayn mounted it. Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr appeared. Muʿāwiya said, “Welcome, elder of Quraysh and its master, son of the Ṣiddīq of this community. Bring a mount for Abū Muḥammad.” A mule was brought, and he rode it. Then Ibn ʿUmar appeared. Muʿāwiya said, “Welcome, companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ son of al-Fārūq and master of the Muslims.” He called for a mount for him, and Ibn ʿUmar rode it. Then Ibn al-Zubayr appeared. Muʿāwiya said to him, “Welcome, son of the ḥawārī of the Messenger of God ﷺ son of the Ṣiddīq and son of the Messenger of God’s aunt.” Then he called for a mount for him, and he rode it. Muʿāwiya proceeded among them, with no one riding alongside him except them, until he entered Mecca. They were the first to enter and the last to leave. No morning passed on earth without their receiving hospitality and honor. He did not mention to them anything about the matter at issue until he had completed his rites, his baggage had been loaded, and his journey to the Kaʿba was near. His camels were made to kneel, and some of the people turned to one another and said, “People, do not be deceived. By God, he has not done this out of love for you or to honor you; he has done it only for what he wants. Prepare an answer for him.” They turned to al-Ḥusayn and said, “You, Abū ʿAbd Allāh?” He said, “Among you is the elder and master of Quraysh; he has more right to speak.”

They said to ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr, “You, Abū Muḥammad?” He said, “I am not the one for that; among you is the companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ and the son of the master of the Muslims”—meaning Ibn ʿUmar. They said to Ibn ʿUmar, “You?” He said, “I am not your man, but appoint Ibn al-Zubayr to speak; he will suffice you.” They said, “You, Ibn al-Zubayr?” He said, “Yes, if you give me your pledges and covenants that you will not oppose me, I will deal with the man for you.” They said, “That is yours.”

Permission was granted, and they were admitted. Muʿāwiya spoke, praised God and extolled Him, then said, “You know my conduct among you, my keeping ties with your kin, my forbearance toward you, and my bearing with what comes from you. Yazīd, the son of the Commander of the Faithful, is your brother and cousin, and the one among you who wishes you best. I only wanted you to advance him under the name of the caliphate, while you would be the ones to remove and appoint, levy and distribute; he would have no part in any of that.” The people fell silent. He said, “Will you not answer me?” They stayed silent. He turned to Ibn al-Zubayr and said, “Speak, Ibn al-Zubayr, for by my life you are the spokesman of the people.” He said, “Yes, Commander of the Faithful. We give you a choice of three options; whichever you take is yours, as you wish.” Muʿāwiya said, “May your father be blessed; state them.” He said, “If you wish, do as the Messenger of God ﷺ did; if you wish, do as Abū Bakr did, for he was the best of this community after the Messenger of God ﷺ; or, if you wish, do as ʿUmar did, for he was the best of this community after Abū Bakr.” Muʿāwiya said, “May your father be blessed. What did they do?” He said, “The Messenger of God ﷺ died without making a bequest or appointing anyone as his successor, and the Muslims accepted Abū Bakr. If you wish, leave this matter until God decrees His judgment concerning it and the Muslims choose for themselves.” Muʿāwiya said, “There is no one among you today like Abū Bakr. Abū Bakr was a man for whom heads would be cut off, and I do not feel secure against division among you.” Ibn al-Zubayr said, “You are right, by God; you do not wish to leave us in charge of this community.” Muʿāwiya said, “Then do as Abū Bakr did.” Ibn al-Zubayr said, “May your father be blessed.” Muʿāwiya said, “What did Abū Bakr do?” Ibn al-Zubayr said, “He selected a man from a distant branch of Quraysh, who was neither of his father’s clan nor of his closest kin, and appointed him as successor. If you wish, choose whichever Qurashī man you wish, so long as he is not from Banū ʿAbd Shams, and be content with him.” Muʿāwiya said, “May your father be blessed. What is the third option?” He said, “Do as ʿUmar did.” Muʿāwiya said, “What did ʿUmar do?” He said, “What did ʿUmar do? He made the matter a council of six Qurashīs, none of whom was one of his sons, of his father’s clan, or of his close kin.” Muʿāwiya said, “Have you anything else?” He said, “No.” Muʿāwiya said, “And you?” They said, “We also [have nothing else].” He said, “Since that is so, I wanted to make this clear to you beforehand: a warner has discharged his duty. One of you might stand up before me and call me a liar in public, and I would bear it and pardon him. I am now making a statement: if I speak truly, I have truth on my side; if I lie, I bear the lie. I swear to you by God: if anyone among you answers me with a word while I am in this place, he will not get that word back before his head reaches me first. Let each man look to himself.” Then he summoned his chief of guard.

He said, “Place two of your guards at the head of each of these men. If anyone tries to answer me with a word during this speech, whether truthfully or falsely, let the two of them strike him with their swords.” Then he went out, and they went out with him. When he ascended the pulpit, he praised God and extolled Him, then said, “These men are the leaders and best of the Muslims. We will not act independently of them in any matter, nor decide anything without their counsel. They have agreed and pledged allegiance to Yazīd, the son of the Commander of the Faithful, as his successor. So pledge allegiance, in the name of God.” They pledged allegiance to him. Then he sat on his mount and departed. The people met them and said, “You claimed this and that, but when you were satisfied and pleased, you did it!” They said, “By God, we did not do that.” The people asked, “Then what prevented you from answering the man when he lied?” Then the people of Medina and the others pledged allegiance, and he departed for Syria.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mahdī reported to us: Sufyān reported to us, from Muḥammad ibn al-Munkadir, who said: Ibn ʿUmar said, when Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya was pledged allegiance to, “If it is good, we accept it; if it is a trial, we endure it.”

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān also reported to us: Abū ʿAwāna reported to us, from Dāwūd ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Awdī, from Ḥumayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, who said: We went in to a man among the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ when Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya had been appointed caliph. He said, “Do you say that Yazīd is not the best of Muḥammad’s community, nor the most learned in it, nor the most distinguished in honor?” We said, “Yes.” He said, “I say that too. But, by God, it is dearer to me that Muḥammad’s community be united than that it be divided. Consider a doorway that, if Muḥammad’s community entered it, would accommodate them: would it be unable to accommodate one man if he entered it?” We said, “No.” He said, “Consider if every man in Muḥammad’s community were to say, ‘I will not spill my brother’s blood or take his property.’ Would that be enough for them?” We said, “Yes.” He said, “That is what I am telling you.” Then he said, “The Messenger of God ﷺ said, ‘Modesty brings nothing but good.’”

Ismāʿīl ibn Sinān said: Ḥammād ibn Salama reported to us, from Yaʿlā ibn ʿAṭāʾ, from his paternal uncle, who said: I was with ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr when Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya sent him to ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr. He said: I heard ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr say to Ibn al-Zubayr, “You know that I found in the Book that you will be afflicted and will afflict, and will be called the caliph, though you are not the caliph; and I find that the caliph, Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya, is ashhal.”

Ibn ʿAwn reported to us, from Muḥammad, from ʿUqba ibn Aws al-Sadūsī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ, who said: “Muʿāwiya and his son ruled the Holy Land.” Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān performed the pilgrimage in the year 51. In it, Ḍafāla ibn ʿUbayd al-Anṣārī wintered in the land of the Romans, at sea. In it, ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm al-Anṣārī, Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bajalī, Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl, Maymūna, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ and Kaʿb ibn ʿUjra al-Anṣārī died. In it, al-Zuhrī was born.

The Year 52

In it, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Bakra made peace with Ratbīl over Kabul and its lands for one million dirhams. Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ performed the pilgrimage. In it, Busr ibn Arṭāʾa wintered in the land of the Romans with Sufyān ibn ʿAwf al-Azdī. In it, Abū Bakra died in Baṣra; Abū Barza al-Aslamī performed the prayer over him, as did ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn in Baṣra.

The Year 53

In it, Ziyād ibn Abī Sufyān died in Kūfa. He appointed Samura ibn Jundub as his successor in Baṣra and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd in Kūfa. Muʿāwiya then dismissed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid and appointed al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays al-Fihrī over it. He dismissed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Bakra from Sijistān and appointed ʿAbbād ibn Ziyād over it. ʿAbbād campaigned against Qandahār until he reached the House of Gold. The Indians assembled against him and fought him, but God routed the Indians. He remained in charge of Sijistān until Muʿāwiya died.

In it, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Umm al-Ḥakam wintered in the land of the Romans. In it, Muʿāwiya appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād over Khurāsān. In it, Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab was born. Ziyād died when he was fifty-three years old. It is also said that ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq died in it.

The revolt of Qarīb and Zaḥḥāf. During Ziyād’s governorship of Iraq, Qarīb and Zaḥḥāf—who were maternal cousins—rose in revolt. Wahb ibn Jarīr reported to us: My father reported to me, from Jarīr ibn Yazīd, who said: Qarīb and Zaḥḥāf went out during Ziyād’s governorship with seventy men. This was in the month of Ramaḍān. They came upon Banū Ḍubayʿa while they were in their mosque and encountered a man among them called Ruʾba ibn al-Mukhabbil, whom they killed. Wahb said: My father said: al-Zubayr ibn al-Khurayt reported to me, from Abū Labīd, that Ruʾba ibn al-Mukhabbil …

He said, on the evening of the night in which he was killed, about something that had happened to him: “If I am truthful, may God grant me martyrdom before I return to my house.” They encountered him that night before he reached his home and killed him. Then they came to the mosque of Banū Quṭayʿa.

Wahb said: My father also reported to me, from Qaṭan al-Azraq, from one of their elders, who said: We had no idea what was happening, and we were standing in the mosque, until they seized the mosque’s entrances, took control, and turned on the people in the mosque, killing them. The people leapt over the walls and rushed toward the entrances. A man climbed the minaret and began calling, “O cavalry of God, mount!” They climbed up to him and killed him. When no one remained in the mosque except the slain, and those who could fled, they went out and took control of the lane. A man of Banū Quṭayʿa came out of the door of his house and encountered the group as they reached his door. One of the men struck him with a sword as he put out his head, cutting his beard. He went back inside and shut the door. He had recently married. His wife came to him and bound him with a headscarf of hers dyed with brazilwood; the wound closed and healed. Qaṭan said: I saw him, and there was a crookedness in his mouth. He also said: That man related their story to me as well. He said: They went on, and a man from the quarter came toward them with a sword in his hand. Someone who was looking down at him from the roofs of the houses called, “So-and-so, beware of the Kharijites!” One of them said, “We are not the Kharijites; we are the guards.” The man felt safe and stood still until they reached him, then they killed him. They went on until they entered the Mosque of the Adzes and killed those inside it. Then they went on until they came out into the open space of Banū ʿAlī.

Wahb reported to us: My father reported to me: Jarīr ibn Yazīd reported to us that they reached the open space of Banū ʿAlī. The Banū ʿAlī came out against them; they were archers, and they shot at them with arrows until they had felled them all. He said: When morning came, we went out early while we were still young men, and there they were, crucified by the pit of the Saʿdīs. A girl came with a bowl containing dirhams. She looked at them and said, “Peace be upon you for what you patiently endured.”

“So excellent is the final abode!” She took [the money] and was crucified with them. He said: It is as though I can see her, with the dirhams, and no one approached her. He said: Qurayb and Ziḥāf were the sons of a maternal aunt.

Wahb reported to us: Ghassān ibn Muḍarr reported to me: Saʿīd ibn Yazīd reported to me: Qurayb and Ziḥāf went out while Ziyād was in Kūfa and Samura was in Baṣra. He said: They set out one night and alighted at the cemetery of Banū Yashkur. They had arranged with the Khārijites of Muḍar to assemble together at the cemetery of Banū Yashkur, but the Khārijites of Muḍar did not meet them. Some said to one another, “What if we disperse?” They said, “Each man among you is known by where he came from, and they will pursue you to your homes and kill you.” This was in the month of Ramaḍān, and they were seventy men. They set out and passed by Banū Ḍubayʿa. They came upon an old man from among them called Ḥabkān. When he saw them, he said, “Welcome, Abū al-Shaʿthā!” He thought that he was Ibn Ḥiṣn, and he was in charge of the police. They killed him. He said: They dispersed among the mosques of al-Azd. One group among them went to Banū ʿAlī, and another group came to the mosque of al-Maʿāwil. Sayf ibn Wahb came out against them with a shield and a spear, accompanied by some of his men. He would thrust at a man with a blow that lifted him off the ground, and he killed those who came to him. Young men from Banū ʿAlī and young men from Banū Rāsib came out against Qurayb and Ziḥāf with arrows. Qurayb said, “Is so-and-so among the people?”—meaning ʿAbd Allāh ibn Aws al-Ṭāḥī, the uncle of Ṭawq. Aws had previously been his opponent. They said, “Yes.” He said, “Then come out to single combat.” ʿAbd Allāh ibn Aws killed him and brought his head. He said: Ziyād came from Kūfa, with Masʿūd ibn ʿAmr alongside him. Ziyād spoke to him, reproaching him: “You did this and that.” Masʿūd said, “This is false.” Ziyād said, “Am I to call a Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ—a liar?” Then he said, “People of Ṭāḥiya, had you not struck among them, I would have sent you to prison.” He said: Banū ʿAlī claimed that they had killed them, and Banū Rāsib claimed that they had killed them. They said, “Let the arrows decide between us and them.” They found more arrows belonging to Banū ʿAlī among the dead.

Saʿīd ibn Yazīd said: Qurayb and Ziḥāf were the first of the Ḥarūriyya to rise up after the people of al-Nahrawān. He said: Qurayb was from Banū Iyād, and Ziḥāf was from Banū Ṭayyiʾ, and the two were sons of a maternal aunt.

Wahb said: I heard Ghassān ibn Muḍarr say: I heard Saʿīd ibn Yazīd say: Abū Bilāl said, “Qurayb—may God not bring him near! By God, if I were to fall from the sky to the earth, that would be dearer to me than doing as he did”—meaning the indiscriminate killing. Wahb said: My father said: Ziyād became severe in his campaign against the Ḥarūriyya after Qurayb and Ziḥāf, and he killed them. He ordered Samura to kill them, and Samura killed many of them.

Abū ʿUbayda said: Ziḥāf was a Ṭāʾī, and Qurayb was an Iyādī, from Iyād ibn Sūd. They went out and killed Rawʾaba of al-Mukhabbal; then they killed Jābir ibn Kaʿb al-Jadīdī, and struck Bakīr ibn Wāʾil al-Ṭāḥī on his arm, which he used to shield himself.

Abū ʿUbayda said: Ziyād rode out, and Shaqīq ibn Thawr, Ḥajjār ibn Abjar, and ʿAbbād ibn Ḥuṣayn al-Ḥabaṭī caught up with him. They wounded Shaqīq in the forehead and knocked Ḥajjār ibn Abjar down, but Shaqīq rescued him. They claim that Ziyād said to Banū ʿAlī, “You will receive no stipend from me if they escape.” The fighters fought them, and the dependants pelted them from atop the houses until they were killed. Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ performed the pilgrimage.

The Year 54

In it, Muʿāwiya dismissed Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ from Medina and appointed Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam over it. Marwān appointed Muṣʿab ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf as judge. In it, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād campaigned in Khurāsān. He crossed the river to Bukhara on camels, and was the first Arab to cross the river to Bukhara. He conquered Zāmin and half of Baykand, both of which belong to Bukhara.

Muʿāwiya dismissed Ibn Jundub from Baṣra and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ghīlān al-Thaqafī over it for six months. In it, Muʿāwiya appointed al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays over Kūfa. In it, Muḥammad ibn Mālik spent the winter in Roman territory, and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam performed the pilgrimage. In it, Maslama ibn Mukhallad sent Khālid ibn Thābit al-Fahmī on a campaign to the lands of the Maghrib and ordered him to appoint Abū al-Muhājir Dīnār, one of the Anṣār, as his deputy. He returned and left Abū al-Muhājir [in charge]. Ḥātim ibn Muslim reported to me: Al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays, when he was in charge of Kūfa, sent Maṣqala ibn Hubayra al-Shaybānī to Ṭabaristān. He made peace with its people for five hundred thousand dirhams, a weight of five, one hundred cloaks, and three hundred captives. There, Diḥya, Bayrak’s slave, was killed; he killed him. In it, Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām, Makẖrama ibn Nawfal, Abū Qatāda, Ḥuwayṭib ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy, Thawbān, and Saʿīd ibn Yarbuʿ al-Makhzūmī died.

The Year 55

In it, Muʿāwiya dismissed ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ghīlān from Baṣra and appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād over it. He remained governor until he died, and Yazīd confirmed him in office.

In it, Sufyān ibn ʿAwf spent the winter in Roman territory. In it, Yazīd ibn Shajara al-Ruhāwī campaigned and was killed. Some said that he was not killed on this campaign, but was killed afterward. In it, Saʿd died, and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Mālik performed the pilgrimage. Abū al-Yusr said: Abū al-Ḥasan and Zayd ibn Thābit.

The Year 56

The campaign to Samarqand. In it, Muʿāwiya dismissed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād from Khurāsān and appointed Saʿīd ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān over it. Saʿīd campaigned with al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra, Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khalaf—Ṭalḥa of the Ṭalḥas—Aws ibn Thaʿlaba of Banū Taym Allāt, and Rabīʿa ibn ʿAsal al-Yarbūʿī. He campaigned against Samarqand, and the people of Sogdia came out against him and fought him. He forced them back into their city, and they made peace with him and gave him hostages. In it, Masʿūd ibn Abī Masʿūd spent the winter in Roman territory; it is also said [that it was] Junāda ibn Abī Umayya. Al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān performed the pilgrimage. In it, Isḥāq ibn Yaḥyā ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh died in Khurāsān. In it, Juwayriya bt. al-Ḥārith, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ—died.

The Year 57

In it, Muʿāwiya dismissed al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays from Kūfa and appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Umm al-Ḥakam over it. In it, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān sent Ḥassān ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Ghassānī to Ifrīqiya. He made peace with the Berbers in the neighboring areas and imposed the land tax on it. He remained there until Muʿāwiya died. In it, Muʿāwiya dismissed Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam from Medina and appointed al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān over it. He remained governor there until Muʿāwiya died. He appointed al-Walīd ibn Zamʿa al-ʿĀmirī as judge over Medina.

In it, Saʿīd ibn ʿUthmān was dismissed from Khurāsān and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād was appointed over it. In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays spent the winter in Roman territory. In it, ʿĀʾisha, Mother of the Believers, and Abū Hurayra died. Al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān performed the pilgrimage.

The Year 58

Khalīfa said: In it, Mālik ibn ʿAbd Allāh spent the winter in Roman territory. In it, Yazīd ibn Shajara al-Ruhāwī campaigned and he and his companions were struck down. Al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān performed the pilgrimage. In it, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and ʿUqba ibn ʿĀmir al-Juhanī died. Baqī said: It was read aloud to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, that he said: In the year fifty-eight there was the campaign of Akdar, and Saʿīd ibn Yazīd [went to] Rhodes, and the campaign of Mālik ibn al-Abjar [was to] Ifrīqiya. In it, Marwān was removed from the people of Medina, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba was put in charge. Baqī said: Bakkār ibn ʿAbd Allāh wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: Al-Walīd reported to me: Someone other than Yazīd reported to me, saying: In the year fifty-eight, ʿAmr ibn Murra spent the winter at al-Badhandoon, and al-Ḥuṣayn ibn Numayr raided the Roman summer expedition.

Khalīfa and al-Layth said: That year, al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān led the people on the pilgrimage.

The Year 59

Abū al-Muhājir campaigns against Carthage.

Khalīfa said: In it, Dīnār Abū al-Muhājir campaigned and alighted at Carthage. They met in battle, and killing and wounds were numerous on both sides. Night separated them, and the Muslims withdrew during that night and encamped on a mountain south of Tunis. Then they resumed fighting them, and they made peace with them on the condition that they leave the island to them. Al-Muhājir reached the springs of Abū al-Muhājir and conquered Mīla. His stay on this campaign lasted about two years. In it, ʿAmr ibn Murra al-Mahrī spent the winter on land in Roman territory; there was no sea campaign that year. In it, Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ, Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim, Shayba ibn ʿUthmān, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz died. In it, the son of ʿAwf ibn Abī Jamīla al-Aʿrābī died.

The death of Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān. In it, Muʿāwiya died in Damascus on Thursday, with eight nights remaining in Rajab. His son Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya prayed over him. It is also said that Yazīd was not present, and that al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays prayed over him. Muʿāwiya died at the age of eighty-two; it is also said eighty, and it is also said eighty-six. His rule lasted nineteen years, three months, and twenty days. He was born in Mecca, in the house of Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb; it is also said, in the house of ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿa.

At the end of Muʿāwiya’s rule, Usāma ibn Zayd, ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf, and Ṣafwān ibn al-Muʿaṭṭal; ʿUthmān ibn Ḥunayf, Mujammaʿ ibn Jāriya, Abū Ḥumayd al-Sāʿidī, Khirāsh ibn Umayya, Ibn Buḥayna, Qays ibn Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda, Abū Jahm ibn Ḥudhayfa, Maslama ibn Mukhallad, Bilāl ibn al-Ḥārith al-Muzanī, al-Ḥārith ibn al-Azmaʿ al-Hamadhānī, and Mihjan ibn al-Adraʿ lived into the time of Muʿāwiya. Faḍāla ibn ʿUbayd and Shaddād ibn Aws [also lived then]; it is said of him that he died in the year forty-one.

The campaign to Rhodes. Baqī said: It was read aloud to Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year fifty-nine, Junāda ibn Abī Umayya, ʿAlqama ibn Junāda al-Ḥijrī, and ʿAlqama ibn al-Aḵtham campaigned against Rhodes. Muḥammad ibn Abī Sufyān led the people on the pilgrimage that year. He said: Bakkār ibn ʿAbd Allāh wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, from al-Walīd, from a man, who said: In the year fifty-nine, Junāda ibn Abī Umayya spent the winter in Roman territory. He said: Ibn Numayr reported to us: Abū Hurayra died in the year fifty-nine.

The judges during the caliphate of Muʿāwiya. Khalīfa said: In Baṣra, ʿUmayra ibn Yathribī al-Ḍabbī was in charge; Ibn ʿĀmir appointed him. He appointed ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn, who asked to be excused, and he excused him. He appointed Ziyād ibn Faḍāla, the brother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Faḍāla al-Laythī, and Zurāra ibn Awfā al-Ḥarashī. Shurayḥ served as judge alongside Ziyād in Baṣra for a year. During Muʿāwiya’s caliphate, Zurāra ibn Awfā served as judge for ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Udhayna also served as his judge.

In Kūfa, Shurayḥ continued as judge until Ziyād took him along with him to Baṣra. Masrūq ibn al-Ajdaʿ served as judge there after him until Shurayḥ returned to Medina. Marwān appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith as judge there, and he continued as judge until Marwān was dismissed in the year forty-eight. Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ then took office and appointed Abū Salama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf as judge. He continued as judge until Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ was dismissed and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam took office for the second time, in the year fifty-four. Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam appointed Muṣʿab ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf as judge. He continued as judge there until Marwān was dismissed in the year fifty-seven, at the end of Dhū al-Qaʿda, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān took office. He appointed Ibn Zamʿa al-ʿĀmirī as judge until Muʿāwiya died.

Those in charge of correspondence, the fiscal administration, the chamberlain’s office, the police, the guard, and the seal.

He said: The secretary of correspondence was ʿUbayd ibn Aws al-Ghassānī. Sarjūn ibn Manṣūr al-Rūmī was in charge of the fiscal administration and all its affairs. His chamberlain was Abū Ayyūb, his client. Yazīd, his client, was in charge of his police. When Yazīd died, he appointed Qays ibn Ḥamza al-Hamadhānī; then he dismissed him and appointed Dhuahl ibn ʿAmr al-ʿUdhrī.

He was the first to establish a guard officer and the first to institute the register of the seal. Al-Mukhtār, a client of Ḥimyar, was in charge of the guard, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr al-Ḥimyarī was in charge of the seal. Muʿāwiya ﵀—died on Thursday, with eight nights remaining in Rajab, in the year fifty-nine. He said: Ziyād ibn Abī Sufyān was the first for whom Iraq was united; Muʿāwiya united it for him, and that was in the year fifty. He remained governor over it until he died in the year fifty-three.

The Year 60

He said: He remained [in office]. It was read aloud to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year sixty, the Commander of the Faithful, Muʿāwiya, died in Rajab, four nights having passed of it. He appointed Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya as his successor. In that year the people of Egypt carried provisions to Rhodes. In that year al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba was removed from Medina, and ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd was appointed over Medina, Mecca, and al-Ṭāʾif. That year ʿAmr led the people on the pilgrimage. Then he was removed at the beginning of Dhū al-Ḥijja, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba was appointed. Ḥarmala added in his transmission from Ibn Bukayr: And al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ﵁ set out for Iraq, and Ibn al-Zubayr set out for Mecca. He said: Bakkār ibn ʿAbd Allāh wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: Others besides al-Walīd related to us the commanders of Muʿāwiya on the summer campaigns, and I wrote that down as I heard it.

The report of Muʿāwiya with ʿAmr ibn Muʿāwiya al-ʿUqaylī

Among this is what Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAyyāsh related to us, from Ṣafwān ibn ʿAmr, from Saʿīd ibn Ḥanẓala: Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān appointed ʿAmr ibn Muʿāwiya al-ʿUqaylī over the summer campaign. When he returned, he asked him about the fifth [of the spoils]. He told him, and Muʿāwiya said, “Where is it?” ʿAmr said, “You ask me about the fifth while I see a man of the Emigrants walking on his own feet, and I do not give him a mount.” Muʿāwiya said, “Then indeed you shall not receive it for as long as you live.” He said, “In that case, I do not care,” and began to recite:

“Quraysh share out my spoils in Damascus, while I leave my companions behind; that is not justice …”

“… I am no emir who gathers wealth as a merchant, nor do I seek a long tenure as emir through miserliness …

“… If the old man of Damascus keeps his wealth … then I do not keep my own wealth under lock and key …”

Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh said: Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAyyāsh related to me, from Ṣafwān ibn ʿAmr, from Abū Ḥasba, that when ʿAmr ibn Muʿāwiya al-ʿUqaylī was in command of the army, he would camp and share with his companions in the market for captives, meat, and ramak, with his lower legs bared.

Muḥammad said: Marwān ibn Muḥammad related to me, from Rashdīn ibn Saʿd, from al-Ḥasan ibn Thawbān, from Yazīd, that ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays al-Fazārī was over the people of Syria on their return, ʿAwwām al-Yaḥṣubī over the people of Egypt, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān over the people of Medina, and ʿAwwām over the main body. Muḥammad said: Marwān ibn Muḥammad related to me, from Rashdīn ibn Saʿd, from al-Ḥasan ibn Thawbān, who said: Yazīd said, “ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays al-Fazārī captured a stronghold during Muʿāwiya’s caliphate. Their spoils that day were one hundred dinars, an ounce of raw gold, and a brass ewer.” He said: I did not ask Marwān about these commanders mentioned in the first report: whether they were all together on this campaign or whether this was a campaign before theirs.

Muḥammad said: al-Walīd ibn Muslim related to me, who said: The last thing Muʿāwiya enjoined upon them was: “Tighten the noose around the Byzantines, for by doing so you will keep the other nations in check.” Al-Walīd said: Muʿāwiya died in Rajab of the year sixty. His caliphate lasted nineteen years and six months. Muḥammad said: al-Wāqidī related to me that Muʿāwiya died at the age of seventy-eight.

The caliphate of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān. Muḥammad said: al-Walīd ibn Muslim said: Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya became caliph and raided in that year Malik of Syria. He said: Ibn Numayr related to us, who said: Bilāl ibn al-Ḥārith al-Muzanī died in the year sixty. Muʿāwiya died in Rajab of the year sixty, and Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya was given the oath of allegiance. He appointed ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ over Medina, and ʿAmr led the people on the pilgrimage in the year sixty.

The departure of al-Ḥusayn for Iraq and the killing of al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī on the tenth day of Muḥarram in the year sixty-one. Then ʿAmr was removed from Medina in the year sixty. Khalīfa said: In that year al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib sent his cousin Muslim ibn ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib to the people of Kūfa so that they might pledge allegiance to him. Many people pledged allegiance to him. Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya assigned Iraq to ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād, who set out with the people of Iraq and killed Muslim ibn ʿAqīl and Hānī ibn ʿUrwa al-Murādī.

In that year al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī left Mecca intending for Kūfa. Al-Farazdaq said: “I had set out intending to perform the pilgrimage. When I was at Dhāt ʿIrq, I saw tents pitched, and I asked, ‘Whose are these?’ They said, ‘Al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī’s.’ So I turned aside to him and said, ‘O son of the Messenger of God ﷺ—what has made you hurry away from the pilgrimage?’ He said, ‘These people,’ meaning the people of Kūfa, ‘have written to me, describing the state they are in.’ Then he asked me, ‘How did you leave the people behind you?’ I said, ‘May my father and mother be your ransom: I left the hearts with you, the swords with the Umayyads, and victory in heaven.’”

He said: In the year sixty, Qatāda ibn Diʿāma al-Sadūsī, Hishām ibn ʿUrwa, Sulaymān ibn Mihrān al-Aʿmash, and Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Khālid were born.

Yazīd seeks from the governor of Medina that the oath of allegiance be taken for him

He said: In that year Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya sent his client Ruzayq to al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba.

Wahb ibn Jarīr related to me, saying: My father related to me, from Muḥammad, who said: Ruzayq, the client of Muʿāwiya, related to me, saying: When Muʿāwiya died, Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya sent me to al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba, who was governor of Medina. He wrote to him about Muʿāwiya’s death and that he should summon that group of men and order them to pledge allegiance to him. I arrived in Medina at night and said to the doorkeeper, “Ask permission for me to enter.” He said, “He has gone in, and there is no way to reach him.” I said, “I have come to him with an important matter.” He went in and informed him, and he gave permission. He was on his couch. When he read Yazīd’s letter announcing Muʿāwiya’s death and Yazīd’s succession, he grieved deeply over Muʿāwiya’s death; he kept rising to his feet and throwing himself onto his bed. Then he sent for Marwān, who came wearing a white shirt and a rose-colored mantle. He announced Muʿāwiya’s death to him and informed him that Yazīd had written to him to summon that group of men and call them to pledge allegiance to Yazīd.

He said: Marwān prayed for mercy upon Muʿāwiya and wished him well. He said, “Send for that group of men at once and call them to pledge allegiance. If they pledge allegiance, all well; if not, strike off their heads.” He said, “Glory be to God! Am I to kill al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī and Ibn al-Zubayr?” He said, “That is what I am telling you.”

Wahb related to me, saying: Juwayriya ibn Asmāʾ related to me, saying: I heard countless elders of ours among the people of Medina relate that Muʿāwiya died while al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān was then in Medina. News of his death reached him, and he sent for Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam and some men of the Umayyads and informed them of what had reached him. Marwān said, “Send for al-Ḥusayn and Ibn al-Zubayr at once. If they pledge allegiance, all well; if not, strike off their heads.” ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr had already died before that. Ibn al-Zubayr came to him, announced Muʿāwiya’s death, prayed for mercy upon him, and wished him well. He said to him, “Pledge allegiance.” He replied, “This is not the time for a pledge of allegiance, and a man like me does not pledge allegiance to you here. Rather, ascend the pulpit, and I will pledge allegiance to you, and the people will pledge allegiance to you openly, not secretly.”

Marwān leapt up and said, “Strike off his head, for he is a man of sedition and evil.” He said, “You are a vile aggressor, son of al-Zarqāʾ!” They exchanged insults. Al-Walīd said, “Get the two of them out of my presence.” He was a gentle, noble, generous man, so they were taken out from before him. Then al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī came in that situation, but nothing was said to him until they had both returned together. Marwān returned to al-Walīd and said, “By God, you will not see him again after this meeting except in a situation that displeases you.” He sent spies after him. When he entered his house, he did no more than call for water for ablution and stand with his feet together; he continued standing thus and praying. He ordered his son Ḥamza to take his riding mount ahead to al-Ḥulayfa, a post-stage from Medina in the direction of al-Farʿ. He had great property at al-Ḥulayfa. He continued standing with his feet together. When it was late in the night and the spies had withdrawn from him, he mounted his beast and rode until he reached al-Ḥulayfa. He mounted his riding camel there and then set out for Mecca. Al-Ḥusayn left that same night, and the two met in Mecca. Ibn al-Zubayr said to him, “What keeps you from your partisans and your father’s partisans? By God, if I had as many as they do, I would go to them.”

He said: Yazīd appointed ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd as governor over Medina, above al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba, fearing al-Walīd’s weakness. When ʿAmr entered, he ascended the pulpit, praised and extolled God, then mentioned Ibn al-Zubayr and what he had done. He said, “He has sought refuge in Mecca. By God, we shall raid him; and by God, if he enters Mecca, we shall burn it down over him, despite whoever resents it.” Wahb said: Juwayriya said: Musāfiḥ related to me that a Qurashī man, whose name I have forgotten, related to him that he was sitting with ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān beneath the pulpit of ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd when he said, “despite whoever resents it.” ʿAbd al-Malik put his finger on his nose and then said, “O God, let my nose be humbled if he raids Your Sacred House.” There is a report concerning this. ʿAmr led the pilgrimage.

Chapter 8

Yazīd, the civil war and ʿAbd al-Malik

The Year 61

The killing of al-Ḥusayn and his companions. In it al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵀ was killed on Wednesday, ten days into Muḥarram, the Day of ʿĀshūrāʾ, in the year sixty-one. Jaʿfar ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib was killed with him. Abū ʿUbayda said: Jaʿfar ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib was killed with him; his mother was Umm al-Banīn, daughter of Ḥizām ibn Khālid, of Banū al-Waḥīd, one of Banū Kilāb. Abū al-Ḥasan said: ʿUthmān ibn ʿAlī was killed with him; his mother was also Umm al-Banīn. Abū ʿUbayda and Abū al-Ḥasan said: al-ʿAbbās al-Aṣghar and Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī al-Aṣghar, the two sons of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, were killed with him; their mother was Lubāba, daughter of ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās. Abū al-Ḥasan said: His mother was a concubine. Abū ʿUbayda and Abū al-Ḥasan said: ʿAlī ibn Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī was killed with him; his mother was Laylā or Lubnā, daughter of Abī Murra ibn ʿUrwa ibn Masʿūd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Muʿattib al-Thaqafī, and her mother was Maymūna, daughter of Abī Sufyān ibn Ḥarb ibn Umayya. Abū al-Ḥasan said: ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib was killed with him; his mother was al-Rubāb, daughter of Imruʾ al-Qays of Kalb. Abū Bakr ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib was killed with him. Al-Qāsim ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar were killed with him; his mother was al-Khawṣāʾ, daughter of Khaṣaf ibn Thaqf ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿĀʾidh, of Banū Taym Allāt ibn Thaʿlaba ibn ʿUkāba. Muslim ibn ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib was killed with him; his mother was a young woman called Ḥalaba. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muslim was killed with him; his mother was a young woman. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muslim ibn ʿAqīl was killed with him; his mother was Ruqayya, daughter of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib. Muḥammad ibn Abī Saʿīd ibn ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib was killed with him.

Muḥammad ibn Muʿāwiya related to us, from Sufyān, from Abū Mūsā, who said: I heard al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī say: “Sixteen men from his household were struck down alongside al-Ḥusayn. No household on the face of the earth that day had their like.” Al-Ḥasan ibn Abī ʿAmr related to us, saying: I heard Fiṭr ibn Khalīfa say: I heard Mundhir al-Thawrī, from Ibn al-Ḥanafiyya, say: “Seventeen men were killed alongside al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī, all of them having stirred in Fāṭima’s womb.” The one who undertook the killing of al-Ḥusayn was Shimr ibn Dhī al-Jawshan, and the commander of the army was ʿUmar ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik.

In that year Mālik ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Khathʿamī raided Byzantine territory. He had an engagement at Qūniya. Al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage. In that year Ḥamza ibn ʿAmr al-Aslamī died. ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz and Saʿīd ibn Iyās al-Jarīrī were born in that year. It was read aloud to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year sixty-one, al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī and his companions ﵃ were killed, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba led the people on the pilgrimage. Ibn Numayr related to us, saying: Then ʿAmr was removed from Medina and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba was appointed; he led the people on the pilgrimage in the year sixty-one, and al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī was killed ten days into Muḥarram. Bakkār ibn ʿAbd Allāh wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, from al-Walīd ibn Muslim, who said: In the year sixty-one, Mālik ibn ʿAbd Allāh’s summer campaign took place—the campaign to Qūniya.

The Year 62

In it Salm ibn Ziyād raided Khwārazm, and its people made peace with him for a large sum of money. Then he crossed over to Samarqand, and its people made peace with him.

In that year ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād appointed al-Mundhir ibn al-Jārūd to the frontier post of Qandābīl. Al-Mundhir died at the frontier post, and al-Ḥakam ibn al-Mundhir ibn al-Jārūd came out and seized control of Qandābīl. Ibn Ziyād sent Sinān ibn Salama, who conquered al-Mūqān. Then, after that, Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya sent ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Yazīd al-Hilālī there. In that year the people of Kābul broke [their agreement] and took Abū ʿUbayda ibn Ziyād ibn Abī Sufyān captive. Yazīd ibn Ziyād ibn Abī Sufyān marched and attacked the enemy, who fought him. Yazīd ibn Ziyād was killed, and killed with him were Zayd ibn Judʿān, Abū ʿAlī ibn Zayd ibn Judʿān al-faqīh; Ṣila ibn Ashyam, Abū al-Ṣahbāʾ al-ʿAdawī, and his son; ʿAmr ibn Qutayba; Budayl ibn Nuʿaym al-ʿAdawī; ʿUthmān ibn Ādam al-ʿAdawī; and men from among the people of truthfulness. In that year ʿAbd Allāh ibn Asad ibn Kurz al-Qasrī raided Caesarea in the direction of al-Ḥadath. In that year there was a summer campaign under Ḥuṣayn ibn Numayr al-Sakūnī, who raided Syria. In that year ʿAlqama ibn Qays al-Nakhaʿī died, and ʿUthmān ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage.

The Year 63

the Battle of al-Ḥarra. In it occurred the affair of al-Ḥarra. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: ʿUthmān ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage in the year sixty-two, then arrived in Medina and stayed for a month. Then he sent a delegation to Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. Among them were ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī ʿAmr ibn Ḥafṣ …

Ibn al-Mughīra al-Makhzūmī, Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm al-Anṣārī, a man from Banū Surāqa of Banū ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb, and men from Quraysh. They came to Medina, openly reviled Yazīd, disavowed him, and deposed him.

Wahb ibn Jarīr related to us: Juwayriya ibn Asmāʾ related to me: I heard elders from the people of Medina relate that among those who went as a delegation to Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥanẓala, accompanied by eight of his sons. Yazīd gave him one hundred thousand, and gave each of his sons ten thousand dirhams, in addition to their clothing and mounts. When ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥanẓala came to Medina, people came to him and asked, “What news do you bring?” He said, “I have come to you from a man whom, by God, I would fight even if I had no one but these sons of mine.” They said, “We have heard that he gave you a generous gift, honored you, and gave you money.” He said, “He did so, but I accepted it from him only so that I might use it to gain strength against him.” He urged the people on, and they pledged allegiance to him.

Abū al-Yaqẓān said: They called for approval and consultation. They appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muṭīʿ al-ʿAdawī over Quraysh, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥanẓala al-Ghasīl over the Anṣār, and Maʿqil ibn Sinān al-Ashjaʿī over the tribes of the Muhājirūn. They expelled ʿUthmān ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Sufyān from Medina, along with whoever of Banū Umayya was there.

Wahb related to me: My father related to me, from Ayyūb, from ʿIkrima, from Ibn ʿAbbās: He asked about them while he was at al-Ṭāʾif. He was told, “They appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muṭīʿ over Quraysh and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥanẓala over the Anṣār.” He said, “Two commanders—if the people perish!”

Wahb said: My father related to me: When the people of Medina expelled Banū Umayya and Marwān, they alighted at Jufayl. Marwān wrote to Yazīd about the people’s position. Yazīd ordered a pavilion to be set up for him outside his palace, and dispatched the Syrian forces with Muslim ibn ʿUqba al-Murrī. Three [days] had not passed before he had finished; then, on the third morning, he reviewed the divisions, saying:

“Tell Abū Bakr, when the army has set out, when the army comes to Wādī al-Qurā …

‘You will see the women of the people gathered.’

Juwayriya ibn Asmāʾ related to me: I heard elders from the people of Medina relate that when death was upon Muʿāwiya, he summoned [Yazīd] and said to him, “You have a day to reckon with the people of Medina. If they do this, attack them with Muslim ibn ʿUqba, for he is a man whose loyalty we have come to know.” When the people of Medina did what they did, he sent Muslim ibn ʿUqba against them. The people of Medina had gone to every water source between them and the people of Syria, poured a skinful of pitch into it, and spoiled it. God sent rain upon them, so they did not draw water with a bucket before they reached Medina.

Abū al-Yaqẓān and others said: Yazīd appointed Muslim ibn ʿUqba while he was ill, and said, “If something happens to you, appoint Ḥuṣayn ibn Numayr.”

Wahb said in his report from Juwayriya: The people of Medina went out in great numbers and in a manner the like of which had never been seen. When the Syrians saw them, they were awed by them and loath to fight them. Muslim ordered his bed to be placed between the two lines, then ordered his herald: “Fight for me, or leave!” The people pressed into the fighting against them. They heard the takbīr behind them, in the heart of the city, and the Syrians of Banū Ḥāritha charged them while they were fiercely engaged. The people were routed. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥanẓala was leaning against one of his sons, asleep. His son roused him; when he opened his eyes and saw what had happened, he ordered his eldest son forward. The son advanced until he was killed. ʿAbd Allāh kept sending them forward, one by one, until they were all dead. Then he broke the scabbard of his sword and fought until he was killed. Muslim ibn ʿUqba entered Medina and summoned the people to pledge allegiance on the condition that they were Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya’s dependents, and that he could rule over their families, blood, and property as he wished. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa was brought to him. He was a friend and intimate of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. He said, “Pledge allegiance on the condition that you are a dependent of the Commander of the Faithful, and that he may rule over your blood, family, and property.” He said, “I pledge allegiance to you on the condition that I am the cousin of the Commander of the Faithful, and that he may rule over my blood, family, and property.” He said, “Strike off his head!” Marwān sprang up, embraced him, and said, “He will pledge allegiance to you on whatever terms you wish.” He said, “By God, I will never accept that from him. If he does not yield, then kill them both together.” Marwān let him go, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa was beheaded.

Abū al-Ḥasan said: ʿAwāna said: Yazīd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa was brought to Muslim, who said, “Pledge allegiance.” He replied, “I pledge allegiance to you according to the Book of God and the Sunna of His Prophet.” Muslim ordered that he be killed.

Wahb related to us: My father related to me: al-Ḥasan said: Zaynab’s two sons were killed on the Day of al-Ḥarra and carried to her. She said, “We belong to God and to Him we return. How great is the calamity upon me because of them both! Yet it is greater for me in this one than in that one. As for this one, he extended his hand and fought until he was killed, and I fear for him. As for this one, he held back his hand until he was killed, and I hope for him.

Wahb ibn Jarīr related to us: Abū ʿAqīl al-Dawraqī related to us: I heard Abū Naḍra relate: Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī entered a cave on the Day of al-Ḥarra. A man entered after him, then came out and said to a Syrian, “Shall I show you a man to kill?”

When the Syrian reached the cave’s entrance, he said to Abū Saʿīd—whose sword was around his neck—“Come out to me.” He said, “No. If you come in to me, I will kill you.” The Syrian entered, so Abū Saʿīd laid down his sword and said, “Take on my sin and your sin, and be among the inhabitants of the Fire; that is the recompense of the wrongdoers.” Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī said, “Are you [that man]?” He said, “Yes.” He said, “Then ask forgiveness for me.” He said, “May God forgive you.”

The enumeration of those killed on the Day of al-Ḥarra

Of Banū Hāshim among Quraysh: then, of Banū Hāshim: Abū Bakr ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib; al-Faḍl ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib; and ʿAbbās ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Lahab.
