Of their confederates, and of the confederates of Abū Ṭālib, from Banū Sulaym ibn Manṣūr: Sulaymān ibn Ṣafwān ibn ʿAbbād ibn Shaybān; al-Aswad ibn ʿAbbād ibn Shaybān; ʿUtba ibn Maʿbad—or Maʿbad ibn Shaybān; Muḥammad ibn ʿUqba ibn Dibiya ibn Jābir and his brother Sulaymān; and Jurayy ibn Ḥazm ibn Jābir.

Of Banū al-Muṭṭalib: and of Banū al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Manāf: Yaḥyā ibn Nāfiʿ ibn ʿUjayr ibn ʿAbd Yazīd ibn Hāshim, of Banū al-Muṭṭalib; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Nāfiʿ ibn ʿUjayr.

Of their confederates, and of their confederates from Banū Sulaym: Jayfar ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik; though it is said that he was rather Jaʿfar ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik.

Of Banū Nawfal: and of Banū Nawfal ibn ʿAbd Manāf: Dāwūd ibn al-Walīd ibn Qurẓa ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn Nawfal; his son al-Walīd ibn Dāwūd; and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUtba ibn Ghazwān, their confederate from Banū Māzin ibn Manṣūr.

Of Banū Umayya: and of Banū Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Manāf: Ismāʿīl ibn Khālid ibn ʿUqba ibn Abī Muʿayṭ; Abū ʿAlbāʾ, the client of Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam; and Sulaymān, ʿAmr, and al-Walīd, the sons of Yazīd, [whose mother was] the sister of al-Nimr.

Of Banū Asad: and of Banū Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Quṣayy: Wahb ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa ibn al-Aswad ibn al-Muṭṭalib ibn Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā; Yazīd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa, who was executed while in captivity; Abū Salama ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa; al-Miqdād ibn Wahb ibn Zamʿa; Yazīd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Wahb ibn Zamʿa; Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa; a son of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa whose name is unknown; al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Sāʾib ibn Abī Ḥubaysh ibn al-Muṭṭalib ibn Asad; ʿAbd Allāh and ʿAmr, the sons of Nawfal ibn ʿAdī ibn Nawfal ibn Asad; a son of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Dhuʾayb ibn ʿAdī ibn Nawfal ibn Asad; and ʿAdī ibn Tuwayt ibn Ḥabīb ibn Asad.

Of their confederates: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿa, from Namir of al-Azd; and Usāma ibn Khiyār.

Of Banū ʿAbd al-Dār: and of Banū ʿAbd al-Dār ibn Quṣayy: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Musāfiʿ ibn Ṭalḥa ibn Abī Ṭalḥa—the name of Abū Ṭalḥa was ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Dār; Muḥammad ibn Ayyūb ibn Thābit ibn ʿAbd al-Mundhir ibn ʿAlqama ibn Kalda; Muṣʿab ibn Abī ʿUmayr ibn Abī ʿAzīz; Yazīd and Zayd, the sons of Musāfiʿ; and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Aswad.

Of Banū Zuhra: and of Banū Zuhra: Zayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf; Abān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwf; ʿIyāḍ ibn Ḥasan ibn ʿAwf—Ḥasan died during the sedition of Ibn al-Zubayr; Muḥammad ibn al-Aswad ibn ʿAwf; al-Ṣalt ibn Makhrama ibn Nawfal ibn Wahb ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Zuhra; Muḥammad ibn al-Muṣawwar ibn al-Miswar ibn Makhrama; ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Aswad ibn ʿAbd Yaghūth; Ismāʿīl ibn Wahb ibn al-Aswad ibn ʿAbd Yaghūth; ʿUmayr and ʿAmr, the sons of Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ; Isḥāq ibn Hāshim ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Waqqāṣ; ʿImrān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Nāfiʿ ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Waqqāṣ; and Muḥammad ibn Nāfiʿ ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Waqqāṣ.

Of their confederates: ʿUthmān, al-Jallās, and Muḥammad, the sons of al-ʿAlāʾ ibn Jāriya, from Thaqīf; Abū ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mawhab ibn Rabāḥ; and ʿAbd Allāh and ʿUbayd Allāh, the sons of Bishr ibn al-Sāʾib.

Of Banū Taym ibn Murra: and of Banū Taym ibn Murra: Yaʿqūb ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh; ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUthmān ibn Lāʿabīd Allāh ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb; ʿAbd Allāh b. b. Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq; and Maʿbad ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Khālid ibn Ṣakhr ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb.

Of their confederates: Mūsā ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ṭufayl, from Daws—though it is said he was from Azd Shanūʾa. He was ʿĀʾisha’s maternal brother, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr was their maternal brother. Also al-Ḥārith ibn al-Munqidh ibn al-Ṭufayl; al-Ṭufayl Abū al-Ḥuṣayn, the maternal brother of Ibn Abī ʿAtīq; ʿAmmār ibn Ṣuhayb; and Muṣʿab and Khālid, the sons of Muḥammad ibn Ṣuhayb.

Of Banū Makhzūm: and of Banū Makhzūm: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī ʿAmr ibn Ḥafṣ ibn al-Mughīra; Abū Saʿd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām—his mother was from Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Kaʿb; ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Rabīʿa; and Muslim—or, it is said, Maslama—ibn Abī Bard ibn Maʿbad ibn Wahb ibn ʿĀʾidh.

Of Banū ʿAdī: and of Banū ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb: Abū Bakr ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb; ʿAbd Allāh and Sulaymān, the sons of ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb; ʿUmar—or ʿAmr—ibn Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl; Abū Bakr ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl; Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn Muṭīʿ ibn al-Aswad ibn Ḥāritha ibn Naḍla ibn ʿAwf ibn ʿUbayd ibn ʿUwayj; ʿAbd al-Malik ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muṭīʿ; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Nāfiʿ ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Naḍla; Ibrāhīm ibn Nuʿaym ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Naḥḥām—though it is said he was Ibrāhīm ibn Nuʿaym ibn ʿAbd Allāh; Muḥammad ibn Abī al-Jahm ibn Ḥudhayfa ibn Ghānim, and they were killed in captivity; and Khudayj—or Ḥudayj—ibn Abī Ḥathma ibn Ḥudhāfa ibn Ghānim.

Of their confederates: Iyās and Yaʿlā, the sons of al-Sirrī; and Yūsuf ibn Ḥabīb, from Banū Layth.

Of Banū Sahm ibn ʿAmr: and of Banū Sahm ibn ʿAmr ibn Huṣayṣ: Dhuʾayb ibn ʿAmr ibn Khunays ibn Ḥudhāfa ibn Saʿd ibn Sahm; his son; and Miyāḥ ibn Khalaf and Faḍāla ibn Miyāḥ, two confederates of theirs.

Of Banū Jumḥ: and of Banū Jumḥ ibn ʿAmr: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ḥaṭṭāb; and al-Ḥārith ibn Maʿmar ibn Ḥabīb.

Of their confederates: ʿUthmān—or, it is said, ʿAmr—ibn Kathīr ibn al-Ṣalt; and Nuʿaym ibn Lawṭ—or Lawṭ ibn Nuʿaym ibn al-Ṣalt al-Kindī.

Of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy:

And of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥuwayṭib ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā; ʿAbd al-Malik ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥuwayṭib; Rabīʿa ibn Sahm—or Sahl—ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Zamʿa ibn Qays; ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa; ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zamʿa; ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥāṭib; Sulayṭ ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Hāshim, keeper of the Quraysh document; Hāshim ibn Ḥamza; Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Aswad ibn Hāshim ibn Kināna; Hishām ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Kināna; Rabīʿa and Kināna, the sons of Hāshim ibn Kināna ibn ʿUthmān ibn Ḥiṣn; al-Khayyār ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Khayyār; Abū Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Khayyār; Sulaymān ibn Uways ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ; Abū ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Uways; al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Kināna; and Abū Qays ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAdī, a maternal nephew of theirs from Banū Muʿayṣ.

Of Banū Ḥujayr:

And of Banū Ḥujayr—or Ḥujr—ibn Muʿayṣ: Faḍāla ibn Khālid ibn Nāʾila ibn Rawāḥa; ʿIyāḍ ibn Khālid ibn Nāʾila ibn Hurmuz—or Hurm—ibn Rawāḥa; al-Ḥārith and Muslim, the sons of Khālid; Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ṭufayl; ʿIyāḍ ibn Abī Salām ibn Yazīd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik ibn Rabīʿa ibn Wahb; and Zayd—or Yazīd—ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Musāfiʿ ibn Anas ibn ʿAbd ibn Wahīb ibn Ḍibāb.

From the Banu al-Harith ibn Fihr:

Among the Banu al-Harith ibn Fihr were Shuʿayb ibn Abi ʿAbd Allah, Mirdas ibn ʿAwf, Ibrahim ibn Israʾil, and Musʿab ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Abi Khaythama.

From the Banu Qays ibn al-Harith ibn Fihr:

Among the Banu Qays ibn al-Harith ibn Fihr, who are al-Khalaj, were Zufar ibn al-Harith or ibn Suwayd, Ibn Malik ibn Suwayd, ʿUqayl ibn Zufar, Rabiʿa ibn Ziyad, Athatha, al-ʿAlaʾ—the two sons of Shayba—Zuhayr ibn ʿAbd Allah, and Ziyad ibn Abi Umama.

From the Banu Muharib ibn Fihr:

Among the Banu Muharib ibn Fihr were ʿAbd al-Rahman, ʿAbd Allah, and Qatan, the sons of Nufayl ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Wahb ibn Saʿd ibn ʿAmr ibn Habib ibn ʿAmr ibn Shayban or Shayba ibn Muharib; ʿAbd Allah ibn Nadla ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Wahb; Saʿid ibn Rabah ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Mughtarif ibn Hijwan ibn ʿAmr ibn Habib; Aban ibn Hasal or ibn Hassan ibn Rabah ibn ʿAmr; ʿAmr ibn Hassan ibn Rabah; al-Walid ibn ʿIsma; al-ʿAlaʾ ibn Yazid ibn Anas ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Hijwan; Habib ibn Nafiʿ ibn Mudris; al-Walid ibn Hamama ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Hijwan; and Khuthaym ibn Nafiʿ ibn Mudris. Thus, all those of Quraysh who were killed from among their own people numbered ninety-seven men.

Among the Ansar, then, from the Banu ʿAwf:

Among the Ansar, from the Aws, then from the Banu ʿAwf, ʿAbd Allah ibn Hanzala and seven of his sons were killed: ʿAbd al-Rahman, al-Harith, al-Hakam, ʿAsim, Yahya, ʿAbd Allah—the two sons of Mujammaʿ—and ʿIsa ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Yazid; and ʿUkasha ibn Yazid ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Yazid.

From the Banu Hanash ibn ʿAwf:

Among the Banu Hanash ibn ʿAwf ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf were Sahl ibn ʿUthman ibn Hunayf, ʿAmr ibn Sahl, Muhammad ibn ʿUthman ibn Hunayf, and Habib ibn ʿAbbad ibn Hunayf.

From the Banu Thaʿlaba:

Among the Banu Thaʿlaba were Habib and ʿUmar or ʿAmr, the two sons of Khawat.

From the Banu Jahjaba:

Among the Banu Jahjaba ibn Kulfa were ʿIyad ibn ʿAmr ibn Bulayl, ʿAmr ibn ʿAmr ibn Bulayl, ʿAmr ibn ʿUqba ibn ʿAtwara, and Dhakwan, the client of Ibn Hanzala.

From the Banu al-ʿAjlan:

Among the Banu al-ʿAjlan were ʿAmara or ʿAmmar ibn Salama and ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn al-Harith ibn Salama.

From the Banu Muʿawiya ibn Malik:

Among the Banu Muʿawiya ibn Malik were Muhammad ibn Bashir; ʿAbd Allah ibn Kulayb or ibn ʿUbayd, who was wounded and died of his wound; Muhammad and ʿUtba or ʿUbayd, the two sons of Jubayr; ʿUbayd Allah and al-ʿAlaʾ, the two sons of Thabit; al-Saʾib ibn ʿAbd Allah; Thaʿlaba and ʿAmir, the two sons of al-Harith ibn Thaʿlaba; Saʿd ibn ʿAbd Allah; ʿAbd Allah ibn Hazm ibn ʿAmr ibn Umayya; and ʿUtba ibn al-Ashʿath ibn Kaʿb.

From the Banu ʿAbd al-Ashhal:

Among the Banu ʿAbd al-Ashhal were ʿAbd Allah ibn Saʿd ibn Muʿadh and Muhammad ibn Bashir ibn Muʿadh.

From the Banu Zaʿuraʾ:

Among the Banu Zaʿuraʾ were ʿAmr ibn Yazid ibn al-Sakan, ʿAbbad ibn Rashid ibn Rafiʿ ibn Qays, Musa ibn ʿAbd Allah, Jaʿfar ibn Thaʿlaba, Salama or Muslim ibn ʿAbbad ibn Silkan, ʿAbbad ibn Silkan ibn Salama ibn Waqsh, and Shayba ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAziz.

From the Nabit:

Among the Nabit were Saʿid ibn Jubayr, ʿAbd Allah ibn Saʿd, ʿAbbad, Saʿida, and Abu Jubayra, the sons of Saʿd.

From the Banu Haritha ibn al-Harith:

Among the Banu Harith ibn al-Harith were ʿAbd Allah ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Sahl; Kinana ibn Sahl ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Aws ibn Qayzi; ʿAbd Allah ibn Uways; Sahl ibn Abi Umama, an ally; Jaʿfar ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Muhayyisa; Saʿida ibn Asad ibn Saʿida; and Yazid ibn Muhammad ibn Salama.

From the Banu Zufar:

Among the Banu Zufar were ʿAmr, Muhammad, and Yazid, the sons of Thabit ibn Qays ibn al-Khatim, and Muhammad ibn Abi Namla ibn Zurara.

From the Banu Malik ibn al-Najjar:

Among the Khazraj, then from the Banu Malik ibn al-Najjar, were ʿAmr ibn Saʿid ibn al-Harith ibn al-Simma; Saʿid, Sulayman, Zayd, Yahya, and ʿUbayd Allah, the sons of Zayd ibn Thabit ibn al-Dahhak; Muhammad and Zayd, the two sons of ʿAmara ibn Zayd ibn Thabit ibn al-Dahhak; Muhammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Hazm; ʿAbd al-Rahman, ʿUthman, and ʿAbd al-Malik, the sons of Muhammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Hazm; ʿAbd Allah, Jabir, and Muʿawiya, the sons of ʿAmr ibn Hazm. From the Banu ʿAdi ibn al-Najjar:

Among the Banu ʿAdi ibn al-Najjar were Bakr ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Qays ibn Sirma; Malik ibn Sawad ibn ʿUdhayya; ʿAwn ibn Rifaʿa; ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Allah; al-Harith ibn Suraqa; and ʿAbd Allah and Yahya, the two sons of Anas ibn Malik.

From the Banu Dinar ibn al-Najjar:

Among the Banu Dinar ibn al-Najjar was Saʿd ibn ʿUmayr ibn Uhayb.

From the Banu Mazin:

Among the Banu Mazin ibn al-Najjar were ʿAmr ibn Tamim ibn Ghaziyya; Nuʿman ibn ʿAmr ibn Saʿd ibn ʿAmr ibn Ghaziyya; Saʿd and Jaʿfar, the two sons of Abi Dawud ibn ʿUmayr ibn Malik; ʿAbd Allah ibn Zayd ibn ʿAsim and his son Abu Hasan; and ʿAbd Allah ibn Harith ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Kaʿb and his two brothers.

ʿAbd al-Rahman, Qays, and ʿAmr ibn Abi Hasan; ʿUtba ibn Jarir; and Hakim ibn Abi Quhafa, an affiliated member of theirs from among the people of Yemen.

From the Banu al-Harith:

Among the Banu al-Harith ibn al-Khazraj were ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Khubayb ibn Isaf; Muhammad ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Khalid ibn Isaf; Muhammad and ʿAbd Allah, the two sons of Khalid ibn Isaf; ʿUbayd Allah ibn Anis ibn Sakan ibn Isaf; Saʿd ibn Kulayb ibn Isaf; Muhammad, Yahya, and ʿAbd Allah, the sons of Thabit ibn Qays ibn Shammās; Abu Nuʿaym ibn Abi Fudala ibn Thabit; Labib ibn Bisr ibn Yazid; ʿAbd Allah ibn ʿUtba ibn Sammak; ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Husayn; ʿAbd Allah ibn al-Rabiʿ ibn Suraqa; al-Saʾib ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Thaʿlaba; ʿAmir ibn al-Harith ibn Thaʿlaba; Saʿd ibn ʿUbayd Allah; ʿAbd Allah ibn Hasan ibn ʿAmr ibn Umayya; and ʿUtba ibn al-Ashʿath ibn Kaʿb, who was among the Zurayqites.

From the Banu ʿAwf:

Among the Banu ʿAwf ibn al-Khazraj was ʿAbd Allah ibn Rabiʿa ibn Bilal.

From the Banu Salim ibn ʿAwf:

Among the Banu Salim ibn ʿAwf were Nawfal ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAbbad ibn ʿUbada ibn al-Samit; Muhammad ibn Kaʿb ibn ʿUjra and his brother Saʿd ibn Kaʿb; and Thabit ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Iyas.

From the Banu Salima:

Among the Banu Salima were Muʿadh ibn al-Simma; Ayyub ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Muʿadh; ʿAmr ibn Khashram; ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Abi Qatada ibn Ribʿi; Yazid ibn Abi al-Yusr; Yahya ibn Sayfi ibn al-Aswad ibn Wahb ibn Kaʿb ibn Malik; and Muhammad ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Abi al-Mundhir.

From the Banu Bayada:

Among the Banu Bayada was ʿAbd Allah ibn Ziyad ibn Labid.

From the Banu Zurayq:

Among the Banu Zurayq were ʿUrwa ibn Abi ʿUmara and his son ʿUthman ibn ʿUrwa; ʿUqba ibn Abi ʿUmara and his brother Masʿud; Saʿd ibn ʿUthman ibn Khalda; Salama ibn Qays ibn Thabit ibn Khalda; ʿAmir ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn ʿAmr; al-Muttalib ibn ʿAmir ibn ʿAmr ibn Khalda; al-Harith ibn Rifaʿa ibn Rafiʿ ibn Malik; and Sulayman ibn Abi ʿAyyash ibn Muʿawiya ibn Samit.

From the family of al-Muʿalla:

Among the family of al-Muʿalla were Saʿid ibn Abi Saʿid ibn Aws ibn al-Muʿalla; Sahl ibn Abi Saʿid; al-Harith ibn ʿUtba ibn ʿUbayd ibn al-Muʿalla; Muhammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Qays; and Kathir ibn Aflah, the client of Abu Ayyub al-Ansari.

Thus, all those of the Ansar who were killed numbered one hundred and seventy-three men, and all those of Quraysh and the Ansar who were killed numbered three hundred and six men. Abu al-Hasan said: I was present at the Battle of al-Harra on the three nights remaining in Dhu al-Hijja of the year sixty-three.

Those executed while in captivity:

Maʿqil ibn Sinan al-Ashjaʿi was executed while in captivity; Muhammad ibn Abi Hudhayfa al-ʿAdawi was executed while in captivity; and Muhammad ibn Abi al-Jahm ibn Hudhayfa was executed while in captivity. In the year sixty-three, Salam ibn Ziyad appointed Talha ibn ʿAbd Allah ibn Khalaf al-Khuzaʿi

The martyrdom of ʿUqba ibn Nafiʿ

In that year ʿUqba ibn Nafiʿ went on campaign and appointed Zuhayr ibn Qays al-Balawi as his deputy in al-Qayrawan. He reached the farthest Sus, took booty, and returned safely. Kisayla ibn Kayzam, who was a Christian, met him and killed ʿUqba ibn Nafiʿ, Abu al-Muhajir, one of the Ansar, and most of his companions. Kisayla then set out and met Zuhayr ibn Qays one barid from al-Qayrawan. Zuhayr killed Kisayla and his companions, and they were slaughtered in a terrible slaughter.

In the year sixty-three, ʿAbd Allah ibn al-Zubayr led the pilgrimage. It is said that the people reached an agreement on ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Zayd ibn al-Khattab, who led the people in prayer; and it is said that no emir performed the pilgrimage. ʿUthman ibn ʿUthman informed us, saying: Hisham ibn ʿUrwa narrated to us, from his father, who said: ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Zayd ibn al-Khattab delivered a sermon at the pilgrimage gathering and mentioned a report about sighting the crescent. Shayba ibn ʿUthman lived into the time of Yazid ibn Muʿawiya; Rabiʿa ibn Kaʿb al-Aslami lived through al-Harra; Nawfal ibn Muʿawiya al-Duʾali died during the days of Yazid ibn Muʿawiya; Burayda al-Aslami and ʿAbd al-Muttalib ibn Rabiʿa ibn al-Harith ibn ʿAbd al-Muttalib ibn Hashim [also died]. During Ibn Ziyad’s governorship of Iraq, Maʿqil ibn Yasar al-Muzani, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—died, as did ʿAʾidh ibn ʿAmr al-Muzani and Abu Barza al-Aslami. All of these died in Baṣra. Masruq ibn al-Ajdaʿ and Abu Bashir al-Mazini died after al-Harra.

Ibn al-Zubayr refuses to pledge allegiance to Yazid. Abu al-Hasan said, from a man of Mecca, from Salih ibn Kaysan, from ʿAbd al-ʿAziz ibn Marwan, who said: Yazid sent Ibn ʿUdah al-Ashʿari to Ibn al-Zubayr, summoning him to pledge allegiance to him, and with him was a jāmiʿa

“I will not yield to any but the truth that I seek, until stone yields to the chewer’s molar.”

Then he said, “By God, I will not pledge allegiance to Yazid, nor enter into obedience to him.”

The burning of the Kaʿba. Al-Ansari and Ghundar narrated to us; both said: Ibn Jurayj narrated to us, saying: Ibn al-Zubayr took the mosque as a fortress, and tents and pavilions were set up in it. A man from Syria set fire to the gate of the Banu Jumah, and the fire spread until it reached the gate of the Kaʿba, which burned. Ibn Jurayj said: I heard Ibn Abi ʿAmmar say: A man from Syria called out from the bank of Zamzam, “The two factions have perished!”—or he said, “The two factions!”—“By Him in whose hand is the soul of Muhammad!” Ibn Jurayj said: Ibn Abi Mulayka said: Ibn al-Zubayr withdrew to one side of Dar al-Nadwa, in that quarter, and kept saying, “My Lord, my Lord! Had I known this would happen, my Lord, my Lord! The core of the Kaʿba has grown frail, and its structure has weakened, so that birds fall onto it and its stones scatter.” Abu al-Hasan narrated to us, from Baqiyya ibn ʿAbd al-Rahman, from his father, who said: When Yazid ibn Muʿawiya learned that the people of Mecca wanted Ibn al-Zubayr to accept the pledge of allegiance and he refused, he sent al-Nuʿman ibn Bashir al-Ansari and Hammam ibn Qubaysa al-Numayri to Ibn al-Zubayr, calling on him to pledge allegiance to Yazid, on the condition that he grant him authority over the Hijaz and whatever he wished and desired in the way of authority for his family. The two men came to Ibn al-Zubayr and presented to him what Yazid had ordered them to convey. Ibn al-Zubayr said, “Are you ordering me to pledge allegiance to a man who drinks wine, neglects prayer, and hunts?” Hammam said, “You are more deserving of what you have said than he is.” A Qurayshi man punched him, and the two returned to Yazid. He became angry and swore that he would not accept Ibn al-Zubayr’s pledge unless there were a jāmiʿa in his hand.

The Year 64

The death of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. It was read to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: “The Commander of the Faithful Yazīd died in the year 64, on the night of the full moon, in the month of Rabīʿ I. In it, the Kaʿba was burned on Saturday, when three nights of Rabīʿ II had passed.”

The pledge of allegiance to Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam. In it, the Commander of the Faithful Marwān was pledged allegiance in Dhū al-Qaʿda at al-Jābiya. In it, the battle of Rāhiṭ took place in Dhū al-Ḥijja, two nights after the Feast of Sacrifice. In it, Zuhayr’s conquest of the Maghrib took place on the day Aksil was killed. Ḥarmala added in his transmission from Ibn Bukayr: “Ibn al-Zubayr led the pilgrimage.”

The lifting of the siege of Ibn al-Zubayr and the Khārijites’ breaking away from him. Ibn ʿAyyāsh said: “When Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya died, the people of Syria departed with al-Ḥuṣayn, and those of Ibn al-Zubayr’s followers who departed did so. Some of the Khārijites said to one another, ‘Will you not ask him about ʿUthmān, what he says of him?’ They went to him and said, ‘What is your position on ʿUthmān?’ He looked around and saw that his companions were few, so he said, ‘Go away until evening.’ He ordered his companions to be present, and the Khārijites came. They said, ‘What is your position on ʿUthmān?’ He said, ‘I support him in life and in death.’ They said, ‘May God disavow you!’ Then they departed. Najda set out in al-Yamāma, Nāfiʿ ibn al-Azraq set out in Baṣra, and the Khārijites split apart.” Bakkār wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: “Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya died in the middle of the month” of Rabīʿ I in the year 64. His caliphate lasted three years and eight months. Muḥammad said: ʿAbd al-Aʿlā narrated to me that Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya died at the age of thirty-eight. Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh said: Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya sent Yazīd ibn Asad on a campaign to the land of the Romans.

Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Numayr narrated to us: Yazīd appointed ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ over Medina, and ʿAmr led the people on pilgrimage in the year 60, the year in which Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya was pledged allegiance. He was pledged allegiance in Rajab of the year 60. Then he removed ʿAmr from Medina and appointed al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba over Medina. Al-Walīd led the people on pilgrimage in the year 61. Then al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba also led the people on pilgrimage in the year 62. Then he removed al-Walīd and appointed ʿUthmān ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Sufyān. The people of Medina expelled him and expelled the Banū Umayya who were in Medina. ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr led the people on pilgrimage in the year 63, before allegiance was pledged to him.

Ibn Numayr narrated to us: Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya died when fourteen nights of Rabīʿ I had passed. His caliphate lasted three years and nine months. Ibn Numayr narrated to us: Then the people of Syria pledged allegiance to Marwān, and he lived for nine months. Ibn Numayr narrated to us: Ibn al-Zubayr was pledged allegiance in the year 64, and the Kaʿba was burned on Saturday, when three nights of Rabīʿ I of the year 64 had passed. ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr led the people on pilgrimage for eight consecutive pilgrimages, from the year 64 through the year 71.

The siege of the Kaʿba. Khalīfa said: In that year, Muslim ibn ʿUqba al-Murrī died—may God not have mercy on him, and may He curse him. He had set out with the people toward Mecca while gravely ill, until, when he had left al-Abwāʾ behind, his condition worsened. When he realized that death had come upon him, he summoned Ḥuṣayn ibn Numayr al-Kindī and said: “I have summoned you, but I do not know whether to appoint you commander over the army or bring you forward and strike off your head.” He said: “May God set you right; your arrow is yours, so shoot me wherever you wish.” He said: “You are a coarse, uncouth Bedouin. No one has ever been able to get this clan of Quraysh by the ears without their prevailing over his judgment. Go with this army, and when you meet the people, beware of letting them get you by the ears. It must be nothing but standing firm, then taking counsel with the trustworthy, then withdrawing.” Ḥuṣayn ibn Numayr set out with that army. His army continued besieging the people of Mecca until Yazīd died. Ibn al-Zubayr learned of Yazīd’s death before the news reached Ḥuṣayn, and Ibn al-Zubayr called out to them, “Why are you fighting, when your master has died?” They said, “We are fighting for his successor.” He said, “The successor whom he appointed has died.” They said, “We are fighting for whoever was appointed after him.” He said, “He appointed no one.” Ḥuṣayn said, “If what you say is true, the news will come soon enough.” Muslim ibn ʿUqba died in Ṣafar of the year 64. Ḥuṣayn’s siege lasted fifty days, until Yazīd died. Ḥuṣayn set up mangonels against the Kaʿba and burned it on Tuesday, when five nights of Rabīʿ II of the year 64 had passed. During the siege, al-Miswar ibn Makhrama was killed, and Muṣʿab ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf died.

The death of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya

In that year, Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya died at Ḥawwārīn, in the region of Ḥimṣ. His son Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya performed the funeral prayer over him on the night of the full moon, in the month of Rabīʿ I. His mother was Mayṣūn, daughter of Bajdal al-Kalbiyya. He died at the age of thirty-eight. They also said: forty-some years old. His rule lasted three years, nine months, and twenty-two days. He appointed his son Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya as his successor. He confirmed his father’s officials and appointed no one. He remained ill until he died, at the age of twenty-one; it is also said, twenty. Al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān performed the funeral prayer over him. His rule lasted about a month and a half. It is also said that Muʿāwiya died forty days after his father Yazīd, at the age of eighteen.

The death of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya

In that year, Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya died at Ḥawwārīn, in the region of Ḥimṣ. His son Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya performed the funeral prayer over him on the night of the full moon, in the month of Rabīʿ I. His mother was Mayṣūn, daughter of Bajdal al-Kalbiyya. He died at the age of thirty-eight. They also said: forty-some years old. His rule lasted three years, nine months, and twenty-two days. He appointed his son Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya as his successor. He confirmed his father’s officials and appointed no one. He remained ill until he died, at the age of twenty-one; it is also said, twenty. Al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān performed the funeral prayer over him. His rule lasted about a month and a half. It is also said that Muʿāwiya died forty days after his father Yazīd, at the age of eighteen.

The judges during Yazīd’s caliphate. Khalīfa said: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Udhayna al-ʿAbdī was over Baṣra until the civil strife broke out; Shurayḥ was over Kūfa, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUthmān al-Taymī was over Medina, appointed by ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd.

The killing of Mirdās ibn Udayya. During Ibn Ziyād’s governorship of Iraq, the affair of Mirdās ibn Udayya took place. He was Mirdās ibn Ḥudayr, of Banū Rabīʿa ibn Ḥanẓala. He went out with forty men, killing no one and obstructing neither the road nor anyone’s property. Their provisions ran out, their funds were exhausted, and they became destitute, to the point that they began giving alms. Ibn Ziyād sent an army against them, and it defeated them. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥiṣn al-Thaʿlabī commanded the army, and they were killed among his men. Then ʿAbbād ibn Akhḍar was sent, and he killed them all on the bank of Maysān.

He said: Someone who was in a caravan bound for Fāris narrated to me: “We met them, and their horses were being led. Abū Bilāl spoke and said, ‘You have seen what used to be done to us. Perhaps, had we been patient, it would have been better for us. We have fallen into need, so give alms; God rewards those who give alms.’ The merchants brought bags of money and placed them before him. He said, ‘No—only two dirhams for each man. Perhaps we will not eat it before we are killed.’ He took eighty dirhams for himself and his companions. The people of Baṣra sent a force against them, and it killed them.

“The departure of Nāfiʿ ibn al-Azraq and ʿAbd Allāh and al-Zubayr, the two sons of Māḥūz. Nāfiʿ ibn al-Azraq went out and intercepted the people. Ibn ʿUbayyis went out against him; Nāfiʿ was killed, and Ibn ʿUbayyis was killed. He said: ‘We killed five commanders of theirs, and they killed five commanders of ours. When Ibn ʿUbayyis was killed, Rabīʿa al-Sulayṭī became the leader of the people of Baṣra. When Ibn al-Azraq was killed, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Māḥūz became the leader of the Khārijites. Both were killed, and Ḥāritha ibn Badr al-Ghudānī became the leader of the people of Baṣra, while al-Zubayr ibn Māḥūz became the leader of the Khārijites. Ḥāritha withdrew with the people, and al-Zubayr set out for al-Madāʾin.”

Muʿāwiya ibn Qurra al-Muzanī said: “We went out with Ibn ʿUbayyis, about twenty thousand of us. Ibn ʿUbayyis addressed us and said, ‘People, we have gone out seeking God’s reward. Whoever among you shares our view, let him proceed with us; whoever does not, let him stay behind without blame.’ We were reduced to two thousand. We met him at Dastuwāʾ and fought. Five commanders of ours were killed, and they killed five commanders. My father Qurra was killed, so I charged at my father’s killer and killed him. When evening came, a remnant of them remained, and the Ḥarūriyya numbered about five hundred. Ibn al-Azraq and Ibn ʿUbayyis had been killed. We stood, and they stood, looking at us as we looked at them. There was no man among us who would reach out his hand to fight, from sheer exhaustion. The people said, ‘If only we held back from them until night fell.’ Some said, ‘Do not let them recover from their stumble.’ The people preferred the easier course, and reinforcements from al-Yamāma attacked them by night. We could not keep control of ourselves and fled until we entered Baṣra. Then they prevailed, pledged allegiance to Ibn al-Māḥūz, gained control of al-Ahwāz and Fāris, and levied the tax. In that year, Hammām ibn al-Ḥārith and Abū Maysara died.

Ibn al-Zubayr takes the pledge of allegiance for himself

In the year 64, Ibn al-Zubayr called people to himself, after the death of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. He was pledged allegiance in Rajab, when seven nights of the year 64 had passed. He had neither called people to it nor had anyone called them to pledge allegiance to him until Yazīd died.

He said: Abū Ḥurra, the man with the cloak, was a man of the clients, brave, a poet, and a fighter. He said, ‘Ibn al-Zubayr, we shed blood and killed people only for your sake.’ He said, ‘Then whom will you pledge allegiance to besides me?’ He said, ‘Why, then, did you not wait until we ourselves called you?’ He left him, then began to recite:

… The clients have become resentful of the clients, complaining of hunger and war …

… What have we to do with it, and what loss would it have caused us? Which kings have gained dominion over what they were given? …

… We make a covenant with God that we will not break: we will not ask the age for counsel after it has passed …

Ibn al-Zubayr had previously been calling for the matter to be decided by consultation among the community. But three months after the death of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya, he called for allegiance to himself, and allegiance to the caliphate was pledged to him when nine nights of Rajab of the year 64 had passed.

The account of ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād in Baṣra after Yazīd’s death

Ibn Ziyād had addressed the people, announcing Yazīd’s death, and said, “Choose for yourselves.”

Al-Aḥnaf said, “We are content with you until the people have come together.” Ibn Ziyād said, “Go in the morning to collect your stipends.” He set up the register and distributed the stipends. Salama ibn Dhuʾayb al-Riyāḥī came out and called for allegiance to Ibn al-Zubayr in the area of al-Mirbad. Ibn Ziyād suspended the distribution of stipends and consulted his brothers and household about fighting whoever disobeyed and opposed him. They advised him to refrain from that, so he withdrew and went to Masʿūd in Jumādā II of the year 64. He stayed with him for more than two months; he went to the residence only in Shaʿbān. It is also said that Ibn Ziyād stayed with Masʿūd for forty days, and it is said that he stayed with him for three months. The governor’s residence was plundered. Al-Aḥnaf came and said, “No one shall enter Ibn Ziyād’s residence while I am alive.” He protected it and sent men to the treasury, the prison, and the register, fortifying them. The people of Baṣra gathered to appoint an emir over themselves, and they agreed on ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Nawfal ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, whose mother was the daughter of Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb ibn Umayya. Mālik ibn Mismaʿ and Suwayd ibn Manjūf went to Masʿūd ibn ʿAmr to make an alliance with him and return Ibn Ziyād to the governor’s residence. Ibn Ziyād said to ʿAbbād ibn Ziyād, “Strengthen the alliance between them.”

They drew up a document between them, and Masʿūd sealed it with his seal. He also wrote a document for Mālik ibn Mismaʿ and sealed it with his seal. He handed both documents to Dhirāʿ Abū Hārūn ibn Dhirāʿ al-Numayrī, and they placed them in his hand. Then they said to Ibn Ziyād, “Come along so that we may return you to the governor’s residence.” Ibn Ziyād said to them, “Go ahead; Masʿūd is with you. If you prevail, then you will decide what to do.” Masʿūd and his companions set out, intending to go to the residence. Masʿūd’s companions entered the mosque and killed a short man who was in the mosque courtyard. It reached

Al-Aḥnaf, and when he learned of that, he sent to Banū Tamīm. They came, and a man from Banū Tamīm came to Masʿūd, who was standing on his mule in the plaza of Banū Sulaym, and killed him. The Asāwira shot arrows, and people were killed in the mosque. Mālik ibn Mismaʿ fled and went to Banū ʿAdī. The people were routed. Ṭawwāf ibn al-Muʿallā al-Sadūsī went out and took a position at the palace of Aws. The people pelted him with stones, and his horse carried him off and threw him into the flood-channel of Baṣra.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Khaṭmī over the prayers in Kūfa, and Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh over the tax collection, in the month of Ramaḍān of the year 64. He confirmed ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith and placed him over Baṣra for forty days. Then he wrote to Anas ibn Mālik to lead the people in prayer.

The rule of Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam. The people pledged allegiance to Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam in the middle of Dhū al-Qaʿda of the year 64. His mother was Āmina, daughter of ʿAlqama ibn Ṣafwān al-Kinānī.

The battle of Rāhiṭ. In that year, the battle of Rāhiṭ took place in Syria. The people of Syria had pledged allegiance to Ibn al-Zubayr, except for the people of al-Jābiya, those of Banū Umayya and their clients, and Ibn Ziyād. These pledged allegiance to Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam, and after him to Khālid ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. This was in the middle of Dhū al-Qaʿda. Then they marched against al-Ḍaḥḥāk and met him at Marj Rāhiṭ. They fought for twenty days, then al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays was routed. Al-Ḍaḥḥāk and his companions were killed. Marwān had thirteen thousand men, while al-Ḍaḥḥāk had sixty thousand. They stayed for twenty days, fighting each day. Ibn Ziyād said to Marwān: ‘Al-Ḍaḥḥāk is among the horsemen of Qays…’

Qays, and we will not obtain what we want from them except by a stratagem. Ask them for a truce and refrain from fighting. Prepare the cavalry, and when they stop, attack them with it.’ Messengers went between them, and al-Ḍaḥḥāk refrained from fighting. Marwān then charged them with the cavalry. They hurried to their standard without forming ranks. Al-Ḍaḥḥāk was killed, and a group of Qays’s horsemen were killed. Three sons of Zufar ibn al-Ḥārith were killed that day. About this Zufar ibn al-Ḥārith said:

… By my life, the battle of Rāhiṭ has left a wide rift between Marwān and us …

… Show me my weapon, damn you, for I see that the war grows ever more prolonged …

… After ʿAmr’s son and Maʿn’s son followed one another, and Hammām was killed, my hopes promised me security …

… The Kalb have escaped our spears, while the slain of Rāhiṭ are left as they are …

… Before this, you never saw me falter: fleeing and leaving my companion behind me …

… That evening, I rode between the two forces, seeing no one among the people except one on my right and one on my left …

… Would one single day, if I did wrong in it, erase my good days and my fine deeds? …

… There will be no peace until the horses descend among the spears, and I avenge my women upon the women of Kalb …

… For pasture may grow upon the dust of a grave, while the grudges of hearts remain as they are …

In the year 64, Ibn al-Zubayr demolished the Kaʿba and rebuilt it, incorporating into it about seven cubits of al-Ḥijr. After the death of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya, the people of Rayy rebelled. ʿĀmir ibn Masʿūd, the governor of Kūfa, sent Muḥammad ibn ʿUmayr ibn ʿUṭārid against them, but they defeated him. ʿAttāb ibn Warqāʾ al-Riyāḥī was then sent, and he killed al-Barjān; the polytheists were routed. In that year Yūnus ibn ʿUbayd was born. In that year Marwān renewed the pledge of allegiance to himself and to his son after him, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān, then to ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān. This was at the beginning of the year 65.

The Year 65

It was read aloud to Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 65, Marwān entered Egypt at the new moon of Rabīʿ II, then left Egypt in Jumādā II, and died at the beginning of Ramaḍān.

The rule of ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān. The Commander of the Faithful, ʿAbd al-Malik, was invested with the caliphate at Īliyāʾ in the month of Ramaḍān. In that year Ḥubaysh ibn Dilja was killed, and the Commander of the Faithful, ʿAbd al-Malik, offered the sacrifice at Ḥimṣ. Ibn al-Zubayr led the people in the pilgrimage. Bakkār wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: al-Walīd said: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān was pledged allegiance, and he alighted at Baṭnān Ḥabīb. Ibn Numayr related to us, saying: The people of Syria pledged allegiance to ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān. Ibn ʿAyyāsh said: Muḥammad ibn al-Muntashir related to us, saying: al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra related to us. He said: He often used to say to us during that fighting of ours—that is, the fighting against Qaṭarī or the fighting against the confederates—“I doubt, by God …”

“ʿAbd al-Malik will surely rule,” and we would say to him, “May God set the Commander aright: by what do you know this?” He would reply, “Al-Muhallab sent me from Khurāsān to Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya in Syria. I came to him, and by God, I was standing beside his bed, at his head, with my hand on his armrest, when the doorkeeper came and said to him, ‘This is ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān asking permission to enter.’ Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya said, ‘Have we not already taken care of his father’s needs?’ He said, ‘He only asked to speak with you while standing, and not to sit.’ Yazīd said, ‘Admit him.’ Al-Muhallab said: A dark-complexioned man with large, dark eyes and a smooth, handsome face entered. He wore a black turban, which he had let hang down in front and behind, as reciters do. He spoke to him, and Yazīd said, ‘Yes, gladly.’ When he turned to leave, Yazīd followed him with his gaze, then turned to me and said, ‘O Muhallab!’ I said, ‘At your service, Commander of the Faithful.’ He said, ‘The people of the books claim that this man will rule.’ I said, ‘God knows best. By God, if he rules, he will be chaste in Islam and distinguished among his clan.’ He said: This report about al-Muhallab reached ʿAbd al-Malik, and he continued to thank him for it until he wrote to him as he did; then he appointed him governor of Khurāsān.

Khalīfa said: In that year Marwān sent ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād to Iraq with sixty thousand men, in the month of Rabīʿ II. In that year Sulaymān ibn Ṣurad, al-Musayyab ibn Najaba, and ʿAbd Allāh al-Taymī of Taym Allāt ibn Thaʿlaba were killed. In that year Ibn al-Zubayr summoned Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, Ibn al-Ḥanafiyya, to pledge allegiance to him, but he refused. Ibn al-Zubayr imprisoned him in the ravine of Banū Hāshim along with a number of his companions, among them ʿĀmir ibn Wāthila Abū al-Ṭufayl, and threatened them severely. Then al-Mukhtār sent Abū ʿAbd Allāh al-Jadalī, who brought them out of the siege. Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam was then pledged allegiance in Damascus when three nights of Ramaḍān had passed in the year 65. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam—his mother was ʿĀʾisha daughter of al-Mughīra ibn [unclear] Abī al-ʿĀṣ—died at the age of sixty-three. His son ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān performed the funeral prayer over him. His rule lasted nine …

Yaḥyā ibn Qays al-Ghassānī was in charge of his police, his secretary was Sarjūn ibn Manṣūr al-Rūmī, and his chamberlain was Abū Sahl al-Aswad, his freedman. It is also said that he died on the last day of Shaʿbān, at the age of sixty-four.

The Year 66

It was read aloud to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 66 were the first expedition to Baṭnān, the killing of ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād and his companions at al-Khāzir, the killing of Nātil and his companions in Palestine, and the Commander of the Faithful offered the sacrifice at that time in Salamiyya. The plague struck Egypt, and the battle of Ajnādayn took place. Ibn al-Zubayr led the people in the pilgrimage.

Khalīfa said: In that year al-Mukhtār ibn Abī ʿUbayd gained control of Kūfa. At the graveyard of al-Sabʿ he killed Rifāʿa ibn Shaddād, Ḥabīb ibn Ṣahbān, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ, and his son Ḥafṣ ibn ʿUmar ibn Saʿd.

In that year Ibrāhīm ibn al-Ashtar killed Ibn Ziyād at al-Khāzir, in the land of al-Mawṣil, along with Ḥuṣayn ibn Numayr al-Sakūnī and Sharḥabīl ibn Dhī al-Kalāʿ, among a group of the people of Syria. Among the companions of Ibn al-Ashtar, Hubayra ibn Buraym, from whom Abū Isḥāq al-Sabīʿī related, was killed. In that year Najda ibn ʿĀmir performed the pilgrimage. Ibn al-Ḥanafiyya stood with his companions, Najda stood with his companions, and Ibn al-Zubayr stood with the assembled people.

In that year Zayd ibn al-Arqam al-Anṣārī and Asmāʾ ibn Khārija ibn Badr al-Fazārī died. In that year the jurist ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwn ibn Arṭabān was born. Ibn al-Kalbī said: ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim al-Ṭāʾī died during the time of al-Mukhtār.

The Year 67

It was read aloud to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 67 were the expedition to Baṭnān, the killing of al-Mukhtār ibn Abī ʿUbayd, and the killing of ʿUmar ibn Saʿd. The Commander of the Faithful offered the sacrifice in Damascus. Ḥarmala added in his transmission: Ibn al-Zubayr led the people in the pilgrimage.

The Battle of al-Madhār. Khalīfa said: In that year was the Battle of al-Madhār. In that year ʿUmar ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Muḥammad ibn al-Ashʿath ibn Qays were killed. Al-Mukhtār ibn Abī ʿUbayd was killed: Ṭarīf and Ṭirāf, two brothers from Banū Ḥanīfa, entered the palace and killed him. They brought his head to Muṣʿab, who gave them thirty thousand. In that year Abū al-Kanūd, whose name was ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir, a companion of Ibn Masʿūd, was killed. In that year al-Aḥnaf ibn Qays died in Kūfa. Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr performed the funeral prayer over him and walked in his funeral procession without a cloak. It is said that he was the first to walk in a funeral procession without a cloak.

The Year 68

It was read aloud to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 68 was the expedition of al-Rayyān to Yemen with the mawālī, and the Commander of the Faithful led the pilgrimage that year …

In that year ʿĀbis ibn Saʿīd died. The Commander of the Faithful offered the sacrifice that year in Damascus, and Ibn al-Zubayr led the people in the pilgrimage. Bakkār wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: There was famine, so the people of Syria left off campaigning in the year 68. In that year Ibn ʿAbbās died in al-Ṭāʾif. Khalīfa said: In that year Jābir ibn al-Aswad al-Zuhrī sought to obtain from Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab a pledge of allegiance to Ibn al-Zubayr, but he refused, so Jābir struck him sixty lashes. In that year Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī, Zayd ibn Khālid al-Juhanī, Abū Wāqid al-Laythī, and Abū Shurayḥ al-Khuzāʿī, companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ died.

The Year 69

It was read aloud to Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 69 were the later expedition to Baṭnān and the expedition of Ḥassān to Awrās. In that year the companions of Ibn Muḥriz were detained. The Commander of the Faithful offered the sacrifice that year in Damascus. Bakkār wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: In the year 69, ʿAbd al-Malik alighted at Baṭnān Ḥabīb, the year of al-Radgha. The people of Syria held back from campaigning, and one fifth of their stipends was taken from them.

The Year 70

Khalīfa said: In that year the plague of al-Jārif occurred, and many of Anas ibn Mālik’s children died in it.

In that year ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās died in al-Ṭāʾif, and Ibn al-Ḥanafiyya performed the funeral prayer over him.

The year 70. It was read aloud to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 70 the Commander of the Faithful led the pilgrimage. In that year ʿUmayr ibn al-Ḥubāb was killed. The Commander of the Faithful offered the sacrifice that year in Damascus, and Ibn al-Zubayr led the people in the pilgrimage. Bakkār wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: The people of Syria held back from campaigning in the year of al-Radgha, so one fifth of their stipends was taken from them in the year 70.

Khalīfa said: ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ renounced ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān and drove ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Umm al-Ḥakam out of Damascus, where he had been governor. ʿAbd al-Malik marched against him, and the two of them reached an agreement: ʿAmr would be caliph after ʿAbd al-Malik, and ʿAmr would have an agent alongside every governor. He captured the city, and ʿAbd al-Malik entered. Then he betrayed ʿAmr and killed him. He said to him, ‘If I knew you would survive and preserve our kinship, I would ransom you with the blood of the eyes. But rarely do two stallions come together among camels without one driving the other away; then he kills him.’ He began to recite:

… I brought him close to me to guard against his treachery, then I struck like a resolute man, firmly established …

… in anger and zeal for my religion; indeed, the way of the evildoer is not like that of the doer of good …

The poetry is by al-Bāhī; he only recited it as an example. In that year Abū Fidīk, Najda ibn ʿĀmir, was killed. Rāshid ibn ʿAmr sent Abū Hāshim against him, and he killed him. In that year ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb died. In that year Abū al-Ashhab al-ʿAṭāridī, Jaʿfar ibn Ḥayyān, was born.

The Year 71

It was read aloud to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 71 was the expedition to Furasṭā. The Commander of the Faithful offered the sacrifice that year in Damascus, and Ibn al-Zubayr led the people in the pilgrimage.

Khalīfa said: In that year Abū Fidīk, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Thawr ibn Qays ibn Thaʿlaba, moved to al-Baḥrayn. Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr sent ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Iskāf against him. They met at Juwāthā, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and the people of Baṣra were routed.

The Year 72

It was read aloud to Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 72 was the expedition of the Commander of the Faithful to Kūfa …

In that year Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr was killed at Maskin. In that year Ḥassān ibn Nuʿmān campaigned in Awrās for the conquest. The ḥajj that year was led for the people by al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf, who fought Ibn al-Zubayr and led the pilgrimage.

Ibn ʿAyyāsh said: When ʿAbd al-Malik arrived at al-Nukhayla and Muṣʿab was killed, he appointed Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya over Baṣra. He said to him, ‘Honor your men of al-Jafra’—meaning those who fought with him on the day of al-Jafra and supported him against Ibn al-Zubayr’s governors. He appointed them and honored them. He removed al-Muhallab from fighting the Azāriqa and appointed him over al-Ahwāz and the districts of the Tigris. He appointed al-Mughīra ibn al-Muhallab over Fasā and Dārābjird, and appointed Saʿīd ibn al-Muhallab over Arrajān and Sābūr.

Khalīfa said: In that year Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr was killed. During the rule of Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr, al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Ḥadrad died. Muṣʿab was killed when he was forty years old.

In that year Qubayṣa ibn Jābir al-Asadī, Ṣila ibn Zufar al-ʿAbsī, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāmit al-Laythī, Suwayd ibn Manjūf al-Sadūsī, and ʿUbayda ibn Qays al-Salmānī died. It is said that ʿUbayda died during the time of al-Mukhtār ibn Abī ʿUbayd. In that year Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik was born.

In that year ʿAbd al-Malik appointed his brother Bishr ibn Marwān over Kūfa. Ṭāriq ibn ʿAmr, the freedman of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, gained control of Medina, called for allegiance to ʿAbd al-Malik, and expelled from it Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwf, who had been governor for Ibn al-Zubayr.

In that year ʿAbd al-Malik sent al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf to the people of Mecca to fight Ibn al-Zubayr.

In it, the first encounter between them took place in Dhū al-Qaʿda, and in it al-Ḥajjāj set up the mangonel against the Kaʿba.

The Year 73

In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr was killed on Tuesday, with thirteen nights of Jumādā II remaining. He was killed at the age of seventy-three. He was born in the year of the Hijra. In the year 73, ʿAwf ibn Mālik al-Ashjaʿī and Asmāʾ daughter of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq died. In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān ibn Umayya was killed while clinging to the coverings of the Kaʿba. A stone from a mangonel struck ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muṭīʿ, and he died from it. When Muṣʿab was killed, Ḥamrān ibn Abān gained control of Kūfa. He called for allegiance to ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān, and al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf led the pilgrimage.

The Judges

In Baṣra, Hishām ibn Hubayra ibn Faḍāla al-Laythī. Shuʿrayḥ withdrew, so Muṣʿab appointed Saʿīd ibn Nimrān al-Hamadhānī as judge over Kūfa. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUtba ibn Masʿūd. He remained judge until Muṣʿab was killed.

The people united behind ʿAbd al-Malik. ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr led the people in the pilgrimage from the year 64 until the pilgrimage season of 72 arrived. Ibn al-Zubayr performed the pilgrimage with the people, but they did not stand at the Station. Al-Ḥajjāj performed the pilgrimage with the people of Syria, but they did not circumambulate the House.

Ibn al-Zubayr’s chamberlain was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd, the client of Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿa. He entrusted all his affairs to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān ibn Umayya ibn Khalaf. Ibn al-Zubayr’s rule lasted until he was killed: nine years, two months, and some days. He was born in Jumādā I in the house of Abū Bakr at al-Sunḥ, in the year 2.

In the year 73, it was read to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 73, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr was killed in Jumādā I, and Kurayb ibn Abraha descended upon Alexandria. In it, Abrad ibn Habbār went out with the army to Ifrīqiya, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muʿāwiya crossed to Rashīd by ferry. That year al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf performed the pilgrimage with the people.

Bakkār wrote to me, from Ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: In the year 73, Muḥammad ibn Marwān’s campaign was against Sebaste. He engaged the Byzantines and defeated them. Ibn Numayr related to us, saying: Ibn al-Zubayr was killed on Tuesday, with seventeen nights of Jumādā in the year 73 elapsed. Khalīfa said: Al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf performed the pilgrimage with the people in the year 73.

The Year 74

It was read to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 74, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān descended upon Alexandria. In it, al-Kāhina was killed, and Sufyān ibn Wahb went out to Ifrīqiya. In it, Muḥammad ibn Marwān engaged the Byzantines at al-Zāb. That year al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf performed the pilgrimage with the people. Bakkār wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: In the year 74, Muḥammad ibn Marwān raided Andarliya.

Khalīfa said: In it, ʿAbd al-Malik assigned Iraq to his brother Bishr ibn Marwān, who arrived in Baṣra in Dhū al-Ḥijja of the year 74. In it, al-Ḥajjāj demolished the wall of the Kaʿba adjoining al-Ḥijr, removed al-Ḥijr from the Kaʿba, blocked the door at the back of the Kaʿba, and built the wall of the Kaʿba on the side adjoining al-Ḥijr. He led the people in the pilgrimage.

In it, Rāfiʿ ibn Khadīj, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī, and Salama ibn al-Akwaʿ died. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Khaythama died after the killing of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr.

The Year 75

It was read to Ibn Bukayr while I listened, from al-Layth, who said: In the year 75, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān set out for Syria, and Khabbāb ibn Marthad descended upon Alexandria. Ziyād ibn Ḥanāṭa died, and al-Aṣbagh ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz was appointed. That year the Commander of the Faithful, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān, performed the pilgrimage with the people. In it, ʿUmayr ibn ʿUbayd al-Khawlānī went out with the army to Ifrīqiya. Bakkār wrote to me, from Muḥammad ibn ʿĀʾidh, who said: In the year 75, Muḥammad ibn Marwān conducted the summer campaign. The Byzantines went out to al-Aʿmāq in Jumādā I. Abān ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba ibn Abī Muʿayṭ and Dīnār ibn Dīnār met them, and God defeated them.

Al-Ḥajjāj’s Fighting against the Azāriqa

In the year 75, al-Ḥajjāj then left Kūfa and urged the people to fight the Azāriqa. He set out and camped at Rustuqābādh. They deposed him and pledged allegiance to ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Jārūd. They fought, and al-Jārūd and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥakīm al-Mujāshiʿī were killed. Al-Ghaḍbān ibn al-Qabʿatharī and ʿIkrima ibn Rabīʿ al-Fayyāḍ of Banū Taym Allāt fled with a group of men from Iraq, and they reached Syria. They have an account.

The Revolt of Dāwūd ibn al-Nuʿmān

Abū ʿUbayda said: In it, Dāwūd ibn al-Nuʿmān, one of Banū Māzin ibn ʿAbd al-Qays, revolted at Mawqūʿ, in the area of Ṭaff Baṣra. He was the first to make it a place of emigration. Al-Ḥakam ibn Ayyūb al-Thaqafī, the governor of Baṣra, sent against him and killed him.

ʿĀmir ibn Ḥafṣ related to me, saying: Dāwūd revolted; he was from the people of al-Baḥrayn. His father said to him, “Abandon this opinion, and I will give you this garden of mine, one hundred jarībs.” He replied, “Father, your garden has a blemish in it, and I want a garden with no blemish in it.” Then he came to Baṣra and went to Mawqūʿ. Al-Ḥakam ibn Ayyūb sent against him ʿAbbād ibn Ḥuṣayn with cavalry, and Dāwūd was killed. Regarding this, [the poet] says: “Remember Dāwūd: when he sold himself, and gave it freely, seeking lofty gardens …”

Ibn al-Kalbī said: In it, Muḥammad ibn Marwān conducted the summer campaign when the Byzantines went out toward al-ʿUmq from the direction of Marʿash. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān led the pilgrimage.

Abū ʿĀṣim said, from Ibn Jurayj, from his father, who said: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān performed the pilgrimage among us in the year 75, two years after Ibn al-Zubayr was killed. He addressed us in a sermon and said: “To proceed: Those caliphs before me used to eat from this wealth, and others were fed from it. By God, I shall treat this community’s ailments only with the sword. I am neither the weak caliph”—meaning ʿUthmān—“nor the conciliatory caliph”—meaning Muʿāwiya.

“O people, we will put up with all your frivolous talk, so long as no banner is raised and no one leaps onto a pulpit. Here is ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd: his due is his due, and his kinship is his kinship.” He made this gesture with his head, and we said, “With our sword, like this.”

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: Bishr ibn Marwān took charge of Iraq in the year 74 and died at a manzil in the year 75. He was over forty years old.

During Bishr’s rule, Jābir ibn Samura al-Sawāʾī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ; Abū Juḥayfa Wahb al-Sawāʾī; Kharasha ibn al-Ḥurr al-Fazārī; Aws ibn Ḍamʿaj; ʿUbayd ibn Faḍāla al-Khuzāʿī; ʿĀṣim ibn Ḍamra al-Salūlī; Shaddād ibn al-Azmaʿ; ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUtba ibn Masʿūd; and Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Sulamī died.

The Year 76

The Revolt of Ṣāliḥ ibn Musarriḥ and Shabīb ibn Yazīd

In it, Ṣāliḥ ibn Musarriḥ revolted in Ṣafar in the region of al-Jazīra. Muḥammad ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam sent ʿAdī ibn ʿAdī ibn ʿUmayra al-Kindī against him, and ʿAdī was defeated. Muḥammad ibn Marwān then sent Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Sulamī and al-Ḥārith ibn Jaʿūna al-ʿĀmirī against him. They fought fiercely, and Ṣāliḥ ibn Musarriḥ withdrew toward Iraq, but they did not pursue him. He then sent al-Ashʿath ibn ʿUmayra al-Hamdānī against him. They met at Jūkhā and fought fiercely. Ṣāliḥ was carried from the battlefield wounded, then died on Tuesday, with thirteen nights of Jumādā II remaining, in the year 76. Ṣāliḥ appointed Shabīb ibn Yazīd as his successor. Shabīb met Sawra ibn Abjar, then marched on and met Saʿīd ibn ʿAmr al-Kindī. They fought fiercely, then Shabīb withdrew. He came to Kūfa and entered it. There he killed Abū Sulaym, the client of ʿAnbasa ibn Abī Sufyān, Abā al-Layth ibn Abī Sulaym. He also killed ʿAdī ibn ʿAmr and Azhar ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-ʿĀmirī. Ghazāla entered the mosque of Kūfa, recited her daily portion there, and climbed the pulpit; she had vowed to do that. Regarding this, ʿAtbān ibn Waṣīla al-Shaybānī says: “Ghazāla is one of us, praiseworthy, a woman of vows; she has a share among the Muslims’ arrows …”

ʿImrān ibn Ḥiṭṭān al-Sadūsī said, reproaching al-Ḥajjāj: “A lion against me, but in battle an ostrich, with its wings spread, fleeing the caller’s whistle …”

“… Why did you not come out to Ghazāla in battle? Rather, your heart was in the breast of a bird …”

“… Ghazāla shattered his heart with horsemen, leaving behind a sight like yesterday’s departed …”

Then Shabīb left Kūfa, and al-Ḥajjāj sent Zāʾida ibn Qudāma al-Thaqafī against him with a force. They met in the lower Euphrates region, and Zāʾida was killed. Al-Ḥajjāj then sent ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ashʿath, but he did not fight him. He then sent ʿUthmān ibn Qaṭan al-Ḥārithī. They met in Dhū al-Ḥijja of the year 76. ʿUthmān ibn Qaṭan was killed, and his companions were defeated. In the year 76, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Allāh made an incursion in Sijistān. His way was blocked, so he paid money, and they cleared the road for him. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān dismissed him and sent Mūsā ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh. In it, Muḥammad ibn Marwān raided Byzantine territory from the direction of Malatya. In it, al-Aswad ibn Yazīd, Murra ibn Sharāḥīl al-Hamdānī, Saʿīd ibn Wahb al-Khaywānī, and ʿAmr ibn Maymūn al-Awdī died. It is also said: in the year 74.

The Year 77

The Account of the Fighting between al-Ḥajjāj and Shabīb

In it, al-Ḥajjāj sent ʿAttāb ibn Warqāʾ al-Riyāḥī against Shabīb. He encountered him in the rural district of Kūfa. ʿAttāb was killed, and his companions were defeated. That day the horses trampled Zahra ibn Ḥawiyya al-Aʿrajī, who was an elderly man, and he died. A man from Banū Taghlib, named Qubayṣa, was killed; he is said to have been a Companion. Then he sent

Al-Ḥajjāj sent al-Ḥārith ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Zurʿa ibn Masʿūd al-Thaqafī against him. They met at Zarāra, and al-Ḥārith was killed; his companions were defeated. Then Shabīb crossed the Euphrates and camped at al-Sabkha, where he built a mosque. Al-Ḥajjāj did not go out against him for three days. Then, on the fourth day, he went out and sent Abū al-Ward, the client of Banū Naṣr, who was killed by Shabīb. He sent Ṭahmān, the client of ʿUthmān, who was killed by Shabīb. Al-Ḥajjāj himself went out against him and drove Shabīb away from his mosque. They fought fiercely, and Ghazāla was killed. When night fell, Shabīb crossed with three thousand men and met al-Ḥajjāj at al-Anbār. The two sides stood firm until night came between them. Shabīb marched on and came to al-Ahwāz, where Muḥammad ibn Mūsā ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh was. He sought to fight Shabīb and asked for single combat. Shabīb came out against him and killed him. Shabīb went on to Kirmān and stayed for about two months. Then he returned to al-Ahwāz. Al-Ḥajjāj sent Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Zayd al-Ḥakamī and Sufyān ibn al-Abrad al-Kalbī. Shabīb met them at the bridge of Dujayl, and they fought until night came between them. Shabīb set out at dawn, and when he reached the bridge, he cut it. Shabīb drowned, and al-Baṭīn succeeded him. Al-Baṭīn asked for safe conduct, and Sufyān granted it to him; al-Ḥajjāj later killed him. Abān ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān led the pilgrimage. Ibn al-Kalbī said: Al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided Byzantine territory and reached the area between Malatya and al-Maṣṣīṣa.

The Year 78

In it, al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra came to al-Ḥajjāj after he had driven out the Azāriqa. Al-Ḥajjāj sent Sufyān ibn al-Abrad al-Kalbī, who killed Qaṭarī ibn al-Fujāʾa. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Sūra ibn Abjar al-Dārimī and Bādhān, the client of Ibn al-Ashʿath, were responsible for killing Qaṭarī.

In that year, ʿAbd Rabbih, the client of Banū Qays ibn Thaʿlaba, was killed.

In that year, al-Ḥajjāj appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Bakra over Sijistān and appointed al-Muhallab over Khurāsān. ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Bakra sent his son Abū Bardhaʿa, who hemmed them in at the pass. Shurayḥ ibn Hāniʾ al-Ḥārithī was killed, and the Muslims suffered severe hardship and hunger. The bulk of that army perished. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was also killed.

In that year, al-Ḥajjāj sent Saʿīd ibn Aslam ibn Zarʿa to Makrān. Muḥammad and Muʿāwiya, the two ʿAlafī brothers of Banū Sāma ibn Luʾayy, killed him.

Ibn al-Kalbī said: In that year, Muḥriz ibn Abī Muḥriz raided the land of the Byzantines and conquered Azqala. When he was returning, severe rain struck them beyond the pass of al-Ḥadath, and many people were lost in it.

In that year, Ḥassān ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Ghassānī returned from al-Qayrawān and came to ʿAbd al-Malik, who sent him back to Ifrīqiya and added Ṭarābulus to his charge. He came to ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān in Egypt, but ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz did not send him on; instead, he appointed Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr. Ḥassān then came to ʿAbd al-Malik, who ordered him to remain at home. Al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik led the pilgrimage that year. In that year, Zayd ibn Khālid al-Juhanī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ghanm al-Ashʿarī died.

In that year, Shurayḥ ibn Hāniʾ al-Ḥārithī and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib were killed with Ibn Abī Bakra in Sijistān; ʿAmr ibn Ḥurayth al-Makhzūmī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—was also killed.

In that year, al-Ḥajjāj killed Sulaymān ibn Kandīr al-Qushayrī.

The Year 79

In it, al-Ḥajjāj appointed Mujāʿ ibn Saʿr, one of Banū Murra ibn ʿUbayd, over Makrān and ordered him to pursue the ʿAlafī brothers. The two escaped, and Mujāʿ died.

The rising of al-Rayyān al-Nakrī in al-Baḥrayn

In that year, al-Ḥajjāj appointed Muḥammad ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa al-Kilābī over al-Baḥrayn and attached ʿUmān to his charge. He removed Ziyād ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī and appointed Muḥammad ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa. Muḥammad ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa appointed ʿAbd al-Malik ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Rajāʾ al-ʿAwḏī, keeper of the palace of Abū Rajāʾ in the environs of Baṣra. Al-Rayyān al-Nakrī rose against them in a village called Ṭāb, in al-Khaṭṭ of al-Baḥrayn. Maymūn al-Ḥarūrī came to him from ʿUmān. ʿAbd al-Malik was defeated, and Muḥammad ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa fled. He took ship and came to al-Ḥajjāj. Al-Ḥajjāj had already sent Yazīd ibn Abī Kabsha as a reinforcement for Muḥammad ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa, but Muḥammad fled before Yazīd ibn Abī Kabsha arrived.

In that year, al-Ḥajjāj appointed Hārūn ibn Dhirāʿ al-Numayrī over the frontier of India and ordered him to pursue the ʿAlafī brothers. He killed one of them, and the other escaped. In that year, Ibn al-Ḥakam raided the land of the Byzantines and captured mounts at Marj al-Shaḥm. In that year, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided from the direction of Malatya, taking booty and captives.

Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr’s raid in the Maghrib

In that year, Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr raided the land of the Maghrib. Bakr ibn ʿAṭiyya related to me from ʿAwāna, who said: “The first Berber tribe that Mūsā ibn”

Nuṣayr attacked were those who had killed ʿUqba ibn Nāfiʿ. He marched against them in person, killing and taking captives, while their king, Kusaila, fled.” Muḥammad ibn Saʿīd said: “Mūsā killed and took captives until he reached Ṭabna and Ṣanhāja. Their captives numbered twenty thousand. That was in the year eighty-one.” Abān ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān led the pilgrimage. In the year seventy-nine, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Bakra died in Sijistān. In that year, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd died. Between the seventies and the eighties, Hishām ibn Hubayra al-Laythī, the judge, Ḥaṭṭān ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Raqāshī, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Maʿmar al-Taymī, Ṣafwān ibn Muḥriz al-Māzinī, and Ḥabba ibn Juwayn al-ʿAranī died, during al-Ḥajjāj’s first arrival in Iraq.

The Year 80

In it, al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra raided Kashsh and Nasaf, in the lands of Khurāsān, and besieged them until the letter of Ibn al-Ashʿath came to him, calling on him to renounce al-Ḥajjāj. This was in the year eighty-one. Al-Muhallab then turned back from them, retreating.

In that year, Yazīd ibn Abī Kabsha met al-Rayyān al-Nakrī in al-Baḥrayn. With al-Rayyān was a woman of the Azd called Jaydāʾ. They met at the field of al-Zāra, and al-Rayyān, Jaydāʾ, and the bulk of al-Rayyān’s companions were killed. Yazīd then returned. Al-Ḥajjāj appointed Qaṭan ibn Ziyād ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī over al-Baḥrayn. Dāwūd ibn ʿĀmir ibn al-Ḥārith rose against him, and Dāwūd was killed. In that year, the people of Syria were afflicted by a severe plague, so they undertook no raid that year.

Abān ibn ʿUthmān led the pilgrimage. In the year eighty, al-Sāʾib ibn Yazīd ibn Ukht al-Nimr, Junāda ibn Abī Umayya, Abū Idrīs al-Khawlānī, Jubayr ibn Nufayr, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd al-Qārī, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib died. Abū al-Ḥasan said: “ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar died in the year eighty-four.”

The Year 81

Ibn al-Ashʿath renounces al-Ḥajjāj. In it, Ibn al-Ashʿath renounced al-Ḥajjāj in Sijistān and set out, intending to attack al-Ḥajjāj.

Abū al-Ḥasan and Abū al-Yaqẓān related to me that when Ibn al-Ashʿath resolved to march to Iraq, he summoned Dhurā, Abū ʿUmar ibn Dharr al-Hamdānī, clothed him, gave him a gift, and ordered him to incite the people. He would preach every day and speak against al-Ḥajjāj. Then they marched, having renounced al-Ḥajjāj, but they did not mention renouncing ʿAbd al-Malik.

Umayya ibn Khālid related to me. He said that when Ibn al-Ashʿath marched, he recited:

“Kings have been cast down, and beneath his banner marched The trees of al-ʿArā and the ʿArāʿir of the peoples ...”

“... And the most noble of the descendants of al-Araqim—how resolute, Bright of brow, accustomed to bold action ...”

And he recited:

“... A traveler, dwelling beside Jurm: have I brought them A war that divides neighbors who were mingled together? ...”

“... Have you left the women of the quarter out in the open In the courtyard of the house, lighting fires with al-ghibṭ? ...”

Luʾayy ibn Shaqīq ibn Thawr al-Sadūsī came to al-Ḥajjāj and informed him. At once, al-Ḥajjāj conveyed him to ʿAbd al-Malik, who sent him back to al-Ḥajjāj, ordering him to exert himself and be resolute until the troops came to him. Al-Ḥajjāj marched, and they met at Tustar—it is said that this was on the Day of Sacrifice. Al-Ḥajjāj was routed until he entered Baṣra, and Ibn al-Ashʿath pursued him.

Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Zādhān Farrūkh said to al-Ḥajjāj: “Leave Baṣra to him. For those with him who are from Baṣra, when they smell their wives and children, will abandon him.” So al-Ḥajjāj went out to the outskirts of Baṣra, and Ibn al-Ashʿath entered Baṣra. The majority of those who had been with him from among the people of Baṣra then abandoned him.

Muḥammad ibn Muʿādh related to me from his father, from his grandmother, who said: “I heard Ibn al-Ashʿath’s herald say: ‘Where are those who pledged allegiance at al-Rukhkhaj?’” Someone who had heard Quraysh ibn Anas related to me from Ibn ʿAwn, who said: “I saw Ibn al-Ashʿath sitting cross-legged on the pulpit, threatening severely those who had stayed behind from him.” In the year eighty-one, Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr came to Ṭabna, killing and taking captives.

Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik led the pilgrimage.

The Year 82

The battle of al-Zāwiya. In it, the battle of al-Zāwiya took place in al-Muḥarram. Abū al-Ḥasan and Abū al-Yaqẓān related to me. They said: “Ibn al-Ashʿath went out and met al-Ḥajjāj at al-Zāwiya. They fought a fierce battle. On that day, Abū al-Jawzāʾ al-Rabaʿī, ʿUqba ibn ʿAbd al-Ghāfir al-ʿAwḏī, ʿUqba ibn Wassāj al-Barsānī, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ghālib al-Jahḍamī were killed.”

Sallām ibn Qutayba related to me. He said: Sallām ibn Miskīn related to us. He said: “ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ghālib was killed on the day of al-Zāwiya.”

Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad related to me from Ghassān ibn Muḍar, from Saʿīd ibn Yazīd, who said: “Abū al-Jawzāʾ, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ghālib, and ʿUqba ibn ʿAbd al-Ghāfir were killed on the day of al-Zāwiya.” Sulaymān ibn Ḥarb related to me. He said: Ḥammād ibn Zayd related to us from al-Muʿallā ibn Ziyād, from Murra ibn Dabbāb, who said: “I passed by ʿUqba ibn ʿAbd al-Ghāfir on the day of al-Zāwiya, and he was lying fallen. He called out to me: ‘The world and the Hereafter are gone!’”

On that day, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwsaja al-Nahmī of Hamdān was also killed. He was on Ibn al-Ashʿath’s right wing. Al-Ḥajjāj was brought ʿImrān ibn ʿIṣām al-Ḍabʿī and executed him.

ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿUbayd Allāh related to me from ʿUmar al-Bakrāwī, who said: “ʿAbd al-Malik wrote to al-Ḥajjāj: ‘Call the people to pledge allegiance. Whoever admits to unbelief, let him go, except a man who raised a banner or insulted the Commander of the Faithful.’ He called the people to pledge allegiance on that basis. When Banū Ḍubayʿa came, he read the letter to them. ʿImrān ibn ʿIṣām rose, and al-Ḥajjāj summoned him. He said: ‘Do you testify against yourself to unbelief?’ He replied: ‘I have not disbelieved in God since I believed in Him.’ So he killed him.

“Ibn al-Ashʿath was routed and left his army behind. The people fought at the rear of al-Mirbad for three days, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib took charge of them. Maṭar ibn Nājiya al-Riyāḥī rose up and gained control of Kūfa. Ibn al-Ashʿath came to him, and Maṭar ibn Nājiya pledged allegiance to him. Al-Ḥajjāj pursued him, and they met at Dayr al-Jamājim.

“Umayya ibn Khālid related to me from ʿAwāna, who said: ‘There were eighty-one engagements between them at al-Jamājim, all of them going against al-Ḥajjāj except the final engagement, which went against Ibn al-Ashʿath. He was routed. Among the Qurʾān reciters killed at Dayr al-Jamājim were Abū al-Bakhtarī Saʿd, the client of Ḥudhayfa, and Abū al-Bakhtarī al-Ṭāʾī.’

“Ghundar related to me. He said: Shuʿba related to us from ʿAmr ibn Murra, who said: ‘On the day of Dayr al-Jamājim, the Qurʾān reciters came to Abū al-Bakhtarī al-Ṭāʾī, asking him to lead them. He said: “I am a client.” So they appointed a man from the Arabs’”

So they appointed Jahm ibn Zaḥr ibn Qays.

Someone who had heard Sufyān related to me from Abān ibn Taghlib, who said: Salama ibn Kuhayl related to me, saying: “I saw Abū al-Bakhtarī at Dayr al-Jamājim. A man charged at him with a spear and stabbed him.” Ibn al-Ashʿath was routed from Dayr al-Jamājim and went to Baṣra. Al-Ḥajjāj pursued him, and he left Baṣra for Maskin, in the land of Dujayl in al-Ahwāz. Al-Ḥajjāj pursued him, and they met at Maskin. Ibn al-Ashʿath was routed; many of his companions were killed, and many people drowned.

Umayya ibn Khālid related to me. He said: Shuʿba related to us from ʿAmr ibn Murra, who said: “On the night of Dujayl at Maskin, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Shaddād ibn al-Hād, and Abū ʿUbayda ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd were found to be missing.”

ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh related to me. He said: Sufyān related to us. He said: Abū Farwa related to me, who said: “I found Ibn Abī Laylā missing at Sūrā.” Al-Ḥajjāj took many people prisoner, among them ʿImrān ibn ʿIṣām al-ʿAnazī, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Tharwān, al-Aʿshā of Hamdān, and Fayrūz Ḥuṣayn. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Sallām ibn al-Jārūd ibn Abī Sabra al-Hudhalī related to me. He said: “ʿImrān ibn ʿIṣām al-ʿAnazī was brought to al-Ḥajjāj. He said: ‘ʿImrān?’ He replied: ‘Yes.’ He said: ‘Did I not come to Iraq and send you as a delegate to the Commander of the Faithful, though someone like you would not ordinarily be sent as a delegate?’ He replied: ‘Indeed.’ He said: ‘And I married you to the leading woman of her people, Māwiya bt. Mismāʿ, though you were not her equal?’ He replied: ‘Indeed.’ He said: ‘Then what made you go out with the enemy of God, Ibn al-Ashʿath?’ He replied: ‘Bādhān made me go out.’ He said: ‘Then why were you not with your wife in her bridal chamber?’ He replied: ‘Bādhān made me go out.’ He said: ‘Then why were you not at the ruin of Baṣra?’ He replied: ‘Bādhān made me go out.’ A man pulled the turban from his head, revealing that he was shaven. He said: ‘Shaven as well! May God not spare me if I spare you!’ He ordered him struck, and his neck was cut.”

He said: “Afterward, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān asked about ʿImrān ibn ʿIṣām al-ʿAnazī. He was told: ‘Al-Ḥajjāj killed him.’”

He said: “And why was it said that he went out with Ibn al-Ashʿath?” He replied: “He should not have killed him after his saying: ‘... I sent from the descendants of al-Aghar a falcon called Muʿtab, whose dove seeks shelter in the boxthorn ...’

“... When it is cooked over his fire, it cooks us through; When it is cooked over another’s, it does not cook through ...

“... He is the champion when he seeks a prey; No cry of the howler can save it from him ...”

Then Ibn al-Ashʿath marched, intending to go to Khurāsān, and the cavalry pursued him. He left them and went to Rutbīl in Sijistān. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib took command of the people. Al-Mufaḍḍal ibn al-Muhallab, who was governor for his brother Yazīd, met him at Herat, defeated him, and captured some of his companions, among them Muḥammad ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik and al-Halqām ibn Nuʿaym.

ʿĀmir ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn Rustam related to me. He said: Abū Bakr al-Hudhalī related to me, who said: “Among the prisoners were Yazīd ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khalaf of the Ṭalḥāt, al-Naḍr ibn Anas ibn Mālik, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Faḍāla al-Zahrānī, and Saʿd ibn Najd, along with many people from among the Yemenis. Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab released them, clothed them, and sent al-Muḍariyya to al-Ḥajjāj. The first to speak to him was al-Halqām ibn Nuʿaym, who said: ‘May God curse you, al-Ḥajjāj! If this Muzaynī—meaning Yazīd—escapes you—’ He said: ‘Why?’ He replied: ‘... For he is a generous man in releasing his prisoners, And led toward you, in their chains, Muḍar ...

“... He shielded his prisoners from the heat of death, While your people were of least account to her ...’”

He said: “You lie,” and ordered him killed. He related to me, saying: Al-Ḥajjāj said to Muḥammad ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik: “O shadow of Satan, most arrogant of men!”

Are you content to be a muezzin for the slave of Banū Naṣr? He meant ʿAmr ibn Abī al-Ṣalt ibn Kinārā. Then he ordered him killed.

The first engagement between them was on the day of Tustar, on the Day of Sacrifice, at the end of the year eighty-one. The second engagement was at al-Zāwiya in Muḥarram, at the beginning of the year eighty-two. The third engagement was behind al-Mirbad in Ṣafar, on Sunday, in the year eighty-two. The fourth engagement was at Dayr al-Jamājim; the defeat took place in Jumādā, on the fourteenth night having passed, in the year eighty-two. The fifth engagement was in Shaʿbān of the year eighty-two, on the night of Dujayl.

Abū ʿUbayda, from ʿAmr ibn ʿĪsā Abū Naʿāma al-ʿAdawī, said: I came from Mecca while the people were at al-Zāwiya in Muḥarram. I came to al-Ḥuraysh, who was seated beneath a lion skin, wearing it as a mantle. I greeted him, and he returned my greeting and asked my lineage; he recognized me. A man came and said, The Ḥajjāj has brought his battalions out of the trench. He raised his head, looked at the sun, and said, This is no time for fighting. Then another came and said the same thing. Then another came, and he said to a man, Climb up on this stump and look. He said, I see the battalions coming out, and the two armies have already risen. He said, Bring out my horse and my weapons. He put on his armor. I looked at his arm: it was as though it were a lion’s arm. Then he sat on a chair, and the horsemen streamed down. The first to come to him was Abū al-ʿAlj and his son, a client of Quraysh’s Banū Taym. Then Mujāhid ibn Balʿāʾ al-ʿAnbarī came, and Jahḍam ibn ʿAbbād ibn Ḥuṣayn came. Then horsemen of Banū Taym kept streaming down until I had counted sixty. He mounted, and I followed them, watching what they would do. He came to the ranks of al-Azd, urged them on, recalled their deeds, disparaged the people of Syria, and said to his companions, Charge! They broke through the ranks, and they did this repeatedly. He continued doing it until evening. I returned to my lodging. That was Wednesday, at the end of Muḥarram. I could hear their voices for a while during the night; then the voices grew quiet, and they separated. Then on Thursday morning they fought fiercely, and both sides stood firm until night came between them. Then they met on the third day after the sun had passed its zenith, and they fought with thrusts and blows. The Ḥajjāj ordered ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muslim to take the route along the embankment until he reached Baṣra. When the people of Iraq heard of this, al-Ḥuraysh confronted them. They met at the bridge, and thirty of al-Ḥuraysh’s companions were killed. Abū Bakr ibn al-Ḥantaf ibn al-Sijif advanced. A man from among the Syrians struck al-Ḥuraysh on the head, and he was carried to Baṣra. They said, Al-Ḥuraysh has been killed. The two sides stood firm. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwsaja, the commander of Ibn al-Ashʿath’s right wing, was killed, along with ʿAbd Allāh ibn Razzām, Ziyād ibn Muqātil, and Ṭufayl ibn ʿĀmir, all companions of Ibn al-Ashʿath. Sufyān ibn al-Abrad charged, the people scattered, and the steadfast and patient remained. ʿUqba ibn ʿAbd al-Ghāfir was killed among a group of Qurʾān reciters. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Mismaʿ was killed, along with about three hundred. Kathīr Abū ʿUmar, companion of al-Kattān and client of ʿAnaza, was killed, and two hundred clients were killed with him. The people were routed, and Sufyān ibn al-Abrad pursued them until they entered Baṣra; he killed them. Then he returned and killed, in his path, whoever he encountered—four hundred or more.

List of the Qurʾān reciters who went out with Ibn al-Ashʿath: Muslim ibn Yasār al-Muzanī—also said to have been a client of Abū Bakr, and also said to have been a client of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān; ʿUqba ibn ʿAbd al-Ghāfir al-ʿAwḏī, killed in battle; ʿUqba ibn Wasāj al-Barsānī, killed in battle; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ghālib al-Jahḍamī, killed in battle; al-Naḍr ibn Anas ibn Mālik; Abū al-Jawzāʾ, killed in battle; ʿImrān ibn ʿIṣām al-Ḍabʿī, killed after being taken prisoner; Sayyār ibn Salāma Abū al-Minhāl al-Riyāḥī; Mālik ibn Dīnār; Murra ibn Dabbāb al-Harārī; and Abū Najīd al-Jahḍamī

Umayya ibn Khālid told me: Ḥammād ibn Zayd reported to us, from Ayyūb, who said: Ibn al-Ashʿath was told, If you wish them to be killed around you as they were killed around ʿĀʾisha’s camel, bring out al-Ḥasan.

Among the people of Kūfa were Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, ʿĀmir al-Shaʿbī, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Shaddād ibn al-Hād—he missed the night of Dujayl—and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā—he missed the night of Dujayl.

Ghundar told me: Shuʿba told me, from Ḥuṣayn, who said: I saw Ibn Abī Laylā urging the people on during the nights of al-Jamājim. Abū ʿUbayda ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd, al-Maʿrūr ibn Suwayd, and Muḥammad ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik were killed after being taken prisoner. Ṭalḥa ibn Muṣarrif al-Ayāmī, Zubayd ibn al-Ḥārith al-Ayāmī, ʿAṭāʾ ibn al-Sāʾib, client of Thaqīf, and Abū al-Bakhtarī al-Ṭāʾī were killed in battle.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān told me: Ḥammād reported to us, from Ayyūb, who said: No one who fell with Ibn al-Ashʿath failed to wish he had been spared that death, and everyone who escaped them praised God for having kept him safe.

I was told on the authority of Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa: Zubayd saw me with al-ʿAlāʾ ibn ʿAbd al-Karīm while we were laughing. He said, Had you witnessed al-Jamājim, you would not be laughing. I wish my hand—or, he said, my right hand—had been cut off at the upper arm and that I had not witnessed it.

Abū al-Ḥasan said: ʿAwāna said that at Maskin the Ḥajjāj killed five thousand prisoners, or four thousand.

He also said, from al-Ḥasan al-Jaʿfarī, from Mālik ibn Dīnār, who said: Five hundred Qurʾān reciters went out with Ibn al-Ashʿath, all of whom advocated fighting.
