Ṭufayl ibn ʿĀmir ibn Wāʾila was killed. Al-Aṣmaʿī said: ʿUthmān al-Shaḥḥām told me: When the Ḥajjāj brought al-Shaʿbī to him, he reproached him

Al-Shaʿbī said, The countryside became barren for us, our dwelling became desolate, fear clung to us, and tribulation buffeted us. In it we were neither righteous, God-fearing men nor powerful wicked men. He said to him, May God bless your father!

In this year, the year eighty-two, Suwayd ibn Ghafala, Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh—and it is said that Zirr died before al-Jamājim—Abū Wāʾil, Zādhān, Ribʿī ibn Ḥirāsh, Zayd ibn Wahb, Hudhayl ibn Shuraḥbīl, and Abū al-Shaʿthāʾ all died after al-Jamājim; Maymūn ibn Abī Shabīb died at al-Jamājim.

In it the Ḥajjāj killed Kumayl ibn Ziyād al-Nakhaʿī. In it al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra died at Marw. In it Qutayba ibn Muslim killed ʿAmr ibn Abī al-Ṣalt ibn Kinārā, Abā al-Ṣalt, al-Ṣalt ibn Abī al-Ṣalt, Mūsā ibn Kathīr al-Ḥārithī, and Bukayr ibn Abī Hārūn al-Bajalī. In it ʿAbd al-Malik sent his brother Muḥammad to Armenia. Its people met him, and he defeated them. Then they asked him for peace, and he made peace with them and appointed Nubayḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-ʿAnazī over them. They betrayed him and killed him. In it ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān conquered the fortress of Sinān, in Roman territory, in the direction of al-Maṣṣīṣa. In it Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr sent al-Mughīra ibn Abī Burda al-ʿAbdī on an expedition against Ṣanhāja, and Abān ibn ʿUthmān conducted the pilgrimage.

The Year 83

In it the Ḥajjāj appointed Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim over Fārs and ordered him to kill the Kurds. In it the Ḥajjāj sent ʿAmāra ibn Tamīm al-Qaynī to Ratbīl concerning Ibn al-Ashʿath Ratbīl made peace, left Ibn al-Ashʿath to him, and bound him and a number of his household in chains. He set out toward the Ḥajjāj, with a man nicknamed Abū al-ʿAnaz chained alongside him. When he reached al-Rukhkhaj, he threw himself from the top of the palace, and they both died. The head of Ibn al-Ashʿath was carried to the Ḥajjāj. Abū al-Ḥasan told us: When his head was brought to the Ḥajjāj, he recited:

... His fate and folly would have nothing but recklessness; then he met a man of stout arm, fierce ...

... foul of face, valiant, resolute, a horseman, fierce against the necks of champions ...

... So far away and utterly gone be Ibn Wāhiṣat al-Ḥaṣā; he met the Merciful while he was blameworthy ...

Then he sent it to ʿAbd al-Malik, and ʿAbd al-Malik sent it to ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz in Egypt. In it ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān sent his brother Muḥammad to Armenia. They made peace with him, and he appointed Abū Shaykh ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ghanawī and ʿAmr ibn al-Ṣiddī al-Ghanawī over them. They betrayed the two men and killed them. In it ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān raided Roman territory and met the Romans at Sūriyya and Luʾluʾa; the Romans were defeated. In it Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl al-Makhzūmī conducted the pilgrimage.

The Year 84

In it ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān died in Egypt, and ʿAbd al-Malik secured the allegiance for his two sons, al-Walīd and Sulaymān. Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm summoned Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab to pledge allegiance to al-Walīd and Sulaymān, but he refused to pledge allegiance to two commanders. He struck him a hundred lashes. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab said to Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl, If ʿAbd al-Malik wants me to pledge allegiance to al-Walīd, let him depose himself. He said to him, Enter by this door and come out

Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Abū al-Miqdām told me: They passed by us with Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab while we were at school. He had been given a hundred lashes and was wearing a haircloth tabbān. They had taken him away, threatening him with crucifixion. Saʿīd later said, Had I known they would not crucify me, I would not have worn the tabbān for them. When ʿAbd al-Malik heard what Hishām had done to Saʿīd, he said, How badly Hishām acted! A man like Saʿīd should not be beaten with whips: he should either have been beheaded or left alone. Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl al-Makhzūmī conducted the pilgrimage.

In it Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr raided Shakūmā in the land of Africa. He came down upon Awraba, and they fought him. Then God granted victory, and he killed and took captives.

Abū Khālid ibn Saʿīd told me, from Abū Barāʾ al-Numayrī, who said: The Romans advanced against Muḥammad ibn Marwān in Armenia, but God defeated them. This was the year of the conflagration. That was because, after the people’s defeat, Muḥammad ibn Marwān sent Ziyād ibn al-Jarrāḥ, client of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, and Hubayra ibn al-Aʿraj al-Ḥaḍramī, who burned them in their churches, places of worship, and villages. The conflagration was at al-Nashwā and al-Basfurrujān.

Abū Barāʾ said: On that expedition, the mother of Yazīd ibn Asīd was taken captive from al-Sīsajān. She was the daughter of the governor of al-Sīsajān

Ibn al-Kalbī said: In this year ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān raided Roman territory until he reached the land of Ṭaranda. In it ʿAbd al-Malik ibn ʿAbd al-Malik built al-Maṣṣīṣa.

The Year 85

In it Muḥammad ibn Marwān raided Armenia, and he camped there for the summer and winter. Abū Khālid told me, from Abū al-Numayrī, who said: Muḥammad ibn Marwān returned and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Bāhilī. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥātim died, so Muḥammad ibn Marwān appointed his brother ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ḥātim. He built the city of Dabīl, the city of al-Nashwā, and the city of Barḏaʿa.

Ibn al-Kalbī said: In the year eighty-five, while ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik was at al-Maṣṣīṣa, he sent Yazīd ibn Ḥunayn. The Romans met him with a large force, and the troops suffered losses at Sūsana. Maymūn al-Jurjumānī was killed, along with about a thousand people of Antioch, at Ṭuwāna.

Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm al-Makhzūmī conducted the pilgrimage.

In the year eighty-five, Wāthila ibn al-Asqaʿ al-Laythī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ died; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Rabīʿa died, having heard from the Prophet ﷺ.

The Year 86

In it Qutayba ibn Muslim ibn ʿAmr arrived in Khurāsān as governor. The landholders of Balkh met him and marched with him. The king of al-Ṣaghāniyān came to him with gifts and a golden key, and surrendered his territory to him

Ibn al-Kalbī said: In the year eighty-six, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided the lands of the Romans and conquered the fortress of Tūliq and the fortress of al-Akhram, before ʿAbd al-Malik’s death. In it Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr sent al-Mughīra ibn Abī Burda al-ʿAbdī aboard ships, and he conquered Awliyya, the first of the cities of Sicily in the land of the Maghrib.

The death of ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān. In the year eighty-six ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān died. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām told me, from his father, from his grandfather, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father, both said: ʿAbd al-Malik died in Damascus on the middle day of Shawwāl in the year eighty-six, at the age of sixty-three. Al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik performed the funeral prayer over him. ʿAbd al-Malik was born in Medina, in the house of Marwān among Banū Ḥudhayla, in the year twenty-three; it is also said, in the year twenty-six. In it Qubayṣa ibn Dhuʾayb al-Khuzāʿī died, as did Abū Umāma al-Bāhilī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Awfā al-Aslamī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ; and Muṭarrif ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Shikhkhīr. During the reign of ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān, Busr ibn Arṭāʾ died, as did ʿUmar ibn Abī Salama, both of them Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ; and ʿAlqama ibn Waqqāṣ al-Laythī, Ghunaym ibn Qays al-Māzinī, and Umayya ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd

The governors appointed by ʿAbd al-Malik

Medina: When Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr was killed, in the year 72, Ṭāriq ibn ʿAmr, the client of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, gained control of Medina and called for allegiance to ʿAbd al-Malik. When ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr was killed, ʿAbd al-Malik appointed al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf over Mecca, Medina, and Ṭāʾif; this was in the year 73. When al-Ḥajjāj went to Mecca, he used to appoint ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays ibn Makhrama as his deputy over Medina. Then al-Ḥajjāj was appointed over Iraq and departed. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān appointed Yaḥyā ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Marwān; this was in the year 75. Yaḥyā ibn al-Ḥakam departed for Syria and appointed Abān ibn ʿUthmān as his deputy. ʿAbd al-Malik confirmed him, then dismissed him in the year 83 and appointed Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl al-Makhzūmī, who remained governor until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Mecca: Al-Ḥajjāj departed in the year 75 and appointed Qays ibn Makhrama as his deputy over Mecca. ʿAbd al-Malik dismissed him and appointed Nāfiʿ ibn ʿAlqama ibn Ṣafwān, who remained in charge until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Yemen: Muḥammad ibn Yūsuf, until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Baṣra: When Muṣʿab was killed, ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd over Baṣra. He arrived there at the end of the year 72. Then ʿAbd al-Malik dismissed him and added it to the charge of Bishr ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam. Bishr arrived there in Dhū al-Ḥijja, at the end of the year 74. He stayed there for a month, then died, having appointed Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd as his deputy. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān dismissed him and appointed al-Ḥajjāj. Al-Ḥajjāj arrived in Iraq in Rajab of the year 75 and appointed al-Ḥakam ibn Ayyūb al-Thaqafī over Baṣra in the year 75. He remained there until Ibn al-Ashʿath revolted and came to Baṣra.

This was at the beginning of the year 82. Al-Ḥakam ibn Ayyūb joined al-Ḥajjāj, and Ibn al-Ashʿath appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Isḥāq ibn al-Ashʿath over Baṣra. Then he dismissed him and appointed a man from the family of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughaffal, a Gāmidī, according to what Ḥātim ibn Muslim claimed. Then Ibn al-Ashʿath was defeated, and al-Ḥajjāj appointed al-Ḥakam ibn Ayyūb over it.

Kūfa: When Muṣʿab was killed, ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Qaṭan ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥārithī over it for some months. Then he dismissed him and appointed Bishr ibn Marwān for about two years. Then he added Baṣra to his charge, and Bishr departed, appointing ʿAmr ibn Ḥurayth al-Makhzūmī as his deputy. Then al-Ḥajjāj arrived in the year 75 and appointed ʿUrwa ibn al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba over it. It is also said that he appointed Ḥawshab ibn Ruwaym al-Shaybānī. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Barāʾ ibn Qubayṣa al-Thaqafī. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir al-Ḥaḍramī. Maṭar ibn Nājiya al-Riyāḥī drove him out and called for allegiance to Ibn al-Ashʿath. Then Ibn al-Ashʿath came there, and afterward set out for Dayr al-Jamājim, appointing ʿAbd Allāh ibn Isḥāq ibn al-Ashʿath as his deputy. Then al-Ḥajjāj came there after Ibn al-Ashʿath was defeated at al-Jamājim. Then he departed for Baṣra and appointed ʿUmayr ibn Hāniʾ, one of the people of Damascus. He dismissed him and appointed al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī ʿAqīl over the prayer, and Ziyād ibn Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh over the police, until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Khurāsān: In the year Muṣʿab was killed, ʿAbd al-Malik wrote to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khāzim appointing him governor of Khurāsān. He sent the letter with Sūra ibn Abjar al-Dārimī. Ibn Khāzim said to him, “Were it not that I dislike setting Banū Tamīm against Sulaym, I would have killed you. But eat your letter.” So he ate it. Then ʿAbd al-Malik wrote to Bukayr ibn Washāḥ al-Ṣuraymī: “If you kill him or drive him out of Khurāsān, you are the commander.” Bukayr killed Ibn Khāzim and remained governor until Umayya ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd arrived. Umayya dismissed him and appointed Umayya. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra in the year 79. Al-Muhallab died in the year 82, and his son Yazīd succeeded him. ʿAbd al-Malik confirmed him for two years or more. Then he attached Khurāsān to al-Ḥajjāj, who appointed Qutayba ibn Muslim over it. Qutayba arrived there in the year 86, before the death of ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān.

Sijistān: ʿAbd al-Malik appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAdī ibn Ḥāritha ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿAbd Shams over it. Then he dismissed him and attached it, together with Khurāsān, to Umayya ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd. This was in the year 73. Umayya appointed his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn Umayya over it for about three years. ʿAbd al-Malik dismissed him and appointed Muḥammad ibn Mūsā ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh. Shabīb the Khārijite killed him at al-Ahwāz before he reached it; this was in the year 77. Then Umayya was dismissed, and it was attached to al-Ḥajjāj, who appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Bakra in the year 78. ʿUbayd Allāh died in the year 79, and his son Abū Barḏa succeeded him. Al-Ḥajjāj wrote to al-Muhallab, “Send a man from your side to Sijistān.” So he sent Wakīʿ ibn Bakr ibn Wāʾil al-Azdī. Then al-Ḥajjāj appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ashʿath over it in the year 80. He revolted against al-Ḥajjāj and marched to Iraq, appointing a deputy; this was at the end of the year 81. Then al-Ḥajjāj appointed ʿUmāra ibn Tamīm al-Qaynī—or al-Lakhmī. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sulaym; this was in the year 84. Then ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān wrote to al-Ḥajjāj, “Appoint Mismāʿ ibn Mālik over Sijistān,” and he appointed him. He remained in charge until he died. He appointed his brother’s son Muḥammad ibn Shaybān, but al-Ḥajjāj dismissed him and appointed al-Ashʿath ibn Bishr al-Kalbī. Then he dismissed him and attached it to Qutayba ibn Muslim. Qutayba sent his brother ʿAmr ibn Muslim, then Qutayba himself came there. Then he departed from it, appointing ʿAbd Rabbih ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar al-Laythī as his deputy. All of that was in the year 86 and part of the year 87. ʿAbd Rabbih remained governor until Qutayba dismissed him in the year 93.

The judges

Judges of Baṣra: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān appointed Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Asīd over Baṣra in the year 72, when Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr was killed. Khālid appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Bakra as judge over Baṣra. He remained judge until al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf arrived and confirmed him. Then al-Ḥajjāj appointed Hishām ibn Hubayra al-Laythī. Then he appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Udhayna al-ʿAbdī.

Kūfa: When the people united behind ʿAbd al-Malik upon the killing of Muṣʿab, he reinstated Shurayḥ. Then al-Ḥajjāj arrived and confirmed him as judge. Shurayḥ asked to be excused, and al-Ḥajjāj excused him and appointed Abū Burda ibn Abī Mūsā al-Ashʿarī. After al-Jamājim, he asked to be excused, and al-Ḥajjāj excused him and appointed Abū Bakr ibn Abī Mūsā al-Ashʿarī as judge. He remained judge until he died. Then he appointed ʿĀmir ibn Sharāḥīl al-Shaʿbī as judge.

Medina: Ṭāriq ibn ʿAmr, the client of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, gained control of Medina when Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr was killed and called for allegiance to ʿAbd al-Malik. Then, in the year 73, ʿAbd al-Malik appointed al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf over it. Al-Ḥajjāj appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays ibn Makhrama as judge. He remained judge until al-Ḥajjāj departed for Iraq, leaving him as his deputy over Medina. Then in the year 76, ʿAbd al-Malik appointed his paternal uncle Yaḥyā ibn al-Ḥakam over Medina and appointed Abān ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān as his deputy. ʿAbd al-Malik confirmed him. Abān ibn ʿUthmān appointed Nawfal ibn Musāḥiq al-ʿĀmirī as judge. He remained judge until Abān was dismissed in the year 83 and ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm al-Makhzūmī over Medina. Hishām appointed ʿAmr ibn Khalda al-Zuraqī as judge; he remained so until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Syria: ʿAbd al-Malik’s judge was Abū Idrīs al-Khawlānī.

Sind: Al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf appointed Saʿīd ibn Aslam al-Kilābī over it in the year 78. Muḥammad and Muʿāwiya, the two sons of al-Ḥārith al-ʿAlāfī of Banū Sāma ibn Luʾayy, killed him. Then al-Ḥajjāj appointed Mujāʿ ibn Saʿr, one of Banū Murra ibn ʿUbayd, over it in the year 79. Mujāʿ died, and al-Ḥajjāj appointed Muḥammad ibn Hārūn ibn Dhiraʿ al-Numayrī over it in the year 80. He remained in charge until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Al-Baḥrayn: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān sent ʿUmar ibn ʿUbayd Allāh, who killed Abū Fudayk. Then ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Ibn Asīd ibn al-Akhnas ibn Sharīq al-Thaqafī over it. Al-Ḥajjāj appointed Sinān ibn Salama ibn al-Muḥabbiq al-Hudhalī over it. He died, and his son Mūsā ibn Sinān ibn Salama succeeded him. Then al-Ḥajjāj appointed Saʿīd ibn Ḥassān al-Asadī. Then he appointed Ziyād ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī. He dismissed him in the year 79 and appointed Muḥammad ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa al-Kilābī. Muḥammad ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa appointed ʿAbd al-Malik ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-ʿAwḏī over it. Al-Rayyān al-Nakrī rose against him, so ʿAbd al-Malik fled, and Muḥammad fled. Al-Ḥajjāj sent Yazīd ibn Abī Kabsha, who killed al-Rayyān and crucified him. Then Yazīd returned, and al-Ḥajjāj appointed Qaṭan ibn Ziyād ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī over it. He remained in charge until al-Ḥajjāj and al-Walīd died.

ʿUmān: Al-Ḥajjāj sent Mūsā ibn Sinān ibn Salama there; this was in the year [such-and-such] and seventy. Then Saʿīd and Sulaymān, the two sons of ʿAbbād, gained control of it. Al-Ḥajjāj sent Ṭufayl ibn Ḥuṣayn al-Bahrānī, who drove them out of it. Al-Ḥajjāj wrote to him, ordering him to appoint a deputy and return. He appointed Ḥājib ibn Shayba as his deputy; Ḥājib died there, and Ibn ʿAbbād gained control of it. Al-Ḥajjāj sent Mujāʿ ibn Saʿr, then removed him from it and appointed Muḥammad ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa. Ibn ʿAbbād killed him, so al-Ḥajjāj sent Sūra ibn al-Ḥurr, who killed Ibn ʿAbbād. Al-Ḥajjāj appointed Saʿīd ibn Ḥassān al-Asadī over it.

Egypt: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān governed it. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz died in the year 84, and ʿAbd al-Malik appointed his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik over it. He remained governor until ʿAbd al-Malik died, in the year 86. ʿAbd al-Malik had appointed Ḥassān ibn al-Nuʿmān over it in the year 74, and he departed from it on campaign in the year 78. He appointed Sufyān ibn Mālik al-Fahmī as his deputy and came before ʿAbd al-Malik, who sent him back; ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz did not approve him and appointed Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr in the year 79, [then] Badr ibn Sufyān ibn Mālik.

Ifrīqiya: Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr was appointed over it in the year 79 and remained in charge until ʿAbd al-Malik died. ʿAbd al-Malik had previously appointed Ḥassān ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Ghassānī before Mūsā, but ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, while in Egypt, did not put him into office and put Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr into office.

Al-Jazīra: ʿAbd al-Malik appointed his brother Muḥammad ibn Marwān over it. He remained in charge until ʿAbd al-Malik and al-Walīd died.

Armenia and Ādharbayjān: He attached them to Muḥammad ibn Marwān in the year 83, until ʿAbd al-Malik died. Muḥammad ibn Marwān was dismissed in the year 85 and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Bāhilī as his deputy over Armenia and Ādharbayjān. ʿAbd Allāh died, and Muḥammad ibn Marwān appointed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān.

Yamāma: Yazīd ibn Hubayra, then Ibrāhīm ibn ʿArabī al-Laythī, until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

The summer campaign: Mālik ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-Ḥanafī, then his son al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, then Muḥammad ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam, then ʿAmr ibn Muḥriz al-Ashjaʿī.

The Syrian districts

Palestine: His son Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Ḥimṣ: His son ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

The Jordan: Abū ʿUthmān ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam.

Al-Balqāʾ: Muḥammad ibn ʿUmar al-Thaqafī, the brother of Yūsuf ibn ʿUmar.

The pilgrimage season: In the year 73, al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf; in 75, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān; in 76 and 77, Abān ibn ʿUthmān.

In the year 78, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; in 79 and the year 80, Abān ibn ʿUthmān; in 81, Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; in 82, Abān ibn ʿUthmān; in 83, 84, 85, and 86, Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl al-Makhzūmī.

The police: Yazīd ibn Abī Kabsha al-Saksakī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Abū Nātil, Riyāḥ ibn ʿAbda al-Ghassānī. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zayd al-Ḥakamī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Kaʿb ibn Ḥāmid al-ʿAbsī, who remained in office until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Scribe of correspondence: lā Abū al-Zuʿayziʿa, his freedman.

The land tax and the army: Sarjūn ibn Manṣūr the Byzantine. Sarjūn died, and he appointed Sulaymān ibn Saʿd, the client of Khashīn, a clan of Quḍāʿa. He was the first to translate the Syrian register into Arabic.

The seal, treasuries, and storehouses: Qubayṣa ibn Dhuʾayb al-Khuzāʿī. Qubayṣa died, and he appointed ʿUmar ibn al-Ḥārith.

The chamberlain: Abū Yūsuf, his freedman.

The guard: ʿAdī ibn ʿAyyāsh, the client of Ḥimyar. Then he assigned it to Abū al-Zuʿayziʿa, then to al-Rayyān ibn Khālid ibn al-Rayyān, the client of Banū Muḥārib. Al-Rayyān died, and he appointed his son Khālid ibn al-Rayyān, who remained in office until ʿAbd al-Malik died.

ʿAbd al-Malik’s rule lasted from the time allegiance was united behind him for thirteen years, three months, and twenty-eight days.

Chapter 9

The rule of al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik

Then allegiance was given to al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik in the middle of Shawwāl, in the year 86. Al-Walīd’s mother was Wallāda, daughter of al-ʿAbbās ibn Juʾz ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Zuhayr ibn Judhayma, of Banū ʿAbs ibn Baghīḍ.

Al-Walīd was born in Medina, in ʿAbd al-Malik’s house among Banū Ḥudhayla, in the year 52; it is also said, earlier than that.

During the caliphate of ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān died ʿUmar ibn Abī Salama al-Makhzūmī. He transmitted from the Prophet ﷺ; ʿAlqama ibn Waqqāṣ al-Laythī; Zarāra ibn Awfā al-Ḥarashī; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Udhayna, after the year 80 and before the year 90; ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUtba al-Asadī; and ʿUtba ibn al-Nudr al-Sulamī.

The Year 87

In it, Nīzak Ṭarkhān came to Qutayba ibn Muslim, who made peace with him and released the captives in his possession.

Qutayba’s conquest of Bīkand: In it, Qutayba raided Bīkand, from Bukhārā. Its people sought help from the Sogdians, who came to them in a great force. God defeated the polytheists, and the Muslims pursued them, killing many of them and taking captives. Some people took refuge in the city and asked for peace, so he made peace with them and appointed over them a man from Banū Qutayba. He departed from them, but they killed most of his companions. Qutayba returned, and they asked him for peace, but he refused. He took it by force, killed the fighters who were in it, and seized many vessels of gold and silver.

In it, Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr sent his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr on an expedition to Sardinia, in the lands of the Maghrib, and he conquered Qūla. In it, Ibn Nuṣayr also sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥudhayfa al-Azdī to Sardinia; he took booty, captives, and spoils. In it, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik built the Mosque of Damascus.

In this year, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ordered ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz to build the Mosque of the Messenger ﷺ—and he enlarged it. In this year, the plague of the Maidens occurred in Baṣra in Shawwāl. In this year, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided, conquered Fayyim and the Lake of the Horsemen, reached the camp of Qalūdhīmā, and killed and captured people. ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān led the pilgrimage. In the year eighty-seven, Shurayḥ the judge and al-Miqdām ibn Maʿdīkarib died. Abū Nuʿaym said: Shurayḥ died in the year seventy-six. ʿUtba ibn ʿAbd al-Sulamī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—also died. In this year, Shuʿba ibn al-Ḥajjāj was born, and ʿUmar ibn Hubayra al-Fazārī, governor of Iraq, was born.

The Year 88

Qutayba raids Tūmshakath and Armathana. In this year, Qutayba ibn Muslim raided Tūmshakath. Its people met him and made peace with him. Then he marched to Armathana, whose people made peace with him, and he withdrew. The Turks then advanced against them, accompanied by the Sogdians and the people of Farghāna. They intercepted the Muslims, and their commander was the sister’s son of the king of China. They are said to have numbered two hundred thousand. God gave victory to the Muslims and scattered the gathering of the polytheists.

In this year, Muḥammad ibn Marwān raided Armenia, campaigning in summer and winter. In this year, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik and al-ʿAbbās ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided; they were stationed at Antioch and wintered there. The Byzantines mustered a very large force for them and advanced against them. God routed the Byzantines, and many of them were killed; it is said that fifty thousand were killed. God conquered Jarthūma and Ṭuwāna. ʿUmar ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik led the pilgrimage. In this year, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bisr al-Sulamī, one of the Companions of the Messenger of God ﷺ—died.

The Year 89

In this year, Qutayba ibn Muslim raided Wardān Khudhāh, the king of Bukhara, but could not overcome them, so he returned. In this year, Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr sent his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mūsā, who reached Mayūrqa and Manūrqa, two islands between Sicily and al-Andalus, and conquered them. This expedition is called the Raid of the Nobles; the leading men of the people were with him. In this year, Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr sent his son Marwān to the Far Sūs, and the captives numbered forty thousand. In this year, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided ʿAmūriyya and encountered a force of the polytheists. God routed them. ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān led the pilgrimage. In this year, Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Qasrī was appointed over Mecca.

In the year eighty-nine, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Ṣuʿayr died. After eighty and before ninety, Zurāra ibn Awfā, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Udhayna, Maʿbad al-Juhanī, and Ḥumayd died.

Ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥimyarī; Yūnus ibn Jubayr, Abū Ghallāb; Abū Ayyūb al-Azdī; Qusāma ibn Zuhayr; Abū al-Sawwār al-ʿAdawī; Naṣr ibn ʿĀṣim al-Laythī; Yaḥyā ibn Yaʿmar; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and Muslim, the two sons of Abū Bakra; and Khaythama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

The Year 90

In this year, Qutayba ibn Muslim raided Wardān Khudhāh for the second time. Wardān Khudhāh sent to the Sogdians, the Turks, and those around them, seeking their aid. Qutayba met them, and God routed them and scattered their gathering. In this year, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided Syria and conquered the five fortresses there. In this year, al-ʿAbbās ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided as far as Arzan, then returned. ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān led the pilgrimage. In this year, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Miswar ibn Makhrama and Abū Ẓabyān al-Janbī died.

The Year 91

In this year, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik dismissed Muḥammad ibn Marwān from al-Jazīra, Armenia, and Azerbaijan, and appointed Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik over them. In the year ninety-one, Maslama raided the Turks until he reached al-Bāb from the direction of Azerbaijan. He conquered cities and fortresses, and those beyond al-Bāb submitted to him. Al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik led the pilgrimage. In this year, Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī, one of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ—died.

The Year 92

In this year, Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim ibn Abī ʿAqīl al-Thaqafī conquered the city of Qanzbūr, and he also conquered the city of Armāʾīl by treaty. In this year, Qutayba conquered Shūmān, Kash, and Nasaf. Al-Ḥajjāj wrote to him: “March to Rutbīl.” He marched, and Rutbīl made peace with him. In this year, Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr sent his freedman Ṭāriq, who reached Tangier, which is on the seacoast, and crossed to al-Andalus. Its king met him; he killed, captured, and took prisoners, then killed the prisoners and killed their king. Abū Nuʿaym said: ʿAlī ibn Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib died in this year; it is also said that he died in the year ninety-four.

The Year 93

The conquests of Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim al-Thaqafī. In this year, Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim ibn Abī ʿAqīl al-Thaqafī conquered Daybul, then marched to al-Bayrūn. A letter from al-Ḥajjāj came to him: “You are commander of what you have conquered.”

Abū ʿUbayda said: Al-Ḥajjāj appointed him when he was seventeen years old. Concerning this, Yazīd ibn al-Ḥakam says …

“Valor, generosity, and bounty belong to Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim ibn Muḥammad …

… He led the armies for seventeen years—how near that is to noble rank from birth …”

He said: Ibn Kahmas ibn al-Ḥasan related to me; he said: My father related to me; he said: I was with Muḥammad ibn

Bakr ibn ʿAṭiyya related to me from ʿAwāna, who said: Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr raided in al-Muḥarram of the year ninety-three. He reached Tangier, then crossed over, encountering no city without conquering it or its people submitting to his terms. Then he marched to Córdoba, then marched westward. He conquered the city of Bāja, which lies toward the sea, and conquered the city of al-Bayḍāʾ. He sent out armies, and they went on conquering and taking booty.

In this year, Qutayba ibn Muslim raided Khwārazm. They made peace with him for ten thousand persons. Then he marched to Samarqand, and they fought him fiercely. He besieged them until they made peace with him for two million two hundred thousand, on condition that they give him thirty thousand persons that year. He accepted that. In this year, al-ʿAbbās ibn al-Walīd ibn Laʿabd al-Malik raided the land of the Byzantines, and God granted him the conquest of a fortress. Marwān ibn al-Walīd also raided until he reached Khinjara.

In this year, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided and conquered Abī al-Ḥiṣn al-Jadīd, in the vicinity of Malatya. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik led the pilgrimage.

In the year ninety-three, Anas ibn Mālik died.

Abū al-Yaqẓān said: Quṭn ibn Mudrik al-Kilābī performed the funeral prayer over him. Anas had reached the age of one hundred and three years.

In this year, Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyab, ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr, Abū Salama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, Abū Bakr ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām, Maḥmūd ibn Labīd, Khubayb ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr, Jābir ibn Zayd in Baṣra, Tamīm ibn Ṭarafa in Kūfa, and Ibrāhīm ibn Yazīd al-Taymī at Wāsiṭ, in al-Ḥajjāj’s prison, died. It is also said that this was in the year ninety-four.

The Year 94

In this year, Qutayba ibn Muslim raided Kabul and besieged its people until he conquered it. In this year, Qutayba raided Farghāna, besieged its people, and conquered its fortresses. He sent out cavalry, and they conquered al-Shāsh. In this year, Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr arrived from al-Andalus as an envoy to al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, informing him of what God had granted him to conquer and of the wealth and crowns he had with him; he sent him the fifth.

In this year, Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim killed Ṣaṣṣa. In this year, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided the land of the Byzantines and conquered Sandara.

In this year, al-ʿAbbās ibn al-Walīd raided the land of the Byzantines and conquered Antioch and Qārṭa on the coast. In the year ninety-four, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd raided the land of the Byzantines as far as Ghazāla. Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik led the pilgrimage.

The Year 95

In this year, Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim conquered al-Multān. In this year, Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr returned from Ifrīqiya, appointed his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr as his deputy, and carried the wealth on camels and pack animals. With him were thirty thousand persons. He arrived before al-Walīd. In this year, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik conquered the city of al-Bāb in Armenia, demolished its city and laid it waste; nine years later, Maslama rebuilt it.

Maslama conquers Shirwān, Jamrān, al-Barān, Ṣūl, and al-Bāb

Abū Khālid related to me from Abū Barāʾ, who said: Yazīd ibn Asīd related to me; he said: Maslama raided and conquered Shirwān, Jamrān, al-Barān, and the city of Ṣūl, until he came to al-Bāb. Abū Marwān al-Bāhilī related to me from a man of Bāhila who was present with Maslama; he said: Maslama encamped against the city of al-Bāb. A man came to him and asked him to grant him safe-conduct for himself and his household, and to let him show him the city’s vulnerable point. He granted him that. The Muslims entered the city, and the enemy became aware of them, so they fought fiercely. At dawn, one of the Muslim elders cried “God is great,” and God gave victory to the Muslims.

In this year, Qutayba raided al-Shāsh for the second time. News of al-Ḥajjāj’s death reached him, and he returned to Marwān. In this year, al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf killed Saʿīd ibn Jubayr. In this year, al-Ḥajjāj died at the age of fifty-three.

Those who held positions in al-Ḥajjāj’s police force, his guard, and his chancery

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me from his father, from his grandfather; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra from his father; and Abū al-Yaqẓān and others. They said: Iraq was assembled for al-Ḥajjāj, and he arrived in Rajab of the year seventy-five. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿUbayd al-Saʿdī was in charge of Kūfa’s police before al-Ḥajjāj settled at Wāsiṭ, and he added Baṣra’s police force to his charge. When he went down to Baṣra, he appointed his nephew Mawdūd as deputy over Kūfa’s police; when he departed from Baṣra, he appointed over it a police chief from his own men. Then al-Ḥajjāj dismissed him and appointed Ziyād ibn Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bajalī over Kūfa’s police, and ʿĀmir ibn Mismāʿ ibn Mālik over Baṣra’s police. Then ʿAbd al-Malik appointed al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra, who appointed Yazīd ibn ʿUmayr al-Usaydī, then his son ʿUmar ibn Yazīd ibn ʿUmayr. Then he appointed Ziyād ibn ʿAmr al-ʿAtakī. Then Wāsiṭ was built; he was the first to build it. Four men from Syria were in charge of his police force at Wāsiṭ: Mūsā ibn Wajīh al-Ḥimyarī, Mihāṣir ibn Suḥaym al-Ṭāʾī, ʿIkrima ibn al-Awṣāfī al-Ḥimyarī, and Abū ʿAlāqa al-Saksakī. Then Abū ʿAlāqa returned to Syria, and Sufyān ibn Sulaym al-Azdī was appointed.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me; he said: Bishr ibn ʿĪsā related to me from his grandfather, who said: Al-Ḥajjāj passed by us at Wāsiṭ when I was then a boy. Before him were Sufyān ibn al-Abrad and another man, each carrying his spear. ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra related to me from his father, who said: Abū al-Sakan, the freedman of Khashīn—a clan of Quḍāʿa from Ḥimyar—carried his spear.

They said: The scribe of the land tax was Zādhān Farrūkh. When he died, al-Ḥajjāj appointed Yazīd ibn Abī Muslim. The secretary of correspondence was Nāfiʿ, his freedman.

Al-Ḥajjāj died at the age of fifty-three. At the end of al-Ḥajjāj’s governorship, al-ʿAlāʾ ibn Ziyād ibn Maṭar al-ʿAdawī, Sinān ibn Salama ibn al-Muḥabbaq, Ḥakīm ibn Jābir, and Mālik ibn al-Ḥārith died. ʿUqba ibn Ṣahbān died after ninety.

During al-Walīd’s governorship, Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbbād al-Daylī and ʿAbbās ibn Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī died. and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Qatāda and Jaʿfar ibn ʿAmr ibn Umayya al-Ḍamrī at the end of al-Walīd’s reign, and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAdī ibn al-Khiyār, Abū Saʿīd al-Maqburī, and Thābit ibn Abī Qatāda. Bishr ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān conducted the pilgrimage.

Chapter 10

The caliphate of Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik

The Year 96In it al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān died

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra, from his father; and Abū al-Yaqẓān and others [related] that al-Walīd died on Saturday, in the middle of Rabīʿ I. Some said [he died] at the end of the year ninety-six. He was forty-four years old. Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik prayed over him. Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad related to me, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿUmrān, from Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Muʾammal al-Makhzūmī, who said: “Al-Walīd was born in Medina in the year forty-five.” He said: “He died aged fifty-one.” Ḥātim ibn Muslim said: “He was forty-nine.” Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik prayed over him. His reign lasted nine years, five months, and some days.

The pledge of allegiance to Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik

Then allegiance was pledged to Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān. His mother was Walāda daughter of al-ʿAbbās; she was the mother of al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Naming the governors of al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik and al-Ḥajjāj

Over Baṣra: al-Ḥakam ibn Ayyūb during al-Walīd’s reign; then he dismissed him and appointed Ṭalḥa ibn Saʿīd al-Juhanī, one of the people of Damascus; then dismissed him and appointed ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd al-ʿAwḏī, one of the people of Damascus; then dismissed him and appointed Mihāṣir ibn Suḥaym al-Kinānī, one of the people of Ḥimṣ; then dismissed him and appointed Qaṭan ibn Mudrik al-Kilābī; then dismissed him and appointed al-Jarrāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī, who remained governor until al-Ḥajjāj and al-Walīd died.

Kūfa: ʿUrwa ibn al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba al-Thaqafī in the year ninety-five; and Ziyād ibn Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh, chief of police, until al-Ḥajjāj died. Yazīd ibn Abī Kabsha was appointed over it. Ḥarmala ibn ʿUmayr al-Lakhmī [served] until al-Walīd died.

Khurāsān: Qutayba ibn Muslim, until al-Ḥajjāj and al-Walīd died.

Sijistān: It had been under Qutayba, who appointed ʿAbd Rabbih ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmayr al-Laythī over it; then he dismissed him and appointed al-Nuʿmān ibn ʿAwf al-Yashkurī during al-Walīd’s reign, until al-Ḥajjāj and al-Walīd died.

Al-Baḥrayn: Al-Ḥajjāj appointed Qaṭan ibn Ziyād ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī over it in the year seventy-nine. He remained governor until ʿAbd al-Malik, al-Walīd, and al-Ḥajjāj died.

ʿUmān: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sulaym al-Kalbī, then ʿAbd al-Jabbār ibn Subra al-Mujāshiʿī, until al-Ḥajjāj died.

Al-Sind: Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim ibn Abī ʿAqīl in the year ninety-five.

Mecca: ʿAbd al-Malik died while Nāfiʿ ibn ʿAlqama ibn Ṣafwān was in charge there. Al-Walīd retained him for two years, then dismissed him and appointed Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Qasrī in the year eighty-nine. He remained governor until al-Walīd died.

Medina: ʿAbd al-Malik died while Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl al-Makhzūmī was in charge there. Al-Walīd retained him for two years, then dismissed him and appointed ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān in the year eighty-seven, at its beginning or at the end of the year eighty-six. He remained there until the year ninety-three; then he was dismissed and appointed Abū Bakr ibn Ḥazm as his deputy over Medina. Al-Walīd dismissed him and appointed ʿUthmān ibn Ḥayyān al-Murrī over it, who remained governor until al-Walīd died.

Al-Yamāma: ʿAbd Allāh died while Ibrāhīm ibn ʿArabī was in charge there. Al-Walīd retained him.

Al-Jazīra: He retained Muḥammad ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam over it, along with Armenia and Azerbaijan, until he died.

Egypt: ʿAbd al-Malik died while his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik was in charge there. Al-Walīd retained him, then dismissed him. Among al-Walīd’s governors there was Qurra ibn Sharīk al-ʿAbsī.

Ifrīqiya: ʿAbd al-Malik died while Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr was in charge there. He stayed for two years, then set out to al-Walīd in the year ninety-five and appointed his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mūsā as his deputy, until al-Walīd died.

Yemen: Muḥammad ibn Yūsuf was retained there until al-Walīd died.

The Syrian districts

Damascus: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, until al-Walīd died.

Al-Urdunn: His son ʿUmar ibn al-Walīd, until al-Walīd died.

Palestine: Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Ḥimṣ: al-ʿAbbās ibn al-Walīd, until al-Walīd died.

The pilgrimage season: In the year eighty-six, Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl; eighty-seven, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz; eighty-eight, ʿUmar ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; eighty-nine, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz; ninety, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz.

Ninety-one: al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; ninety-two: ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz; ninety-three: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; ninety-four: Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; ninety-five: Bishr ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

The summer campaign: Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, then his son al-ʿAbbās, then his son ʿUmar.

The police: Riyāḥ ibn ʿAbda; then he dismissed him and appointed Kaʿb ibn Ḥāmid al-ʿAbsī, until al-Walīd died.

Correspondence: Janāḥ, his client.

The land tax and the army: Sulaymān ibn Saʿd, client of Khashīn.

The seal: ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, client of ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. He died, so [al-Walīd] handed it to his client Janāḥ.

The treasuries and storehouses: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr.

The guard: Khālid ibn al-Rayyān.

His chamberlain: Saʿīd, his client. It is also said: Muḥammad ibn Abī Suhayl, client of Marwān. Al-Walīd related to me, from his father, from his grandfather; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra, from his father; and others besides them, all agreeing on this.

The judges

The judgeship of Medina: Al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik appointed ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz over Medina in the year eighty-six, at its end, or at the beginning of the year eighty-seven. ʿUmar appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Yazīd ibn Jāriya to the judgeship; then dismissed him and appointed Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm as judge. Al-Walīd then dismissed him and appointed ʿUthmān ibn Ḥayyān al-Murrī; then he appointed Abū Bakr ibn Ḥazm over Medina in the year ninety-three.

And over Kūfa [he appointed] ʿĀmir al-Shaʿbī. In the year ninety-six, Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik placed the Iraq region under Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra and appointed Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān over the land tax. In it, Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab appointed al-Ashʿath ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Jārūd over al-Baḥrayn. Masʿūd ibn Abī Zaynab al-Muḥāribī rebelled against him, so al-Ashʿath withdrew and Masʿūd took control of al-Baḥrayn.

The killing of Qutayba ibn Muslim al-Bāhilī

In it Qutayba ibn Muslim was killed in Khurāsān.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra related to me, saying: My father related to me, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Ḥāḍir al-Asīdī, who said: “I went to Ḥuḍayn ibn al-Mundhir when the people had set out against Qutayba. He said to me, ‘What have the people done?’ I said, ‘I think they will do nothing but kill him, if they reach him.’ He was silent for a long time, then said, ‘Son of Abī Ḥāḍir, how many horses, riding animals, mules, and donkeys do you reckon there are in this army?’ I said, ‘A hundred thousand.’ He said, ‘By God, if they selected ten thousand from those, then selected a thousand from the ten thousand, then sent each one in a different direction seeking someone like Qutayba, they would not be able to find him.’”

In the year ninety-six Ibrāhīm al-Nakhaʿī died, aged fifty-three; and [also died] Ibrāhīm ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf and Maḥmūd ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Khazrajī. Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik sent Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik on the summer campaign, and Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm conducted the pilgrimage. In it al-ʿAbbās ibn al-Walīd raided and conquered Ṭabaras and al-Marzabānayn. Bishr ibn al-Walīd raided and returned, while al-Walīd had already died. In it Jidār was struck down while he was with him in the land of the Romans.

The Year 97

Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab’s campaign to Jurjān

In it Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab raided Jurjān. Abū al-Ḥasan related to us, saying: “Yazīd raided Jurjān during the caliphate of Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. At that time it was not a city, but mountains surrounding it. He moved to Ṣūl and alighted at the lake, an island in the sea. Yazīd had thirty thousand men. Yazīd entered it and seized property, then went out to the lake and besieged Ṣūl. Ṣūl would come out on some days and fight them. They remained thus for months, then withdrew from them in the month of Ramaḍān.”

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather, who said: “Yazīd made peace with them for five hundred thousand dirhams, which they were to pay each year.” Ḥātim ibn Muslim related to me, from Yūnus ibn Abī Isḥāq, who said that he witnessed that with Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab. He said: “He made peace with them for five hundred thousand dirhams, of the weight of five. They sent him garments, ṭayālisān cloaks, and a thousand persons.”

In it Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwf and Saʿīd ibn Marjāna died. Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik raided Barjama and the fort that al-Waḍḍāḥ had conquered, namely the Fort of Ibn ʿAwf. Maslama also conquered the Fort of al-Ḥadīd, Sard, and Sall in the outskirts of the land of the Romans. ʿUmar ibn Hubayra spent the winter at sea, and Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik conducted the pilgrimage.

The Year 98

Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab’s campaign to Ṭabaristān

In it Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab raided Ṭabaristān. The Iṣbahbadh asked him for peace, but he refused. The Iṣbahbadh sought the help of the people of the mountains and the Daylam. They met at the foot of the mountain and fought fiercely. Then God routed the polytheists, and they climbed the mountain. Yazīd sent Ḥayyān al-Nabaṭī, who made peace with the Iṣbahbadh for seven hundred thousand dirhams, four hundred loads of saffron or its value in gold, and four hundred men, each with a burnous, a ṭaylasān cloak, a silver bowl, a silk garment, and clothing. Yazīd accepted this and withdrew from them.

Abū al-Ḥasan said: “The people of Jurjān betrayed the Muslims whom Yazīd had left in charge over them and killed them. When he had finished making peace in Ṭabaristān, he marched against them. They fortified themselves, and their leader was the marzbān. Yazīd fought them for months, then they surrendered and submitted to his judgment. He killed their combatants, took their offspring captive, and crucified them over a distance of two farsakhs. He drove twelve thousand of them to the al-Andar valley of Jurjān and killed them. He made water flow through the valley over the blood, with millstones upon it to grind using their blood. It was ground, made into bread, and eaten. He had sworn to do that.”

In the year ninety-eight Maslama spent the winter in the outskirts of the land of the Romans, and ʿUmar ibn Hubayra spent the winter at sea. Maslama set out from his winter quarters and traveled by sea and land toward Constantinople, crossed the gulf, and conquered the city of the Slavs. The cavalry of Burjān raided Maslama, but God routed them.

Maslama devastated the region between the gulf and Constantinople. In it ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rashrāḥīl was struck down, and ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd conducted the pilgrimage. In the year ninety-eight Kurayb, client of Ibn ʿAbbās, Abū ʿUbayd, client of Ibn Azhar, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Yazīd ibn Jāriya, Qays ibn Abī Ḥāzim, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Kaʿb ibn Mālik died during the caliphate of Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ḥanafiyya died during Sulaymān’s caliphate.

Chapter 11

The caliphate of ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz

The Year 99

In it the Khazars raided Armenia and Azerbaijan, where ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Bāhilī was in charge. God killed most of the Khazars. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz wrote about this to ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz when he assumed the caliphate. ʿUmar appointed ʿAdī ibn ʿAdī over Armenia, and ʿAdī dug a canal that is today called the Canal of ʿAdī.

The death of Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik

In it Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik died at Dābiq.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father; the two said: “Sulaymān died at Dābiq on Friday, when ten nights of Ṣafar had passed, in the year ninety-nine. He was forty-three years old, and ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz prayed over him.” ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz said: “He died aged thirty-three.”

Ḥātim ibn Muslim said: He was forty-five years old. His tenure lasted two years, ten and a half months, or nine and a half months. Sulaymān was born in ʿAbd al-Malik’s house in Medina, among Banū Ḥudhayla. He died at Dābiq, in the territory of Qinnasrīn.

The caliphate of ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz. Then allegiance was pledged to ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān. His mother was Umm ʿĀṣim, daughter of ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.

Those in charge of the police under Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab, and his secretary. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father; Abū al-Yaqẓān; and others. They said: In the year 96, Iraq was placed under Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab. He confirmed Ziyād ibn Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bajalī in charge of the police in Kūfa, and appointed ʿUthmān ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Thaʿlaba al-Hunāʾī in charge of the police in Baṣra, and Ḥarb ibn ʿAbd Allāh in charge of the police in Wāsiṭ. Then he set out for Khurāsān and appointed al-Jarrāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī as his deputy over Iraq. Muḥammad ibn ʿAlqama ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ḥakamī was then in charge of the police in Wāsiṭ. He kept the police in Baṣra and Kūfa as they had been. Yazīd’s secretaries were Kawthar and al-Mughīra ibn Abī Qurra, the client of Banū Sadūs. Yazīd was killed when he was forty-nine years old.

Names of the officials appointed by Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Mecca: he confirmed Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Qasrī there, then dismissed him and appointed Dāwūd ibn Ṭalḥa; then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd Allāh, who remained until Sulaymān died. Medina: al-Walīd died while ʿUthmān ibn Ḥayyān al-Murrī was in charge there. Sulaymān dismissed him and appointed Abū Bakr ibn Ḥazm in Ramaḍān of the year 96; he remained until Sulaymān died.

Yemen: ʿUrwa ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAṭiyya al-Saʿdī, of Banū Saʿd ibn Bakr ibn Muʿāwiya.

Baṣra: In the caliphate of Sulaymān, Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab appointed Sufyān ibn ʿUmayr al-Kindī and dismissed al-Jarrāḥ from it. This was in the year 100. Then Yazīd appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bilāl al-Kilābī there, then dismissed him and appointed Marwān ibn al-Muhallab, who remained until Sulaymān died.

Kūfa: al-Walīd died while Ḥarmala ibn ʿUmayr was in charge there. Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab confirmed him for some months, then dismissed him and appointed Bishr ibn Ḥassān al-Mahrī; then he dismissed him and appointed Sufyān ibn Ḥuraysh al-Khawlānī, who remained until Sulaymān died.

Khurāsān: al-Walīd died while Qutayba ibn Muslim was in charge there. Sulaymān was deposed, and Qutayba was killed. Wakīʿ ibn Abī Sūd al-Ghudānī took charge of the people’s affairs. Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab dismissed him and appointed his son Makhlad ibn Yazīd, then Yazīd himself came there. Then he departed, leaving his son Makhlad as his deputy until Sulaymān died.

Sijistān: Yazīd appointed his brother Mudrik there, then dismissed him and appointed his son Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd, who remained until Sulaymān died.

Sind: Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik wrote to Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān to seize the family of Banū Abī ʿAqīl and hold them to account. Ṣāliḥ appointed Ḥabīb ibn al-Muhallab to command the Indian campaign and Yazīd ibn Abī Kabsha over the tax administration. Yazīd ibn Abī Kabsha stayed there for less than a month, then died and appointed his brother ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī Kabsha as his deputy. Ṣāliḥ dismissed him and appointed ʿUmrān ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Kilāʿī, then combined its command and tax administration under Ḥabīb ibn al-Muhallab.

Bahrayn: Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab appointed al-Ashʿath ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Jārūd there. Masʿūd ibn Abī Zaynab al-ʿAbdī, of Banū Muḥārib, drove him out, seized control of it, and that was in the year 96.

Ifrīqiya: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mūsā ibn Nuṣayr was confirmed there, then dismissed in the year 97. It is also said that Muḥammad ibn Yazīd, client of Rayḥāna daughter of Abī al-ʿĀṣ, was there in the year 97.

Oman: Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Qays al-Laythī there. Then Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab appointed his brother Ziyād ibn al-Muhallab there.

Yamāma: Sulaymān appointed Sufyān ibn ʿAmr al-ʿUqaylī there, then Nūḥ ibn Hubayra.

Armenia: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān. No campaign was made into Armenia until Sulaymān died.

The pilgrimage season: In the year 96, Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm; in 97, Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; in 98, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd.

The summer campaign: Ayyūb ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, then Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

The police: Kaʿb ibn Ḥāmid al-ʿAbsī.

Secretary of correspondence: Layth ibn Abī Ruqayya, client of Umm al-Ḥakam daughter of Abū Sufyān.

Tax administration and the army: Sulaymān ibn Saʿd, client of Khushayn.

The seal: Nuʿaym ibn Abī Salāma, a client of the people of Yemen.

The treasuries, storehouses, slaves, and expenditures: ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, client of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy.

The guard: Khālid ibn al-Rayyān, client of Banū Muḥārib.

His chamberlain: Abū ʿUbayd, his client. During Sulaymān’s caliphate, Sufyān ibn Saʿīd al-Thawrī and Mālik ibn Anas were born. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: I asked the two of them about their ages, and they agreed on that. In the year 99, al-Walīd ibn Hishām and ʿAmr ibn Qays al-Kindī, Abū ʿĪsā ibn ʿAmr, and some of the people of Antioch were among the companions of ʿAmr ibn Qays ibn al-Jaʿd who were struck down. Al-Walīd ibn Hishām captured horsemen from the outskirts of the Byzantines and took many people prisoner.

In that year, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz sent provisions and mounts to Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik in Byzantine territory. He ordered anyone who had a close relative there to send word to him, and sent a detachment with him. He gave the people relief and permitted them to return. In that year, the Turks raided Azerbaijan.

Abū Khālid related to me, from Abū Barāʾ and others, that the Turks inflicted losses on the people. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Bāhilī then went against them. God killed the Turks, and none of them escaped except one fugitive. He came to ʿUmar while he was at Khunāṣira.

In that year, Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab came from Khurāsān. He had not even crossed the bridge when he had already been dismissed. ʿAdī ibn Arṭāʾa arrived as governor of Baṣra on behalf of ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz. Yazīd went to greet him, but he bound him in chains and sent him to ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz. ʿUmar imprisoned him until he died.

In that year, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz sent al-Jarrāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī to Khurāsān. ʿUmar wrote to him: ‘Do not campaign; hold on to what is in your hands.’

Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm led the pilgrimage.

In the year 99, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUtba died. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ḥanafiyya died at the end of Sulaymān’s tenure. Before the year 100, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Aswad ibn Yazīd al-Nakhaʿī and Ḥuḍayn ibn al-Mundhir, Abū Sāsān, died at the beginning of Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik’s caliphate. During Sulaymān’s tenure, Sālim ibn Abī al-Jaʿd died.

The Year 100

Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Khārija ibn Zayd ibn Thābit, Abū Umāma ibn Sahl ibn Ḥunayf, Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm, Busr ibn Saʿīd, client of the Ḥaḍramīs, Abū ʿUthmān al-Nahdī in Baṣra, Muslim ibn Yasār in Baṣra, Tamīm ibn Salama in Kūfa, and Shahr ibn Ḥawshab in Syria died. In that year, Ḥammād ibn Zayd was born.

Chapter 12

The caliphate of Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik

The Year 101

The death of ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz.

In it, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz died.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father: ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz died on Friday, with five days of Rajab remaining, at Dayr Simʿān in the territory of Ḥimṣ. Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān performed the funeral prayer over him. He was thirty-nine years and six months old.

ʿUthmān ibn ʿUthmān related to me. He said: ʿAlī ibn Zayd ibn Judʿān related to us. He said: I heard ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz say: ‘God’s proof against the one who reaches forty has been completed, and he has died at that age.’ Al-Aṣmaʿī said, from Ibn Abī al-Zinād, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz: ‘My father died without having completed forty.’

ʿUmar was born in Egypt in the year 61 and died at Dayr Simʿān in the year 101. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz said: He was born in the year 59. Abū al-Yaqẓān said: He was born in Egypt in the year 61 and died at Dayr Simʿān in the territory of Ḥimṣ. Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik performed the funeral prayer over him.

The pledge of allegiance to Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Allegiance was pledged to Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān. His mother was ʿĀtika daughter of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. In the year 101, Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab entered Baṣra on the night of the full moon in Ramaḍān, and ʿAdī ibn Arṭāʾa, who was governor of Baṣra, fought him.

Names of the officials appointed by ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz.

Baṣra: ʿAdī ibn Arṭāʾa al-Fazārī, until ʿUmar died.

Kūfa: ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Zayd ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, until ʿUmar died.

Khurāsān: al-Jarrāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī. Then he wrote to him, and he appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Nuʿaym al-Ghāmidī as his deputy.

Sijistān: al-Jarrāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh; then he annexed it to ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Nuʿaym, in the year 100. Then, when Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab revolted, he sent his brother Mudrik, but ʿAbd al-Raḥmān prevented him from entering it until Yazīd was killed.

Sind: ʿAdī ibn Arṭāʾa appointed ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Mismaʿ ibn Mālik ibn Mismaʿ there, then dismissed him and appointed ʿAmr ibn Muslim al-Bāhilī, who remained until ʿUmar died.

Bahrayn: Ṣalt ibn Ḥurayth sent some Khārijites from there to ʿAdī. Then ʿAdī dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-Karīm ibn al-Mughīra. I think he was a Bāhilī.

Oman: ʿAdī appointed Saʿīd ibn Masʿūd al-Māzinī. Then ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz appointed ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Ṭalḥa al-Anṣārī there on his behalf.

Yamāma: Zurāra ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

Mecca: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd was confirmed there until he died.

Medina: Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm was confirmed there until he died. ʿUthmān ibn ʿUthmān claimed that Muḥammad ibn Qays ibn Makhrama had governed Medina for ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz.

Yemen: ʿUrwa ibn Muḥammad was confirmed there until he died.

The Jazīra, Armenia, and Azerbaijan: ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān was appointed over Armenia, then ʿAdī ibn ʿAdī; ʿAdī appointed Sawāda, Abū al-Ṣabāḥ ibn Sawāda al-Kindī, as his deputy over the Jazīra.

The Syrian provinces.

Damascus: ʿAbd ibn al-Ḥaṣḥāṣ al-ʿUdhrī.

Jordan: ʿUbāda ibn Nasī al-Kindī.

Palestine: al-Naḍr ibn Yarīm ibn Abraha ibn al-Ṣabāḥ.

Ḥimṣ: Yazīd ibn Ḥuṣayn al-Sakūnī.

Qinnasrīn: al-Walīd ibn Hishām ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba.

al-Balqāʾ: al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAmr al-Ṭāʾī.

Egypt: Ayyūb ibn Shuraḥbīl ibn Abraha ibn al-Ṣabāḥ.

Ifrīqiya: Muḥammad ibn Yazīd was dismissed from it, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muhājir al-Anṣārī, a client of theirs, was appointed. Then Ismāʿīl ibn ʿUbayd Allāh, client of Banū Makhzūm, was appointed. He arrived there in the year 100, and most of the Berbers became Muslims during his governorship. He was of good conduct until ʿUmar died.

The judges.

Judgeship of Baṣra: ʿĀmir ibn Ḥafṣ related to us that ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz wrote to ʿAdī ibn Arṭāʾa: ‘Gather some people from among those with you and consult them about Iyās ibn Muʿāwiya and al-Qāsim ibn Rabīʿa al-Jushamī, and investigate one of them thoroughly.’ ʿAdī gathered some people, and al-Qāsim swore that Iyās was more knowledgeable about judging and more suitable for it than he was. So ʿAdī appointed him.

Sahl ibn Yūsuf related to me. He said: Khālid al-Ḥadhdhāʾ related to us. He said: Iyās ibn Muʿāwiya said to me: ‘This man has summoned me.’ So I went with him and entered upon ʿAdī. Then I came out with a guard. He said, ‘[He/they] refused to excuse me.’ He went to the mosque and prayed two rakʿas, then said to the guard, ‘Bring them forward.’ Before he got up, he had adjudicated seventy cases. Then Iyās left Baṣra over an incident that had occurred, and ʿAdī appointed al-Ḥasan ibn Abī al-Ḥasan.

Judgeship of Kūfa: al-Qāsim ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd.

Judgeship of Medina: Abū Ṭuwāla—his name was ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muʿammar ibn Ḥazm—until ʿUmar died.

The pilgrimage season: In the year 99, Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm; in the year 100, Abū Bakr also.

The summer campaign: he divided it between al-Walīd ibn Hishām and ʿAmr ibn Qays al-Sakūnī.

The police: Yazīd ibn Bishr ibn Yazīd ibn Bishr al-Kalbī.

His secretary: Layth ibn Abī Ruqayya, client of Umm al-Ḥakam daughter of Abū Sufyān.

Tax administration and the army: Ṣāliḥ ibn Jubayr al-Ghudānī.

The seal: Nuʿaym ibn Salāma.

The guard: Ibn Abī ʿAyyāsh al-Alhānī; then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUmar ibn al-Muhājir, the client of the Anṣār.

His chamberlain: Ḥubaysh, his client.

During the caliphate of ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, Muḥammad ibn Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim, al-Qāsim ibn Mukhaymira al-Hamdānī, ʿĪsā ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh, ʿAlqama ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Muzanī, Abū al-Ḍuḥā Muslim ibn Ṣubayḥ, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Murra al-Hamdhānī, from among the people of Kūfa, died. Also [among those who died were] al-Ḥasan ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ḥanafiyya, Yūsuf ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salām, and Abū al-Ṭufayl ʿĀmir ibn Wāthila, who lived to see ʿUmar. In the year 101, Miqsam, the client of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith, died. In that year as well, Dhakwān Abū Ṣāliḥ, Muḥammad ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rāfiʿ ibn Khadīj died. In that year, Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik over Iraq and ordered him to fight Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays al-Fihrī led the pilgrimage.

The Year 102

In it, Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab was killed on Friday, twelve days into Ṣafar, in the year 102. Also in Ṣafar of the year 102, Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd killed ʿAdī ibn Arṭāh and al-Qāsim ibn Muslim, the client of Banū Ghubar, who was the father of Abū Rūḥ and Hishām. Shihāb related to me; he said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra related to me, from his father, who said: I was present at the governor’s residence in Wāsiṭ on the day news came of the killing of Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab. Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd was seated, and then ʿAdī ibn Arṭāh was brought in.

His son Muḥammad ibn ʿAdī, Mālik and ʿAbd al-Malik, the two sons of Mismaʿ, al-Qāsim ibn Muslim, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar al-Naṣrī; he had their necks struck.

In that year, while Yazīd ibn Abī Muslim was in Ifrīqiya, he sent Muḥammad ibn Aws al-Anṣārī on a naval expedition to Sicily, in the lands of the Maghrib, and sent men with him. They took booty and returned safely.

The killing of Yazīd ibn Abī Muslim. In that year, the army rose against Yazīd ibn Abī Muslim and killed him.

Abū al-Yaqẓān related to me, from al-Waḍḍāḥ ibn Khaythama, who said: Dāwūd ibn Abī Hind related to me, who said: Muḥammad ibn Yazīd al-Anṣārī related to me, who said: When ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz assumed office, he sent me to release from the prisons those whom Sulaymān had imprisoned, except Yazīd ibn Abī Muslim, who had vowed to shed my blood. When ʿUmar died, Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik appointed him over Ifrīqiya, while I was there. He seized me, and I was brought before him at night in the month of Ramaḍān. He said, “Are you Muḥammad ibn Yazīd?” I said, “Yes.” He said, “Praise be to God, who has delivered you into my power without a pact or a compact. For a long time I have asked God to put you in my power.” I said, “And I, for a long time, have asked God to protect me from you.” He said, “By God, God has not protected you from me. By God, if the Angel of Death had raced me to you, I would have beaten him to you.” He said: The evening prayer was called, and he prayed one rakʿa. Then the army rushed upon him and killed him, saying, “Take whichever road you wish.”

Abū Khālid said: Muḥammad ibn Yazīd returned from his campaign after Yazīd ibn Abī Muslim had been killed. He wrote to Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik to inform him, and Yazīd wrote to Bishr ibn Ṣafwān al-Kalbī, who was his governor in Egypt, appointing him over Ifrīqiya. Bishr arrived in Ifrīqiya in Shawwāl of the year 102.

In this year, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik sent Hilāl ibn Aḥwaz al-Māzinī to Qandābīl to pursue the men of al-Muhallab. They met, and al-Mufaḍḍal ibn al-Muhallab was killed; the people fled, and Hilāl killed some of the sons of al-Muhallab. He did not search the women or molest them. He sent the children and captives to Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Ḥātim ibn Muslim related to me. He said: When they entered before Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, Kathīr ibn Abī Jumʿa, who was called Kathīr ʿAzza, stood and said: “… Forbearing: when he gains power, he punishes generously with the severest punishment; or, when he pardons, he does not drink …

… So pardon them, Commander of the Faithful, and seek reward thereby; whatever good you reckon for yourself will be recorded …

… They did wrong; but if you forgive, you are able. The greatest forbearance, in hope of reward, is the forbearance of one angered …

… Quraysh drove them from the valleys of Mecca, while a man of Yemen wielded the sharp, Indian sword …”

Yazīd said, “Kinship binds you; there is no way to that. Whoever has a blood claim against the family of al-Muhallab may stand.” He handed them over to the claimants, until about eighty had been killed.

In the year 102, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz over Khurāsān. He went on campaign but achieved no victory. The Sogdians fought him, and men of Banū Tamīm were killed, among them al-Mughīra ibn Ḥabnāʾ, Shuʿba ibn Ẓuhayr al-Nahshalī, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zuhayr al-ʿAdawī. It is said that this occurred in the year 103.

In that year, al-ʿAbbās ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik campaigned and conquered Dabsa in the land of the Romans.

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays al-Fihrī led the pilgrimage.

At the end of the year 102, or at the beginning of the year 103, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik was dismissed from Iraq.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father; and Abū …

Al-Yaqẓān and others said: Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik appointed his brother Maslama over Iraq in the latter part of the year 101 or at the beginning of the year 102, and dismissed him at the end of the year or at the beginning of the year 103. Maslama’s chief of police in Kūfa was Qaṭan ibn Ḥabba al-Kalbī; the chief of police in Kūfa was al-ʿUryān ibn al-Haytham ibn al-Aswad al-Nakhaʿī; and the chief of police in Baṣra was ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sulaym al-Kalbī.

The Year 103

In it, Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik assigned Iraq to ʿUmar ibn Hubayra al-Fazārī. He dismissed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ and appointed Saʿīd ibn ʿAmr al-Ḥarashī. The Sogdians broke their submission and set out with their families and possessions. Saʿīd ibn ʿAmr al-Ḥarashī marched against them, and they asked him for a truce on condition that they return to their lands and pay the jizya. Some of them came out, while others remained. Then they came out against the people, striking them on the right and left. Saʿīd killed them all and took their offspring captive. In that year, Muʿallaq ibn Ṣaffār al-Bahrānī campaigned in Armenia.

Abū Khālid related, from Abū Barāʾ, who said: I met the Khazars with Muʿallaq ibn Ṣaffār at Marj al-Ḥijāra. A group of Muslims was killed. This was in Ramaḍān of the year 103, during the height of winter, and the Khazars gained control of the army.

Muḥammad ibn Marwān led the major summer campaign, and ʿUthmān ibn Ḥayyān the minor summer campaign. In that year, al-ʿAbbās ibn al-Walīd campaigned in the land of the Romans, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays al-Fihrī led the pilgrimage. In that year, in al-Muḥarram, Bishr ibn Ṣafwān sent Yazīd ibn Masrūq al-Yaḥṣubī on a campaign to Sardinia, in the lands of the Maghrib. He took booty and returned safely.

In the year 103, Yaḥyā ibn Waththāb, the client of Banū Asad, from among the people of Kūfa, died, as did Muṣʿab ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik, ʿAṭāʾ ibn Yasār, the client of Maymūna, and ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ḥātim ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Bāhilī in Armenia. In that year, Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ was born.

The Year 104

The governorship of Armenia and the conquest of Balanjar.

In that year, Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik dismissed Muʿallaq ibn Ṣaffār from Armenia and appointed al-Jarrāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī over it. Al-Jarrāḥ campaigned and conquered Balanjar on Sunday, three days into Rabīʿ al-Awwal of the year 104. Then al-Jarrāḥ met Ibn Khāqān two farsakhs this side of the Gate, on the river Aran. They fought fiercely, and Ibn Khāqān was routed. The Muslims pursued them, killing them; they killed a great many and took captives.

Abū Khālid related to me, from Abū al-Barāʾ al-Numayrī, who said: Its inhabitants asked al-Jarrāḥ for a truce on the condition that he relocate them and settle them in the district of Ḥayzān. He relocated them, then marched to the district of Yazʿū and stayed for some days. They asked him for a truce on the condition that he relocate them to the district of Fīla.

Abū Barāʾ said: Sawāda, who was a truthful elder, informed me. He said: We were with al-Jarrāḥ at Balanjar when a Muslim man came out and said, “Who will buy his own life for God?” A group answered him, whose number reached thirty men. They broke the scabbards of their swords and charged the wagons of the outlying settlement. They drove the men away from them and seized one wagon. The wagons were connected to one another. When the wagon rolled down, the rest of the wagons followed it, until they were all in the Muslims’ camp—about three hundred wagons. Then they charged the people of Balanjar. The people came out through the gate, and the ruler of Balanjar escaped on the back of his pack animal. Al-Jarrāḥ gained control of Balanjar. Then al-Jarrāḥ marched to the Turks, who were forty households. They asked him for a truce on condition that they join him against the Khazars. He accepted this from them and marched to Warthān.

In that year, ʿUthmān ibn Ḥayyān al-Murrī and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sulaym al-Kalbī campaigned. They encamped at Sisara and conquered it. ʿUthmān also conquered Qayṣariyya, a fortress among the Roman fortresses.

In that year, Bishr ibn Ṣafwān, while governor of Ifrīqiya, sent ʿAmr ibn Fātik al-Kalbī on a naval expedition. He took booty and returned safely. This was in the year 104. ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Naṣrī led the pilgrimage. In the year 104, Sulaymān ibn Yasār, the client of Maymūna, Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿa, Mujāhid ibn Jabr, Abū Maʿbad, the client of Ibn ʿAbbās, Abū Qilāba al-Jarmī, ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik, and Yazīd ibn al-Aṣamm died.

Ḥātim ibn Muslim related to me, from ʿUthmān ibn Mawhab, who said: Al-Shaʿbī, Mūsā ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh, and Abū Burda ibn Abī Mūsā al-Ashʿarī died on a Friday at the end of the year 103 or at the beginning of 104. Abū Nuʿaym said: They died in the year 104. During the caliphate of Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, ʿIkrima ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām died.

Chapter 13

The caliphate of Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik

The Year 105

In it, Jābān advanced toward Armenia with a great host of Turks, and al-Jarrāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī advanced. They met at a place called al-Zamm, between the Kurr and the al-Rass, in the month of Ramaḍān. They fought for days; then God routed the polytheists.

Abū Khālid said: Abū al-Barāʾ said: Mālik ibn Adham related to me, who said: We were with al-Jarrāḥ and fought them until night separated us. God granted victory to the Muslims.

Ibn al-Kalbī said: In that year, al-Jarrāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh campaigned in al-Lān until he reached towns beyond Balanjar. He conquered some, evacuated others, killed, and took booty. This was in the year 105. Ibn al-Kalbī said: In that year, Marwān ibn Muḥammad led the right summer campaign and conquered a city in the land of the Romans, in the direction of ʿAnj.

The death of Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. In the year 105, Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān died.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām related to me, from his father, from his grandfather; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father; and others [related] that Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik’s mother was ʿĀtika bint Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. He was born in Damascus in the year 71 or 72 and died at Irbid in the land of al-Balqāʾ on Friday, five days before the end of Shaʿbān in the year 105. His brother Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik performed the funeral prayer over him. He was thirty-three or thirty-four years old, and his rule lasted four years and one month. Jarīr said …

… “He clothed himself in the robe of a king who had not usurped it before the age of thirty; indeed, kingship is fleeting …”

The pledge of allegiance to Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik was pledged allegiance to. His mother was Umm Hishām, daughter of Ismāʿīl ibn Hishām al-Makhzūmī.

The names of the governors under Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Medina: He dismissed Abū Bakr ibn Ḥazm from it and appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays al-Fihrī over it in the year 101. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn ʿAbd Allāh of Banū Naṣr ibn Muʿāwiya in the year 104. He remained in office there until Yazīd died.

Mecca: He dismissed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd from it and joined it with al-Ṭāʾif under ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays in the year 103. Then he dismissed him and joined it with al-Ṭāʾif under ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Naṣrī in the year 104, where he remained until Yazīd died.

Yemen: ʿUrwa ibn Muḥammad was confirmed in office over it.

Baṣra: Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab rebelled and arrived in Baṣra on the Night of Decree in Ramaḍān of the year 101. ʿAdī ibn Arṭāh was there. Yazīd prevailed over it and imprisoned him. Then Yazīd set out for Wāsiṭ and left his brother Marwān ibn al-Muhallab as his deputy over Baṣra. When Yazīd was killed, in Ṣafar of the year 102, the people of Baṣra agreed upon Shabīb al-Māzinī, Abū ʿĪsā ibn Shabīb. Then Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik arrived, while he was over Iraq; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sulaym al-Kalbī was in charge of the garrison. Then Maslama appointed ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Bishr ibn Marwān. Then Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik appointed ʿUmar ibn Hubayra al-Fazārī over Iraq. He arrived in the year 103 and appointed Saʿīd ibn ʿAmr al-Ḥarashī over Baṣra, then Ḥassān ibn ʿAbd …

ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. Masʿūd al-Fazārī, from among the people of Damascus; then Firās b. Sumayy al-Fazārī, who was the husband of ʿUmar b. Hubayra’s mother, until Yazīd died.

Kūfa: ʿUmar b. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz died while ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd b. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. Zayd b. al-Khaṭṭāb was in charge of it. Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik confirmed him in office. Then, when Maslama b. ʿAbd al-Malik was governor of Iraq, he dismissed him and appointed Muḥammad b. ʿAmr b. al-Walīd b. ʿUqba b. Abī Muʿayṭ over it. Then Ibn Hubayra dismissed him in the year 103 and appointed al-Ṣaʿr b. ʿAbd Allāh of Murra Ghatafān, who remained in office until Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Khurāsān: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. Nuʿaym al-Ghāmidī was there. When Yazīd b. al-Muhallab rebelled, he sent his brother Mudrik, but ʿAbd al-Raḥmān prevented him from entering. When Maslama arrived in Iraq, he sent Saʿīd b. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz b. al-Ḥārith b. al-Ḥakam b. Abī al-ʿĀṣ. Ibn Hubayra confirmed him in office, then dismissed him and appointed Saʿīd b. ʿAmr al-Ḥarashī in the year 103. Then he dismissed him and appointed Muslim b. Saʿīd b. Aslam b. Zarʿa al-Kilābī in the year 104.

Sijistān: Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik appointed al-Qaʿqāʿ b. Suwayd, of Banū Munqir b. ʿUbayd and from among the people of Kūfa, over it. Ibn Hubayra dismissed him and appointed al-Sayyāl b. al-Mundhir b. ʿAwf b. al-Nuʿmān.

The Sind: ʿUmar died while ʿAmr b. Muslim was in charge of it. Then Yazīd b. al-Muhallab appointed Fulān al-Shaybānī over it. When Yazīd b. Waddāʿ b. Ḥumayd al-Azdī gained control of Baṣra, he remained in office there until Hilāl b. Aḥwaz arrived there on behalf of Maslama b. ʿAbd al-Malik. That was in the year 102. Then Ibn Hubayra appointed ʿUbayd Allāh b. ʿAlī al-Sulamī over it in the year 103. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd b. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān of Murra Ghatafān, who remained in office until Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik died.

Bahrain and Yamāma: Ibrāhīm b. ʿArabī was restored to authority over them.

Armenia: Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Muʿallaq b. Ṣaffār b. Falḥas b. Janb al-Jammār b. Mawqid al-Nār al-Bahrānī, from among the people of Ḥimṣ, over it in the year 103. Then he dismissed him in the year 104 and appointed al-Jarrāḥ b. ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī.

The Jazīra: Fāyid b. Muḥammad al-Kindī and al-ʿAris b. Qays b. Shuʿba b. al-Arqam al-Kindī.

Ifriqiya: Yazīd b. Abī Muslim, in Dhū al-Qaʿda of the year 101; he was killed there. Then Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Bishr b. Ṣafwān in the year 102. Then Bishr went out as a delegate to Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik and appointed Yaḥyā b. Māʿiṣa al-Kalbī as his deputy in the year 105. He arrived after Yazīd had died.

Egypt: Bishr b. Ṣafwān al-Kalbī; then he appointed him over Ifriqiya.

The judges

The judgeship of Baṣra: Maslama b. ʿAbd al-Malik appointed ʿAbd al-Malik b. Bishr b. Marwān over Baṣra as judge. ʿAbd al-Malik b. Bishr appointed al-Naḍr b. Anas b. Mālik as judge. Then Maslama b. ʿAbd al-Malik appointed Mūsā b. Anas b. Mālik in the year 102. Then Ibn Hubayra arrived and appointed ʿAbd al-Malik b. Yaʿlā in the year 103.

Kūfa: He confirmed al-Qāsim b. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. ʿAbd Allāh b. Masʿūd in office there. Then, in the year 103, he dismissed him and appointed al-Ḥusayn b. al-Ḥasan al-Kindī as judge.

Medina: Yazīd appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. al-Ḍaḥḥāk b. Qays over it, and he appointed Maslama b. ʿAbd Allāh b. Salama al-Makhzūmī as judge. Then he held office in Baṣra in the year 104. ʿAbd al-Wāḥid b. ʿAbd Allāh al-Naṣrī appointed Saʿd b. Ibrāhīm b. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. ʿAwf as judge, then dismissed him and appointed Saʿīd b. Sulaymān b. Zayd b. Thābit, who remained in office until Yazīd died.

The pilgrimage season: in the years 101, 102, and 103, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. al-Ḍaḥḥāk b. Qays al-Fihrī; and in the year 104, ʿAbd al-Wāḥid al-Naṣrī.

The summer campaign: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. Sulaym al-Kalbī, until Yazīd died.

The police: Kaʿb b. Ḥāmid al-ʿAbsī, until he died.

The land tax, the army, and correspondence: Ṣāliḥ b. Jubayr al-Ghadānī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Usāma b. Zayd, a client of the people of Yemen.

The signet, the treasuries, and the public funds: Muṭayr, his client. Ḥātim b. Muslim said: “Usāma b. Zayd was in charge of the signet.”

The guard: Ghaylān, the father-in-law of Abū Maʿn. Ḥātim said: “Abū Mālik al-Saksakī was in charge of the guard.”

His chamberlain: Khālid, his client.

Ibn Hubayra’s governorship of Iraq and those who served in his police and chancery

ʿAbd Allāh b. al-Mughīra related to me, from his father; and al-Walīd b. Hishām, from his father, from his grandfather; and others. They said: “Iraq was placed under ʿUmar b. Hubayra al-Fazārī in the year 103, from its beginning. His police at Wāsiṭ were Suwayd al-Murrī, Abū Ziyād b. Suwayd, and Ḥawthara b. Suhayl al-Bāhilī. His police in Kūfa were Muḥammad b. Manẓūr al-Asadī, and his police in Baṣra were Ibn Riyāṭ. His scribe was a man from Syria called ʿUthmān, and Saʿd b. ʿAṭiyya. Ibn Hubayra died when he was a little over fifty years old.”

During Ibn Hubayra’s governorship, Saʿd b. ʿUbayda al-Sulamī, from among the people of Kūfa, died, as did Abū Mijlaz, Muwarriq al-ʿIjlī, and Abū al-Salīl.

During Ibn Hubayra’s governorship, Masʿūd b. Abī Hubayra rebelled; Masʿūd b. Abī Zaynab rebelled and gained control of Bahrain and Yamāma. Sufyān b. ʿAmr al-ʿUqaylī killed him.

Also in the year 105, Ḥumayd b. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. ʿAwf died, as did ʿAmāra b. Khuzayma b. Thābit, Sinān b. Abī Sinān al-Duʾalī, ʿIkrima the client of Ibn ʿAbbās, Abū Rajāʾ al-ʿUṭāridī, al-Musayyib b. Rāfiʿ, and al-Ḍaḥḥāk b. Muzāḥim. During the governorship of Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik, Abān b. ʿUthmān died, as did ʿUbayd b. Ḥunayn, the client of the family of Zayd b. al-Khaṭṭāb.

The Year 106

In it, Muslim b. Saʿīd b. Aslam b. Zarʿa campaigned in Farghāna. The Turkish forces met him; he killed the nephew of the Khāqān and a group of the polytheists. This was during Ibn Hubayra’s governorship. Then Khālid b. ʿAbd Allāh al-Qasrī arrived as governor over Iraq. Khālid appointed his brother Asad b. ʿAbd Allāh over Khurāsān. He met Muslim b. Saʿīd, and the army returned. That was in the month of Rabīʿ al-Ākhir of the year 106.

In it, al-Jarrāḥ b. ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī campaigned from the lands of Armenia. Abū Khālid related to me, from Abū Barāʾ al-Numayrī, who said: “Al-Jarrāḥ b. ʿAbd Allāh al-Ḥakamī advanced deep into the land of the Khazars.
