In it, Jaʿfar ibn Abī Jaʿfar, Commander of the Faithful—Abū ʿĪsā ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Jaʿfar—led the pilgrimage. In it, al-Mīdh entered from the sea, and they came to the Tigris at Baṣra.

In it, Sulaymān ibn Mihrān al-Aʿmash, Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā, Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn Ḥusayn, and Muḥammad ibn ʿAjlān, client of Fāṭima bt. ʿUtba, died.

The Year 149

In it, Abū Jaʿfar dispatched al-Ḥasan ibn Qaḥṭaba. He reached Kamakh in the lands of al-Jazīra, but its people resisted, so he withdrew without accomplishing anything.

In it, Ashnāshīsh revolted, and the Commander of the Faithful sent Jibrīl ibn Yaḥyā and Muʿādh ibn Muslim against him; he defeated them.

In it, the Mīdh, Mahalbān, entered the Tigris at Baṣra.

In it, the enemy encountered Abū Jayfar at Khārik, and he and those aboard his vessel were struck.

In it, Zakariyyā ibn Abī Zāʾida, Kahmas ibn al-Ḥasan, Thābit ibn ʿAmāra, ʿImrān ibn Ḥudayr, al-Muthannā ibn al-Ṣabbāḥ, al-Waḍīn ibn ʿAṭāʾ, and Ḥarām ibn ʿUthmān al-Anṣārī died.

The Year 150

In it, ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib led the pilgrimage.

In it, Ibrāhīm ibn Yazīd al-Khūzī died.

In it, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Jurayj and Abū Ḥanīfa al-Nuʿmān ibn Thābit died.

In it, Ashnāshīsh was killed.

In it, ʿUqba ibn Salm left Baṣra and appointed his son Nāfiʿ ibn ʿUqba as his deputy. He was dismissed, and Jābir ibn Tawba al-Kilābī was appointed.

The Year 151

In it, the Mīdh entered the Tigris at Baṣra, and Abū ʿUbayda al-Saʿdī confronted them.

In it, Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī led the pilgrimage.

In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwn, Ḥanẓala ibn Abī Sufyān al-Jumaḥī, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir al-Aslamī, one of the people of Medina, died.

In it, Maʿn ibn Zāʾida was killed in Sijistān. The Khārijites killed him treacherously; there was no summer campaign.

The Year 152

Abū Jaʿfar al-Manṣūr, Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

ʿAbd al-Wahhāb ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad went on the summer campaign, but did not penetrate [the enemy’s territory].

In it, Jābir ibn Tawba was dismissed from Baṣra, and Yazīd ibn Manṣūr was appointed for a month; then he was dismissed, and Abū al-Jamal was appointed for a second term.

In it, Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, author of the Sīra, Ṣāliḥ ibn Rustum al-Khazzāz, Abū ʿĀmir, Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAmr al-Ḥaḍramī, and ʿAbbād ibn Manṣūr died.

The Year 153

Al-Mahdī, son of the Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

In it, the Mīdh entered the Commander’s River, in the Tigris at Baṣra; they killed and took captives.

Naḍla related to me that he witnessed them at the Commander’s River and fought them, with a group alongside him, until they reached their vessels; they recovered what was in their hands.

In it, Hishām ibn Abī ʿAbd Allāh al-Dastawāʾī, Abū Makīn Nūḥ ibn Jaʿūna, Abān ibn Ṣamʿa, Fiṭr ibn Khalīfa, Maḥall ibn Muḥriz, al-Ḥasan ibn ʿUmāra, Misʿar ibn Kidām, Maʿmar ibn Rāshid, ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd ibn Jaʿfar, Usāma ibn Zayd, and al-Ḍaḥḥāk

Muʿayūf ibn Yaḥyā was appointed to the summer campaign, but did not penetrate [the enemy’s territory].

Bukayr ibn Mismār and Bardān ibn Sālim—whose name was Ibrāhīm—died.

The Year 154

In it, Qurra ibn Khālid al-Sadūsī, ʿAlī ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn Ḥayy, al-Ḥakam ibn Abān in Yemen, Muḥammad ibn ʿImrān ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh—who died, “Qāqāḍī”—and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mawhab, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Nāfiʿ, client of Ibn ʿUmar, Jaʿfar ibn Barqān in al-Jazīra, ʿUmar ibn Isḥāq, brother of Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, author of the Sīra, Rabīʿa ibn ʿUthmān, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mūsā, and ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn Ḥafṣ died.

Abū Jaʿfar appointed Zufar ibn ʿĀṣim ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Hilālī to the summer campaign. He entered al-Maṣṣīṣa, reached al-Qazza, sent out raiding parties, took booty, and left by the Darb Marʿash.

The Year 155

In it, Shihāb ibn ʿAbd al-Malik was appointed to the sea [command].

In it, Yazīd ibn Asīd al-Sulamī set out; this was the expedition of Dān Qasha in the region of the Caspian Sea.

In it, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn al-Haytham al-Numayrī was dismissed and al-Haytham ibn Muʿāwiya was appointed. The people of Baṣra were put to work on the trench.

In it, Nāfiʿ ibn Thābit ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām and ʿImrān ibn Abī ʿĀtika, client of ʿUmar al-Khaṭṭāb, died; he was reliable in hadith.

The Year 156

In it, al-Haytham ibn Muʿāwiya was dismissed from Baṣra, and Sawwār was appointed along with the judgeship. Sawwār ibn ʿAbd Allāh died at the end of Dhū al-Ḥijja in the year 156 AH. Ibn Dulaj performed the funeral prayer over him, and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan led the people in prayer.

In it, Saʿīd ibn Abī ʿArūba, ʿAbd al-Ḥakam ibn Abī Farwa, and Aflaḥ ibn Saʿīd died. Al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage.

Zufar ibn ʿĀṣim al-Hilālī raided the lands of the Byzantines and attacked Qunba and Qūniya.

The Year 157

In it, Ibrāhīm ibn Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās led the pilgrimage.

Yazīd ibn Asīd al-Sulamī went on the summer campaign, took booty, and returned safely.

In it, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan was appointed judge of Baṣra and prayer leader, and Ibn Dulaj was placed over the aḥdāth, until Abū Jaʿfar died.

In it, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAmr al-Awzāʿī, Muṣʿab ibn Thābit, and ʿUmar ibn Ṣahbān, client of Aslam, died.

The Year 158

The Commander of the Faithful, Abū Jaʿfar, attended the pilgrimage and died in Mecca, at Biʾr Maymūn, one day before the Day of Tarwiya. That was on the seventh day of Dhū al-Ḥijja. Mālik ibn Mighwal died in Dhū al-Ḥijja.

Aflah ibn Ḥumayd. Maʿyūf ibn Yaḥyā had been sent toward the Byzantines; he entered their territory by the Darb al-Ḥadath and returned safely by the Darb al-Rāhib.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām narrated to me, from his father, from his grandfather; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughīra, from his father; and Abū al-Yaqẓān and others said: Abū Jaʿfar was born at al-Ḥumayma in the land of Syria and died at Biʾr Maymūn on the seventh day of Dhū al-Ḥijja in the year 158. He was sixty-four years old. ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā prayed over him; it is also said that it was Ibrāhīm ibn Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad.

ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz said: He was born in the year 95 and died at the end of the year 158, aged sixty-two. His caliphate lasted twenty-two years less six days.

Muḥammad ibn Amīr al-Muʾminīn al-Mahdī was given the pledge of allegiance. His mother was Umm Mūsā, daughter of Manṣūr, a woman from Ḥimyar. Ibrāhīm ibn Yaḥyā ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Maʿyūf ibn Yaḥyā campaigned; he was killed, and captives were taken.

The Year 159

In it, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Abī Rawwād, the client of al-Mughīra ibn al-Muhallab; Mālik ibn Mighwal; ʿIkrima ibn ʿAmmār al-Yamāmī; Yūnus ibn Abī Isḥāq al-Sabīʿī; and Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Mughīra ibn Abī Dhiʾb, of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy, died in Kūfa. Makhrama ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ashajj died at the beginning of al-Mahdī’s caliphate, and Hishām ibn Saʿd also died at the beginning of al-Mahdī’s caliphate; he was a client of the family of Abū Lahab.

Yazīd ibn Manṣūr, al-Mahdī’s maternal uncle, led the pilgrimage. Al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad entered and sent out his detachments, then returned with booty and safely.

The Year 160

In it, Yūsuf al-Barm went out in Khurāsān. Saʿīd ibn Salm ibn Qutayba ibn Muslim ibn ʿAmr met him, defeated him, and plundered his army.

Al-Mahdī, Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

The summer campaign: Thumāma ibn al-Walīd ibn al-Qaʿqāʿ ibn Khulayd al-ʿAbsī; he gained booty and returned safely.

In it, Shuʿba ibn al-Ḥajjāj, al-Rabīʿ ibn Ṣabīḥ, Khalīfa ibn Khayyāṭ, and Ayyūb ibn ʿUtba died.

In it, Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ayyūb was dismissed from Baṣra, and Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān was appointed over it. ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī was dismissed, and Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān was appointed over Mecca and Ṭāʾif. Al-Mahdī appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAzzān al-Taymī and Muḥammad ibn Yaʿqūb al-Anṣārī as judges over Mecca.

Naming the officials of Abū Jaʿfar

Medina: Ziyād ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-Ḥārithī was retained over it. Then he dismissed him in the year 141 and appointed Muḥammad ibn Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Qasrī over it. Then he dismissed him in the year 143 and appointed Rabāḥ ibn ʿUthmān al-Murrī. Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥasan ibn Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib revolted in Rajab of the year 145. Rabāḥ ibn ʿUthmān took a firm stand, so Abū Jaʿfar sent ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī, who killed Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh. He appointed Kathīr ibn al-Ḥuṣayn, one of Banū ʿAbd al-Dār, over Medina. He held office for a month; then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī. Then Abū Jaʿfar dismissed him in the year 146 and appointed Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās. Then—

Mecca: Ziyād ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-Ḥārithī was retained over it, along with his governorship of Medina. Then he dismissed him in the year 141 and appointed al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maʿbad ibn ʿAbbās. Then he dismissed him and appointed Ismāʿīl ibn Ayyūb al-Makhzūmī. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Haytham ibn Muʿāwiya al-ʿAtakī in the year 141. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Sarī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith in the year 143. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Kathīrī, who remained in office until he died.

Yemen: ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī was retained over it. Then he dismissed him and appointed Maʿn ibn Zāʾida al-Shaybānī in the year 142. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Ḥajjāj ibn Manṣūr. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Furāt ibn Sālim. Then Yazīd ibn Manṣūr governed it until Abū Jaʿfar died.

Baṣra: Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās was retained over it. Then he dismissed him and appointed Sufyān ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab, who arrived in Ramaḍān of the year 137. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUmar ibn Jaʿfar Hazārmard in the year 138. Then he dismissed him in the year 140 and appointed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Azdī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Sawwār ibn ʿAbd Allāh, with the judiciary. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUmar ibn Jaʿfar Hazārmard for a second time, in the year 142. Then he appointed al-Sanad; and he appointed Abū al-Jamal ʿĪsā ibn ʿAmr al-Saksakī over Baṣra in the year 143. Then he dismissed him and appointed Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās in that same year, 143. Ismāʿīl went out and appointed Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī as his deputy. Then he dismissed him and appointed Sufyān ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab, who went out to Abū Jaʿfar and appointed his son al-Mughīra ibn Sufyān as his deputy. Then Sufyān returned, and Ibrāhīm ibn ʿAbd Allāh revolted in Ramaḍān. Sufyān surrendered the city to him without fighting.

Then Ibrāhīm left Baṣra in Shawwāl and appointed his son al-Ḥasan ibn Ibrāhīm as his deputy. Ibrāhīm was killed in Dhū al-Qaʿda, and Sulaymān ibn Mujāhid, a client of Banū Ḍubayʿa, took control of Baṣra. Then Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān arrived and led the people in prayer on the Day of Sacrifice. Then Abū Jaʿfar appointed Salm ibn Qutayba, who held office for two months. Then he dismissed him and appointed Muḥammad ibn Abī al-ʿAbbās, whom the people of Baṣra nicknamed Abū al-Dibs. That was in the year 146. Then Muḥammad ibn Abī al-ʿAbbās left in the year 149 and appointed ʿUqba ibn Salm al-Hunāʾī as his deputy; Abū Jaʿfar confirmed him. Then ʿUqba ibn Salm left in the year 150 and appointed his son Nāfiʿ ibn ʿUqba as his deputy. He dismissed him and appointed Jābir ibn Tawba al-Kilābī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Yazīd ibn Manṣūr in the year 152. He held office for a month; then he dismissed him and appointed ʿĪsā ibn ʿAmr Abū al-Jamal for a second term. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ayyūb al-Numayrī in the year 150. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Haytham ibn Muʿāwiya in the year 155. Then he dismissed him and appointed Sawwār ibn ʿAbd Allāh over the prayer, and Ibn Dulaj over the security forces in the year 155. Sawwār died at the end of the year 156, at the end of Dhū al-Ḥijja. ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan led the people in prayer, and Abū Jaʿfar confirmed him in the leadership of the prayer.

Kūfa: ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā was retained over it. Then he dismissed him in the year 139 and appointed Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān, who served for eight years. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAmr ibn Zuhayr, the brother of al-Musayyib ibn Zuhayr, who remained in office until Abū Jaʿfar died.

Khurāsān: After Abū Muslim, Abū Dāwūd of Banū Dhuhl governed it; then ʿAbd al-Jabbār ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Azdī; then Khāzim ibn Khuzayma in one district and Jibrīl ibn Yaḥyā in another; then Asad ibn ʿAbd Allāh; then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik al-Khuzāʿī; then Abū ʿAwn al-Ḥimṣī; then Ḥumayd ibn Qaḥṭaba. He died there and appointed his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥumayd as his deputy.

Sijistān: Among Abū Jaʿfar’s officials over it were Ibrāhīm ibn Ḥumayd ibn Muḥammad al-Marwazī and Maʿn ibn Zāʾida. He was killed there in the year 151, and Maʿn appointed Yazīd ibn Mazīd as his deputy.

Al-Mahdī dismissed him during Abū Jaʿfar’s caliphate and appointed Tamīm ibn ʿUmar, of Banū Taym Allāt ibn Thaʿlaba. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAlāʾ, who remained in office until Abū Jaʿfar died.

Al-Sind: Mūsā ibn Kaʿb; then he departed and appointed his son ʿUyayna ibn Mūsā as his deputy, who continued as governor until ʿUmar ibn Ḥafṣ Hazārmard arrived in the year 143. ʿUyayna did not surrender the territory to him and fought him. ʿUmar besieged him at al-Manṣūra for eleven months. Then ʿUyayna asked him for a peace agreement on condition that he depart from there, and he made peace with him. ʿUyayna departed from there, and the country came under ʿUmar ibn Ḥafṣ’s control. Then Abū Jaʿfar wrote to him, ordering him to depart, so he left and appointed his maternal half-brother Jamīl ibn Ṣakhr as his deputy. Then he dismissed him and appointed Hishām ibn ʿAmr al-Taghlibī. Then he went to Abū Jaʿfar and appointed his brother Basṭām ibn ʿAmr as his deputy. Abū Jaʿfar then dismissed him and appointed Saʿīd ibn al-Khalīl, a man from Banū Tamīm. He died at al-Manṣūra and appointed his son Muḥammad ibn Saʿīd as his deputy, who remained in office until Abū Jaʿfar died.

Al-Baḥrayn: Among Abū Jaʿfar’s officials over it were ʿAbd Rabbih ibn Shurayṭ ibn ʿAbd Rabbih, ʿUqba ibn Salm, and Yazīd ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Hilālī.

Al-Yamāma: Qutham ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās.

Al-Jazīra: During the caliphate of Abū al-ʿAbbās, Abū Jaʿfar had appointed Muqātil ibn Ḥakīm al-ʿAkkī over it. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī marched after Abū al-ʿAbbās’s death and besieged Muqātil ibn Ḥakīm in the city of Ḥarrān until he surrendered it under a peace agreement. Abū Jaʿfar sent Abū Muslim to ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī. They met at Naṣībīn in Jumādā of the year 137, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī was defeated. Abū Jaʿfar appointed Mukhāriq ibn al-ʿAqqār over al-Jazīra, then Ḥumayd ibn Quṭba, then al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās, then Mūsā ibn Sulaym, a man from Khurāsān, then Mūsā ibn Muṣʿab, a client of al-Yaman.

Ifrīqiya: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān was killed in the year 138. The people pledged allegiance to al-ʿAbbās ibn Ḥabīb, so ʿUyayna ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥabīb fought him, killed al-ʿAbbās, and entered al-Qayrawān in—

Jumādā al-Ākhira of the year 138. ʿĀṣim ibn Jamīl revolted, and Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān left al-Qayrawān. The people of Ifrīqiya appointed Ḥumayd ibn Ḥurayth al-Maʿāfirī, who was their judge. Then ʿĀṣim ibn Jamīl entered it in al-Muḥarram of the year 140. Then ʿĀṣim ibn Jamīl was killed. Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Khālid ibn ʿUmrān ibn Ayyūb al-Sahmī entered it in the year 141; Makraz ibn Jamīl ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Abī al-Jaʿd killed him. Abū al-Khaṭṭāb, the Ibāḍī, revolted, killed Makraz, entered al-Qayrawān, and took the people’s pledge of allegiance. Abū Jaʿfar appointed Muḥammad ibn al-Ashʿath, who killed Abū al-Khaṭṭāb in the year 143. Then the army revolted against him and expelled him, and appointed ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā, one of Abū Jaʿfar’s commanders. Abū Jaʿfar dismissed him and appointed al-Aghlab ibn Sālim, of Banū Tamīm. Al-Ḥasan ibn Ḥarb al-Kindī revolted against him and killed al-Aghlab. Then he killed al-Mukhāriq ibn ʿAqqār al-Ṭāʾī and took control of it. The Commander of the Faithful wrote to him, confirming his appointment.

Then Abū Jaʿfar appointed ʿUmar ibn Ḥafṣ Hazārmard over it. He remained there for some time, then was killed. His maternal half-brother Jamīl ibn Ṣakhr took charge of the people’s affairs. Abū Ḥātim, a Berber, then fought him for some time. Then Abū Ḥātim granted him a guarantee of safety, and Ifrīqiya came under Abū Ḥātim’s control. Abū Jaʿfar sent Yazīd ibn Ḥātim, who defeated Abū Ḥātim and drove him out of the country. Abū Ḥātim remained in exile until Abū Jaʿfar died.

The judiciary

Baṣra: [A stray letter appears in the source.] Abū Jaʿfar appointed Sawwār ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-ʿAnbarī as judge. He died at the end of the year 156, and Abū Jaʿfar appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan al-ʿAnbarī.

Kūfa: Ibn Abī Laylā was retained over it. He died in the year 148, and Abū Jaʿfar appointed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā as judge; then [he appointed] Sharīk ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Nakhaʿī, who remained in office until Abū Jaʿfar died.

Medina: Ibn Abī Sabra was in charge of it. Then Muḥammad ibn Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Qasrī was appointed over Medina in the year 141, and he appointed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Muṭṭalib as judge. Then Muḥammad ibn Khālid was dismissed in the year 143, and Riyāḥ ibn ʿUthmān al-Murrī was appointed; he confirmed him [as judge]. Then Kathīr ibn Ḥuṣayn, one of Banū ʿAbd al-Dār, took office and confirmed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Muṭṭalib. Then Abū Jaʿfar dismissed Kathīr ibn al-Ḥuṣayn and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Ḥārithī, who confirmed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Muṭṭalib. Then Abū Jaʿfar dismissed ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Rabīʿ in the year 146 and appointed Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī, who confirmed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Muṭṭalib as judge. Then Abū Jaʿfar dismissed Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī and appointed al-Ḥasan ibn Zayd ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī in Ramaḍān of the year 149. He appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Qāsim ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr as judge. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān died, so he appointed al-Ḥasan ibn ʿUmrān al-Taymī as judge. Then he dismissed him and appointed Rabʿī al-Makhzūmī for some days. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Kathīrī. Then Abū Jaʿfar dismissed al-Ḥasan ibn Zayd and appointed ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh in the year 155. ʿAbd al-Ṣamad appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Salama ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb as judge.

The pilgrimage season: We have recorded in the history of the years who led the pilgrimage in each year.

The police: Abū Jaʿfar appointed ʿAbd al-Jabbār ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Azdī as chief of police, then al-Musayyib ibn Zuhayr al-Ḍabbī. Ḥamza used to ride before him with the spear, along with al-Musayyib ibn Ḥarb al-ʿAdawī. Then he assigned the spear to al-Musayyib, who held it until Abū Jaʿfar died.

Correspondence: Abū Ayyūb al-Mūriyānī.

Director of the land-tax department: Yazīd ibn al-Fayḍ.

Conquest correspondence: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ḥumayd.

The treasuries: Abān ibn Ṣadaqa, until Abū Jaʿfar died.

The storehouses: Sulaymān ibn Mujālid. He died, and his nephew Ibrāhīm ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn Mujālid was appointed; he remained in office until Abū Jaʿfar died.

The register of the Khurāsān army, the crown lands, and their districts: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ḥumayd. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAmāra ibn Ḥamza over the land of Baṣra, and ʿAmr ibn Ṣulayʿ over the land tax of Kūfa. Then he dismissed him and appointed Thābit ibn Mūsā. Over the land tax of Syria and the Syrian army: Ibn Raghbān.

Over the army’s administrative register: Isḥāq ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn Mujālid.

Over the guard and the seal: ʿUthmān ibn Nuhayk. ʿUthmān died, and ʿĪsā ibn Nuhayk was appointed. ʿĪsā died, and Abū al-ʿAbbās al-Ṭūsī was appointed.

Over the seal register: Abū Manṣūr, the scribe, from Khurāsān.

His chamberlain: ʿĪsā ibn Najīḥ, his client; then Abū al-Khaṣīb, his client; then al-Rabīʿ, his client. Then al-Mahdī was given the pledge of allegiance.

Chapter 18

The caliphate of Muḥammad al-Mahdī

Then Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās, al-Mahdī, was given the pledge of allegiance. His mother was Umm Mūsā, daughter of Manṣūr, from Ḥimyar. This was at the end of the year 158.

The Year 161

In it, Michael the patrikios set out from the direction of the Darb al-Ḥadath. He came to ʿAqabat Ḥartana and overcame its inhabitants. He came to the village of ʿAnzaran, killed and captured people, and burned it with fire. Then he came to Maʿrash, where ʿĪsā ibn ʿAlī was.

Mūsā, the Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Ḥarb ibn Shaddād died in Baṣra, and Zāʾidah ibn Qudāmah died in Kūfa.

The Year 162

In that year, al-Ḥasan ibn Qaḥṭabah campaigned. He came to Baṭnah and sent out his detachments, which demolished, burned, and took captives. He sent his son Muḥammad ibn al-Ḥasan to Amorium, then al-Ḥasan came there. There was a skirmish between them, and then he withdrew. In that year, the Muḥammira rebelled in Jurjān. Ibrāhīm ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Jaʿfar led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Abū al-Ashhab al-ʿAṭāridī, Yazīd ibn Ibrāhīm al-Tustarī, and Sufyān ibn Saʿīd al-Thawrī died in Baṣra; Isrāʾīl ibn Yūnus ibn Abī Isḥāq died in Kūfa; and Khālid ibn Abī Bakr ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb and Abū Bakr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Sabrah, of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy, also died.

The Year 163

In that year, Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, campaigned in the summer expedition during his father’s caliphate. He took many captives, kharthiyā, and goods.

In that year, al-Mahdī dismissed Muʿādh ibn Muslim from Khurāsān and appointed al-Musayyab ibn Zuhayr.

In that year, Saʿīd al-Ḥarashī killed al-Muqannaʿ in Khurāsān.

ʿAlī ibn al-Mahdī led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Hammām ibn Yaḥyā died in the month of Ramaḍān, and Mūsā ibn ʿAlī ibn Rabāḥ died.

The Year 164

In that year, ʿAbd al-Kabīr ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Zayd ibn al-Khaṭṭāb campaigned in the summer expedition and was defeated. Al-Mahdī became angry with him, imprisoned him in al-Muṭbiq, and wanted to kill him.

Ṣāliḥ ibn Abī Jaʿfar led the pilgrimage.

In that year, al-Mubārak ibn Faḍālah died in Baṣra, and Shaybān Abū Muʿāwiyah died in Kūfa.

The Year 165

In that year, Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, campaigned in the summer expedition during his father’s caliphate, reaching al-Khalīj. He returned in the year 166.

In that year, al-Musayyab ibn Zuhayr was dismissed from Khurāsān, and Abū al-ʿAbbās al-Ṭūsī was appointed. Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān was dismissed from Baṣra, and Ṣāliḥ ibn Dāwūd, the freedman of Banū Sulaym, was appointed. Ṣāliḥ departed and appointed his freedman Abū Miqātil as his deputy.

Ṣāliḥ ibn Abī Jaʿfar led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Ibn Abī Ḥabībah died; he was Ibrāhīm ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Ḥabībah.

The Year 166

Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Ṣāliḥ ibn Dāwūd was dismissed from Baṣra, and Rūḥ ibn Ḥātim was appointed.

The Year 167

Yaḥyā ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage.

In that year, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan was dismissed from the judgeship of Baṣra, and Khālid ibn Ṭalīq al-Khuzāʿī was appointed for several months. Then he was dismissed, and ʿUmar ibn ʿUthmān, of Banū Taym Quraysh, was appointed. In that year, the exchange of captives with the Byzantines took place at the hands of ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk.

In that year, ʿUqbah ibn Muslim was killed at ʿĪsābādh.

In that year, Ḥammād ibn Salamah died at the end of the year, in Dhū al-Ḥijjah, after the sacrifice; and Abū Hilāl al-Rāsibī, Salām ibn Miskīn, Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz al-Tanūkhī, Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥah ibn Muṣarrif, al-Ḥasan ibn Ṣāliḥ, and Jaʿfar al-Aḥmar also died.

The Year 168

Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage; it is also said that ʿAlī ibn al-Mahdī did so.

In that year, al-Mahdī wrote to ʿAlī ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī, ordering him to build the city of al-Ḥadath. ʿAlī sent al-Musayyab ibn Zuhayr, who ordered its construction.

In that year, Qays ibn al-Rabīʿ, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan, Mandal ibn ʿAlī, Abū al-Ghuṣn Thābit ibn Qays, the freedman of Banū Ghifār, and Muḥammad ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn Dīnār al-Tammār died.

The Year 169

In that year, the Commander of the Faithful al-Mahdī died at Mā Sabadhān, with eight nights remaining in al-Muḥarram. His son Hārūn ibn al-Mahdī prayed over him. He was forty-eight years old.

He was born at al-Ḥumaymah, in the land of Syria, in the year 121. It is also said that he died at the age of forty-three.

ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz said: “He was forty-one.” His rule lasted ten years, one month, and a half.

Listing of al-Mahdī’s governors

Medina: Abū Jaʿfar died while ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī was in charge there. Al-Mahdī dismissed him and appointed Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Kathīr ibn al-Ṣalt. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān al-Jumaḥī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī in the year 160. He dismissed him in the year 166 and appointed Ibrāhīm ibn Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad. When he died, Isḥāq ibn Yaḥyā was appointed and remained in office until al-Mahdī died.

Mecca: Abū Jaʿfar died while Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Kathīr ibn al-Ṣalt was in charge there. Al-Mahdī dismissed him and combined its governorship with that of Medina for Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān. Then he appointed over it ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Qutham ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās, then Aḥmad ibn Ismāʿīl, who remained until al-Mahdī died.

Yemen: He confirmed Yazīd ibn Manṣūr in office there, then dismissed him and appointed Rajāʾ ibn Rūḥ, a descendant of Rūḥ ibn Zinbāʿ; then ʿAlī ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās; then Sulaymān ibn Yazīd al-Ḥārithī; then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sulaymān al-Hāshimī.

Baṣra: Abū Jaʿfar died while ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan was in charge of the prayer and Saʿīd ibn Daʿlaj was in charge of the police. Al-Mahdī dismissed them both and appointed ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ayyūb al-Numayrī. Then he dismissed him in the year 160 and appointed Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās. Then he dismissed him and appointed Ṣāliḥ ibn Dāwūd in the year 165. Ṣāliḥ departed and appointed his freedman Abū Miqātil as his deputy. Then Rūḥ ibn Ḥātim was appointed over it and remained until al-Mahdī died.

Kūfa: Abū Jaʿfar died while ʿAmr ibn Zuhayr, the brother of al-Musayyab ibn Zuhayr al-Ḍabbī, was in charge there. Al-Mahdī then dismissed him and appointed ʿĪsā ibn Luqmān ibn Muḥammad ibn Ḥāṭib al-Jumaḥī. Then he dismissed him and appointed the judge Sharīk ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Nakhaʿī. Sharīk appointed Isḥāq ibn al-Ṣabbāḥ ibn ʿImrān ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ashʿath over the police. Then al-Mahdī dismissed Sharīk and appointed Isḥāq

Khurāsān: Abū Jaʿfar died while ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥumayd ibn Qaḥṭabah, acting on behalf of his father, was in charge there. Al-Mahdī dismissed him and appointed Abū ʿAwn al-Ḥimṣī. Then he dismissed him and appointed Muʿādh ibn Muslim for two years. Then he dismissed him and appointed al-Musayyab ibn Zuhayr in the year 163. Then he dismissed him and appointed Abū al-ʿAbbās al-Ṭūsī in the year 165, who remained until al-Mahdī died.

Sijistān: Al-Mahdī appointed Ḥamzah ibn Mālik ibn Zuhayr ibn Muḥammad al-ʿĀʾidhī over it, then Saʿīd ibn Daʿlaj, who died and appointed Tamīm ibn Saʿd as his successor. Al-Mahdī then appointed Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn al-Ashʿath.

Sind: Abū Jaʿfar died while Muḥammad ibn Maʿbad ibn al-Khalīl, a man of Banū Tamīm, was in charge there. Al-Mahdī dismissed him and appointed Rūḥ ibn Ḥātim in the year 159. Then he dismissed him and reinstated Naṣr ibn Muḥammad al-Khuzāʿī. Then his appointment letter reached him while he was in al-Balad. Then he departed from it. Later he was dismissed, and Sufayḥ ibn ʿAmr, the brother of Hishām ibn ʿAmr al-Taghlibī, was appointed. Then he was dismissed and al-Layth, his freedman, was appointed and remained until al-Mahdī died.

The Jazīrah: Abū Jaʿfar died while Mūsā ibn Muṣʿab was in charge there. Al-Mahdī dismissed him and appointed al-Musayyab ibn Zuhayr. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī, then al-Faḍl ibn Ṣāliḥ, then ʿAlī ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī, then ʿImrān ibn al-Minhāl, then ʿAlī ibn Sulaymān for a second time. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ held it twice, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ also held it.

Ifrīqiyah: Abū Jaʿfar died while Yazīd ibn Ḥātim was in charge there. Al-Mahdī confirmed him in office, and he remained until he died.

The judiciary

Baṣra: Abū Jaʿfar died while ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan al-ʿAnbarī was judge of Baṣra. Al-Mahdī confirmed him in office, then dismissed him in the year 169 and appointed Khālid ibn Ṭalīq, a descendant of ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn, over it for several months. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUmar ibn ʿUthmān, of Taym Quraysh.

Kūfa: Abū Jaʿfar died while Sharīk ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Nakhaʿī was in charge there, and al-Mahdī confirmed him in office. ʿĀfiyah ibn Yazīd al-Awdī and Ibn ʿUlāthah al-ʿUqaylī served as judges for al-Mahdī.

Medina: When al-Mahdī assumed power, he appointed Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Kathīr ibn al-Ṣalt over Medina. He appointed ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Muṭṭalib as judge, until Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh was dismissed and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān was appointed over Medina. He retained ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz. Then the judgeship of Medina passed to the caliph, and al-Mahdī appointed Muḥammad ibn ʿImrān al-Taymī on his behalf, then Saʿīd ibn Sulaymān ibn Masāḥiq.

The pilgrimage season: We have already recorded, in the chronology of the years, those who led the people in the pilgrimage.

The police: Naṣr ibn Mālik. Then he died, and al-Mahdī appointed Ḥamzah ibn Mālik, then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik.

Correspondence secretary: Abū ʿUbayd Allāh Muʿāwiyah ibn ʿUbayd Allāh. Then he dismissed him and appointed ʿUmar ibn Buzayʿ.

Over the register of Khurāsān: Bakr ibn Muʿāwiyah al-Bāhilī. Then he dismissed him.

Over the register of expenditures, the treasuries, the storehouses, the slaves, and the register of Syria: Abū Samīr, a freedman of Banū Fihr from Syria, whose name was Ayyūb. Ḥātim ibn Muslim said: “Ayyūb ibn Abī Samīr.”

Ḥātim said: “Ibrāhīm ibn Ṣāliḥ was the register secretary.” He died, and Abū al-Wazīr ʿUmar ibn Muṭarrif was appointed.

Over the register of the army of Khurāsān: ʿAbd al-Jabbār ibn Shuʿayb.

Over the treasury: Faraj ibn Faḍālah.

The grievances tribunal: Salām, his client.

The seal keeper: Khālid ibn Yazīd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mawhab al-Hamdānī. He died, and ʿAlī ibn Yaqṭīn was appointed.

The guards: Muḥriz Abū al-Qāsim. Then Abū al-ʿAbbās al-Ṭūsī was appointed, then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī al-ʿAbbās al-Ṭūsī.

His chamberlain: his freedman al-Rabīʿ, then al-Ḥasan ibn al-Rabīʿ.

The revolt of ʿAbd al-Salām al-Yashkurī

During al-Mahdī’s caliphate, ʿAbd al-Salām ibn Hāshim al-Yashkurī rebelled in the year 160 in Bājarmā. He came to Naṣībīn, where al-Manhāl ibn ʿImrān ibn Qanān al-Kilābī, one of Banū Jaʿfar, was in charge of its land tax. He sent him twenty thousand, but he did not enter it. He went to Raʾs al-ʿAyn, but Banū Tamīm barred him. He then headed toward Āmid, where he encountered ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā al-Khurāsānī. The men of ʿAbd al-Salām were defeated, and he said: “By God, I will begin with you, for you are unbelievers who flee from the advance, and you have no supporting force.” They turned back, and the men of ʿĪsā were defeated. ʿAbd al-Salām charged him, embraced him, threw him down, and killed him. Al-Mahdī wrote to Dāwūd ibn Ismāʿīl al-Rabadhī, who was among a thousand in the Jazīrah. Barāz marched against him, and they surrounded them. The Turks shot at them and killed them.

Abū al-Ḥasan said: Al-Mahdī wrote to ʿAbd al-Salām: “God singled out His troops for felicity, supported His party with guidance, admitted those who answered Him into His Garden, bestowed His bounty upon those who feared Him, and made His punishment the lot of whoever disobeyed Him. I am astonished by your outrages and rebellion: what grievance did you have when you judged by a word of truth, seeking by it that with which God will disgrace you and about which He will question you? This is despite your opposition to His caliph, your withdrawal of your hand from obedience to Him, your reviling Abū al-Ḥasan ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, your attack upon him, your disparagement of him, and your allegiance to those who opposed him. Thus you disobeyed God and opposed His Prophet. A sound certainty and a true report have come to you from the Prophet ﷺ—and his saying, ‘For whoever has me as his patron, ʿAlī is his patron.’ Yet you were one who denied that and turned away from it, when your appointed term had run out, you sought help from your partisans, and you persisted in your transgression. So I swear—”

“I shall surely set upon you obedient armies and impregnable commanders, who will scatter your assembled force and tear down your structure. So act for your own sake, or leave it.”

ʿAbd al-Salām wrote back to him:

“From ʿAbd al-Salām ibn Hāshim to Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh: Peace be upon whoever follows guidance, avoids error, and upholds the truth. You followed no guidance, avoided no error, and upheld no truth.

“To proceed: By His might, power, mercy, and help, God is the Lord of lords, severe in retribution. He is one in His sovereignty. He did not leave the community of Muḥammad in a state of confusion without setting it right and sending among them someone to attend to what they ought to attend to. Your letter came to me, expressing astonishment at what grievance I had when I judged. I will not leave you in the darkness in which you are, although you have only deceived yourself about this. You know that I was grieved and judged when you left the community bewildered and in turmoil, establishing none of its limits and fulfilling none of its rights, while occupying yourself with your slave girls and elaborating your buildings, all the while persisting in hunting. The falcons and leopards, spare mounts, and battalions would go out with you. When you returned from your hunt and entered your pavilion, and followed the company of your brothers, you would eat and sing. Glory be to God! How outrageous this is from one who claims to be God’s caliph. The non-Arabs used to condemn things less than this. Then, when you preach, you lie; when you make a pact, you break it. In your letter you claimed that you would send against me obedient armies and impregnable commanders. May God scatter your force, rout your army, and kill your commanders. Whenever you wish, we await this from you and are ready to face it. What increased my anger was that you took the name al-Mahdī—how far from the one who named you! What an excellent Mahdī you are, since you have sold the people a lie and led them far astray. Yaʿqūb ibn Dāwūd deceived you: a brother whom you made your brother and a friend with whom you were intimate. He called you, and you answered; he deceived you, and you complied. What religion permits you this, or what book did you find authority in, when you rush after an appointment or desire an increase—to reduce an allotment, select an estate, boast in a mount, plunge into hunting, or throw it into an outing; to turn a blind eye to a soldier, withhold a stipend, forget someone who campaigned, or punish with the whip one who sheds blood—”

The blood-shedder is put to death in retaliation, the adulterer is given his prescribed punishment, and the thief’s hand is cut off. Do not personally inspect the prisons, nor look into them with your own eyes. You have forgotten this, and overlooked that, O tyrant! Is there any life for you after this? Look to yourself: my eye is not asleep where you are concerned. You will meet someone who believes you, and encounter someone who kills you. I am not the one who resolves to conquer; conquest is in God’s hand. He judges as He wills. I am only one of His servants; I cannot refuse Him, nor defend myself against Him. There is no power and no strength except through God.

Chapter 19

The Caliphate of al-Hādī

Then allegiance was sworn to Mūsā ibn al-Mahdī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās in al-Muḥarram of the year 169. His mother was al-Khayzurān.

In that year, al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī set out and headed for Mecca in Dhū al-Qaʿda of the year 169. Al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad, Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā, and Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī met him. They had come for the pilgrimage. He was killed at Fakhkh before the pilgrimage.

Mūsā sent Maʿyūf ibn Yaḥyā on the summer campaign; he dispatched raiding parties and took booty.

Sulaymān ibn Abī Jaʿfar, Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

In that year, al-Sarī ibn Yaḥyā al-Baṣrī died in Mecca, as did Wahīb ibn Khālid, the associate of the cloth merchants. Before the year 170, Sulaymān ibn al-Mughīra and ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Muslim al-Qasmalī died.

The Year 170

In it, the Commander of the Faithful Mūsā ibn al-Mahdī ibn al-Manṣūr, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī, died.

Al-Walīd ibn Hishām, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Mughīra, and others related to me: “Mūsā was born at al-Rayy in the year 146 and died in Madīnat al-Salām on the middle day of Rabīʿ I in the year 170. He was twenty-four years old.”

ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Abī Thābit said: “He was twenty-one years old.” The Commander of the Faithful Hārūn prayed over him. His rule lasted one year, two months, and twenty-two days.

The Naming of Mūsā’s Officials

Mecca: ʿUbayd Allāh (ibn Qutham) ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās, until Mūsā died.

Yemen: Ibrāhīm ibn Salm ibn Qutayba, until Mūsā died.

Baṣra: al-Mahdī died while Rūḥ ibn Ḥātim was in charge there. Mūsā dismissed him and appointed Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī, who remained until Mūsā died.

Kūfa: al-Mahdī died while Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā was in charge there. Mūsā retained him until he died.

Khurāsān: al-Mahdī died while Abū al-ʿAbbās al-Ṭūsī was in charge there. Mūsā retained him until he died.

Sijistān: al-Mahdī died while Tamīm ibn Saʿīd was in charge there. Mūsā dismissed him and appointed Kabīr ibn Salm, who remained until Mūsā died.

Sind: al-Mahdī died while al-Layth, his client, was in charge there. Mūsā wrote to him ordering him to come down. He came down and appointed his son Muḥammad ibn al-Layth as his deputy. Mūsā died before al-Layth reached him.

The Jazīra: He appointed over it a man from Khurāsān known by the kunya Abū Hurayra, and he appointed Ibrāhīm ibn Ṣāliḥ over it.

Ifriqiya: He retained Yazīd ibn Ḥātim over it until Mūsā died.

Baṣra: He retained ʿUmar ibn ʿUthmān as judge of Baṣra. Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī dismissed him and appointed Muʿādh ibn Muʿādh.

Kūfa: Mūsā dismissed Sharīk and appointed al-Qāsim ibn Maʿn as judge.

Medina: Abū Yūsuf and Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Jumaḥī served as judges for Mūsā.

The pilgrimage season: Sulaymān ibn Abī Jaʿfar.

The guard: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik al-Khuzāʿī.

The correspondence and bureau: ʿUmar ibn Buzayʿ.

The seal: ʿAlī ibn Yaqṭīn.

The bodyguard: ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān.

The treasuries and public funds: ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā, together with the bodyguard.

His chamberlain: al-Faḍl ibn al-Rabīʿ.

His vizier and the one in charge of all his affairs: Ibrāhīm ibn Dhakwān al-Ḥarrānī.

Chapter 20

The Caliphate of Hārūn al-Rashīd

Then allegiance was sworn to the Commander of the Faithful al-Rashīd ibn Hārūn ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās, whose mother was al-Khayzurān, on the middle day of Rabīʿ I in the year 170. He retained Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī over Baṣra. Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān sent Yaḥyā ibn Saʿd al-Saʿdī in thirteen ships into the sea of Baṣra, until he reached Oman; he encountered no resistance.

In that year, the Romans came for the exchange of captives.

Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Jarīr ibn Ḥāzim died in Baṣra; Abū Maʿshar al-Madanī and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar al-Makhzamī, of Banū Zuhra, died in Medina; and ʿAmr ibn Thābit died at the beginning of Hārūn’s caliphate.

The Year 171

In it, Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Aṣamm campaigned and entered the lands of the Romans, taking booty and returning safely.

In that year, Ṭarsūs was built by the hand of Faraj the eunuch.

ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Mahdī ibn Maymūn and Ḥibbān ibn ʿAlī died.

The Year 172

In it, Zufar ibn ʿĀṣim al-Hilālī campaigned in the summer. He sent his son ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Zufar, who went as far as the Ceyhan. He was struck by cold, so he turned back.

Yaʿqūb ibn Abī Jaʿfar, the Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Ṣāliḥ ibn Bashīr al-Murrī died in Baṣra; Sulaymān ibn Bilāl died in Medina; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Ḥafṣ ibn ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar died at the beginning of the year 171.

The Year 173

In it, Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās died in Baṣra, where he was governor, in Rajab. He appointed a deputy. Then the Commander of the Faithful appointed Sulaymān ibn Abī Jaʿfar, the Commander of the Faithful.

In that year, Juwayriya ibn Asmāʾ and Salām ibn Abī Muṭīʿ died near its end, as did ʿUthmān ibn Miqsam al-Murrī.

The Commander of the Faithful Hārūn led the pilgrimage.

The summer campaign: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī.

The Year 174

The Commander of the Faithful Hārūn also led the pilgrimage. There was no summer campaign, except that ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ sent his son ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ, who reached ʿAqabat al-Rikkāb and captured prisoners and kharthiyā.

In that year, Ḥammād ibn Numayr campaigned in the lands of Sarast and conquered the city of al-Bardān.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Lahīʿa died in that year.

The Year 175

The Commander of the Faithful Hārūn also led the pilgrimage.

In that year, al-Layth ibn Saʿd died in Egypt.

In that year, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ campaigned against the Romans. This was the campaign of Iqrīṭiyya, with all the people of the frontier regions. He advanced through al-Ṣafṣāf, took nineteen thousand captives, and returned by way of Darb al-Ḥadath.

The Year 176

Sulaymān ibn Abī Jaʿfar, the Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage. There was no summer campaign. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī wrote to Makhlad ibn Yazīd ibn ʿUmar ibn Hubayra and ordered him to march to Dabsah until ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ reached him. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān came to it and conquered it; there is a long account concerning it.

In that year, the Commander of the Faithful Hārūn came to Baṣra with al-Mājishūn, Abū Yūsuf, and Ibn Abī Yaḥyā.

He dismissed Muslim ibn Ziyād al-Aṣamm from command of the sea and its ships at Sulaymānān.

In that year, Abū ʿAwāna al-Waḍḍāḥ, the client of Yazīd ibn ʿAṭāʾ, died, as did ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Bakr ibn Ḥazm al-Anṣārī.

The Year 177

The Commander of the Faithful Hārūn led the pilgrimage.

In that year, ʿAmr al-ʿArabī campaigned in the lands of the sea of Baṣra and prevailed at Bāriḥa, at the head of al-Jamḥa.

In that year, ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn Ziyād died.

Hārūn sent ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī on the summer campaign, and Sulaymān ibn Rāshid al-Thaqafī on the winter campaign. ʿAbd Allāh returned safely, while winter bore down severely on Sulaymān. Some of his companions disobeyed, some of them died, and he reached Malatya.

In that year, Yasār ibn Saqlāb campaigned with the people of al-Maṣṣīṣa. He advanced through al-Ṣafṣāf, then to Ṭuwāna, then turned back and encamped at Marj al-Shaḥm. He took booty and returned safely.

The Year 178

Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage.

Muslim ibn Ziyād al-Aṣamm campaigned in the sea of Baṣra and captured eleven warships.

In that year, al-Walīd ibn Ṭarīf rebelled in the lands of the Jazīra.

In that year, Sharīk ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Nakhaʿī died in Kūfa; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Najīḥ al-Madanī died in Baṣra; and Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān al-Ḍubaʿī died.

In that year, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ appointed al-Bukhturī ibn Sharīk ibn al-ʿAlāʾ al-ʿAbsī. He took booty and returned safely. His spoils amounted to two hundred thousand dinars and fifty-three thousand dinars.

The Year 179

The Commander of the Faithful Hārūn led the pilgrimage.

In that year, Ḥammād ibn Zayd died on Thursday and was buried on Friday, six days into the month of Ramaḍān.

In that year, Mālik ibn Anas and Abū al-Aḥwaṣ Salām ibn Sulaym died.

In that year, Muslim ibn Ziyād al-Aṣamm captured four warships.

The Year 180

Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā led the pilgrimage.

In that year, the Commander of the Faithful Hārūn came to Mecca for a second visit, then entered Baṣra on Monday, when ten nights of al-Muḥarram had passed. On that same day, Abū ʿUbayda ʿAbd al-Wārith ibn Saʿīd died.

In that year, Ibn Abī al-Zinād, al-Munkadir ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Munkadir, Ibn Abī Ḥāzim, Salama ibn Ṣāliḥ, and Sulaym ibn Akhḍar died.

The Revolt and Killing of al-Walīd ibn Ṭarīf the Khārijite

In that year, Yazīd ibn Mazīd killed al-Walīd ibn Ṭarīf the Khārijite in the land of the Jazīra. Al-Walīd ibn Ṭarīf, one of the Banū Ḥayy ibn ʿAmr—who are called “the dogs’ molars” among Banū Taghlib—had rebelled. He set out along the bank of the Euphrates with thirty men and came to Raʾs al-ʿAyn. He encountered a man from Baṣra, a merchant called ʿAmr ibn Manṣūr, who was accompanied by a Christian man called Nastas. He met them near Raʾs al-ʿAyn, killed ʿAmr ibn Manṣūr, took his property, and let the Christian go. Then he came to Raʾs al-ʿAyn, burned it, but did not enter the walled enclosure. Then he came to Bāʿarabāyā, from Naṣībīn. At Tall Abī al-Jawzāʾ, he encountered Bazzār, a man of Banū Taghlib. Bazzār fled, and men of his companions were killed.

Bazzār came to Naṣībīn. Then al-Walīd came to Dārā and ransomed it for twenty thousand. He came to Āmid and sold it to ʿIṣma ibn ʿIṣām and his household for twenty thousand. Then he came to Mayyāfāriqīn, and they ransomed it for twenty thousand. Then he crossed the Sarbaṭ, a valley that comes from Armenia, and crossed it to Arzan. He stayed there, and they ransomed it for twenty thousand. He killed one of its notables, a man of Banū Shaybān called Murra. Then he came to Khilāṭ and besieged them for twenty days; they ransomed themselves for thirty thousand. Then he went to Ādharbayjān, then came to Ḥulwān, where he encountered al-Ḥarashī Yaḥyā. He routed him and killed his companions. Then he came to Ḥūlāyā, then to al-Sūdqāniyya. He crossed to the western side of the Tigris and came to a town; they ransomed it for one hundred thousand. Then he came to Naṣībīn. Ibrāhīm ibn Khāzim was there, along with Bazzār among Banū Taghlib. Al-Walīd came and stopped on the hill opposite the Gate of the Romans. He entered through a breach in the city wall that they had neglected. Ibrāhīm ibn Khāzim and Bazzār went out through the Gate of the Romans. Al-Walīd pursued them and caught up with Ibrāhīm on Wednesday in Dhū al-Ḥijja of the year 178. Ibrāhīm was killed by a black man called Abū al-Ḥawārī. They washed his head and beard and set them on a spear for two days, then sent them to the desert. The Bedouins chanted:

“Indeed, ʿAdiyy, its servant, has disgraced it; Through him God has shed its blood; And laid waste the inhabited parts of its villages.”

Al-Walīd’s men gave Naṣībīn over to plunder for five days. They killed five thousand there and seized much property and livestock. They captured al-Muʿāfā ibn Ṣafwān, who was a friend of Bazzār, and killed him. Jaʿfar ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Hāshim al-Taghlibī came to him and bought the city from him for fifty thousand. Then Yazīd ibn Mazīd went to him and killed him in the desert. One of Yazīd’s companions said:

“We have been tried as steadfast men, while deaths loom near, Fearing disgrace, with al-Walīd a threat.

You will learn, O Khāqān, when a stand is made again, And ranks arrive, with ranks behind them.

From those who endure the heat of swords when there appear The stars of a day whose sun is eclipsed.”

Yazīd ibn Mazīd said:

“Make ready, O Walīd, for we have come, Swiftly, for encounter and for battle.”

…Then I am no man of Mazīd if you do not see us Engaging you in combat as though we were the jinn of a valley.

Al-Walīd said: You will know, O Yazīd, when we meet On the bank of the Zāb, what kind of man you have encountered.

Ibn al-Baṭṭāḥ said: Some of Wāʾil kill others of their own; Only iron can blunt iron.

Al-Walīd’s sister said, lamenting him: O trees of the Khābūr, why are you still leafy, As though you had not grieved for Ibn Ṭarīf? …A young man who wanted no provision except piety, And no wealth except spears and swords. …We have lost him as one loses the spring; if only we had Ransomed him with thousands from among our chiefs. …And no hoard except every sleek, sturdy mare, And every keen blade, double-edged and trusty. …Light upon the back of a horse when it gallops, But no light burden upon his enemies.

The Revolts of Bāsasīr, Ṣaḥṣaḥ, and al-Fuḍayl ibn Abī Saʿīd

Bāsasīr, a man of Banū Tamīm, revolted and remained for two months; then Abū Hurayra killed him. Ṣaḥṣaḥ al-Shaybānī revolted, and Bazzār and Dāwūd ibn Ismāʿīl killed him. Al-Fuḍayl ibn Abī Saʿīd revolted from Rādhān, and he was…

…was affiliated with Banū Shaybān. He set out with twenty horsemen and came to the region of al-Jazīra. They made peace with him for one hundred thousand, and he killed no one. Then he came to the territory of Nuʿmān, five parasangs short of Naṣībīn, and there he killed twelve men of Taghlib. Then he came to Naṣībīn with five hundred men. He stopped at the gate, and his companions entered and brought the people out to him through the Gate of al-Rūm. He said, “Sell them,” and offered them two dirhams for the people, but no one answered him. He threw two dirhams to them and sent them back into the city. Then he came to a house and made peace with its people for five thousand. Then he came to Āmid, and made peace with them for twenty thousand. He crossed to Mayyāfāriqīn, and made peace with them for ten thousand. Then he came to Arzan, where he stayed twenty nights; he made peace with them for twenty thousand. Then he came to Khilāṭ, where he stayed for several days. Then he returned to Naṣībīn with two hundred men. Maʿmar ibn ʿĪsā al-ʿAbdī, one of Banū Ghanm, was sent against him with twelve thousand. Al-Fuḍayl came to al-Mawṣil, then went to the Zāb. Maʿmar caught up with him at the Zāb, and Maʿmar was routed. Then the people rallied and fell upon al-Fuḍayl and his companions, and they were killed.

The Revolt of Jarāsha ibn Shaybān

Abū al-Ḥasan said, from ʿAlī ibn Sulaym: Jarāsha ibn Shaybān revolted in the year 179. He came to al-Sawād, then to al-Bandānījīn, where he killed ʿUmar ibn ʿUmrān ibn Jamīl al-Fazārī. Then he proceeded to al-Dīnawar, where al-Layth met him. Jarāsha routed him and killed thirty-odd of his companions. Jarāsha returned to Ḥulwān. Al-Layth wrote to Mālik ibn ʿAlī al-Khuzāʿī, who was in charge of Ḥulwān and Māh, that Jarāsha was heading toward him, routed and broken, with only a small band. Mālik called on the people to go out. His secretary said to him, “I adjure you by God, consider your own life: what do you want with a man who has not come to you?” He said, “Be quiet! I hope to take him prisoner.” He went out, and some Arabs who had come to visit him went out with him. He left one day before the Feast of Breaking the Fast; that was in the year 180. He intercepted Jarāsha and met him at a place called Qandāb, six parasangs from Ḥulwān. Forty-five of the Kharijites were killed there. One of the Kharijites struck Mālik in the mouth with a spear, and he fell. His companions cried, “The commander has been killed!” and fled. They pursued them and killed them.

…They killed one hundred and fifty of Mālik’s companions. Then Jarāsha came to Shahrazūr. The merchants gathered and dug a trench around themselves, and he besieged them for eighteen days. Yaḥyā ibn al-Naḍr made peace with him from his own wealth, on condition that two men enter the village and arbitrate there, then leave. He did so, then left and came to Sandābādh. He sent men to collect taxes from Nihāwand and Hamadhān. Al-Faḍl ibn Yaḥyā sent Ibrāhīm ibn Jibrīl with two thousand men, and appointed him on the day of the Feast of Breaking the Fast. He traveled until he came to Māh, then set out intending to reach Jarāsha. Jarāsha met him with one hundred and eighty men, and Abū Thawr, a man from Armenia, was with him. They fought fiercely. A man from Sogdia charged Jarāsha and struck him with a spear, throwing him across his horse’s neck. Lances surrounded him, and a man threw a spear that struck the forehead of his horse. The horse reared with him, then he returned to the saddle; the horse carried him off, and his companions followed him. We killed one hundred and fifty of them, and Abū Thawr was killed. Ibrāhīm ibn Jibrīl pursued them, but Jarāsha reached Qaṣr al-Luṣūṣ before him, eight parasangs from the place where he had met him. Then he took the road to Qirmāsīn, turned toward the meadows of the Commander of the Faithful, seized mounts, and took the road to Shahrazūr. Abū Bahja, one of Jarāsha’s companions, took the road to Māh. Jarāsha came to Shahrazūr with sixty men; they traveled three days, ninety parasangs, to reach it. He spent a night with Yaḥyā ibn al-Naḍr. Then he crossed the Tigris. Ibrāhīm ibn Jibrīl came to Shahrazūr and stayed four days, by which time his mounts had perished. Then he pursued Jarāsha, who had reached al-Anbār and stayed there eighteen days. Then he took the road to al-Qādisiyya. Ibrāhīm traveled, reinforcements reached him, and Khālid ibn Yazīd ibn Ḥātim arrived with two thousand men and camped at al-ʿUdhayb. Jarāsha went out and took the road to Baṣra, with Ibrāhīm pursuing him. Jarāsha returned to Wādī al-Sibāʿ, then took the road by al-ʿUdhayb and entered the wilderness. Ibrāhīm turned back, pursuing Jarāsha, and camped at al-Qaṭqaṭāna. Then he came to Qaṣr Muqātil, then to ʿAyn al-Tamr. He was told, “He is passing today; he intends to go to Hīt.” So he camped at Wādī al-Bardān. He was told, “He has gone ahead of you.” Ibrāhīm pursued him to Thumayl. There were men of Bakr ibn Wāʾil there after nightfall. They said, “He came to us at the afternoon prayer.” He killed five men and detained the people. When night had fallen, he went out—we did not know where he was headed. Ibrāhīm took the guides and sent them out, saying, “Bring me news of him.” They came to him at midnight and told him that Jarāsha had returned to Wādī al-Bardān. Ibrāhīm returned to Wādī al-Bardān and sent out the guides. They said, “He took the road toward al-Junayba.” He learned that the cavalry was at the Euphrates, so he returned to Thumayl. Ibrāhīm pursued him to Thumayl. Then he traveled until he camped two parasangs from Thumayl, at a place called al-Rummānatān. Then he proceeded, intending to go to Hīt, while Ibrāhīm pursued him on a dark night. The guide lost the road, and morning came with eighteen parasangs between them and Hīt. Jarāsha camped at ʿAyn Abʿad. He sent a man to Hīt to find out whether there was cavalry there. The man met Jarāsha’s messenger, Muslim ibn Bakkār ibn Muslim, and found him suspicious. He seized him, and the man said, “I will guide you to Jarāsha.” Muslim set out toward him, accompanied by ʿUbayd ibn Yaqṭīn. They came upon Jarāsha while he was asleep and killed him. They killed thirteen of his companions. When Ibrāhīm learned of his death, he returned to Hīt.

The Year 181

The Commander of the Faithful Hārūn led the pilgrimage, and al-Aṣamm raided in the direction of Baṣra, gaining booty and returning safely.

In this year Abū Yūsuf Yaʿqūb ibn Ibrāhīm al-Qāḍī, al-Aghaḍaf ʿAmr ibn al-Walīd, and Khalaf ibn Khalīfa al-Ashjaʿī died.

In this year Saʿīd ibn Salm met Jarāsha al-Shārī at Jubb Samāqā and routed him.

The Year 182

Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā led the pilgrimage.

In this year Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ died on Wednesday, eight days into Shawwāl; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Zayd ibn Aslam and Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Ṭaḥḥān al-Wāsiṭī also died.

The Year 183

Al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā led the pilgrimage.

In this year Hushaym ibn Bashīr, Ibrāhīm ibn Saʿd ibn Ibrāhīm in Baghdad, and Sufyān ibn Ḥabīb, Ziyād ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bakkāʾī, and Yaḥyā ibn Zakariyyā ibn Abī Zāʾida—his teknonym was Abū Saʿīd.

The Year 184

Ibrāhīm ibn al-Mahdī led the pilgrimage.

In this year Abū Umayya ibn Yaʿlā al-Thaqafī died.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣāliḥ sent Aḥmad ibn Hārūn al-Shaybānī on a raid into the lands of the Byzantines; he gained booty and returned safely.

In this year Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, sent Ṣāliḥ ibn Bayhas al-Kilābī to the Byzantine queen Qaṣṣa; it is also said: Ghuṣṣa, for the purpose of the exchange of captives.

The Year 185

Manṣūr ibn al-Mahdī led the pilgrimage.

In this year ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī and Yazīd ibn Mazīd died.

In this year Wakīʿ ibn al-Jarrāḥ arrived at ʿAbbādān, and there was no summer campaign.

The Year 186

Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage and renewed the oath of allegiance to his two sons, Muḥammad al-Makhlūʿ and ʿAbd Allāh al-Maʾmūn. He wrote terms between them and hung the document in the Kaʿba.

In this year Abū al-Khaṣīb, a man from the people of Nasā, revolted and gained control of Ṭūs and Sarakhs. He was killed at Marw; ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān killed him.

In this year Khālid ibn al-Ḥārith al-Hujaymī, ʿAbbād ibn al-ʿAwwām, and Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī at Salamiya died.

The Year 187

ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage.

In this year al-Muʿtamir ibn Sulaymān al-Taymī died in al-Muḥarram; Bishr ibn al-Mufaḍḍal died in Jumādā; and ʿAlī ibn Naṣr al-Jahḍamī and ʿAbd al-Salām ibn Ḥarb died in Kūfa; ʿUmar ibn ʿUbayd al-Ṭanāfisī and al-Fuḍayl ibn ʿIyāḍ died in Mecca.

In this year the Commander of the Faithful killed Jaʿfar ibn Yaḥyā ibn Khālid ibn Barmak at al-Anbār on the first night of Ṣafar.

In this year Ibrāhīm ibn ʿUthmān ibn Nuhayk was killed.

In this year Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, sent his son al-Qāsim on the summer campaign against the Byzantines, with ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ and the people of the frontier districts accompanying him. He entered through Darb al-Ṣafṣāf until he reached Qurra. Nikephoros sent to him, asking him to withdraw and offering him three hundred and twenty Muslim captives. He did so and withdrew.

The Year 188

Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

In this year al-Ḥusayn ibn al-Ḥasan died in Baṣra, and ʿAbda ibn Sulaymān died in Kūfa. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Ṣāliḥ was removed from office, and his son al-Qāsim ibn Hārūn was appointed in his place. Al-Qāsim ibn Hārūn sent Ibn Jibrīl, who entered through Darb al-Ḥadath and met the enemy at Marj ʿAdhrāʾ. God routed the enemy.

The Year 189

Al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās led the pilgrimage.

In this year ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn ʿAbd al-Aʿlā, Muḥammad ibn Yazīd al-Wāsiṭī, Yaḥyā ibn al-Yamān, Muḥammad ibn al-Ḥasan the judge at al-Rayy, and Abū Khālid al-Aḥmar—whose name was Sulaymān—died.

In this year the tyrant raided ʿAyn Zarba, the Black Church, and Adhana.

The Year 190

In this year ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn al-Mahdī led the pilgrimage.

In this year Rāfiʿ ibn Layth ibn Naṣr ibn Sayyār rebelled at Samarqand. ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān, the governor of Khurāsān, sent his son ʿĪsā ibn ʿAlī against him; ʿĪsā was routed.

In this year ʿUmar ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAṭāʾ ibn Muqaddam, Ḥumayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ruʾāsī, Yūsuf ibn Khālid al-Samtī, and Asad ibn ʿAmr died.

In this year the Commander of the Faithful raided the Byzantines and distributed the commanders throughout their lands.

The Commander of the Faithful camped at Ṭuwāna. The tyrant asked him to withdraw and offered him money. He refused unless the tyrant paid him a ransom and sent him a poll tax for himself and his son. The tyrant sent him thirty thousand dinars as poll tax.

The Year 191

Al-Faḍl ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage.

In this year Rāfiʿ ibn al-Layth killed ʿĪsā ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā at Nasaf.

In this year ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā was removed from Khurāsān, and Harthama ibn Aʿyan was appointed in his place. Muʿādh ibn Muʿādh was removed from the judgeship of Baṣra, and Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī was appointed in his place.

In this year Yazīd ibn Mخلad ibn Yazīd ibn ʿUmar ibn Hubayra raided; he returned safely and gained booty.

The Year 192

Al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Jaʿfar, the Commander of the Faithful…

In this year Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh was removed from the judgeship of Baṣra, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-ʿAnbarī was appointed in his place.

In this year ʿAbd Allāh ibn Idrīs al-Awdī—his teknonym was Abū Muḥammad—Ḥumayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Ruʾāsī, ʿAlī ibn Ẓabyān, Yūsuf ibn Abī Yūsuf the judge, and ʿĪsā ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Jaʿfar at Ṭabaristān died.

In this year Tharwān al-Shārī killed Sālim ibn Salm ibn Qutayba ibn Muslim al-Bāhilī at Ṭaff al-Baṣra.

In this year the Khurramites revolted in al-Jabal, so Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, sent Khuzayma ibn Khāzim against them; he killed and took captives.

The Year 193

In this year Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, died at Ṭūs, in the land of Khurāsān, on Saturday night, the first of Jumādā al-Ākhira, in the year 193. He was forty-seven years old.

ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿUmrān ibn Abī Thābit said: “He was born with three days remaining in Dhū al-Ḥijja in the year 150. He died at the age of forty-three years, five months, and three days.”

Al-Walīd and others said: “He was born at al-Rayy in the year 146. His reign lasted twenty-three years, one month, and half a month.”

The Oath of Allegiance to Muḥammad al-Amīn

Al-Makhlūʿ Muḥammad, son of the Commander of the Faithful, was given the oath of allegiance. His mother was Umm Jaʿfar, daughter of Jaʿfar ibn Abī Jaʿfar, the Commander of the Faithful.

Governors appointed by Commander of the Faithful Hārūn

Mecca: He retained ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Qutham over it, then dismissed him and appointed al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās. Then he dismissed him and appointed Sulaymān ibn Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī, then Ibrāhīm ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī. The governorship had been his father Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā’s, so he appointed him to it. Then [he appointed] ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qutham ibn Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās, then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿImrān ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUthmān al-Ṭalḥī, then ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Qutham ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās, who died. Then [he appointed] ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī, then al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā, who appointed Ṭalḥa ibn Bilāl as his deputy. Then he appointed ʿAlī ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā Muḥammad, who appointed Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Salama al-Makhzūmī, the judge, as his deputy. Then [he appointed] Ḥammād al-Barbarī, the client of Commander of the Faithful; then Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿīd ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān; then Sulaymān ibn Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān, who died there; then Aḥmad ibn Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAlī; then al-Faḍl ibn ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī. Then the judge Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Salama was appointed and remained in office until Hārūn died.

Yemen: He appointed his maternal uncle al-Ghuṭrīf over it. Al-Ghuṭrīf sent his maternal cousin al-Walīd, then al-Ghuṭrīf arrived there, and Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm was appointed over it. He sent his son Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad, then dismissed him and appointed his son al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad. Aḥmad ibn Ismāʿīl, Ayyūb ibn Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān, al-ʿAbbās ibn Saʿīd, his client, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muṣʿab al-Zubayrī, Ibrāhīm ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-Ḥajabī, Muḥammad ibn Khālid ibn Barmak, and Ḥammād al-Barbarī were also appointed over it, until Hārūn died.

Baṣra: Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī was appointed over it. Muḥammad died in Rajab of the year 173. Then Commander of the Faithful appointed Sulaymān ibn Abī Jaʿfar over it, then dismissed him at the end of the year 174

Kūfa: Mūsā died while Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn ʿAlī was over it. Commander of the Faithful Hārūn sent him to Egypt and appointed his son al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā [over Kūfa]. Then he dismissed him and appointed Yaʿqūb ibn Abī Jaʿfar, Commander of the Faithful. He did not go there, and appointed al-Ḥajwānī Yaḥyā ibn Bishr ibn Ḥajwān al-Ḥārithī as his deputy. Then he dismissed him and appointed Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā, then dismissed him and appointed al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā for two months. Then he dismissed him and appointed Isḥāq ibn al-Ṣabbāḥ al-Kindī for three months. Then he dismissed him and appointed Jaʿfar ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Jaʿfar. He did not go there, and appointed Manṣūr ibn ʿAṭāʾ al-Khurāsānī, the client of Banū Layth. Then he dismissed him and appointed Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā, who remained until Commander of the Faithful Hārūn died.

Khurāsān: He retained Abū al-ʿAbbās al-Ṭūsī over it, then dismissed him and appointed Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ashʿath, then al-ʿAbbās ibn Jaʿfar, then al-Ḥasan ibn Qaḥṭaba for some days, then al-Ghuṭrīf, the maternal uncle of Commander of the Faithful, then Ḥamza ibn Mālik, then al-Faḍl ibn Yaḥyā ibn Khālid ibn Barmak. Al-Faḍl appointed ʿUmar ibn Ḥaml al-Yarbūʿī over it. Then al-Faḍl ibn Yaḥyā dismissed him and appointed Manṣūr ibn Yazīd, then Jaʿfar ibn Yaḥyā ibn Khālid, who did not travel there; he appointed ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān over it. Then he was dismissed in the year one hundred and

Sijistān: Mūsā died while Kathīr ibn Salm was over it, and the troops [revolted?]. Aṣram ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd al-Ṭāʾī came to them from Khurāsān. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥumayd ibn Qaḥṭaba was appointed, then ʿUthmān ibn ʿUmāra ibn Ḥarīm, then Dāwūd ibn Yazīd on behalf of al-Ghuṭrīf, then Yazīd ibn Jarīr on behalf of al-Faḍl ibn Yaḥyā ibn Khālid, then Ibrāhīm ibn Jarīr, also on behalf of al-Faḍl ibn Yaḥyā, then al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī on behalf of ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān, then Yazīd ibn Jarīr, also on behalf of ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān, then ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥasan ibn Qaḥṭaba, then Aṣram ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān, who appointed a client of his over it. Then Aḥmad ibn al-Ḥuṣayn ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥamīd al-Ṭāʾī, who died there and appointed as his deputy a man from its people called Ibn Salama; then ʿAlī al-Qūmasī; then al-Ḥakam ibn Sinān al-Bāhilī on behalf of Harthama, until Hārūn died.

Sind: al-Layth, the client of Commander of the Faithful, was appointed over it, then dismissed. Al-Barnasī Sālim, the client of Commander of the Faithful, was appointed over it; he died there and appointed his son Ibrāhīm ibn Sālim as his deputy. He governed it for a year, then was dismissed, and Isḥāq ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī was appointed over it. Then he was dismissed, and Muḥammad ibn Ṭayfūr al-Ḥīrī was appointed over it—also said to have been a client of Commander of the Faithful. Then he was dismissed and Saʿīd ibn Salm ibn Qutayba was appointed. He sent his brother Kathīr ibn Salm. Then he was dismissed and Muḥammad ibn ʿAdī, the nephew of Hishām ibn ʿAmr, was appointed, but the people of Multān prevented him. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sulaymān was appointed, then departed and appointed ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAlāʾ al-Ḍabbī as his deputy. Then Ayyūb ibn Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī was appointed. Ayyūb sent Sulaymān ibn Saʿīd ibn Zayd at the head of his vanguard. Then Ayyūb died before entering it, so Dāwūd ibn Yazīd ibn Ḥātim was appointed. He remained over it until Hārūn died.

Al-Jazīra: Among Hārūn’s governors over it were Muḥammad ibn Khālid ibn Barmak, Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm, Khuzayma ibn Khāzim, and Yazīd ibn Mazīd; then Sulaymān ibn Abī Jaʿfar, then Muḥammad ibn Jamīl, then Khuzayma ibn Khāzim for a second term, until Hārūn died.

Egypt: Jaʿfar ibn Yaḥyā ibn Khālid did not travel there, and Ibn al-Musayyib ibn Zuhayr was appointed over it. Then Hārūn appointed Ibrāhīm ibn Ṣāliḥ over it, then dismissed him and appointed Musallama ibn Yaḥyā, the brother of Jibrīl ibn Yaḥyā, then Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā, then Isḥāq ibn Sulaymān, then Harthama ibn Aʿyan, then ʿUbayd Allāh

Ifrīqiya: He retained Yazīd ibn Ḥātim over it until Yazīd died, and Yazīd appointed his son Dāwūd ibn Yazīd as his deputy. Then he dismissed him in the year 171 or 172 and appointed Rūḥ ibn Ḥātim. He died in the year 174 or 175 and appointed his son Qubayṣa ibn Rūḥ as his deputy. He dismissed him and appointed Naṣr ibn Ḥabīb for a year and a half. Then al-Faḍl ibn Rūḥ was appointed, but the troops revolted against him, with a man from the people of Herat called ʿAbdawayh at their head. They killed al-Faḍl and gained control of the country. Then Harthama ibn Aʿyan arrived, granted ʿAbdawayh safe conduct, and took him to Baghdad. Then Muḥammad ibn Muqātil al-ʿUkkī was appointed over it, but a man among the Abnāʾ called Tammām revolted against him, drove Muḥammad out, and gained control of it. Then Muḥammad returned, drove Tammām out, gained control of it, and it came into his hands. The Abnāʾ revolted against him, drove him out, and appointed Ibrāhīm ibn al-Aghlab ibn Sālim. A mandate from Commander of the Faithful arrived, and he remained governor until Hārūn died.

The pilgrimage season: We have recorded, in the book The History of the Years, who led the pilgrimage each year.

The judiciary

The judgeship of Baṣra: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muḥammad al-Makhzūmī was appointed over it, then dismissed, and ʿUmar ibn Ḥabīb al-ʿAdawī was appointed in the year 172. Then he was dismissed in the year 181, and Muʿādh ibn Muʿādh was appointed. Then he was dismissed in the year 191, and Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī was appointed. Then he was dismissed in the year 192, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-ʿAnbarī was appointed. Commander of the Faithful died while he was judge.

Kūfa: He retained al-Qāsim ibn Maʿn over it, then dismissed him and appointed Nūḥ ibn Darāj, the client of al-Nakhaʿ; then dismissed him and appointed Sharīk; then dismissed him and appointed Ḥafṣ ibn Ghiyāth; then al-Ḥasan ibn Ziyād al-Luʾluʾī.

Medina: He did not mention a judge for it.

Hārūn’s judge was Abū Yūsuf. Abū Yūsuf died, and he appointed Wahb ibn Wahb as judge.

Abū al-Bakhtarī and Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Jumaḥī. He died, and al-Ḥusayn ibn al-Ḥasan al-ʿAwfī was appointed judge.

The police: Khuzayma ibn Khāzim, then al-Musayyib ibn Zuhayr. His son Muḥammad ibn al-Musayyib would walk before him carrying the spear. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik.

Abū al-Ḥasan said: Mūsā died while ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik was in charge of his police, so Hārūn confirmed him in the post. Then he dismissed him, appointed Wahb ibn Ibrāhīm, called him Wahb ibn ʿUthmān, and dropped the name Ibrāhīm. Hārūn died while he was in charge of his police.

Secretary of correspondence: Ismāʿīl ibn Ṣubayḥ, from the people of Ḥarrān. Yaḥyā ibn Sulaym wrote for him.

The bureaus, tax revenue, and army: Abū Ṣāliḥ. He attached these to Ismāʿīl ibn Ṣubayḥ.

The seal: Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ashʿath, then he appointed him over Khurāsān and gave the seal to Ḥamza ibn Mālik. Then he gave it to Abū al-ʿAbbās al-Ṭūsī. When Abū al-ʿAbbās died, the seal passed to Yaḥyā ibn Khālid ibn Barmak, then to Jaʿfar ibn Yaḥyā, then he returned it to Yaḥyā ibn Khālid. Then it came into the hand of Commander of the Faithful Hārūn.

The guard: Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Ashʿath, then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik, then ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān. Then he assigned the guard to Jaʿfar ibn Yaḥyā ibn Khālid. Jaʿfar appointed Ṣāliḥ ibn Shaykh ibn ʿUmayra. Then Jaʿfar appointed Harthama ibn Aʿyan, and Hārūn confirmed him.

His chamberlain: Bashīr ibn Maymūn, his client; then Muḥammad ibn Khālid ibn Barmak; then al-Faḍl ibn al-Rabīʿ.

His vizier and the one in charge of all his affairs was Yaḥyā ibn Khālid ibn Barmak, then his son Jaʿfar ibn Yaḥyā. Then he killed him, and al-Faḍl ibn al-Rabīʿ took his place.

In the year 193, Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī led the pilgrimage.

In the year 193, Abū Bakr ibn ʿAyyāsh, the client of Wāṣil ibn Ḥayyān al-Aḥdab, died. In that year Ghundar died, in Dhū al-Qaʿda.

Chapter 21

The caliphate of al-Amīn

The Year 194

ʿAlī ibn Hārūn, Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

In that year Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm, whose mother was ʿUlayya, died, as did ʿAbd al-Wahhāb ibn ʿAbd al-Majīd al-Thaqafī, Muḥammad ibn Abī ʿAdī, the client of Banū Sulaym, and Ḥafṣ ibn Ghiyāth al-Nakhaʿī.

In that year Abū al-Bakhtarī Wahb ibn Wahb was dismissed from Medina, and Ismāʿīl ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad was appointed over it.

In that year Rāfiʿ ibn al-Layth came over to obedience to Commander of the Faithful al-Maʾmūn and presented himself before him. This was before the deposed caliph was killed.

The Year 195

Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī led the pilgrimage.

In that year the deposed caliph sent ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān to Khurāsān, and Commander of the Faithful al-Maʾmūn sent Ṭāhir ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn Muṣʿab. They met at Rayy in Shaʿbān, and ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā was killed.

In that year the caliphate was proclaimed for al-Maʾmūn in Khurāsān.

In that year Ṭāhir ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jabala al-Abnāwī was killed.

In that year Harthama set out to fight the deposed caliph.

In that year Muḥammad ibn al-Fuḍayl ibn Ghazwān, ʿUthām ibn ʿAlī, Muʿādh ibn Muʿādh, Isḥāq ibn Yūsuf al-Azraq, Abū Muʿāwiya al-Ḍarīr, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥīm al-Muḥāribī died in Kūfa.

The Year 196

Al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās led the pilgrimage and called for al-Maʾmūn to be caliph during the pilgrimage season in Mecca and Medina.

In that year al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿĪsā ibn Māhān rose up in Baghdad, deposed Muḥammad, the deposed caliph, and summoned the people to pledge allegiance to al-Maʾmūn. He seized Muḥammad, the deposed caliph, on Tuesday in Rajab and imprisoned him. The troops rose against al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī and killed him, and released the deposed caliph from prison.

In that year ʿAwn ibn Jumhān al-Saʿdī rose up in Baṣra and called for al-Maʾmūn. Manṣūr ibn al-Mahdī deposed Muḥammad, the deposed caliph, and called for al-Maʾmūn.

In that year Muḥammad ibn Yazīd ibn Ḥātim was killed at al-Ahwāz.

In that year Ṭāhir ibn al-Ḥusayn arrived in Baghdad, and the Ḥarbiyya pledged allegiance to him.

In that year Zuhayr ibn al-Musayyib was defeated in Baṣra on Tuesday, eight nights having passed in the month of Ramaḍān.

The Year 197

Al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib led the pilgrimage.

In that year the deposed caliph was besieged in Baghdad, and the forces of Ṭāhir ibn al-Ḥusayn, Harthama ibn Aʿyan, and Zuhayr ibn al-Musayyib surrounded him.

In that year Wakīʿ ibn al-Jarrāḥ died at Fayd, as did ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿImrān ibn Abī Thābit al-Aʿraj.

Chapter 22

The caliphate of al-Maʾmūn

The Year 198

Al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā also led the pilgrimage.

In that year the deposed caliph was killed on Sunday night, with two nights of Muḥarram remaining. Quraysh al-Dandānī was in charge of killing him. Ṭāhir ibn al-Ḥusayn displayed his head for an hour during the day and sent it to al-Maʾmūn.

In that year Sufyān ibn ʿUyayna, Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd al-Qaṭṭān, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mahdī, and Maʿn ibn ʿĪsā died.

In that year ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār was dismissed from the judgeship of Baṣra, and Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī was appointed.

The deposed caliph was born in Baghdad in the year 170 and was killed in Baghdad in Muḥarram of the year 198, aged twenty-eight. His rule lasted until he was deposed and allegiance was pledged to al-Maʾmūn as caliph in Khurāsān for two years; then he was deposed in Iraq and the Ḥijāz for two years. Thus his rule lasted until he was killed: four years and eight months. Rule was established for Commander of the Faithful ʿAbd Allāh al-Maʾmūn, son of Commander of the Faithful.

In charge of his police: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khāzim, then Muḥammad ibn Ḥamza ibn Mālik. He dismissed him and reinstated ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khāzim.

The deposed caliph’s judge was Ismāʿīl ibn Ḥammād ibn Abī Ḥanīfa. He appointed Abū al-Bakhtarī Wahb ibn Wahb in the year 175, then appointed Ibn Abī Ḥanīfa to the judgeship of Kūfa, where he died.

Baṣra: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sawwār.

The Year 199

In that year al-Ḥasan ibn Sahl arrived as governor over Iraq and took up residence in Madīnat al-Salām.

The revolt of Ibn Ṭabāṭabā in Kūfa

In that year Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Ḥasan ibn Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib rose up in Kūfa on Thursday, with eight nights of Jumada remaining. He is the one called Ibn Ṭabāṭabā.

… al-Ākhira. Abu al-Saraya, whose name was al-Sarī ibn Manṣūr al-Shaybānī, met him that very day. The governor of Kūfa fled, and it came into their hands without a fight. Then Muhammad ibn Ibrahim died at the beginning of Shaʿbān that year, and allegiance was given to Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Zayd ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

The uprising of Muhammad ibn Jaʿfar in Baṣra and Muhammad ibn Sulaymān in Medina

In it, Muhammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī rose up in Baṣra, and it came into his hands without a fight. Muhammad ibn Sulaymān ibn Dāwūd ibn al-Ḥasan ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī rose up in Medina, and it came into his hands without a fight.

Abu al-Saraya defeats the forces sent by al-Ḥasan ibn Sahl

In it, al-Ḥasan ibn Sahl sent Zuhayr ibn al-Musayyib to Kūfa. Abu al-Saraya encountered him, defeated Zuhayr, and seized his ships and baggage. Al-Ḥasan ibn Sahl then dispatched Aynama ʿAbdus ibn Muhammad ibn Abī Khālid. Abu al-Saraya encountered him at the congregational mosque, killed ʿAbdus and most of his companions, and seized his army. Al-Ḥasan ibn Sahl sent Abu al-Baṭṭ Aḥmad ibn ʿAmr al-Dhuhlī, and Muhammad ibn Muhammad ibn Zayd dispatched Muhammad ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn. They met at Sābāṭ in the land of al-Sawād, and Abu al-Baṭṭ was defeated. Ibrahim ibn Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn came to Yemen and expelled Isḥāq ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā from it.

The uprising of Ibn al-Afṭas in Mecca

Al-Maʾmūn sent Sulaymān ibn Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā to conduct the pilgrimage. Ibn al-Afṭas, whose name was Muhammad ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥasan ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, rose up in Mecca and made [something] white.

Sulaymān withdrew and did not proceed to ʿArafa. The people stood without an imam, and al-Ḥusayn ibn al-Ḥasan came to ʿArafāt on the Night of Sacrifice, after the people had departed from it, and led the people on the morning of Jamʿ.

In it, Harthama ibn Aʿyan set out in the month of Ramaḍān to fight Abu al-Saraya and his companions.

In it, Abu Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Fudayk and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Numayr died in Kūfa.

The Year 200

In it, Harthama defeated Abu al-Saraya, who was accompanied by Muhammad ibn Muhammad. They entered Kūfa on Sunday, in the middle of Muḥarram. Abu al-Saraya and Muhammad ibn Muhammad fled, but Ḥammād al-Andaghūsh captured them in the region of al-Sūs. He sent them to al-Ḥasan ibn Sahl, who killed Abu al-Saraya and crucified him on two pieces of wood.

In it, ʿAlī ibn Abī Saʿīd, Aḥmad ibn Saʿīd ibn Salm, and al-Jalūdī entered Baṣra. The commander was ʿAlī ibn Abī Saʿīd. Zayd and the Ṭālibids who were there in Baṣra departed.
