Abu Isḥāq, son of the Commander of the Faithful, conducted the pilgrimage. Muhammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAlī and Ibn al-Afṭas rose up in Mecca, but Abu Isḥāq, son of the Commander of the Faithful, prevailed over them and sent them to al-Maʾmūn.

In it, Asbāṭ ibn Muhammad, ʿAlī ibn ʿĀṣim, and Dāwūd ibn Yazīd ibn Ḥātim died in al-Sind.

The Year 201

In it, al-Maʾmūn gave the oath of allegiance to ʿAlī ibn Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar as caliph after him. He deposed al-Qāsim ibn Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, ordered the black garments discarded, and green garments to be worn.

In it, al-Ḥasan ibn Sahl was expelled from Baghdad, and allegiance was given in Baghdad to Ibrahim ibn al-Mahdī, whose mother was Shakla. Kūfa and most of al-Sawād were taken for him.

In it, Zuhayr ibn al-Musayyib was killed in Baghdad, and Muhammad ibn Abī Khālid was killed: he had been struck a blow and died from it.

In it, Harthama ibn Aʿyan died on Sunday night, three nights into al-Muḥarram.

Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā conducted the pilgrimage.

The Year 202

In it, the Commander of the Faithful, al-Maʾmūn, set out from Khurāsān, intending for Baghdad.

In it, al-Faḍl ibn Sahl was killed at Sarakhs in Shaʿbān. The Commander of the Faithful then killed ʿAlī ibn Abī Saʿīd, Mūsā ibn ʿImrān, and ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿImrān, accusing them of killing al-Faḍl ibn Sahl.

Ibrahim ibn Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar, the brother of al-Riḍā, conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Ḥammād ibn Masʿada died.

The Year 203

In it, al-Riḍā, ʿAlī ibn Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar, died on Saturday, the last day of Ṣafar, and al-Maʾmūn arrived in Baghdad on Sunday in the month of Ramaḍān.

Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Zayd ibn al-Ḥubāb, Muhammad ibn Bishr, Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī al-Juʿfī, Yaḥyā ibn Ādam, the client of the family of ʿUqba ibn Abī Muʿayṭ, and Muhammad ibn Bakr al-Bursānī died in Dhū al-Ḥijja; and Abu Aḥmad Muhammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Zubayrī [died].

The Year 204

ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib conducted the pilgrimage, and he was also governor of Mecca and Medina.

In it, al-Maʾmūn alighted at al-Ruṣāfa and ordered the green garments discarded.

In it, Abu Dāwūd Sulaymān ibn Dāwūd al-Ṭayālisī died in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal.

The Year 205

ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib also conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Muhammad ibn ʿUbayd al-Ṭanafisī, Yaʿqūb ibn Isḥāq al-Ḥaḍramī, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn ʿAmr, and Abu ʿĀmir al-ʿAqdī died; the latter two died in Jumādā, on Sunday.

The Year 206

ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās also conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Yazīd ibn Hārūn, Shabāba ibn Sawwār, Abu Dāwūd al-Ḥafarī, al-Ḥajjāj al-Aʿwar, Yaḥyā ibn Muʿādh ibn Muslim, and al-Haytham ibn ʿAdī died.

The Year 207

Abu ʿĪsā ibn Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Azhar ibn Saʿd al-Sammān and Wahb ibn Jarīr ibn Ḥāzim died—the latter at its beginning—as did Jaʿfar ibn ʿAwn al-Makhzūmī, Kathīr ibn Hishām, ʿUmar ibn Ḥabīb, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Abān, Muhammad ibn ʿUmar al-Wāqidī, and Abu al-Naḍr Hāshim ibn al-Qāsim. In it, Ṭāhir ibn al-Ḥusayn died in Khurāsān, so the Commander of the Faithful appointed his son ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṭāhir over Khurāsān together with al-Jazīra, and appointed his brother Ṭalḥa ibn Ṭāhir over Khurāsān.

The Year 208

Ṣāliḥ ibn Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Ṣafwān ibn ʿĪsā, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bakr Abu Wahb al-Sahmī, and Saʿīd ibn ʿĀmir died in Shawwāl, as did Shādhān ibn ʿĀmir, Yūnus ibn Muhammad al-Muʾaddib, Ashhal ibn Ḥātim, and Yaʿqūb ibn Ibrahim ibn Saʿd.

In it, al-Qāsim and Abu ʿĪsā, the sons of the Commander of the Faithful Hārūn, died, as did Thābit ibn Naṣr, governor of the frontier regions, and al-Faḍl ibn al-Rabīʿ.

The Year 209

Ṣāliḥ ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, ʿUthmān ibn ʿUmar ibn Fāris, Bishr ibn ʿUmar al-Zahrānī, and al-Ḥasan ibn Mūsā al-Ashyab died.

In it, the Khurramiyya captured Aḥmad ibn al-Junayd and Muʿādh ibn Hāniʾ.

The Year 210

Ṣāliḥ ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muhammad conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Yaʿlā ibn ʿUbayd al-Ṭanafisī and Yaḥyā ibn Isḥāq al-Saylaḥīnī died.

In it, al-Maʾmūn, the Commander of the Faithful, captured Ibrahim ibn al-Mahdī and pardoned him.

The Year 211

Ṣāliḥ ibn al-ʿAbbās also conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, ʿAbd al-Razzāq ibn Hammām, ʿAbd al-Raḥīm ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muhammad al-Muḥāribī, al-Muʿallā ibn Manṣūr al-Rāzī, and Abu Zayd al-Harawī died.

The Year 212

ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAlī conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Abu ʿĀṣim al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Makhlad died in Dhū al-Ḥijja, as did Zakariyyā ibn ʿAdī.

The Year 213

ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUbayd Allāh also conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dāwūd and ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Mūsā died.

In it, Ḥamza al-Shārī died.

In it, Muhammad ibn ʿAbbād ibn ʿAbbād was removed from al-Baḥr, and it was assigned to Ghassān ibn ʿAbbād; then Muhammad ibn ʿAbbād ibn ʿAbbād was appointed.

In it, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yazīd al-Muqriʾ died.

The Year 214

Isḥāq ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Ibn Ḥumayd al-Ṭūsī was killed by the Khurramiyya; he was the commander.

The Year 215

ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUbayd Allāh…

In it, Muhammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī died.

In it, ʿAlī ibn Hishām was put in charge of fighting the Khurramiyya.

In it, Ibrahim ibn al-Layth was killed at Ardabīl.

In it, Ibrahim ibn Muhammad al-Taymī was put in charge of al-Baḥr.

In it, Isḥāq ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muhammad died in Yemen.

In it, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Qurayb al-Aṣmaʿī died.

The Year 216

ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās conducted the pilgrimage.

The Year 217

Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, al-Ḥajjāj ibn al-Minhāl al-Anmāṭī and Saʿīd ibn Salm ibn Qutayba died.

In it, ʿAlī ibn Hishām was killed.

The Year 218

Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, al-Maʾmūn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Hārūn, the Commander of the Faithful, died, and allegiance was given to al-Muʿtaṣim bi-Allāh Abu Isḥāq, the Commander of the Faithful.

The naming of al-Maʾmūn’s officials

Medina: ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās, with Mecca added to his jurisdiction.

Then ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn so-and-so ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, and Qutham ibn Jaʿfar ibn…

Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās in the year two hundred and eight; then he dismissed him and appointed Muhammad ibn so-and-so ibn Ḥusayn ibn Zayd; then he dismissed him and appointed Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī in the year two hundred and thirteen.

The Year 219

Ṣāliḥ ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muhammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Abu Nuʿaym al-Faḍl ibn Dukayn died in Shaʿbān, as did Mālik ibn Ismāʿīl Abu Ghassān al-Nahdī. In it, the Zutt were brought from al-Baṭīḥa to Baghdad by the hand of ʿAjīf.

The Year 220

Ṣāliḥ ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muhammad conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, ʿAffān ibn Muslim died on Thursday, with eleven nights remaining in the month of Rabīʿ al-Ākhir, as did ʿUthmān ibn al-Haytham the caller to prayer, Abu Ḥudhayfa, Abu ʿUmar al-Ḍarīr, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maslama ibn Qaʿnab.

In it, al-Ḥasan ibn Muṣʿab raided the lands of the Byzantines, but suffered a setback and was defeated, and most of his army was captured.

The Year 221

Muhammad ibn Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, ʿĪsā ibn Abān the judge died.

The Year 222

Muhammad ibn Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā conducted the pilgrimage.

In it, Muslim ibn Ibrāhīm and al-Walīd ibn Hishām ibn Qaḥdham died.

And in it, the campaign of al-Afshin against the infidel Babak took place. He defeated him, killed men in his lands, seized his camp, and freed the Muslim captives who were in his lands. Babak fled, then was captured as a prisoner. A letter announcing the victory was sent to the Commander of the Faithful.

The Year 223

Muḥammad ibn Dāwūd led the pilgrimage.

In it, Ḥarmī ibn Ḥafṣ, Muḥammad ibn Kathīr, Mūsā ibn Ismāʿīl, Abū ʿAbd Allāh al-Khuzāʿī, Abū Umayya, and Abū Ṣāliḥ, the secretary of al-Layth, died.

In the year 223, the Commander of the Faithful, Abū Isḥāq al-Muʿtaṣim bi-llāh, set out with a large force and advanced deep into Byzantine territory. He sent al-Afshin Khayḏar ibn Kāwus, who met the tyrant at a place called Lūrla, two miles from it. He defeated him and killed four thousand of his men. The Commander of the Faithful marched on and encamped at Amorium. Al-Afshin joined him, and the Commander of the Faithful took the city by force on Tuesday, with thirteen nights remaining in the month of Ramaḍān. He inflicted a great slaughter there, took many captives, and ruined and demolished the city. Before the Commander of the Faithful reached Amorium, he had destroyed the villages along his route, and the Byzantines fled in every direction.

In it, Ancyra was destroyed. Then the Commander of the Faithful set out on his return on Saturday, with nine nights remaining in the month of Ramaḍān. He had four thousand of the prisoners beheaded, and continued killing prisoners and burning as he journeyed, until he reached Muslim territory.

In it, the infidel Babak was brought to the Commander of the Faithful as a prisoner. The Commander of the Faithful ordered his hands and feet cut off, then had him beheaded and crucified. This was in Ṣafar of the year 223.

In it, al-Ḥasan ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan al-ʿAnbarī, judge of Baṣra, died.

In it, Aḥmad ibn Riyāḥ was appointed judge of Baṣra.

The Year 224

Muḥammad ibn Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā led the pilgrimage.

In it, ʿĀrim ibn al-Faḍl died, and Sulaymān ibn Ḥarb died several months after him; also ʿAmr ibn Marzūq and Ibrāhīm ibn Suwayd.

The Year 225

Aḥmad ibn Dāwūd also led the pilgrimage. In it, Aḥmad ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan al-ʿAnbarī was removed from the governorship of the sea of Baṣra. The year 226.

Muḥammad ibn Dāwūd also led the pilgrimage.

In it, Aḥmad ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan was removed from the governorship of the sea of Baṣra.

The Year 227

In it, al-Muʿtaṣim bi-llāh, Commander of the Faithful, died on Thursday, with eleven nights remaining in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal of the year 227.

Al-Wāthiq, Hārūn ibn the Commander of the Faithful, was given the oath of allegiance.

In it, Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, Abū al-Walīd, the man with the ṭayālisah, Sahl ibn Bakkār, Aḥmad ibn Yūnus, one of the people of Kūfa, and ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Wahhāb al-Ḥajabī, one of the people of Baṣra, died.

Al-Mutawakkil Jaʿfar ibn al-Muʿtaṣim, Commander of the Faithful, led the pilgrimage.

The Year 228

In it, Musaddad ibn Masrahad, ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn Ḥafṣ, known as Ibn ʿĀʾishah, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Mubārak, client of Banū Taym ibn Thaʿlabah, and Dāwūd ibn Sulaymān died.

Muḥammad ibn Dāwūd led the pilgrimage.

Ibrāhīm ibn Hāshim conducted a campaign in the sea of Baṣra on behalf of Aḥmad ibn Riyāḥ.

The Year 229

Muḥammad ibn Dāwūd led the pilgrimage.

Ibrāhīm ibn Hāshim conducted a campaign in the sea of Baṣra on behalf of Aḥmad ibn Riyāḥ.

The Year 230

Muḥammad ibn Dāwūd led the pilgrimage.

Ibrāhīm ibn Hāshim conducted a campaign in the sea of Baṣra, reached the outer parts of the land of Sarisht, burned some of its villages, and took captives.

The Year 231

Muḥammad ibn Dāwūd led the pilgrimage.

In it, Muḥammad ibn al-Minhal al-Ḍarīr, transmitter of Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ; Umayya ibn Basṭām al-ʿĀʾishī; Khalaf ibn Sālim al-Makhzamī; Hārūn ibn Maʿrūf, one of the people of Baghdad; and ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn Asmāʾ died.

Baqī ibn Makhlad said: In it, Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bukayr died on Saturday, with twelve nights remaining in Ṣafar. His son ʿAbd Allāh informed me of this.

In it, the ships of the volunteer fighters were wrecked in the sea of Baṣra between Janābah and Sīnīz, and some of the volunteer fighters suffered casualties there.

In it, the exchange of captives with the Byzantines took place. The governor was Aḥmad ibn Saʿīd ibn Salm al-Bāhilī. He ransomed about four thousand Muslim men and roughly six hundred Muslim women and children from the Byzantines.

The Year 232

In it, Muḥammad ibn Dāwūd led the pilgrimage.

Translation notes

Prologue: how the years are counted
  • The transmitted name ناقرة بن خالد is unclear; rendered Nāqira ibn Khālid without emendation. from the birth of the Messenger of God ﷺ and his death.
Prologue: how the years are counted
  • The phrase محيلا أعقله is obscure; rendered cautiously as indicating an age at which Qubāth could recognize the elephant dung. - The question about أكبر is retained as an age question in this chronological context; the respectful reply is translated as greater, contrasting with older in years. The Arabic permits wordplay.
The Year 1The age of the Prophet
  • The Arabic passage after «فَعَرَفْنَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ» is visibly garbled and duplicated («قيل ذلك فركبه الناس فلم ن... فركبه الناس»). The English ellipsis signals a gap, but the missing wording cannot be established from this page.
The Year 1The age of the Prophet
  • The passage about Abū Bakr shading the Prophet is corrupt in the supplied Arabic around “we recognized … then people crowded around him”; the missing wording cannot be recovered from this text.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • Al-dhabḥah is an uncertain illness term in this account; it is retained in transliteration.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • Abū Ayyūb’s date wording is rendered literally: the source gives alternatives between elapsed nights and nights remaining.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The phrase about Abū Sufyān’s return repeats “he returned,” and “the remnants of Quraysh” is a literal rendering of fall Quraysh.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • “Mubashshir ibn ʿAbd al-Mundhir Zubayr” has unclear name boundaries in the supplied text; “they say Zanbar” is retained as the reported alternative form.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The final phrase is cut off in the supplied unit; only the words present are translated.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The opening words, “During his expedition … he sent,” continue a sentence from the preceding page; the supplied text does not identify a new subject.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The first report’s wording is incomplete in Arabic: “that the Messenger … toward the Sanctuary of Jerusalem.” The bracketed “[was]” marks the omitted predicate without supplying a specific verb. - In the report from Muʿādh ibn Jabal, the Arabic says «أن رسول الله ... نحو بيت المقدس» without an explicit verb before «نحو بيت المقدس». The translation’s bracketed “[was] toward” preserves the gap, but the intended predicate cannot be established from this page alone.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The Arabic phrase describing ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr as the “first newborn” contains an apparent textual corruption; the translation preserves its apparent sense.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The opening phrase “a mine in the Ḥijāz” continues the previous report. The heading-like Arabic “ḥaṣala Banū Qaynuqāʿ” is unclear; rendered here as “The affair of Banū Qaynuqāʿ.” - The heading «حصل بني قينقاع» is grammatically unclear and may be corrupt. The following text calls it «أمر بني قينقاع» (“the affair of Banū Qaynuqāʿ”), but the heading itself cannot be corrected confidently.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The phrase “a black, patterned mantle” renders marṭ murḥal; the precise pattern or textile description is uncertain.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The tribal name الحُبلى is unclear in this reading; it is transliterated rather than identified.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The wording about Usāma al-Aʿwar’s patronymic is explicitly given in two forms: ibn ʿUbayd and, it is said, ibn Zayd.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The Arabic name reads «خالد بن ال الليثي», while the English supplies «Khālid ibn al-Bukayr al-Laythī». Since «al-Bukayr» is not present in this page's Arabic and the name is unclear here, the English name cannot be corrected with confidence from the supplied text.
The Year 2The age of the Prophet
  • The reading الرجيع ماء لهذيل at the start of the sentence is awkward; it is rendered as al-Rajīʿ, a watering place belonging to Hudhayl.
The Year 4The age of the Prophet
  • The noun الشَّرَكَ in the account of Khubayb’s body is unclear; translated as “the cord.” The phrase صعدت خشبته is also ambiguous, rendered literally as climbing the wooden structure.
The Year 6The age of the Prophet
  • The wording “or to that of a cousin of his” preserves the source’s alternative account of whose share Juwayriyyah fell to.
The Year 7The age of the Prophet
  • “Seven heads” translates the source’s literal measure (sabʿat arʾūs); the text does not specify the persons counted.
The Year 7The age of the Prophet
  • The name “Maʿrū” appears truncated in the source; the commonly expected fuller form is not supplied here.
The Year 8The age of the Prophet
  • The first sentence’s referent for “men” is carried over from the preceding text; the wording here does not identify them further.
The Year 8The age of the Prophet
  • The reading “ʿUmayr ibn Wadhfah” is uncertain because the name is unusual in this form. - The wording رميمة رمية بها is unusual, so the precise nature of the injury remains uncertain.
The Year 8The age of the Prophet
  • The wording about ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Umayyah is damaged or unusual (“from a stone wound, a stone with which he was struck”); the translation gives its apparent sense without certainty.
The Year 11The age of the Prophet
  • The report’s wording “seventy days and nights” is unusual, but is translated as written.
The Year 11The age of the Prophet
  • The referent of the death clause following ʿAttāb’s appointment is not supplied beyond the Arabic pronoun; the Abū Bakr clause is preserved without harmonizing the chronology.
The Year 11The age of the Prophet
  • The text gives “Qays ibn al-Aṣamm al-Asadī” without an explicit assignment phrase; the listing is retained as transmitted.
The Year 11The age of the Prophet
  • The phrase “Ṣaʿṣaʿa” continues the preceding page’s “Banū ʿĀmir ibn”; “outlying groups of Hawāzin” renders the ambiguous عجز هوازن.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The phrase after “had what befell my father” is corrupt in the supplied Arabic; “it would have crushed them” is an uncertain reading. - The wording in the report attributed to ʿĀʾisha, «فلو نزل بالجبال الراسيات ما نزل بأبي لها ضها», appears corrupt. The English says it would have crushed the mountains, but the supplied Arabic does not securely establish that wording.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The phrase concerning Abū Bakr’s instructions is textually unclear; the supplied wording appears to join “instructed Khālid” with “those he sent.” - The phrase «عهد أبو بكر إلى خالد وأمرأته الذين وجه إلى الردة» is unclear as supplied. The translation interprets it as Abū Bakr instructing Khālid and the forces he sent, but the Arabic wording does not securely support that reading.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The wording “Could Khālid have done anything beyond interpreting [the matter] and being mistaken?” is a compact, somewhat elliptical statement in the source.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The poetic phrase about the three nursing she-camels and the young camel’s trail is obscure. - The direction and subject of the foster-nursing image (أظآر ثلاث روائم) require specialist verse review; reversal to three foster-young is not established.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The supplied poem has an obscure reading in the phrase translated “steady rain” and at the end of the verse; “castor-oil plant” renders خَرْوَعَا tentatively.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The wording of Thābit’s rebuke at the start of this unit is unusual; the supplied Arabic reads “your peers” (أقرانكم).
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • “A little, pale, short man” renders the diminutive descriptive words ruwayjil, usayfir, and uḥaymish; their precise intended shades are uncertain.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The source gives two alternative figures for the Qurʾan bearers: fifty or thirty.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The opening wording, “Abu Maʿshar said—and al-Hakam … in Abu Maʿshar’s account,” is syntactically unclear in the supplied text.
The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The wording after “Al-ʿAlaʾ attacked them by night” is compressed and unclear; the translation preserves the stated night attack and severe killing without supplying a missing object.
The Year 12The caliphate of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq
  • The name printed as ʿAnāb ibn Asīd may be a variant or corrupt reading; retained as written. - The final phrase about the dogs is truncated at the unit boundary. - Unapplied proposed correction: “From the water” is not in this page’s Arabic. The continuation on the next page specifies that the dogs lapped from vessels of gold and silver, so the added words are also inaccurate. in vessels of gold and silver. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: “The people of Nahr al-Marʾa made terms with him for twelve thousand dirhams, and he departed from them.” ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad said: “He made terms with him from Raʾs al-Fahraj to Nahr al-Marʾa.” Al-Walīd ibn Hishām, from his father, from his grandfather, [reported] that Khālid entered Maysān and obtained spoils and captives there from the people of the villages. Ṭamāhīj, the mistress of Nahr al-Marʾa, made terms with him. Then he returned to Baṣra, then set out toward the Sawād, taking the route by Kaskar, Zand, and Rad. He appointed Quṭba ibn Qatāda al-Sadūsī over Baṣra. ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad, Abū ʿUbayda, Abū al-Yaqẓān, and others said: Ibn Ṣalūtā made terms concerning Ullays and the villages of the Sawād in Ṣafar of the year twelve for one thousand dinars. Someone who heard Yaḥyā ibn Zakariyyā ibn Abī Zāʾida from his father, from al-Shaʿbī, narrated to me: He said, “The people of Ullays made terms with Khālid on Saturday, three days into Rajab, in the year twelve, for one thousand dinars.” He conquered Hurmuzjird, Nahr al-Malik, and Bārūsmā. ʿAbd al-Masīḥ ibn ʿAmr ibn Buqayla and Iyās ibn Qubayṣa al-Ṭāʾī made terms with him for ninety thousand. Then he set out for al-Anbār, whose people made terms with him. He sent al-Muthannā ibn Ḥāritha al-Shaybānī to the market of Baghdād, and he raided it.
